A row is persisted only when something actually changed (URL, tools, or name).

**Write path and deferral contract (M21 F01 L03)**: `update_my_description(text, apply)` / `update_my_system_prompt(text, apply)` — `apply` is `"next_turn"` or `"next_session"`, whole-value replacement (no append/partial edit; a future `AgentConfig` field stays unwritable because no tool addresses it, DD6's "by construction" contract). Both tools follow the same order: resolve caller (`_calling_agent`, DD9) → validate (`_validate_write_input`, LD5: non-empty `str`, ≤ 64 KiB UTF-8-encoded, `apply` one of the two literals — else a plain `ValueError`, the module's one error mechanism) → `SQLiteAgentStore.load → model_copy(update=…) → save` (persist FIRST, always — `LD2`) → if `apply == "next_turn"`, `pending.record(agent_name, **{field: text})` → return a truthful result (`applied_to_store`, `effective`, `field`, `length`, a one-sentence explanation) that never claims the running conversation already changed.

- **Why persist-first matters (LD2/LD3)**: `next_session` needs no further machinery — `_get_or_restore_session` always rebuilds `Agent` from the stored row, so a rebuild picks up the revision whether or not a pending entry exists. If the live session's `Agent` object is rebuilt mid-turn (a rejoin), the pending entry becomes a no-op — moot, because the rebuild already read the fresh row. This resolves the flight's open question about a pending revision surviving a mid-turn rebuild: it doesn't need to, because the store already has it.
- **Why `next_turn` needs a deferral at all (DD7/LD1)**: `Agent._run_iterations` re-binds `request_messages = self.messages` INSIDE its `while` loop, and tool dispatch happens in that same loop — an in-place mutation of `messages[0]` from inside the write tool would land on the NEXT ITERATION OF THE SAME TURN, which the mission explicitly rules out. The write tool therefore only persists and records; nothing about the LIVE `Agent` object changes until an explicit hook fires. Contextvars set around `agent.step` (`current_chat_session_id`, `current_tool_call_id`) are NOT visible to the write tool — it runs inside the loopback request's own task — so the deferral is keyed by AGENT NAME (`the_one/self_configuration/pending.py`'s `_pending: dict[str, dict[str, str]]`), not by session or contextvar.
- **The hook, and EXACTLY where it is wired**: `pending.apply_pending_revision(agent_name, agent)` pops the agent's pending entry (if any) and (`await`ed; M27 F1) mutates `agent.system_prompt` (recomposed: base + persona + enabled rules, see "Rules and prompt composition (M27 F1)") plus replaces `agent.messages[0]` with a fresh `Message.system(...)` — the same two-line pattern `Agent.reset()` uses, minus the history wipe (`description` has no live-conversation counterpart to mutate; it is store-only). It is called from EXACTLY two sites in `the_one/interfaces/web/app.py`, both non-paused turn-SETTLE points: inside `_process_and_broadcast`'s `if not paused:` block (before its `done` broadcast) and inside `_resume_paused_session`'s `if not re_paused:` block (before ITS `done` broadcast — NOT the unconditional save/usage tail after it, which also runs when the resume RE-pauses; a re-pause is not a settle). Both call sites pass `session.agent_name or "default"` — the `or "default"` is LOAD-BEARING: `Session.agent_name` is stored as `None` for the default agent while the pending registry and `_calling_agent` key on the literal string `"default"`; a skip-on-`None` guard would silently degrade `next_turn` to `next_session` for single-agent installs.
- **Merge and consumption semantics**: `pending.record()` MERGES into any existing entry for the same agent — two different-field revisions (a description AND a system-prompt revision) made in the same turn both apply at the SAME settle; two same-field revisions in one turn both persist in order, and the LAST one wins everywhere (store and pending entry alike). `apply_pending_revision` POPS the entry — a second settle for the same agent with nothing new pending is a no-op. `pending.py` also logs an INFO line with the agent name, the revised field(s), and a per-agent-per-process revision COUNT at record time — the leg does not rate-limit revisions (Flight 2's revision log is the real mitigation), so this count is the only visibility into a runaway self-revision loop today.
- **Reachable only through the confined surface**: same gate confinement, ownership guards, and per-tool operator controls as the read tools (see above) — nothing new to the security boundary. The residual risk this leg does NOT mitigate — an injected agent using these tools to shed its own guardrails — is recorded in `SECURITY-MODEL.md`'s "Self-configuration surface" section as a LIVE risk as of this leg, not a deferred one.

**Approval mode + revision queue (M21 F02 L02)**: the operator gate on the write path. Each agent carries `AgentConfig.self_revision_requires_approval: bool = False`, persisted in the v35 `agents.self_revision_requires_approval INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` column. It is **operator-owned and agent-unreachable by construction**: no write tool addresses it (the catalog shape, DD6), the `get_my_configuration` projection omits it (an explicit allowlist), and it is set ONLY by an immediate-post Studio control via a **targeted** `UPDATE agents SET self_revision_requires_approval=? WHERE name=?` (`SQLiteAgentStore.set_self_revision_requires_approval`) — never a full-row `save`, so the toggle dodges the whole-row last-writer-wins window entirely. **DD2 HIGH:** `update_agent` (`api.py`) preserves the field when a Studio Save omits it (Studio rebuilds its payload from the DOM and never sends it), mirroring the `mcp_overrides` preserve — otherwise every unrelated Save would silently reset approval mode to `False`.
- **The branch (DD3)**: `_apply_revision` (`surface.py`) reads `config.self_revision_requires_approval` after loading config. When **True**, it calls `AgentRevisionRepository.upsert_pending_revision(...)` (enqueue one `pending`/`self` row, dedup one per agent+field) and returns `{applied_to_store: False, effective: "pending_operator_approval", field, length, message}` — a truthful, non-retry-inducing result — WITHOUT `save` or `pending.record`. The store and live conversation are untouched; an approved revision propagates next-session only (the proposing turn's `apply` is moot and never replayed). When **False**, the existing Leg 1 persist+log path runs unchanged.
- **New `AgentRevisionRepository` methods (`repositories.py`)**: `count_pending(agent_name)` (per-agent), `upsert_pending_revision(...)` (dedup, returns `(revision, created)`), `_transition`/`mark_applied`/`mark_dismissed` (idempotent-on-self-target, `ValueError`→409 on any other non-`pending` state, single UPDATE stamping `status`+`resolved_at`), `update_base_value(id, base)` (override re-capture), `supersede_pending(agent_name)` (bulk, DD8). The shape mirrors the connector subsystem's `PendingApprovalRepository` (peer-approval queue), removed in the Mission 23 connector reset. No revision-table migration — Leg 1's v34 already ships all four statuses and the `(agent_name, status)` index.
- **Endpoints (`api.py`, beside the self-config routes)**: `PUT /agents/{name}/self-configuration/approval-mode` (`{requires_approval: bool}` → targeted UPDATE → `{requires_approval}`); `GET .../revisions` (name-scoped, newest-first, full rows — `base_value`/`proposed_value` are plaintext description/system_prompt, safe and needed for the decision); `GET .../revisions/pending-count` (`{count}`); `POST .../revisions/{id}/dismiss`; `POST .../revisions/{id}/approve` (`?override=bool`). Every revision route enforces a **cross-ID 404 guard** (`revision.agent_name != {name}` → 404, never leaking that the id exists under another agent) and maps the repo's `ValueError` to **409**.
- **DD4 fail-closed approve**: on a `pending` row, compare `base_value` (the field's value the agent saw at capture) to the CURRENT stored value. Unchanged → apply (`load → model_copy({field: proposed}) → save`) + `mark_applied`. Drifted and no `override` → **409** returning `{error: "stale", current_value, proposed_value, base_value}`, writing nothing. Drifted with `override=true` → `update_base_value(id, current)` first, then apply + `mark_applied`. Idempotent no-op on an already-`applied` row; 409 on dismissed/superseded. **The override branch is intentionally NOT transactional** (`update_base_value` → `save` → `mark_applied` are three commits); a `save` failure after `update_base_value` leaves a still-`pending`, still-re-approvable row — do NOT reorder so `mark_applied` precedes `save`.
- **DD8 supersede-on-disable**: `manage.disable` takes an optional `revisions_repo` kwarg (threaded from the DELETE endpoint) and calls `supersede_pending(agent_name)` **above** the `row is None` early-return, so a disable ALWAYS clears the queue — a revision proposed under a since-revoked surface becomes `superseded` and can never be approved (a follow-up approve is 409). `manage.status` returns `requires_approval` from the loaded config in EVERY config-loaded branch (enabled / row-None / orphaned) so the Studio tab renders the toggle consistently regardless of surface state.
- **Studio UI** (`self-configuration-tab.js` and its `self-config-{status,revisions,history,external}.js` modules + `studio.js` + `studio/agents-list.js` + `agent-editor.js`): an immediate-post approval-mode toggle (mirrors the enable toggle — NOT a `collectFormData()` field), a pending-revision queue rendering approve/dismiss cards (each shows base→proposed on the clean path too, one-click clean approve, a 409-staleness panel with current-vs-proposed and an explicit override button), and a per-agent pending count. On any action the tab relies on `_refreshAgentAfterSelfConfigurationChange` (which re-fetches and refetches its revisions locally (M31 F2 L10a), so the resolved row leaves the pending list) rather than an in-place swap. Every new control carries a `data-testid`. **On the control library (M31 F2 L10c; zero sinks, zero allowance rows):** the enable and approval switches are `App.ui.toggle` (label visible beside the switch; the testid is on the visually hidden `role=switch` input, so tests click the track through `tests/browser/_self_config.py::click_switch`); sections are `App.ui.section({titleTag: 'h3'})`; every status, error and count line is `App.ui.status({led: false, ...})` (alert lines `role: 'alert'`; the history entries' meta line shows the LED, `live: 'off'`); empty notices are `App.ui.emptyState({titleTag: 'p', variant: 'plain'})`; every button is `App.ui.button`; the grant input is `App.ui.textInput` with a plain `<label for>`; revoke is `App.ui.confirm` on a library danger button; the history is an `App.ui.disclosure` (plain variant, M31 F3 L03a). **The copy-once token reveal is `App.ui.copyField`**, created with no value and filled by `setValue(token)` (a property write; the token is never in an attribute, `title`, `data-*` or a log line, pinned by `test_token_never_written_to_an_attribute`), `aria-describedby` set on both its input and its button, announcing `Token copied to the clipboard.` through its own visually hidden `role=status` node (not the status line, which a `liveEl` would clobber). The token is cleared by `setValue('')` plus hiding the reveal on dismiss, disable, `destroy`, and **tab deactivation**: the handle gains `deactivate()`, called by the controller's `setActiveTab` when the active tab leaves `self-configuration` and by Studio's area `deactivate` (`controller.deactivateSelfConfig`).

**Restore (M21 F02 L03)**: operator-direct rollback of a description / system_prompt to any earlier value the field actually held.
- **Endpoint**: `POST /agents/{name}/self-configuration/revisions/{id}/restore`, query `side: Literal["proposed", "base"] = "proposed"` (a `Literal`, so FastAPI auto-422s a bad value — the enum analog of approve's `override` query param). Same cross-ID/unknown-id 404 guard as approve/dismiss. **LD1**: the target must be an `applied` revision (only `applied` rows name a value the field genuinely took — a `pending` was never applied, a `dismissed` was declined, a `superseded` was orphaned); a non-applied target → **409**, store + log untouched. **LD2**: `side` selects the value node — `proposed` reinstates the target row's after-image, `base` its before-image; `side=base` on the earliest applied row reaches the **original** pre-first-revision value (never a `proposed_value`, only the earliest `base_value`), and a mid-timeline `base_value` reaches any out-of-band operator-set value (a direct `PUT /agents/{name}` edit leaves no revision row).
- **DD5 operator-direct**: restore **never consults `self_revision_requires_approval`** and never queues — the operator is the approval authority. It mirrors approve's clean apply arm (`_load_agent_or_404` → `config.model_copy(update={field: reinstated})` → `store.save`), then `append`s a NEW `source='restore'`, `status='applied'` row with `restored_from={id}`, `base_value` = the pre-restore current value, `resolved_at == created_at` — an append-only record, never a pointer rewind. Restore-to-current still logs (an honest record; `base_value == proposed_value` is acceptable). **No new repo method** — reuses `AgentRevisionRepository.append` (already accepts `source`/`status`/`restored_from`) and needs no migration.
- **No agent-facing restore tool** — the write allowlist (tool catalog shape) is unchanged, so the agent cannot restore itself. The mission's "by construction" boundary held: rollback is operator-only.
- **Studio UI**: the Self-configuration tab renders a **revision-history** view (the full `GET .../revisions` list, newest-first, reusing the queue's fetch — the queue is the `status==='pending'` subset, the history the whole list) below the pending queue. Each entry shows field / before→after / timestamp / source (`self`/`restore`) / status. The history renders **one Restore control per distinct value-node** — the dedup of `base_value` + `proposed_value` across all `applied` rows (walked oldest-first so the earliest holder of each value wins) — each restoring via an appropriate `(revision_id, side)`, so every value the field held is restorable with no duplicate buttons. `onRestore(name, id, side)` → `POST .../restore?side=...` → `_refreshAgentAfterSelfConfigurationChange`. New `data-testid`s registered in `docs/ui-testid-registry.md`.

### External-client credential (M21 F03)

Everything above is the agent's OWN `agt` principal reaching its surface over the loopback. Flight 3 adds a SECOND principal on the same surface: an **external client** (a script, a CI job, a separate service) the operator explicitly grants access to tune one agent. The two principals share the `/mcp/self-configuration` mount, the four-tool catalog, and every input/ownership bound; they differ only in credential lifecycle and write provenance.

- **A distinct `ext`-prefixed, per-grant credential (`external_credentials.py`, DD1).** Each operator grant mints its OWN `ext_<id>.<secret>` credential — a different prefix from both `loc` and `agt`, so a token's type is legible at a glance and the other registered credential sources reject it on prefix mismatch, falling through to `ExternalAgentCredentialSource`. Resolution walks the same synchronous seam as `agt`: a process-local `identifier → (source_id, secret)` registry (`external_credentials._ext_registry`), rebuilt at startup from the store and mutated live on mint/revoke/rotate — no per-request DB read. Unlike the single per-agent `agt` credential, an agent can carry MANY external grants at once, each independently revocable.
- **A real store, not a surface-row secret (`agent_external_credentials`, schema v36, DD2/DD8).** The `agt` credential hides inside its surface row's encrypted `secrets["Authorization"]`; the external credentials get their own secrets-bearing table (`identifier` PK, `source_id`, Fernet-encrypted `secret`, `label`, `created_at`), fronted by `AgentExternalCredentialRepository` (encryptor-bearing). The FK keys on the surface ROW — `source_id REFERENCES mcp_sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` — not on `agent_name`, so an agent rename (row id stable) is free and **surface-disable / agent-delete auto-revokes every external grant via the FK cascade** (DD9): an external principal never outlives the operator closing the surface. Hard-revoke is a row DELETE (no `revoked_at` tombstone); the audit of "which external principal tuned this agent" lives in the revision log, not here.
- **Secret-free projection; secret returned exactly once at mint (DD2).** `list_for_source` (behind `GET .../external-credentials`) projects `{identifier, label, created_at}` only — never the secret. The `ext_<id>.<secret>` token is returned ONCE, by `mint` (`POST .../external-credentials`) and by `rotate`, in the response's `token` field; no read path returns it again (mirrors the operator credential's mint-once shape, and the Studio UI's copy-once reveal enforces it in the browser).
- **A second confined `CredentialSource` on the SAME mount (`EXTERNAL_SOURCE_NAME = "self-configuration-external"`).** `CONFINED_SOURCE_PATHS` maps this source name to `MOUNT_PATH` alongside the `agt` source, so the external principal inherits Flight 1's confinement UNCHANGED — no new enforcement code, one extra map entry. `CredentialGateMiddleware` refuses any request whose path is outside `/mcp/self-configuration` with **403** and refuses every WebSocket upgrade outright. This is the **governance invariant**: an external principal reaches ONLY the four confined MCP tools; the operator control plane (`/api/...`, admin surfaces, every other mount) answers it with 403, exactly as it does the agent's own `agt` credential.
- **Per-principal revoke / rotate (`api.py`, DD7)** — contrast the operator credential's rotate-EVERYTHING. `revoke` DELETEs one row and evicts one registry entry (the next request bearing that token is 401, no restart); `rotate` DELETEs the old and mints a fresh credential for the SAME label/agent, returning the new token once and 401-ing the old immediately. Both enforce a cross-ID 404 guard (`cred.source_id` must equal `name`'s surface row id) so a grant can never be revoked/rotated through another agent's route, and NEVER touch the agent's `agt` credential or any sibling external grant. `mint`/`rotate` 409 when the surface is not enabled (they issue a live credential); `revoke` works on an orphaned (detached-but-present) surface too, so an operator can always tear a grant down.
- **External-write provenance in the revision log (schema v37, DD3).** The surface routes an external caller by `caller.source == EXTERNAL_SOURCE_NAME`: `_calling_principal` returns `(agent_name, "external", caller.identifier)` vs `(agent_name, "self", None)` for the agent's own write. Every revision the write path appends carries `source` (`agent_revisions.source` CHECK widened to `'self' | 'restore' | 'external'`) plus a nullable `source_principal` = the `ext_` identifier for an external write (NULL for `self`/`restore`) — so the log answers "which external principal wrote this."
- **External writes honor the SAME per-agent approval flag.** The write path checks `config.self_revision_requires_approval` regardless of source, so an external client's proposal is held in the pending queue (never silently applied) exactly like a `self` write when approval mode is on — the operator gate on the mission's central write-risk covers the external principal with no special-casing.
- **Operational trace: log lines, not a tombstone (Flight 01 Leg 2, Q2).** Mint/revoke/rotate each emit one INFO line on `the_one.interfaces.web.api`'s module logger (agent name, `ext_` identifier, label, principal — never the secret); `manage.disable`/`manage.cascade_delete` each emit one INFO line on `the_one.self_configuration.manage`'s logger at the two points a credential row disappears without an explicit revoke (surface disable, agent delete), carrying the evicted identifiers and their count — the row is already gone by then (FK cascade), so the cascade line can't carry a label the way the mint line does. A `revoked_at` tombstone was considered and rejected: the FK cascade would bypass it (recording explicit revokes but not cascades), and a queryable audit trail is properly an events table — a separate initiative from this flight, alongside the Advisory logging sweep, which inherits this log-line field set as its convention.

### Rules and prompt composition (M27 F1)

An agent's system prompt is composed in ONE place from layers, in this fixed order: **base** (the platform prompt, `the_one/agent/platform_prompt.py::render_platform`, built from ordered `Section`s with no renderer-inserted separator; Flight 2 leg 1b replaced the old `BASE_PROMPT`/`CHAT_ONLY_PROMPT` constants with byte-identical output), **persona** (`AgentConfig.system_prompt`), **rules**, **notes** (`None` when off; M27 F2 fills it, see "Notes store and prompt layer") and, since M28 F1 L2, a trailing **date line** (see "current_time and the date layer (M28)"); notes are no longer the final layer. Layers join with `\n\n---\n\n`; an empty layer is omitted entirely (no stray separator, no empty heading), and with no rules the output is byte-identical to the pre-M27 `{base}\n\n---\n\n{persona}`. `build_system_prompts` (`prompt_layers.py`) composes BOTH prompts at build time (`SystemPrompts(tool_capable, tool_free)`: the same persona, rules, notes and date layers on the tool-capable and the tool-free platform text); `build_system_prompt` is the `str` wrapper for the tool-capable one. The factory passes the tool-free one to `Agent(degraded_system_prompt=...)`, and `_build_degraded_messages` sends it (or `system_prompt` verbatim when unset) with no prefix recovery, so chat-only models keep the rules too. `AgentConfig.get_system_prompt` is gone (a second composition that only tests called).

**Platform prompt conditions (Flight 2 leg 1c, DD3/DD7/DD8).** `Capabilities` (`tools`, `knowledge`, `notes`, `rules`, `time_tool`, `exec_tools`) is derived from the built registry by `capabilities_from_registry` (`kb__*` tool present -> `knowledge`; `shell`/`python_exec` present -> `exec_tools`). Two conditions switch text: the **knowledge-base line** inside `tool_discovery` (candidate 1: a single `KB_LINE` appended after `NO_KB_TOOL_DISCOVERY` only when a KB tool is attached; both pinned verbatim by tests; the line is also a `Fragment` listed for Settings only, not a `Section`, so `sections_for` still tiles the text exactly) and the **`execution` section** (the box / `host_access` bullet), which applies only on `tools and exec_tools`. A disabled or absent KB tool means no KB phrases. The `closing` section was removed in candidate 1. The tool-free variant ignores every other capability. **Tool-free notes layer:** `compose_system_prompt` and `render_notes_block` take `tools: bool = True`; `build_system_prompts` and `pending.py`'s fallback pass `tools=False` for the tool-free prompt, whose notes layer uses `NOTES_HEADING_TOOL_FREE` (candidate 1: identical to `NOTES_HEADING`, which no longer carries save/update/forget instructions, still carrying "(unverified)"). The tool-free prompt also gets its own date line (`tool_available=False`), so it never names `current_time`. `parse_notes_block` reads only the tool-capable `messages[0]`. **Connector seeding (DD7):** `AgentFactory.create` seeds the connector tool pair (`send_to_platform_channel`, `find_platform_destination`) into the starting schemas only when some connector's binding names the agent (`config.default_agent` or any `channel_map` value), via `the_one/db/repositories.py::connector_bindings(conn)` / `agent_names_in_use(conn)` (fetched once per `create()`); `current_time` stays seeded for everyone, and an unmapped agent still finds the pair through `search_tools`. If the bindings cannot be read the factory fails open (seeds the pair). `ConnectorDirectoryRepository.search` uses the same helper, so a malformed connector row (unparseable JSON, a non-dict config or `channel_map`, unhashable values) is now skipped with a log line instead of raising. **Accepted staleness:** an agent mapped to a connector after its web session started sees the pair seeded only when the conversation is rejoined. **`GET /api/info`** replaces `base_prompt` with `platform_sections: [{id, title, applies_when, text, variant}]` (`variant` "tools" with the all-on text plus the `knowledge` fragment row, or "tool_free" with its own rows) and adds `notes_prompt_example_tool_free`; Settings › Prompts renders a titled block per section (`platform-section-{variant}-{id}`, with `-applies-when` and `-text`).

- **Pure composer**: `the_one/agent/config.py::compose_system_prompt(base, persona, *, rules=(), notes=None)`. The rules layer is the DD5 text from `the_one/db/_rules.py` (`RULES_HEADING`: "Rules set by the operator for this agent. Follow every rule. If two rules conflict, the one listed first wins.") then `1. …`, `2. …` for the ENABLED rules in position order. A rule containing a newline is rendered as-is after its number.
- **Async loader**: `the_one/agent/prompt_layers.py::build_system_prompt(agent_name, persona, *, conn=None)` loads the agent's enabled rules (`AgentRuleRepository.list_for_agent(..., enabled_only=True)`) and calls the composer. `conn=None` resolves `the_one.db.connection.get_connection()` (imported lazily). An agent with no stored row has no rules. Callers: `AgentFactory.create` step 3 (passes its own `conn`; every web and connector build goes through it, and both persist `encode_messages(agent.messages)`, system message included) and `self_configuration/pending.py::apply_pending_revision`.
- **`apply_pending_revision` is `async`** (both `app.py` settle sites `await` it, keeping `session.agent_name or "default"`). A pending `system_prompt` entry still carries only the raw persona; the base and enabled rules are recomposed at apply time, which closes the old bug where a `next_turn` revision replaced the whole prompt with the bare persona. **Failure fallback**: if loading the rules raises (locked database, missing table), it logs a WARNING, applies `compose_system_prompt(render_platform(caps), persona)` with no rules and consumes the entry anyway, so the turn still sends `done`.
- **Schema v42 and renames**: `agent_rules(id, agent_name -> agents(name) ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE, text, enabled, position, created_at, updated_at)` with index `(agent_name, position)`. The same migration REBUILDS `agent_revisions` with `ON UPDATE CASCADE` on its `agent_name` foreign key (before this, renaming an agent with revision rows failed with `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`). The rebuild follows the v37 ceremony but declares the self-reference against the FINAL table name (`restored_from ... REFERENCES agent_revisions(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`, correct whether or not `legacy_alter_table` is on), and its guard reads `PRAGMA foreign_key_list(agent_revisions)`: it rebuilds only when the `agents` row has `on_update != 'CASCADE'`. `SQLiteAgentStore.rename` stays a plain `UPDATE agents SET name`; the cascade carries revisions and rules (it still updates `chat_sessions` by hand, which has no foreign key). **Any new agent-keyed table MUST declare `ON UPDATE CASCADE` on its `agents(name)` foreign key**, or rename breaks for agents that have rows in it. `SQLiteAgentStore.save` upserts (`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`), so a General-tab save cannot cascade-delete rules.
- **Repository and limits**: `AgentRuleRepository` (`repositories.py`) plus `the_one/db/_rules.py` (in `the_one.db` so the repository can import it without the `the_one.agent` import cycle): `RULE_MAX_CHARS = 500` (after trimming), `RULES_TOTAL_MAX_CHARS = 4000` (ALL the agent's rules, enabled or not, so enabling never fails). Each mutating method opens ONE `write_transaction` (not re-entrant) and checks the cap inside it. Positions are 0-based and dense after any delete or reorder. Errors: `RuleLimitError` / `RuleOrderError` (both carry a plain `detail`), `KeyError` for a missing or another agent's rule.
- **REST** (all behind the credential gate, all 404 for an unknown agent): `GET /api/agents/{name}/rules` -> `{rules, limits: {rule_chars, total_chars}}`; `POST` `{text, enabled?}` -> 201 with the rule; `PUT /rules/{id}` `{text?, enabled?}`; `DELETE /rules/{id}`; `PUT /rules/order` `{ids}` (an exact permutation else 422; returns `{rules, limits}`). A limit violation or an empty text is 422 with a plain-language `detail` stating the limit and the attempted size; a rule id of another agent is 404. `PUT /api/agents/{name}` never touches rules; rename carries them; delete removes them (FK cascade). Nothing agent-reachable writes rules: no tool or MCP source has "rule" in its name, the self-configuration write allowlist is still exactly `update_my_description` and `update_my_system_prompt`, and `get_my_configuration` returns the RAW persona, not the composed prompt.
- **Known limitations (accepted, DD2)**: (1) a self-revision that settles in an OPEN web conversation reloads the rules at that moment, so rules can reach an open conversation earlier than "applies to new and reopened chats" says; (2) an agent renamed while a web conversation is open settles under the OLD name and gets no rules until the next rejoin; (3) a pending `next_turn` entry is keyed by the old agent name, so it goes stale across a rename (the stored row is the source of truth and a rebuilt agent reads it fresh).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_prompt_layers.py` (repository, loader, real-factory builds, degraded path, codec round trip, AC18), `tests/test_agent_rules_api.py`, `TestSchemaV42` in `tests/test_db_migration.py`, `TestRulesAtSettle` in `tests/test_self_configuration_write.py`, and the connector half in `tests/test_connectors_inbound.py`. Tests that build the factory over an `AsyncMock` connection patch `AgentRuleRepository` (`tests/test_factory.py::_no_rules_repo`).

### Platform prompt (Mission 30 F2)

`the_one/agent/platform_prompt.py` is the one source of the text the platform puts before every agent.

- **Capabilities.** `Capabilities` (`tools`, `knowledge`, `notes`, `rules`, `time_tool`, `exec_tools`; `all_on()`) says what an agent can do. `capabilities_from_registry` derives it from the built tool registry; `tool_free_capabilities` is the chat-only form.
- **Sections and fragments.** `SECTIONS` is an ordered tuple of `Section(id, title, applies_when, ...)`: `identity`, `identity_tool_free`, `tool_discovery`, `tool_use`, `execution`, `tool_use_tail`, `formatting`. The renderer inserts no separator; each section carries its own leading whitespace. `sections_for(caps)` returns the sections that apply and tiles the rendered text exactly. `FRAGMENTS` (today the `knowledge` line) are text that sits inside a section and is listed for Settings only.
- **Two built prompts.** `render_platform(caps)` is the tool-capable text; `render_tool_free(caps)` is the text for a model without native tool support (no catalog, no `continue` semantics). `build_system_prompts` composes both with the same persona, rules, notes and date layers, and the factory passes the tool-free one as `Agent(degraded_system_prompt=...)`, which the loop sends on an ephemeral request copy.
- **Conditional text.** The knowledge-base line applies only when a `kb__*` tool is attached; the `execution` section only on `tools and exec_tools`. The tool-free variant ignores every other capability.
- **Tool-free layers.** The tool-free notes layer uses `NOTES_HEADING_TOOL_FREE` (no save instruction) and its date line is rendered with `tool_available=False`, so it never names `current_time`.
- **Connector tools.** `AgentFactory.create` seeds `send_to_platform_channel` and `find_platform_destination` into the starting schemas only for an agent a connector binding names (fails open if bindings cannot be read). An agent mapped to a connector after its web session began sees them only when the conversation is rejoined.
- **Consumers.** `GET /api/info` returns `platform_sections` (`variant` "tools" or "tool_free") plus the rules and notes examples; Settings › Prompts renders them as `platform-section-{variant}-{id}` blocks; the evals measure the same sections (see "Prompt evals"). A change to any of this text follows the convention in CLAUDE.md's "Prompt evals": an eval run compared against `evals/baselines/m30-platform.json`, with its token cost. See also "Rules and prompt composition (M27 F1)" for the layer order and the paragraph on platform prompt conditions.

### Notes store and prompt layer (M27 F2)

Leg 1 of Flight 2 lands the data model, the prompt contract and the operator REST surface. The agent-facing tools, the per-turn context and the taint computation are Leg 2 (see "Notes tools, turn context and provenance" below); the UI is Leg 3.

- **Schema v43**: `agent_notes(id, agent_name -> agents(name) ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE, number, text, untrusted, untrusted_sources (JSON), origin_kind CHECK web|connector|other, origin_session_id (NO FK, so retention purges never touch notes), origin_label, origin_platform, origin_peer_id, origin_peer_name, created_at, updated_at, updated_by CHECK agent|operator)` with `UNIQUE (agent_name, number)`. `agents` gains `notes_enabled INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` and `notes_next_number INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` (both ADD COLUMNs guarded by `PRAGMA table_info`). **The counter rule**: `notes_next_number` is NOT on `AgentConfig`, not read by `_row_to_config`, and not in `SQLiteAgentStore.save`'s column or `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` lists; only `AgentNoteRepository.create` touches it, inside its transaction, so a stale whole-row `save` cannot move it backwards and reuse a number. Numbers are never reused: `forget`, `delete` and `clear` leave the counter alone; deleting and recreating an agent starts again at 1 (new row). Rename and delete follow the cascade.
- **Switch**: `AgentConfig.notes_enabled` (default False), written by `save`, preserved by `update_agent` when the body omits it, defaulted False by `create_agent`, toggled by `SQLiteAgentStore.set_notes_enabled` (a targeted `UPDATE`, `KeyError` for an unknown agent). Operator-owned: `get_my_configuration` is a purpose-built projection that omits it (a test pins this). Other `save()` callers (`self_configuration/manage.py`) carry it through `model_copy` of a loaded config, with the same narrow whole-row race as the approval flag.
- **`the_one/db/_notes.py`** (in `the_one.db` beside `_rules.py`, so no `the_one.agent` cycle; it duck-types `.number/.text/.untrusted`): constants (`NOTE_MAX_CHARS=400`, `NOTES_TOTAL_MAX_CHARS=4000`, `NOTE_WRITES_PER_TURN=5` (used by Leg 2), `SOURCES_CAP=10`), `normalize_note_text` (`" ".join(text.split())` then removal of remaining `Cc` characters such as NUL; zero-width characters are left alone), `dedup_key` (casefold, trailing `.`/`!` stripped), the errors `NoteLimitError` / `NoteDuplicateError` (with `.number`) / `NotesDisabledError` (each with a `.detail` that gives sizes or a number, never note text), `NOTES_HEADING` and `render_notes_block`. (This supersedes the flight's `the_one/notes/limits.py`; the `the_one/notes/` package holds the provider and tools.)
- **`AgentNoteRepository`** (`repositories.py`; model `AgentNote` in `db/models.py`): `list_for_agent` (by number; LEFT JOINs `chat_sessions` so `origin_label` is the live title while the chat exists, else the stored label, else "Untitled chat", with `origin_exists`), `get` / `get_by_number` (return `None` on a miss), `create(name, text, *, untrusted, sources, origin_kind, origin_session_id, origin_label, origin_platform, origin_peer_id, origin_peer_name) -> (note, created)`, `update_by_agent(name, number, text, *, untrusted, sources) -> (note, changed)`, `forget_by_agent(name, number) -> note`, `update_by_operator(name, note_id, text) -> (note, changed)`, `delete`, `clear -> int`, `total_chars(name, *, exclude_id=None)`, and `list_if_enabled(name)` (the prompt loader's read: the notes when the switch is on, `None` when off or no stored row). Every mutator uses ONE `write_transaction` and never nests; the three agent-facing methods re-read `notes_enabled` inside it (`NotesDisabledError`); operator methods never check the switch. **Flag lifecycle (F3 L2):** `update_by_operator` with a real text change sets `untrusted=0`, `untrusted_sources=[]` and `updated_by='operator'` (the operator reviewed and rewrote it); an identical-text save is a no-op that keeps the flag; agent updates still OR the flag, so a later tainted agent update re-flags it. Mutators raise `KeyError` on a miss (queries are scoped by `agent_name`). A duplicate `create` returns the existing note with `created=False` (and consumes no number); an update that would duplicate ANOTHER note raises `NoteDuplicateError` (the scan excludes the note's own row); identical normalised text is an allowed no-op (`changed=False`, nothing written). An agent update ORs the taint into the stored flag and merges `untrusted_sources` (deduplicated, capped), keeps `origin_*` and sets `updated_by='agent'`; an operator edit keeps the flag and sets `updated_by='operator'`. `forget_by_agent` returns the removed note. The total check on update is `total - old_len + new_len`.
- **Prompt contract**: `compose_system_prompt(base, persona, *, rules=(), notes=None)`; `notes: Sequence[RenderedNote] | None` where `RenderedNote(number, text, untrusted)` is a frozen dataclass in `agent/config.py`. `None` omits the layer (byte-identical to the Flight 1 output), `[]` renders the block with "No saved notes yet.", a list renders `[n] text` lines (`[n] (unverified) text` when untrusted) inside `<saved_notes>`; `<saved_notes` / `</saved_notes` in a note's text are neutralised case-insensitively to `&lt;...`. When `notes is not None` the rules layer gains "Rules take precedence over saved notes." (`render_rules_block(rules, notes_present=False)`; constant `RULES_OVER_NOTES`), placed right after the rules heading. Notes on with no rules yields the notes layer and no rules layer. `build_system_prompt` calls `AgentNoteRepository.list_if_enabled` itself and, when on, gets the notes by number (`notes=None` when off or the agent has no stored row, e.g. the synthesized `default`), so the factory build and the `next_turn` recompose (`pending.apply_pending_revision`) both carry notes; the chat-only degraded path keeps the block.
- **REST** (credential-gated; 404 for an unknown agent, including `default` which has no stored row, and for an unknown or another agent's note; 422 never echoes note text): `GET /api/agents/{name}/notes[?session_id=]` -> `{enabled, notes: [...], pending_known, limits: {note_chars, total_chars, writes_per_turn}, usage: {total_chars}}` (each note: `id, number, text, untrusted, untrusted_sources, origin_kind, origin_session_id, origin_label, origin_exists, origin_platform, origin_peer_id, origin_peer_name, created_at, updated_at, updated_by, pending`). **`session_id` and `pending` (M27 F3 L2):** with a `session_id` of THIS agent's session the server reads that session's current system message (the live web `Session`'s `agent.messages[0]` from `app._sessions`, else the persisted `chat_sessions.messages[0]`), parses its notes layer with `parse_notes_block` (`{number: rendered remainder}`, anchored on the LAST `\n<saved_notes>\n`; `None` when there is no block) and sets `pending` true on every note whose number is absent from it or whose rendered line (`render_note_line_text`: the `(unverified) ` marker then the neutralised text) differs (an edit, or a trust flip); `pending_known` is true. Anything else (no `session_id`, an unknown or another agent's session, a prompt with no notes block, e.g. notes switched on after the build) gives `pending_known: false` and `pending: false` everywhere, intentionally: the panel then shows no Pending section. A note deleted after the build is simply absent. `PUT .../notes/settings` `{enabled: StrictBool}` -> `{enabled}` (registered BEFORE `PUT .../notes/{note_id}`); `PUT .../notes/{note_id}` `{text}` (operator edit, allowed while notes are off); `DELETE .../notes/{note_id}` -> `{"status": "ok"}`; `DELETE .../notes` -> `{deleted: n}`. There is no create route.
- **Known limitations (accepted)**: the pending fallback (`compose_system_prompt(render_platform(caps), persona)` when the database read fails) drops rules AND notes; turning notes on mid-conversation followed by a `next_turn` recompose adds the notes block to an agent whose already-built registry has no notes tools (the reverse case mirrors it; an already-built agent's tools refuse once the switch is off).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_notes_store.py`, `tests/test_agent_notes_api.py`, `TestSchemaV43` in `tests/test_db_migration.py`, the notes cases in `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_prompt_layers.py` and `tests/test_self_configuration_write.py` (`TestRulesAtSettle`).

### Notes tools, turn context and provenance (M27 F2 L2)

- **Tools** (`the_one/notes/tools.py`; `NotesToolProvider` in `the_one/notes/tool_provider.py`): `save_note(text)`, `update_note(number, text)`, `forget_note(number)`. Built per agent only when `config.notes_enabled` (otherwise `[]` with no database call), passing `agent_name` to `Tool.__init__`. They are NOT built-ins (not in `_TOOL_CLASSES`, `tool-categories.js` or either Built-in tools surface); `AgentFactory` registers them after the KB tools OUTSIDE the `is_tool_enabled`/`disabled_names` filter (a built-in-only whitelist never strips them), seeds them into `loaded_schemas`/`loaded_tool_names` (so they are visible from turn one and survive `reset()`; factory-only, `core.py` is frozen), and after `agent = Agent(...)` calls `tool.bind_agent(agent)` for each. `bind_agent` is on the notes tool base class, not on `Tool`. Reaching the database is lazy (`get_connection()` at execute time). Refusals RAISE `ValueError(<exact text>)` and are never wrapped, so the loop's generic handler yields `ToolResult(is_error=True, content="[error] Tool execution failed: <text>")` and the tool stream shows an error. Successes: "Saved note [n].", "Already saved as note [n].", "Updated note [n].", "Note [n] is unchanged.", "Forgot note [n]."; errors: "No note [n].", "Notes are off for this agent.", the repository's limit/duplicate details, "Note limit for this turn reached (5 writes)." A non-integer `number` is refused ("number must be an integer."; an int-string is coerced, a bool is refused).
- **Budget**: `save_note` and `update_note` share 5 writes per turn (`NOTE_WRITES_PER_TURN`), checked BEFORE the write and counted only when a note was created or changed (a duplicate save and an unchanged update do not count; `forget_note` never counts). The count lives on `TurnContext.writes`; with no context the three tools of one Agent share one fallback `_WriteCounter` held by the provider.
- **`TurnContext`** (`the_one/agent/_context.py`): mutable dataclass `peer: TurnPeer | None`, `session_id: str | None`, `writes: int`; `current_turn: ContextVar[TurnContext | None]` (default None). `TurnPeer(platform, connector_id, peer_id, peer_name)`. Tools MUTATE the shared object and never `current_turn.set()` inside a tool (a `set` inside the `asyncio.wait_for` copied context is lost, a mutation is visible; pinned by `tests/test_notes_context.py`). **Set sites** (token pattern, reset in `finally`) that EVERY new `agent.step` / `resume_after_auth` call site must join: `_process_and_broadcast` in `app.py` around its one `session.agent.step(...)` (covers the WebSocket path AND the REST `/api/chat` handler; `session_id=session.id`, `peer=None`), `_resume_paused_session` (`session_id=session.id`; a resumed turn gets a fresh budget), connector `dispatch_inbound` (`session_id=session_row.id`, `peer` from the envelope), the CLI step (empty context), and the two `selftest` steps. The origin session id is `turn.session_id`, falling back to `current_chat_session_id`; `current_chat_session_id`'s own behaviour is unchanged.
- **Origin** (DD7): `origin_kind` is `connector` when `turn.peer` is set (with `origin_platform`/`origin_peer_id`/`origin_peer_name`), `web` when there is a session id, else `other`; `origin_label` is the session row's title at write time (None when unreadable).
- **Taint** (`compute_taint(agent, tool_call_id, turn) -> (untrusted, sources)`, DD6): the tool reads its own call id from `current_tool_call_id` (set by `_execute_tool`). It scans `agent.messages` BACKWARDS for the nearest assistant message whose `tool_calls` include that id (no anchor, a `None` id or an unbound tool gives `(True, ["unknown position"])`). For each message BEFORE the anchor: a TOOL message is attributed, by `m.tool_result.tool_call_id`, to the tool calls of the nearest preceding assistant message, adding that tool's name unless it is in `TRUSTED_TOOL_NAMES`, or `"unknown tool"` when there is no match or no `tool_result`; a USER message with `peer_name is not None` adds `peer:{name}`. Then `turn.peer` adds `peer:{name}` and `turn is None` adds `"unknown context"`. Sources are de-duplicated, ordered and capped at `SOURCES_CAP` (10). Calls in the same assistant message as the save are after the anchor, so their results (not yet seen by the model) are not counted. An update ORs the flag and merges sources (repository), and keeps `origin_*`.
- **`TRUSTED_TOOL_NAMES`** = `{save_note, update_note, forget_note, search_tools, continue}`, defined once in `the_one/agent/taint.py` (since M33 F2 L3 with the four routine tools and `current_time` added; `notes/tools.py` re-exports it). A tool is untrusted unless it is added there deliberately; a new tool must not be added without asking whether its output can carry outside text.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_notes_context.py` (the premise test and `TurnPeer`), `tests/test_notes_tools.py` (taint, origin, budget, fail-closed, resume, injection, switch, limits, the REST set site, the persisted tool stream, and `test_planted_instruction_never_yields_an_unflagged_note`, parametrized over untrusted tools and across same-turn, later-turn and restored histories), `TestNotesToolWiring` in `tests/test_factory.py`, and the connector set site in `tests/test_connectors_inbound.py`.

### Rules and notes tab (M27 F1 L2)

The agent editor's **Rules and notes** tab (`data-tab-id="rules-and-notes"`, testid `tab-rules-and-notes`, panel `agent-tab-panel-rules-and-notes`, route `#agents/{name}/rules-and-notes`, whitelisted in `studio.js`'s hash parse), sits SECOND, after General. Its label fits at 1024x768 in both engines (measured `tablist scroll/client=652/652`, last tab right edge 1005px Chromium / 994px WebKit with six tabs, against a 1024px main edge; after the MCPs tab folded into Tools (M33 F1 L06) five tabs measure 893px Chromium / 883px WebKit; with Routines added (M33 F1 L08) the six tabs measure 979px Chromium / 967.5px WebKit, scroll/client 652/652), so the "Rules" fallback label was not needed; the no-overflow assertion in `test_flow_agents_scale.py` is unrelaxed and expects 6 tabs.

- **Module**: `static/js/areas/studio/rules-notes-tab.js` (`App.studio.renderRulesNotesTab(panel, agent, callbacks)`), DOM-built on the control library (`App.ui.section({titleTag: 'h3'})`, `textarea` / `toggle` / `button`, `App.ui.status` for the `rules-error`/`notes-error` (`role: 'alert'`) and `rules-status`/`notes-status` (polite, visually hidden) lines, `App.ui.emptyState` (`titleTag: 'p'`) for the empty and create-mode notices, `App.ui.confirm` for delete; M31 F2 L09, no sinks, no allowance rows). The tab is mounted once per agent and declares NO `setAgent`: it saves immediately and keys only on the agent's name, so the editor's same-identity patch leaves it out (a rename is a new identity and remounts); `test_flow_agent_rules_notes_patch.py` pins that unsaved Add-rule text survives a General Save with no extra rules/notes GET. List re-renders stay list-local. `App.ui.status` gained a `role` option (`'status'` default, `'alert'`). The panel is built from `makeSection(panel, id, title)` and a self-contained `buildRulesSection`, and Flight 2 L3 appended the Notes section below Rules with the same helpers. `agent` is `null` in create mode: only the `rules-create-mode` notice "Save the agent first to add rules."
- **Save model**: every control (Add, Edit/Save, toggle, Move up/down, Delete) saves IMMEDIATELY through the REST routes (`agent-data.js`: `fetchRules`/`createRule`/`updateRule`/`deleteRule`/`reorderRules` over `App.api.fetchJson`, so a 422's `detail` becomes the error text), then re-reads the list and re-renders the LIST only (the Add textarea keeps focus). A module-local generation counter drops a stale read. While a request is in flight the list's controls and Add are disabled. A failure shows its message in `rules-error` and re-reads the list so it stays as the server has it (a failed inline Save keeps the editor and its text open). None of it joins the General form's dirty state or `App.nav`, so unsaved Add/inline-edit text is lost on navigation without a prompt (accepted, flagged for the HAT).
- **Focus**: after Add the cleared Add textarea; after a Move the same Move button (the other one when it is now at an end); after Delete the neighbour row's Edit (the Add textarea when empty); after an inline Save/Cancel that row's Edit; after a toggle that row's toggle.
- **Testids**: `rules-section`, `rules-applies-note`, `rules-error`, `rules-list`, `rules-row` (`data-id`, `data-enabled`, `.rules-row--off`), `rules-row-text`, `rules-row-toggle`, `rules-row-edit`, `rules-row-edit-input`, `rules-row-save`, `rules-row-cancel`, `rules-row-up`, `rules-row-down`, `rules-row-delete`, `rules-empty`, `rules-add-input`, `rules-add-count`, `rules-add-btn`, `rules-create-mode`. Literal `data-testid` assignments only (the registry guard cannot see ids passed through a helper).
- **Drawer group** (DD7): `agent-drawer.js` adds a `rulesGroup(agentName)` builder (not `group()`), FIRST in "What it can use" (`.chat-drawer-group[data-group=rules]`): the enabled rules in order (`chat-drawer-rules` bulleted `<ul role=list>`, disc markers in `--fg-muted`, each `chat-drawer-rule` `<li>` with the full text in `title`, text clamped to two lines by CSS), `chat-drawer-rules-off` "{n} off" when some are disabled, and "No rules" when none are enabled. Read-only since M27 F3 L2: the Edit rules link is gone (Open agent settings in the footer is the way to the editor). Fetched once per open and agent beside the self-configuration fetch with the same generation guards, through a raw `fetch` so a 404 reads as an empty list ("No rules") while any other failure is "Couldn't load". The default, deleted and loading views show no group. The panel shows the rules as saved when it was opened.
- **Notes section (M27 F2 L3, DD10)**: `buildNotesSection` below Rules (`notes-section`). The `App.ui.toggle` "Let this agent keep notes" (`notes-enabled-toggle`) saves immediately (`setNotesEnabled` -> `PUT /api/agents/{name}/notes/settings`) and the section re-reads, so it always shows the server state; the "Changes apply..." line is repeated (`notes-applies-note`); when off, `notes-off-message` ("Notes are off. This agent can't see or write notes. Saved notes are kept.") shows and the list stays so the operator can prune; `notes-usage` reads "{used} of {total} characters" (thousands separators, from the `usage`/`limits` the GET returns); the empty state is `notes-empty`. Each `notes-row` (`data-id`, `data-number`) shows `[n]`, the text, and a `notes-row-meta` line: a warning-triangle flag (`notes-row-flag`, built by the shared `App.ui.noteFlag({sources})` in `ui/feedback.js` (M31 F6 L03a; `App.noteFlag` and `App.iconNode` are deleted), which the drawer also uses; `role=img`, `title` and `aria-label` "Check this note: it was written after content that didn't come from you ({sources}). It may have been planted.", `--status-warning` via `.note-flag` in `components.css`) for untrusted notes, the time (`App.time.formatClock` today, else `Mon D`, with the year when it is not this year; "Updated ..." when `updated_at != created_at`), the origin (a `#chats/{id}` link built with `App.router.href` when `origin_exists`, "{label} (chat deleted)" otherwise, platform badge + peer for connectors, "Outside a chat" for `other`) and "Edited by you" when `updated_by === 'operator'`. Edit is inline (400-char counter, `aria-invalid` and Save disabled while over); Delete arms through `App.ui.confirm` with the arm announced in `notes-status`; Clear all (`notes-clear-all`, hidden with no notes) opens `App.confirmDialog` "Clear all {n} notes? This can't be undone." and focus goes to the switch afterwards. A 422 `detail` shows in `notes-error`. Create mode adds `notes-create-mode`. **Every note field is written with `textContent` (attacker-influenced text; `test_note_text_with_markup_renders_literally`).** `tests/test_nav_guard_static.py` allows `rules-notes-tab.js` as a second `App.confirmDialog` caller.
- **Shared helpers and the explicit failure origin (F2 L3)**: `makeMutate(cfg)` (one saving-action runner), `buildInlineEditor`, `buildDeleteButton`, `disableList`/`restoreDisabled` and `restoreOrigin` are module-level and serve both sections. `mutate(action, afterFocus, opts)` takes `opts.origin` (`{kind:'row', id, control}`, `{kind:'add'}` or `{kind:'el', el}`) and restores failure focus from it, never from `document.activeElement` (a mouse click can leave focus on `<body>` in WebKit; `test_failed_add_by_mouse_click_lands_focus_on_the_add_textarea`). The registry guard needs literal testids, so shared builders return nodes and the callers set `data-testid` literals.
- **Drawer Notes group (F2 L3; read-only since F3 L2)**: `notesGroup()` is the sibling after `rulesGroup` (`.chat-drawer-group[data-group=notes]`), fetched per open, agent AND session (the view carries `sessionId` for joined/joining chats; `GET .../notes?session_id=`, a raw `fetch`: a 404 reads "No notes yet", any other failure "Couldn't load"). Off: `chat-drawer-notes-off` "Off" plus `chat-drawer-notes-kept` "{n} kept". On: the in-use notes (`chat-drawer-notes` `<ul>`), then, if any are `pending`, `chat-drawer-notes-pending-head` "Pending · used from the next chat" and a muted `chat-drawer-notes-pending` list (`--fg-muted` text, never opacity, to keep AA contrast). Each `chat-drawer-notes-item` is a bullet (no visible number; `data-number` stays as a data attribute; the Rules and notes tab keeps its numbers), for an untrusted note a `chat-drawer-notes-flag` (the `alert-triangle` icon, parsed with `DOMParser` as `markdown.js` does, `role=img`, `--status-warning`; its `title` and `aria-label` are the sentence "Check this note: it was written after content that didn't come from you ({sources}). It may have been planted."), the text clamped to two lines (full text in the `<li>` `title`) and an origin line. There are NO delete, clear, edit or status controls (HAT decision, overrides #230): notes are managed in the Rules and notes tab via Open agent settings. **Live refresh:** the drawer handle's `refreshNotes()` re-fetches the current view's notes (while open); `chat/turn-frames.js` calls it on a live `tool_result` whose tool (mapped from the preceding live `tool_call` by `tool_call_id`, `state.liveToolNames`) is `save_note`/`update_note`/`forget_note`, and `chat/socket.js` after each `session_joined` (a rejoin's rebuild clears Pending).
- **Notes tests (F2 L3)**: `tests/browser/test_flow_agent_notes.py` (agents lane) seeds notes through `seeders.seed_note` (a direct `agent_notes` insert against the instance database, from the `app_with_data_dir` fixture; notes have no operator create route) plus one real-chain test (a mock-provider turn whose `response.tool_calls` run `save_note`); the Notes-group tests are at the end of `test_flow_chat_drawer.py` (chats lane).
- **Tests**: `tests/browser/test_flow_agent_rules.py` (agents lane), the Rules tests at the end of `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_drawer.py` (chats lane), and the six-tab updates in `test_flow_agent_tabs.py` and `test_flow_agents_scale.py`.

### current_time and the date layer (M28)

M28 F1 L2 (server side); the client mirrors, the Time badge and the Prompts page Date layer are leg 2b (L3), see the last bullet.

- **The tool**: `the_one/tools/time_tool.py::CurrentTimeTool` (`name == "current_time"`, constant `CURRENT_TIME_TOOL_NAME` lives in `the_one/time_zones.py`), the last entry of `_TOOL_CLASSES` (23 built-ins; empty default Config, so it cannot change which tool resolves `FilesystemConfig`). Parameter `zone` (optional IANA name). Output is `"\n".join(time_zones.tool_lines(now, eff))`: an ISO line and a long-date line. With a `zone` other than the operator's key, that block is followed by an empty line, `Operator's time zone:` and the operator's two lines. The block label is the requested key (an alias such as `US/Pacific` is labelled so). An unknown, non-tzdata (`localtime`, `../x`) or non-string zone raises `ValueError("Unknown time zone '<echo>'. Use an IANA name such as Asia/Tokyo or Europe/Paris.")` (echo capped at 64 chars, a non-string echoes `<invalid>`); the loop wraps it as an error tool result. `now` and `zone_source` are constructor seams; when unset the tool looks up `time_zones.utcnow` / `load_effective_zone` at call time (so `frozen_clock` works).
- **Whitelist exemption**: `agent/config.py::WHITELIST_EXEMPT_TOOLS = frozenset({CURRENT_TIME_TOOL_NAME})`. `AgentConfig.is_tool_enabled` treats these like the ungated prefixes: the `enabled_tools` whitelist (even `[]`) never removes them, while `disabled_tools` and the operator's `tool_configs.enabled=False` still do. An empty whitelist therefore no longer blocks every built-in. The client mirror is `App.toolCategories.whitelistExempt` in `static/js/tool-categories.js` (pinned equal by `tests/test_tool_categories.py`); the three JS consumers that mirror the whitelist logic (the drawer's `agent-drawer.js::isToolEnabled`, `tools-tab.js`'s `enabledSetFor` and `studio/agent-form-data.js::collectFormData`) are updated in leg 2b and read `App.toolCategories.whitelistExempt` as their single source.
- **Seeding**: `AgentFactory.create` adds `CURRENT_TIME_TOOL_NAME` to the connector seed tuple, so a registered `current_time` is in `_loaded_schemas`/`_loaded_tool_names` from the first turn. `search_tools` still lists it as a match ("Found 3 tools" for a read_file/write_file agent) but `_on_search_match` dedupes against the seed. `describe_my_capabilities` follows from `is_tool_enabled` with no code change.
- **The date layer**: `compose_system_prompt(..., date_line=None)` appends `\n\n---\n\n{date_line}` as the LAST layer (after notes) when given; `None` is byte-identical to the earlier output. `prompt_layers.build_system_prompt(agent_name, persona, *, conn=None, tool_available=False, now=None)` builds it with `time_zones.date_line(now or utcnow(), await load_effective_zone(conn), tool_available=...)`. Wordings (`time_zones.render_date_line`): with the tool, "Today is {date} ({zone}). This date is as of when this conversation was last loaded. For the current date and time, call current_time."; without, "Today is {date} ({zone}), as of when this conversation was last loaded." Any exception building the layer is logged at WARNING and the layer omitted (rules and notes still apply). `parse_notes_block` still anchors on the last notes open tag, so a trailing date layer does not disturb it.
- **`time_tool_available(registry, llm)`** (async, in `prompt_layers.py`; never raises): False when the registry is None/broken or lacks the tool (the LLM is not consulted); otherwise `await asyncio.wait_for(llm.supports_tools(), SUPPORTS_TOOLS_TIMEOUT_S)` (2.0 s, a module global read at call time), failing CLOSED (False) on timeout or any provider error (squawk 0182: the predicate only chooses the date line's WORDING, which must be true under every fallback, so a probe failure gives the tool-free wording; the tool itself stays registered and seeded, and the agent loop's own fail-open dispatch is unchanged). Two callers: `AgentFactory.create` (after the seed blocks) and `pending.apply_pending_revision` (computed in its own `try/except` before the recompose `try`, from `agent.tools` / `agent.llm`; a failure means False). A chat-only model (`supports_tools()` False) therefore gets the tool-free wording, and its degraded system message never mentions `current_time`.
- **Settle fallback**: if the rules/notes read fails at a `next_turn` settle, the fallback is still `compose_system_prompt(render_platform(caps), persona)`, with no date line, by design. `pending` always sets `agent.degraded_system_prompt` beside `system_prompt` and `messages[0]`, from `capabilities_from_registry(agent.tools, ...)`.
- **Client mirrors (L3)**: the drawer treats exempt names as ungated (only `disabled_tools` applies). The Tools tab computes `enabledSet` for an exempt name from `disabled_tools` alone, shows a `time-tool-note` line on the `current_time` row, and `collectFormData` takes exempt toggles out of the whitelist/blocklist branch choice: an unticked one goes to `disabled_tools` in every branch (after the non-exempt disabled names, before the stored ungated names) and never into `enabled_tools`. A "Time" category badge (retired in M31 F6 L09a: the `time` badge kind is deleted and tiles carry no category badge; historical detail follows) (`--badge-time` `#2e6b3f`, white ink, theme-stable; `.badge--time`, `.admin-tool-badge--time`; `BADGE_KINDS` had 15 kinds then, 16 since the `anthropic-plan-token` badge) styles the new tab and tile. Settings › Prompts has a fourth layer, Date, with two `<pre>` blocks (`date-prompt-content`, `date-prompt-content-without-tool`) from `/api/info`. Tests: `tests/browser/test_flow_studio_time_tool.py` plus the drawer, tools-grid, overrides and settings-nav files.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_time_tool.py`, the date-layer and factory cases in `tests/test_prompt_layers.py`, `TestWhitelistExempt` in `tests/test_config.py`, `TestSettleDateLayerToolWording` in `tests/test_self_configuration_write.py`, `tests/test_tool_categories.py`. `tests/conftest.py::expected_date_layer(tool_available)` builds the expected line under `frozen_clock`.

### Connector subsystem contract (Mission 23 Flight 2)

`the_one/connectors/` bridges external chat platforms to the agent loop.
Mission 23 Flight 1 deleted the pre-reset framework outright (a ~430-line
message-routing layer, per-agent ACLs, manual peer-approval flows, a
slash-command surface, worker-pool machinery, the Discord plugin); Flight 2
rebuilt a small, platform-agnostic core from scratch. **The full contract —
Protocol shapes, manager lifecycle, inbound pipeline stages, pairing, reply
policy, the send/history-tool authorization rule, config schema, test
seams, known limitations — lives in `the_one/connectors/README.md`.** This
entry is the architecture-index pointer other subsystem-contract entries
get; don't duplicate the README's content here.

- **Module map**: `base.py` (Protocols + envelope/target dataclasses),
  `registry.py` (type → plugin dict; `discord` registers unconditionally
  at lifespan startup as of Flight 3, `stub` remains test-only
  registration), `manager.py` (`ConnectorManager`: listener-task
  lifecycle + in-memory pairing store, installed via
  `set_manager`/`get_manager` mirroring `agent.factory`'s accessor pair),
  `inbound.py` (`dispatch_inbound`: admission → `/new` → reply policy →
  session resolution → per-session-locked agent dispatch, then a
  `session_updated` broadcast to the chat's WebSocket subscribers in a
  `finally` after the lock is released), `stub.py`
  (`StubPlugin`: in-process test/dev fixture, never registered in
  production), `discord/` (`_gateway.py`/`_rest.py`/`plugin.py`, Flight
  3: the first real `ConnectorPlugin` — full contract in the README's
  "Discord (Flight 3)" section, not duplicated here).
- **Three built-in tools** attached alongside the package:
  `tools/connector_send.py` (`send_to_platform_channel`) and
  `tools/connector_history.py` (`fetch_channel_history`, DD-8, optional per
  plugin via `getattr(client, "history", None)` —
  `optional-plugin-hook-via-getattr-sentinel` above), sharing one
  binding-derived authorization rule (see the README's "Authorization
  rule"); and `tools/connector_lookup.py` (`find_platform_destination`,
  Mission 23 Flight 4 Leg 3, DD-5) — a read-only name-to-id lookup over
  the passively-populated connector directory, CONNECTOR-LEVEL scoped
  (not the send/history pair's per-target rule) and carve-out-seeded
  alongside the send tool (DD-6's generalization of the baseline-
  visibility carve-out below to the connector tool pair). See the
  README's "Lookup tool" section for the full contract.
- **Persisted across the reset, unaffected by the deletion/rebuild**: the
  `Connector` DB model/row, `ConnectorRepository`, and connector-secret
  enrollment in the encryption-rotation invariant above; `Message.visibility`
  and the `ChatSession` session-keying columns (`platform`, `connector_id`,
  `channel_id`, `thread_id`, `peer_id`, `session_metadata`,
  `last_inbound_at`). Flight 1 deleted the dispatcher that populated them;
  Flight 2's `inbound.py` populates them again, on the new contract.
- **Reply visibility** (`tag_post_step_visibility`, `the_one/llm/base.py`)
  is connector-agnostic machinery with a live connector-specific branch
  again: a successful `send_to_platform_channel` tool result is tagged
  peer-visible (`visibility=None`); everything else a turn produces is
  tagged operator-only by default. See
  "`message-additive-back-compat-via-emit-when-non-none`" above for the
  general visibility-field convention this specializes.
- **Delivery primitives (M33 F1 L2)**: `the_one/connectors/delivery.py` holds `deliver_final_reply` (returns nothing, never raises), `send_step_limit_line(target, ...)` and `authorized_agent_for`; no envelope or `inbound.py` dependency. `inbound._auto_deliver_reply`/`_send_step_limit_line` and `SendToPlatformChannelTool` are thin callers. Routine runs never use them: a routine posts to a platform only through the agent's own `send_to_platform_channel` tool (M33 F4 L02).
- **DD-10 auto-reply mechanical fallback (Flight 3, mid-flight amendment)**:
  `dispatch_inbound` (`the_one/connectors/inbound.py::_auto_deliver_reply`)
  runs immediately after `tag_post_step_visibility` and mechanically
  delivers the turn's FINAL assistant text to the ORIGINATING target —
  `PeerTarget`/`ChannelTarget` resolved straight from the inbound
  `InboundEnvelope`, via `manager.client_for(connector_id).send()` — then
  flips that message's `visibility` back to `None` (peer-visible), so the
  persisted history reflects the real delivery. Skip rule: a turn that
  already contains a successful `send_to_platform_channel` result is left
  alone entirely (the agent spoke to the platform deliberately; no
  double-send). Delivery failure (no live client, `ConnectorClientError`,
  any other exception) is logged and the text is left operator-only — no
  retry, no crash. This only fires for `dispatch_inbound`-originated turns
  (a peer's own inbound message); the WS-storage-edge shim's operator-typed
  path (`interfaces/web/app.py`) never runs through `dispatch_inbound` and
  is untouched — an operator's console note still never reaches the
  platform. Landed because live-gate checkpoint 2 attempt 2 proved the
  prior design had NO mechanism for a plain reply to reach a peer at
  all — `dispatch_inbound` hands the model only raw text, so it cannot
  target the send tool, and plain assistant text was operator-only by
  contract; the project's own `prompt-discipline-needs-mechanical-fallback`
  convention, applied to the reply path rather than left to model
  tool-calling reliability (RQ8's lesson). The send tool remains the
  agent-INITIATED path (speak-first, cross-channel, deliberate re-send) —
  unchanged, no new tool, no change to the tool's own visible schema. See
  `the_one/connectors/README.md`'s "Reply visibility" section and
  `tests/behavior/stub-connector-round-trip.md`'s 2026-09-01 re-author note
  for the end-to-end contract.
- **Discord (Flight 3)** is the first real plugin, registered
  unconditionally (not gated like `stub`); **Flight 4** proves the
  `ConnectorPlugin` contract generalizes to further platforms (Slack,
  Matrix, Signal, email — each a small client, none needing a public
  endpoint per the mission's design). The module-level `set_factory`/
  `get_factory` accessor (`the_one/agent/factory.py`) that a real gateway
  listener resolves the application factory through — see
  `lifespan-wiring-same-leg-as-installation` above — is now driven by a
  REAL (non-test) gateway listener for the first time.
- **DD-5 baseline-visibility carve-out (Flight 3 Leg 2, agent loop)**: the
  mission-amended constraint making `send_to_platform_channel` visible to
  the model without a `search_tools` call first. Implemented by SEEDING
  `Agent._loaded_schemas`/`_loaded_tool_names` (the F10 L1 strict-baseline
  containers) with the send tool's schema at factory-build time — never by
  appending inside `Agent._compute_visible_schemas`, which would bypass
  the search-match dedup and risk a duplicate schema reaching the model.
  `Agent.__init__` copies the seed onto `_seed_schemas`/`_seed_tool_names`
  so `reset()` (which unconditionally clears the live containers) can
  re-extend from the seed — the seed itself has no other memory. One
  deliberate, owner-approved touch to the frozen loop, scoped to a single
  baseline entry and pinned by tests; see `the_one/agent/core.py`'s
  `__init__`/`reset()` and `the_one/agent/factory.py`'s
  `SEND_TO_PLATFORM_CHANNEL_TOOL_NAME` seeding block for the
  implementation. The `discord-live-round-trip` behavior spec proves it
  empirically — `test-agent`'s system prompt there carries no tool-
  discipline nudge, the mechanical-fallback proof `mechanical-fallback-
  prompt-truth-pairing` (above) requires.
- **DD-11 send-tool target defaulting (Flight 3, mid-flight amendment)**:
  `send_to_platform_channel`'s `target` argument is now OPTIONAL — when
  omitted, the tool resolves a default from `current_chat_session_id`
  (`agent/_context.py`) → the session's own `chat_sessions` keying
  columns, mirroring `resolve_session`'s keying (`channel_id` is the
  session's primary key, not `peer_id`): `channel_id`-bearing → a
  `ChannelTarget`; `channel_id`-absent + `peer_id`-present → a
  `PeerTarget`. No active session context, or a session not bound to a
  connector at all, is a clear error, never a guess. The resolved default
  is folded into the exact dict shape an explicit `target` takes, so it
  flows through the SAME binding-derived authorization the explicit-target
  path already uses — no restructuring, and a defaulted target that fails
  authorization errors identically to an explicit one. Landed because
  live-gate checkpoint 5 proved the model can call the DD-5 carved-out
  tool by name but has no way to discover its own session's platform ids
  to fill `target` explicitly. See `the_one/connectors/README.md`'s
  "Send-tool target defaulting (DD-11)" section and
  `tools/connector_send.py`.

### MCP F2 Client Foundation (authoritative for F3+)

1. **Module layout** — `the_one/mcp/` is a peer of `the_one/llm/`, NOT a sub-package of `the_one/tools/`. `MCPClient` is a service abstraction; `MCPToolProvider` wraps individual `MCPTool`s into the project's `Tool` abstraction.

2. **`_HANDSHAKE_OFFER_VERSION`** — Defined in `the_one/mcp/__init__.py`. **Read at call time** via `the_one.mcp._HANDSHAKE_OFFER_VERSION`, NOT `from the_one.mcp import _HANDSHAKE_OFFER_VERSION` — the latter binds at module-load time and breaks `monkeypatch.setattr` in tests. Upgrade procedure: bump SDK pin → re-read `mcp.types.version.LATEST_HANDSHAKE_VERSION` → update the constant → run `test_handshake_offer_version_matches_sdk`. Renamed from `_PINNED_PROTOCOL_VERSION` in Mission 29 Flight 2 (DD4). Under `mcp` 2.x the constant is `"2025-11-25"` (the latest handshake revision); `MCPClient.connect` negotiates through `the_one/mcp/_negotiate.py` (modern `server/discover` at `LATEST_MODERN_VERSION` = `"2026-07-28"` first, falling back to `initialize()`), so the constant only feeds hand-built `initialize` payloads and the mismatch text. The tripwires pin it to `LATEST_HANDSHAKE_VERSION` and pin `LATEST_MODERN_VERSION`.

3. **Pool-compat invariants** — `MCPClient` exposes `is_alive: bool` (false until `connect()` returns, false from `disconnect()` start) and `last_activity: float` (monotonic, updated per `list_tools`/`call_tool`). `disconnect()` is **idempotent and never raises**. No thread-locals, no module-globals. Naming is aligned with `PtySession` so the pool builds one reaper code path.
   **Argument coercion (squawk 0052)** — `call_tool` runs `the_one/mcp/_arg_coerce.py::coerce_arguments` immediately before the L6b `jsonschema` validation: a string value whose property schema unambiguously declares `integer`/`number`/`boolean` (no `string` in the type, no `anyOf`/`oneOf`/`allOf`/`$ref`/`enum`/`const`) and parses cleanly is converted on a copy (recursing only through `properties` and single-schema `items`), and the validated copy is what goes on the wire. One direction only; an int sent for a `string` field is still rejected.

4. **`build_stdio_environ`** — `(source, allowlist_env) -> dict[str, str]` performs a 2-layer flat merge: `allowlist_env ∪ (config.env ∪ secrets)`, secrets winning on collision. **Do NOT consume `MCPSource.merged_config()`** for stdio env assembly — that helper produces a flat top-level merge that would inject `command`/`args` as env-var-shaped keys.

5. **Version gate** — `connect()` accepts any negotiated revision in the SDK's `mcp.types.version.HANDSHAKE_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS` plus `MODERN_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS` and raises `MCPVersionMismatch` otherwise; `_HANDSHAKE_OFFER_VERSION` is a documentation marker of the primary-targeted revision and is **not** an equality check (loosened in M07 F6 A6, after Cloudflare announced 2025-06-18; the old "exact match" wording here was stale). The SDK's own `RuntimeError("Unsupported protocol version from the server: …")` is caught and re-raised as `MCPVersionMismatch` so operators see one error type (`client.py::_UNSUPPORTED_VERSION_RE`). `mcp.shared.version` is gone in 2.x.

6. **mcp 2.x client surface (Mission 29 F1)** — `mcp==2.3.0` (pinned exactly; the operator waived the dependency soak). Contracts the port established:
   - **`httpx2`, not `httpx`, under `the_one/mcp/` (DD4).** 2.x is built on the separate `httpx2` package (so are `OAuthClientProvider`, which is an `httpx2.Auth`, and `streamable_http_client(http_client=…)`). Every file under `the_one/mcp/` imports `httpx2`; `tests/test_mcp_httpx_guard.py` is an AST guard that fails on `import httpx` / `from httpx …` there (`httpx2` is not flagged). The two OAuth flow drivers in `interfaces/web/api.py` build `httpx2.AsyncClient(auth=provider)`; the rest of `api.py` and the app (LLM SDKs, web fetch) stay on `httpx`. `httpx2.alias_httpx()` is **not** used (it aliases process-wide). A test patching the driver's client targets `the_one.interfaces.web.api.httpx2.AsyncClient`.
   - **Dump by alias (DD5).** 2.x models are snake_case with camelCase aliases and `model_dump` is snake_case unless `by_alias=True`. Every dump in `client.py` (capabilities, server info, `_meta`, tool annotations, raw content blocks) passes `by_alias=True`, so the cache, the API responses, the editor (`mcp-server-info.js` reads `info.websiteUrl`) and the blocks an agent receives keep camelCase with no JS change. Attribute reads are snake_case (`input_schema`, `structured_content`, `is_error`, `mime_type`). Two accepted key differences: raw content blocks dump `"_meta"` where 1.x dumped `"meta"`, and the capabilities dump gains `"extensions": null` and `"tasks": null` (the editor reads a fixed `SPEC_CAPABILITY_KEYS` list). Tests build real `mcp.types` models, never camelCase `MagicMock`s.
   - **Session properties (DD6).** `MCPClient` reads `session.protocol_version` / `server_capabilities` / `server_info` / `instructions` (era-agnostic `ClientSession` properties), not fields of the `InitializeResult` (only `_meta` still comes from the result).
   - **Errors (DD7).** `McpError(ErrorData)` → `MCPError(code, message, data)` (`mcp.shared.exceptions`); `str(exc)` is the message. `streamable_http_client` yields `(read, write)`. `_is_session_terminated` keeps matching the synthetic `"Session terminated"` message; the drift tripwire drives a real 404 with an `Mcp-Session-Id` issued.
   - **SSE event cap (DD8).** The transport passes `max_sse_event_size=MAX_SSE_EVENT_BYTES` (1 MiB, the SDK default, named in `the_one/mcp/_signin_record.py` so an SDK default change shows in a diff). An oversized event surfaces as `MCPError(-32000, "SSE stream failed: … byte limit")`; `client._is_sse_cap_error` matches code **and** prefix **and** marker (other -32000 errors, e.g. "SSE stream ended without a response", are not matched) and translates to a one-line message naming the limit: `MCPCallError` from `call_tool` (reaches the chat), `MCPConnectionError` from `list_tools`/`connect` (reaches the editor's Test result). The connection is **not** marked dead. An oversized event on the standalone GET stream is logged and retried inside the SDK and is out of the-one's hands.
   - **Accepted behaviour changes forced by 2.x (DD12, operator-approved 2026-10-12)** — with the cap, four deliberate changes, each a plain error rather than a workaround: (1) *redirects* — the MCP path follows only same-origin, method-preserving redirects (`mcp/shared/_httpx_utils.py::stream_within_origin`); a cross-origin redirect fails with `MCPError(INVALID_REQUEST, "Redirect to … not followed")`, i.e. `MCPConnectionError` from `connect`/`list_tools` and `MCPCallError` from `call_tool`; the now-inert `follow_redirects: True` was dropped from `_build_production_httpx_kwargs`. (2) *authorization-server issuer* — 2.x checks the AS metadata's `issuer` against the discovery issuer (`validate_metadata_issuer`) and drops stored client credentials recorded for a different issuer (`credentials_match_issuer`); a mismatch fails sign-in with the existing plain sign-in-required error. Registrations stored by 1.x carry no `issuer` and stay valid. (3) *404 with no `Mcp-Session-Id` issued* — for a stateless server that is its whole connection lifetime — reads `METHOD_NOT_FOUND "Not Found"` instead of "Session terminated"; in 1.x every 404 read "Session terminated", so `call_tool` marked the client dead and the pool reconnected, while in 2.x a stateless server's mid-use 404 becomes an `MCPCallError` with **no self-heal** (a reconnect cannot fix a wrong path). (4) the SSE cap above.
   - **Automatic protocol negotiation (Mission 29 Flight 2).** `MCPClient.connect` calls `the_one/mcp/_negotiate.py::negotiate`, a branch-for-branch port of the SDK's `negotiate_auto` on public names only (one deliberate departure, below) (`server/discover` at `LATEST_MODERN_VERSION` first, then `initialize()`; `tests/test_mcp_negotiate.py` runs 14 parity scenarios against `negotiate_auto` itself, so an SDK change shows as a failing test). **Era memory** (`the_one/mcp/_era.py`: `era_for` / `remember_era` / `forget_era`) is **in memory only**, per source id, and never persisted (every saved `_cache.protocol_version` predates negotiation, so it cannot be trusted).
