A query concurrent with an upsert, flush or rebuild therefore waits on that lock — roughly 1.8s for a 256-vector upsert batch at 1M chunks, 10-12s for a flush at 1M (~24s at 3M), and hours for a rebuild, on Flight 2's measured Windows figures — bounded by `retrieval.SEMANTIC_QUERY_TIMEOUT` (passed into `store.query`'s keyword-only `timeout`, the `waiter-bound-lock-timeout` convention above — bounds only the wait to acquire the lock, never the work done while holding it), not by the agent loop's 120s tool-call ceiling. An earlier draft of this claim said retrieval's query latency is "never coupled to indexing" — false, and corrected here: the manager's embed lock is never taken, but the store's per-KB lock is, and this is the wait that bound exists to cut short.

**`IndexingManager`** (Flight 3, Leg 3, DD-7): `the_one/knowledge/manager.py`, modelled on `ConnectorManager` (the `supervised-per-entity-worker` convention) — a `dict[kb_id, _WorkerEntry]` (`task`, `wake`/`idle` events, `stop_requested`, `state`, `awaiting_flush: list[str]`, a per-entry `FlushPolicy` created once at entry-creation (not per pass, so cadence state — `last_flush_seconds` — survives a worker restart), `current_item_id`), a module-level `_manager` singleton with `set_manager`/`get_manager` (the `connectors/manager.py` accessor shape), and every dependency (`conn_factory`, `store_factory`, `backend_factory`, the three flush constants, `embed_batch`, `pending_page`, `stop_timeout`, `shutdown_flush_timeout`, `clock`) constructor-injectable for tests. One manager-wide `asyncio.Lock` (`_embed_lock`) is passed as `ctx.embed_lock` — DD-5's serialisation of the embed-plus-upsert pair across every KB's worker.

- **Worker pass** (`_pass`, run inside `_run`'s per-KB task on each `wake`): (1) `requeue_stranded(kb_id)` then clear `entry.awaiting_flush` — the resume-not-restart primitive, run at the top of every pass (not just at startup) so a worker killed mid-item resumes on its NEXT wake, not only after a process restart; (2) `_walk_one_folder` for every `list_folders(kb_id)` row — cycles the folder `pending -> indexing` (via `_cycle_folder_to_pending`, which moves it `-> pending` first only from `indexed`/`failed`, never from an already-`pending` folder — `pending -> pending` would raise `KBItemTransitionError`), walks under `to_thread`, and on an `OSError` (root missing/unreadable) moves the folder straight to `failed` with the error and leaves children untouched; on success, `plan_reconcile` is applied by `_apply_reconcile` (new files `-> create_many` as `pending` children with `size_bytes`/`mtime` left `None`; removed items `-> item_repo.delete` then one batched `store.delete(kb_id, ids)`; changed-and-different items are hashed and requeued `-> pending` only from `indexed`/`failed`, never from `pending`; changed-but-identical items get `set_content_identity` with no status change) before the folder flips to `indexed`; (3) `_drain_pending` pages `list_by_status(kb_id, "pending", limit=pending_page, type="file")`, skipping a per-pass `attempted` set, and calls `index_item` per un-attempted row — exits when a page has no un-attempted ids UNLESS that page is FULL, in which case it falls back to exactly ONE unlimited `list_by_status(..., type="file")` read for the whole pass (a `fallback_used` flag bounds it to once), so a cluster of `>= pending_page` permanently-stuck rows can never starve later rows within a pass; a per-item `KBItemTransitionError`/`KeyError` from `index_item` is logged and the row is left for a later pass; a `"stopped"` outcome ends the drain immediately; (4) a final `_flush` if `awaiting_flush` is still non-empty. `_run`'s own `try/except/else` around `_pass` — never a `finally` — is what flips `entry.state`/`entry.idle` on NORMAL completion only (see "idle bookkeeping" below).
- **`state` vocabulary**: `"idle"` (no entry, or a completed pass), `"running"` (set at the top of `_pass`, covers the whole pass including the walk), `"rebuilding"` (a stale-sidecar recovery in progress), `"stopped"` (an unexpected exception escaped the pass, or the cancel fallback fired). `state(kb_id)` reads `"idle"` for a KB with no entry at all.
- **Idle bookkeeping — no `finally`**: `entry.idle`/`entry.state = "idle"` are set ONLY on `_pass`'s normal-completion path (`_run`'s `else:` clause). A `finally` would flip `idle` true for an instant mid-restart of a stale-sidecar rebuild (between the rebuild succeeding and the restarted pass actually beginning) and race a `wait_until_idle` caller into a false idle (design review, high). `request_index` ALSO clears `entry.idle` synchronously, before `wake.set()` — not left for the newly-created (or newly-woken) task to clear on its own next scheduling turn. Without this a caller doing `request_index(); await wait_until_idle()` back-to-back can race a task that has not run yet, observing the entry's still-set `idle` (from creation or the prior pass) and returning immediately instead of waiting for the pass just requested (found while implementing Leg 3's own test suite).
- **Stale-sidecar recovery**: `VectorStoreStaleError` propagating out of `_pass` (from any store call, INCLUDING a `_flush`) is caught in `_run`, never inside `_pass`/`_drain_pending` — state `-> "rebuilding"`, `rebuild_kb(kb_id, ctx, reason=str(exc))`, then `entry.wake.set(); continue` — the next loop iteration re-runs `_pass` from the top, so `requeue_stranded` returns the interrupted item(s) to `pending` (a direct in-place retry would raise `KBItemTransitionError("indexing->indexing")` — DD-6). If `rebuild_kb` itself raises: the item recorded in `entry.current_item_id` (if any — the field `_drain_batch` sets right before every `index_item` call, so a rebuild failure has something to blame) is marked `failed` with a best-effort `update_status` call (`contextlib.suppress(Exception)` — a second failure here must not crash the handler), an ERROR log is emitted, `entry.state = "stopped"`, `entry.idle.set()`, and the worker returns; a later `request_index` starts a fresh task (`start()` sees `entry.task.done()`).
- **Cooperative stop + cancel fallback** (`stop(kb_id)`, DD-7): sets `stop_requested`, wakes the entry, then `await asyncio.wait_for(asyncio.shield(entry.task), stop_timeout)` — `asyncio.shield` so the `wait_for` timeout cancels only its own wrapper, never `entry.task` itself, leaving the fallback's `entry.task.cancel()` as the ONLY cancellation path. On success (cooperative): if `entry.awaiting_flush` is still non-empty (the pass ended some other way than its own normal final flush — e.g. an unexpected exception mid-drain that `_run` swallowed into `state="stopped"` before reaching the final flush), the manager runs ITS OWN `_flush(kb_id, entry)` under `asyncio.wait_for(..., shutdown_flush_timeout)`, catching and logging any exception (a timeout, a `VectorStoreStaleError` — a rebuild is not attempted at shutdown) and leaving those items `indexing` in that case; `entry.state` is left exactly as `_run` set it (typically `"idle"`, or `"stopped"` if the pass itself failed) — `stop` does NOT overwrite it on this path. On timeout (cancel fallback): `entry.task.cancel()` + suppressed await, NO flush is attempted (a cancelled `to_thread` mutation may still be running in its executor thread in the background — flushing over a live mutation would race it), and `entry.state = "stopped"` IS set here (nothing else would update a stale `"running"`/`"rebuilding"` word). In EVERY case `stop` ends by calling `entry.idle.set()` so a `wait_until_idle` caller never hangs on a stopped worker. `stop` on an unknown `kb_id` is a no-op.
- **Manager-owned flush** (`_flush(kb_id, entry)`): resolves its OWN `store`/`item_repo` via the factories (not the worker's `ctx`) so it is self-sufficient when called from `stop()` after the worker task has already exited. Times `store.flush(kb_id)`, records the duration on `entry.policy`, THEN — only after the flush call has returned without raising — flips every id in `awaiting_flush` `indexing -> indexed` (a per-item `KBItemTransitionError`/`KeyError` is logged and skipped) and clears the list. If `store.flush` itself raises, `awaiting_flush` is left untouched (the flip-to-`indexed` loop never runs) and the exception propagates to the caller — an item is never `indexed` before the flush that covers it.
- **`drain()` concurrency and the 20s+20s budget**: stops every entry via `asyncio.gather` (concurrent, not `ConnectorManager.drain`'s serial `for`), then clears the dict — three KBs each needing their full `stop_timeout` complete in ~one `stop_timeout`, not three. Bounded end to end by `stop_timeout + shutdown_flush_timeout` (20s + 20s = 40s at the defaults) regardless of KB count, which must stay under the desktop launcher's 60s `thread.join` (`interfaces/desktop/shell.py::_graceful_shutdown`) — a serial drain across many KBs would risk the launcher abandoning the process mid-drain exactly when items are awaiting flush. `drain()` on an empty manager is a no-op.
- **`start_all()`** (lifespan startup): one bulk `requeue_stranded()` across every KB; writes the FIRST-INDEX `knowledge.vector_store` settings record (`backend`/`dtype`/`dim` from the live store, `model_id=EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIRNAME` — never `get_embedding_backend().model_id`, which would load the model synchronously on the event loop) ONLY IF `AppMetadataRepository.get(VECTOR_STORE_KEY)` is `None` — a present record is never overwritten, even byte-for-byte; then `request_index`s every KB with at least one `folder` item or at least one `pending` `file` item — a KB with neither gets no worker (`state() == "idle"`), so an operator with no knowledge bases (or none needing work) pays nothing.
- **Module accessor**: `set_manager`/`get_manager` in `manager.py`, the `connectors/manager.py:260-271` shape. The lifespan (`app.py`) constructs `IndexingManager()` and publishes it via the accessor BEFORE its guarded `start_all()` call (unlike the connector-manager block, which only publishes on success) — routes reaching `get_manager()` mid-startup see a real, if not-yet-fully-started, instance. Shutdown drains via the lifespan's own local `_indexing_manager` global (not the accessor) right after the in-flight/resume task gathers and before the web-session reaper drain, then clears the accessor — and (Mission 24 Flight 7 Leg 1) captures `.cancelled_kb_ids()` into a local BEFORE that clear, threading it into the `close_vector_store()` shutdown hook later in the same function; see the "Vector-store shutdown hook" block below for the full DD-3/DD-3a contract.
- **Route wiring** (`api.py`): `create_kb_item`'s `_after_folder_registered(kb_id, item)` hook calls `get_manager().request_index(kb_id)` then `get_watch_manager().start(kb_id, item)`, each a no-op when its manager is not installed (pre-lifespan, or a route test with none injected) — widened from a one-argument hook to two by Flight 4 Leg 3, so the watcher can start a subscription against the just-created `KBItem` without a second repository read. `delete_knowledge_base` calls `await manager.stop(kb_id)` BEFORE the repository delete, the whole sequence (Flight 4: `watch.stop_kb(kb_id)` too) wrapped in `watch.suppress(kb_id)` — see the `WatchManager` block below. `delete_kb_item` of a `folder` item calls `stop` before the delete and `request_index` after (the remaining folders re-walk), likewise wrapped in suppression (plus `watch.stop(item_id)`); a `file` item delete calls neither indexing nor watch methods, and enters no suppression (the worker's own per-item `KeyError` catch handles a vanished row, and nothing watches a lone file). The four DD-8 routes (`GET /status`, `POST /reindex`, `GET .../chunks`, `GET .../source`) read `get_manager()`'s `state()`/call `request_index`/`requeue_for_reindex` directly; `/status`'s `"indexer"` field reads `"idle"` when no manager is installed, and (Flight 4) each `folders[]` entry's `"watch"`/`"watch_error"` fields read `"off"`/`None` when no watch manager is installed. `GET .../source` enforces the indexer's own `MAX_FILE_BYTES` (10 MiB) cap itself, independently of the indexer — `stat` then a `413` before extraction if the file is over cap (squawk 0063; previously unchecked despite the docstring claiming it). Route tests install a `_FakeIndexingManager` via `monkeypatch.setattr(manager_mod, "_manager", fake)` — the same slot `get_manager()` reads; Flight 4 adds a matching `_FakeWatchManager` via `monkeypatch.setattr(watcher_mod, "_manager", fake)`.

**`get_kb_status` gains two ADDITIVE signals (Flight 7 Leg 2, DD-6/DD-7)** — no existing key removed or renamed:
- **`"content_freshness"`** (top-level, sibling of `"items"`/`"chunks"`): `KBItemRepository.latest_indexed_at(kb_id, type="file")`, `MAX(indexed_at)` across the KB's `file` items. This is the inventory's content-only-edit signal — it advances **iff** a file item actually reaches `indexed` (the only site that stamps `indexed_at` is `update_status`'s transition into `"indexed"`, run by `_flush` for a real re-index, never on a no-op walk pass), which is the opposite of a **folder** item's `indexed_at` (`_walk_one_folder` stamps it unconditionally on every completed walk, changed content or not) — using the folder signal here would advance the aggregate every poll on a quiet corpus. `studio.js::knowledgeItemsSignature` folds this into its formula (`items | chunks | content_freshness`) so a content-only edit — which moves neither the per-status counts nor the chunk count — still triggers the `/items` re-fetch; `knowledge-folders.js::patchFileItemRow` renders it per item as `Indexed {date}, {time}` (`data-testid="kb-item-indexed-at"`, through `App.time.formatDate` + `formatClock` since the M31 F3 HAT; the precise `indexed_at` is the span's `data-indexed-at` and leads its `title`, with `content hash: …` after it; absent when the item has never indexed — no fabricated timestamp). Tests that need to see the stamp move assert on `data-indexed-at`, since the visible text is minute-grained.
- **`"skipped_dirs"`/`"skipped_entries"`** on each `folders[]` entry (DD-7): the last completed walk's counts, persisted through `AppMetadataRepository` under `the_one.knowledge.settings.walk_skip_counters_key(folder_item_id)` (`"knowledge.walk_skips.{id}"`) — a kv record rather than a schema column, chosen because `kb_items` had no spare column and a v42 migration was avoidable. **One shared key helper for all four call sites** — the write site (`manager.py::_walk_one_folder`, right after a walk completes, alongside the pre-existing skip-count log line), the read site (`get_kb_status`), and both cleanup sites (`delete_kb_item` for a `folder` item — no enumeration needed, since folders cannot nest and only the route's own `item_id` was ever the key; `delete_knowledge_base` — enumerates `item_repo.list_folders(kb_id)` **before** the repository delete, since the folder rows are gone after it) — so a drifted key derivation at any one site cannot silently orphan or fail to clean up a kv row. Cleanup is necessary because `app_metadata` sits outside `kb_items`' FK cascade. Both counts read `None` (not `0`) for a folder whose walk has never completed — `updateFolderMeta` in `knowledge-folders.js` never renders a fabricated "0" for a folder that has not been walked; since the M31 F3 HAT the folder's one meta line is "Last checked {date}, {time}" (`kb-folder-indexed-at`, from the folder's own `indexed_at`, precise value in `title` and `data-indexed-at`) followed by " · Skipped 2 folders, 1 file" (`kb-folder-skip-counts`, correct plurals, naming only the non-zero counts) only when a count is non-zero, so "walked, skipped nothing" shows no Skipped part. `AppMetadataRepository` gained a `delete(key)` method in this leg (it previously had only `get`/`set`).
- Squawk 0087 (fixed in the same leg): `KBItemRepository.list`/`.list_children`/`.list_folders` all gained a `, path_or_url` **ascending** tiebreak on `ORDER BY created_at` — a tied `created_at` batch (a folder walk inserts every discovered file in one pass) previously fell back to unspecified SQLite tie order. Ascending, not descending, because it matches `walk_folder`'s own `files.sort(key=lambda f: f.path)` — the two existing `DESC` tiebreak precedents in `repositories.py` (`AgentRevisionRepository.list_for_agent`, the MCP tool-config identifier list) are "most recent first" listings, a different case.

**`WatchManager`** (Flight 4, Leg 2/3, DD-0/DD-1/DD-2/DD-3/DD-4): `the_one/knowledge/watcher.py` — one supervised, dedicated-thread `watchfiles.watch` subscription per registered folder `KBItem`, the third `supervised-per-entity-worker` instance (see that convention's citation above for exactly what it shares and does not share with `ConnectorManager`/`IndexingManager`). The module docstring is the authoritative contract; this block is an index into it, not a restatement.

- **Contract summary**: the watcher is a latency optimisation, never a source of truth — it takes no lock, never `stat`s a path, and every trigger (an event, the post-subscribe first yield, or the periodic reconcile tick) is exactly one call to `index_manager.request_index(kb_id)`; the walk that call eventually runs is what decides what actually changed.
- **DD-0's pinned-knob rule**: every `watchfiles.watch` keyword is explicit at both call sites (the subscription here and the `doctor` gate below) — no library or environment default is inherited. `ignore_permission_denied` follows a two-step rule (`WatchManager._two_step_subscribe`): subscribe with `False` first; only on `PermissionError` — caught BEFORE `OSError`, since it is a subclass and an `OSError` clause written first would silently remove the retry — and only when the root itself is readable (`os.access(root, R_OK | X_OK)`, checked once at subscribe time, never in the event path), retry with `True`. `ignore_permission_denied=True` swallows every registration error, not only `EACCES` (verified against the library's own docstring), which is why the retry is gated on the root being independently readable rather than applied unconditionally — an unreadable root goes `unavailable` with a reason naming it, visibly, rather than constructing a zero-watch subscription that a first empty yield would then report as `watching`.
- **Message protocol**: a dedicated `threading.Thread` per entry runs the sync `watchfiles.watch` generator and posts `_Ready` (once, after the first yield), `_Changes` (once per non-empty yielded set), `_Error` (a construction or mid-iteration failure — the whole generator body is wrapped, not only construction), and a terminal `_Done` from a `finally` — the task's only termination path; without it every `stop`/`drain`/`apply_settings(False)` would block for its full timeout. Every post goes through `_post` (`loop.call_soon_threadsafe` guarded by `loop.is_closed()` and `try/except RuntimeError`), since a thread abandoned past the drain timeout may post after the loop has closed.
- **State vocabulary** (`state(folder_id)`): `"off"` — no entry (watcher disabled, no manager installed, or the folder was never started). `"starting"` — an entry exists but has not yet reported a first yield. `"watching"` — reported only after the first yield (an empty timeout yield counts — it proves the subscription is established). `"unavailable"` — the subscription is down; `error(folder_id)` names why.
- **The trigger (DD-3)**: `_schedule(kb_id)` arms or re-arms a per-knowledge-base `loop.call_later` for `settle_seconds` (5s default) after the most recent qualifying event, clamped to at most `hold_cap_seconds` (60s default) after the burst's first event. The post-subscribe first yield fires one extra `_schedule` immediately — the registration hook and the reconcile tick both request the index before the subscription exists, so without it any change landing in that window would wait a full interval. An empty timeout yield marks `watching` on first occurrence and does nothing else, ever — re-arming on it would fire a `request_index` per KB every hold cap for the life of the process, invisibly.
- **Suppression**: `suppress(kb_id)` (an `asynccontextmanager`) cancels the KB's armed timer and, for its duration, makes `_schedule`, the timer callback, and the reconcile tick skip that KB — releasing in a `finally` so a delete route's 404 (raised inside the guarded region) still releases it. Both delete routes hold it across their whole stop-then-delete-then-request_index sequence (see the route-wiring bullet above), closing the same race `IndexingManager`'s AC-10 closed for indexing alone.
- **The reconcile loop (DD-4)** runs unconditionally, watcher on or off (with it off, this loop is the only way a change is ever reflected): every `reconcile_interval_seconds` (default 300, read fresh from `MonitoringSettings` each tick — a settings change wakes the loop early via an `asyncio.Event` rather than waiting out the old interval), `request_index` for every KB with at least one folder and not suppressed, then `_resubscribe_unavailable()` retries every `unavailable` entry (root removed, a construction error, a folder that lost its cap slot) against a snapshot of the entries dict. Emergent property: a worker `IndexingManager._run` left `stopped` after a raise is restarted by the tick's `request_index` -> `start()` -> `task.done()` path, so an indexing failure is retried once per interval with no operator action.
- **Root removal (DD-1)**: the native backend reports exactly one `deleted <root>` on removal or rename, then silence. The task recognises a `Change.deleted` whose path (via `os.path.normcase`-compared `PurePath`s, never a raw string compare) equals the root itself, schedules one final trigger (so the next walk marks the folder `failed`), stops the thread, and marks the entry `unavailable`; a plain `modified <root>` (an ordinary directory modification) never tears the watch down. A drive that comes back is re-subscribed by the next reconcile tick without operator action.
- **The cap**: at most `MAX_WATCHED_FOLDERS` (64 default) live subscriptions; the 65th `start` creates an entry with no thread, `unavailable` with reason naming the limit — registration itself is never refused. Each subscription is one inotify *instance* as well as one descriptor per directory under the root (`watchfiles` applies its filter AFTER the native notifier has registered watches for the whole tree, `.git`/`node_modules`/`venv` included — the filter decides what *triggers* a pass, not what consumes a descriptor); `max_user_instances` is 8,192 on this project's dev host but 128 by default on many distributions, so the 64-folder cap can take half of a default budget shared with every other program the operator runs.
- **The WSL `/mnt/c` note**: with the native backend pinned everywhere (DD-0 — the operator's call, so dev/test exercise the same event stream the shipped build uses), a folder under a WSL `/mnt/c` (drvfs) path receives no events at all while still reading `watching`; the periodic walk is what keeps such a folder current there, and it is why the `kb-folder-watch-round-trip` behavior spec's fixture lives on a native filesystem (`/tmp` on this host) instead.
- **Lifespan order (DD-7)**: `app.py` constructs `WatchManager(index_manager=_indexing_manager)` and publishes it via the accessor immediately after the indexing manager's own `start_all()` block, then runs its own guarded `start_all()`. Shutdown drains it immediately BEFORE the indexing manager's drain (no `request_index` can land on an indexing manager that is stopping workers), budget `WATCH_DRAIN_TIMEOUT = 5.0`s — deliberately small since the Rust side returns within one 50ms step of the stop event and the threads are daemon threads regardless.
- **The DD-2 filter divergences from the walk** (both latency-only, never correctness — the walk remains the source of truth): a directory rename or removal whose *name* carries an unsupported suffix (`build.d`) produces no trigger until the periodic tick, since letting any suffixed name's `added`/`deleted` events through would turn ordinary build-tree churn into a walk per settle window; and the filter's `SKIPPED_DIRS` match is case-insensitive (it compares `normcase`d components) where the walk's is case-sensitive, so a `Node_Modules` directory would not trigger a watcher pass but would still be walked.

### Knowledge retrieval (Flight 5, Legs 1-2)

`the_one/knowledge/retrieval.py::search(kb_id, query, *, limit, chunk_repo, item_repo, store, backend) -> SearchResult` is the engine, with no agent, tool, or registry in sight — every dependency is a plain argument, so fusion is unit-testable and reusable unchanged by a future non-agent caller (e.g. an inventory view). It embeds the query once (`asyncio.to_thread`, no lock — see "Knowledge storage"'s per-KB-lock consequence above for the lock retrieval DOES take), runs the vector store's semantic query and `KBChunkRepository.search_keyword`'s keyword query concurrently under `asyncio.gather(..., return_exceptions=True)`, and fuses the two ranked lists by reciprocal rank (`RRF_K = 60`, ties broken toward the keyword side then ascending `chunk_id`). **One result slot is reserved for a keyword-only hit** whenever both lists are non-empty and no keyword-only hit already made the top `limit` on score alone — plain RRF at the mission's candidate depth (`candidate_k = min(max(limit*5, 25), 100)`) lets a both-list chunk always outrank a keyword-only rank-1 hit, so without the reserved slot an exact identifier the embedding model ranks poorly can be crowded out of the returned set entirely. `SearchResult.degraded` carries one of four causes when a side fails — `"semantic index rebuilding"` / `"semantic index busy"` / `"semantic index unavailable"` (keyword-only results survive) or `"keyword index unavailable"` (semantic-only results survive) — logged at WARNING and never raised unless BOTH sides fail (the semantic exception is raised, deterministically).

**`the_one/knowledge/tool.py::KBSearchTool`** is one instance per attached knowledge base, built per agent. `kb_id`/`kb_name` are baked into the instance at construction — never an argument the model supplies, since `ToolRegistry` gates on tool *name* — so an agent can only ever call `search` against a knowledge base the operator actually attached (`AgentConfig.kb_ids`; see `SECURITY-MODEL.md`'s "Knowledge-base indexing" bullet). Every dependency (connection, repositories, vector store, embedding backend) is resolved fresh inside `execute()`, never cached on the instance, since the tool is built once per agent construction and may outlive any one of them. `limit` (1-20, default 5) is rejected out of range with a `ValueError`, never clamped — the SAME convention as `search_keyword`'s own `k` bound, and the tool's own responsibility: `Agent._execute_tool` calls `tool.execute(**args)` with no JSON-Schema validation against `self.parameters`. The returned string is one header line (knowledge base, query, hit count) then, per hit, a citation line `[n] {path} #{seq} ({char_start}-{char_end})` followed by the chunk text — the citation resolves the chunk's own `item_id`/path, never a `parent_id` (a registered folder and each walked file are both items; the file's path is the operator-meaningful one). No hits renders one sentence, never an empty string. A degrade renders one line above the hits, with the accompanying sentence depending on which side degraded (the "unavailable"/"busy"/"rebuilding" trio are keyword-only results; the keyword-side cause is the reverse — semantic-only). No per-hit or total cap is applied in the tool itself — chunk text and `limit` already bound the worst case to roughly 20 KB, and the agent loop's context-budget governor (`Agent._govern`, `truncation_direction = "head"`) already truncates from the correct end since hits are emitted best-first.

**`the_one/knowledge/tool_provider.py::KBToolProvider.get_tools_for_agent(config, *, agent_name) -> list[Tool]`** is async and per-knowledge-base keyed — a sibling to `MCPToolProvider`, not a `the_one/tools/provider.py::ToolProvider` Protocol implementer (that Protocol is synchronous and per-tool-name keyed). Empty `config.kb_ids` returns `[]` with no database call; a `kb_id` that no longer exists is logged at WARNING (naming the agent and the id) and skipped, keeping the rest of the attached set (the MCP orphan-source precedent). Naming itself is delegated to the public `compute_tool_names` seam described next.

**Derived tool-name contract, as it stands after DD-17 (Flight 6, Leg 2)** — `the_one/knowledge/tool_provider.py::compute_tool_names(kbs: list[KnowledgeBase], *, agent_name: str) -> dict[str, str]` is the ONE naming seam; `KBToolProvider` and every cross-entity caller go through it rather than reimplementing the slug/discriminator logic (`_compute_tool_names` was promoted to this public name for exactly that reason — no caller reaches into a private symbol across the package boundary). `slug = _normalize_kb_name(kb.name)` mirrors `the_one/mcp/_tool.py::_normalize_source_name` exactly (every character outside `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]` becomes `_`, re-implemented rather than imported, for knowledge-package independence). The tool name is `kb__{slug}__search`, capped at the same 64-character LLM tool-name ceiling as `mcp__{source}__{tool}`. **The collision discriminator — the first 8 hex characters of `sha256(kb_id)`, stepping to 12 then 16 only if a group still collides at that length, never a prefix of `kb_id` — is applied UNCONDITIONALLY to every knowledge base, not only on collision or overflow.** Before DD-17 the discriminator was conditional (`hash_len_by_id[kb.id] = None` when a slug neither collided nor overflowed within the SET it was computed against), which made a derived name a function of the whole attached set: rename, delete, an attach that created a collision, or a detach that resolved one could all silently move a name a `disabled_tools` entry had to match exactly. DD-17 makes the name a pure function of `(kb.name, kb.id)` alone — independent of `kbs`'s membership or order — so only rename can still move it (DD-5, next). The over-length/collision checks in `compute_tool_names` still run, but only to decide whether to WARN, never whether to discriminate. **Residual, stated rather than hidden**: the uniqueness-assertion loop that widens 8→12→16 on a genuine hash collision is still set-scoped (it has to be, to detect a collision at all), so in that astronomically unlikely case a sibling's name can still move — known and unhandled, not papered over. DD-16's slug budget is 43 characters (not 52), because the discriminator's 9 characters now come out of every name, not just colliding ones.

**`compute_tool_names`'s caller set**: `KBToolProvider.get_tools_for_agent` (the agent's whole attached set, for the tool list actually registered); `interfaces/web/api.py`'s agent-response builder (same whole-set call, so a Studio attach-tab row can render the agent's current derived name — DD-3 — without reimplementing the algorithm in JavaScript); and `update_knowledge_base`'s rename path (two single-knowledge-base calls — `compute_tool_names([existing], ...)` and `compute_tool_names([updated], ...)` — computing the old and new derived name from the row in memory, before and after the write).

**DD-5 — rename rewrites `disabled_tools` across every attached agent (Flight 6, Leg 2)**: `update_knowledge_base` computes both derived names as above, writes the knowledge-base row, and — only if the raw name actually changed AND the two derived names differ (two different raw names can normalize to the same slug) — calls `the_one/knowledge/kb_rename.py::rename_kb_tool_references(store, kb_id, old_tool_name, new_tool_name)`. That function fetches the attached-agent set via `SQLiteAgentStore.list_by_kb(kb_id)` (a `json_each` query over the JSON `kb_ids` column — `list_by_provider`'s plain-equality shape does not transfer), then for each agent replaces every `disabled_tools` entry equal to `old_tool_name` with `new_tool_name` (duplicates collapsed), going through `store.load`/`store.save` so `mcp_overrides` credentials round-trip through the encryptor rather than a bypassing raw `UPDATE`. `enabled_tools` is never touched — `kb__` is in `UNGATED_TOOL_PREFIXES`, so `is_tool_enabled` never consults it for a `kb__` name. **Ordering is forced, not a style choice**: `write_transaction` raises on re-entry by the same task, so the knowledge-base write and the per-agent rewrites are sequential transactions, never one atomic unit — the knowledge-base row commits first, in every case. **Partial-failure semantics**: each agent's load+replace+save is individually wrapped in `try/except Exception`; a failure logs a WARNING naming the agent and continues to the next one rather than propagating (the rename already committed by the time any agent rewrite could fail, so raising here would surface an unhandled 500 after a rename that already happened). This fails OPEN — an agent whose rewrite fails keeps the old, now-stale `disabled_tools` entry — a stated limitation, diagnosable from the WARNING, not an oversight.

**DD-14 — delete writes nothing to any agent (Flight 6)**: `delete_knowledge_base` cascades to `kb_items`/`kb_chunks` and the vector store only; it never touches `agents.kb_ids` or any agent's `disabled_tools`. Two dangling values result, and neither is defended because each degrades safely for a different reason. **`kb_ids`**: a deleted knowledge base's id is left in every attached agent's stored `kb_ids` until that agent's next Studio save — `KBToolProvider.get_tools_for_agent` already logs a WARNING and skips an id that no longer resolves, and the self-configuration projection skips it too, so the dangling id is inert between delete and the next save, which drops it naturally (Studio's Knowledge tab renders only knowledge bases that still exist). **`disabled_tools`**: a deleted knowledge base's derived-name entry is left in `disabled_tools` permanently, but under DD-17 above it is permanently inert rather than a trap — the derived name includes `sha256(kb_id)[:8]`, so a LATER knowledge base created with the same *name* gets a different `kb_id`, therefore a different hash, therefore a different tool name, and can never match the stale entry. Before DD-17 this genuinely was a trap (a same-named replacement could inherit a pre-disabled tool silently); DD-17 removed the trap at its source, so no delete-time strip was added to defend against it. The stale entry remains as dead text in an agent's `disabled_tools`, re-emitted verbatim by any Studio save's ungated-prefix union — cosmetic and bounded, left for a future `kb_id`-keyed rework rather than cleaned up here.

**DD-13 (Mission 24 Flight 7, Leg 1) — read-side reconcile closes the detach -> rename -> reattach hole DD-5/DD-14 leave open**: `rename_kb_tool_references` (DD-5) only reaches an agent's stored `disabled_tools` when the agent is attached to the knowledge base AT RENAME TIME — `SQLiteAgentStore.list_by_kb` returns only currently-attached agents. An agent DETACHED at rename time keeps its `disabled_tools` entry under the OLD derived name forever; reattaching the knowledge base later builds the tool under the NEW derived name, and the stale entry no longer matches it literally — the operator's disable silently stops applying. `the_one/agent/factory.py::_kb_tool_is_disabled(tool, disabled_tools) -> bool` is the fix, called ONLY at the `kb_tools` registration loop (`AgentFactory.create`, not `KBToolProvider` — that provider never consults `disabled_tools` at all) in place of `config.is_tool_enabled(tool.name)` for `kb__` tools specifically (the shared `is_tool_enabled` method itself is untouched — it has other callers). The rule leans on DD-17's already-unconditional discriminator: strip the tool name's `kb__`/`__search` wrapper and, for each `disabled_tools` entry that also has that shape, check whether its body ENDS WITH `"_" + _discriminator(tool.kb_id, L)` for every `L in _HASH_LENGTHS` (8, 12, 16) — never a fixed width, and never by parsing/splitting the slug (a slug can itself contain `_`/`__`). A match disables the tool even though its current name differs from the stale entry's. **Read-side only** — `_kb_tool_is_disabled` never writes to `config.disabled_tools`; a stale entry stays stale on disk (re-emitted verbatim by Studio's ungated-prefix union on every save, per DD-5's note above) until the next rename-while-attached or an explicit operator `PUT`. This is deliberate: writing to an agent row from a read path is a worse trade than carrying the cheap normalization forever. `_kb_tool_is_disabled` is a plain function over a local view (a `getattr(tool, "kb_id", None)` guard makes it inert for any non-kb tool, though the loop only ever calls it on `KBSearchTool` instances) — pinned by `tests/test_factory_disable_enforcement.py::TestKbToolIsDisabledHelper` (pure-function cases: current-name match, stale-slug match, cross-`kb_id` non-match, all three `_HASH_LENGTHS` widths, an underscore-heavy slug, a non-`kb__`-shaped entry, no mutation) and `::TestKbToolStaleDisableReconcile` (the full detach -> rename -> reattach sequence against a real `KBToolProvider` and a real SQLite connection — deliberately NOT built on `StubProviderWithKbTool`, which returns a fixed tool name and therefore cannot reproduce a rename).

**Vector-store shutdown hook (Mission 24 Flight 7, Leg 1, DD-3/DD-3a) — closes the Known Issue `get_vector_store()` had no shutdown flush**: `the_one/vectors/__init__.py::close_vector_store(*, skip_kb_ids=frozenset()) -> None`, exported alongside `get_vector_store`. It is the shutdown-side mirror of `get_vector_store()`: closes the cached module-global `_store` if one was built, and **never** calls `get_vector_store()` — an instance that never touched a knowledge base has `_store is None` and the hook returns without touching disk (`get_vector_store()`'s own `IndexingManager.start_all()` caller builds a store even for zero knowledge bases, so "never builds one" is a property of the hook, not an unreachable state of the process). `_clear_cache()` is shared between this and `_reset_for_test()` so the two cache-clears cannot drift apart. `UsearchStore.close()` (and the `VectorStore` Protocol's `close()`) gained a keyword-only `skip_kb_ids` parameter, defaulted to `frozenset()` so every pre-existing caller is unaffected; the per-KB `try/except` flush loop it already had is unchanged except for skipping any `kb_id` named in the set.

- **The skip set closes DD-3a's torn-save hazard**: `UsearchStore.upsert` holds a per-KB lock across `await asyncio.to_thread(...)`; `IndexingManager.stop()`'s cancel fallback (`manager.py:240-243`) cancels that task, which releases the lock while the worker THREAD may still be mutating the index. Flushing immediately after would acquire the now-free lock and save concurrently with a live mutation. The fix surfaces state rather than guessing a settle duration: `IndexingManager.cancelled_kb_ids() -> frozenset[str]` reads a manager-level `self._cancel_report: dict[str, bool]`, written at the single symmetric site inside `stop()` (`self._cancel_report[kb_id] = cancelled`, unconditionally, on every call; since squawk 0095 a shutdown-flush TIMEOUT also sets it True, because cancelling the awaiting `to_thread` leaves the `index.save` thread running — the report means "the most recent stop left a possibly-running mutation") — **overwritten, never accumulated**: a clean stop for a `kb_id` clears any prior cancelled verdict for that SAME `kb_id`, because a folder delete calls `stop()` then immediately `request_index()` again (`api.py:1786,1798-1799`), so a `kb_id` can cancel once and index cleanly for hours before shutdown; an append-only report would keep skipping it and lose that later, genuinely safe-to-flush write. `_cancel_report` is deliberately a manager-level dict, NOT a `_WorkerEntry` field — `drain()` clears `self._entries` immediately after gathering every `stop()`, which would wipe a flag stored there before the shutdown hook (the one reader that matters) ever gets to read it; `_cancel_report` lives outside `_entries` for exactly that reason and survives `drain()`.
- **Lifespan wiring** (`app.py`): the hook is called unconditionally, between the existing indexing-manager drain block and `close_connection()` — after, because that drain flushes each worker's own knowledge base first; before, because `UsearchStore.flush()` writes through `self.conn` via `_upsert_registry_row()`. "Unconditionally" matters: a dirty store can exist with NO indexing manager at all (`api.py`'s item-delete route reaches `_vector_store()` directly), so gating the hook on `_indexing_manager is not None` would skip exactly the case it exists for — when there is no manager, `skip_kb_ids` is just the empty set. The cancel report is captured into a local (`_cancel_report_kb_ids`) INSIDE the `if _indexing_manager is not None:` block, immediately after `drain()` and BEFORE the block's own `_set_module_indexing_manager(None)` / `_indexing_manager = None` two-line null-out — a read taken after that null-out sees only `None`/empty, which is precisely the silent-empty-skip-set failure mode `tests/test_mcp_pool.py::test_lifespan_shutdown_threads_real_cancel_report_into_vector_hook` (AC-7a) exists to catch: it drives the REAL `app.py` shutdown branch (not a spy standing in for the hook's position) with a fake manager whose `drain()` populates a non-empty `cancelled_kb_ids()`, and asserts the hook is actually called with that value.
- **Test coverage split across three files**, each pinning a different failure mode the other two cannot see: `tests/test_vector_store_shutdown.py` (the hook in isolation — never-builds-to-close, closes-and-clears-cache, flush-with-skip, double-close safety); `tests/test_kb_manager.py::TestCooperativeStopAndCancelFallback`/`TestDrain` (the cancel report itself — overwrite symmetry, and survival through a REAL `drain()` call, not a hand-built `_entries` rig); `tests/test_mcp_pool.py::test_lifespan_shutdown_drain_order` (the hook's position in the real drain-order spy) plus the AC-7a test above (the report actually threading through the real wiring).

**Discovery — `kb:` group, and deliberately no `source_index` entry (DD-7, amended after three review cycles)**: `_search_tools.py::_format_result` gains a `kb__` branch in its prefix parse (beside the existing `mcp__` branch), rendering `kb:{slug}: …` — parsed from the tool name exactly like the MCP branch, and it inherits the identical latent flaw: a slug containing `__` (e.g. `"My  Notes"` normalizing to `"My__Notes"`) mis-renders the group label to `"My"` (`split("__", 2)[1]`). **A knowledge base gets NO `source_index` entry**, unlike an MCP source. `source_index` exists so a query naming an MCP *server* selects all of its tools en bloc; a knowledge base has exactly one tool, so that union adds nothing the per-tool match doesn't already find by the same text — proven, not just argued: `KBSearchTool`'s own `description` commits to carrying the knowledge base's name and description verbatim (DD-4), so the would-be source-level haystack is a strict subset of the per-tool haystack, and `_match_score` is a substring test, so `per_tool_score >= source_score` always. An entry would therefore be provably inert, and three real defects across two design-review cycles were all downstream of keeping it anyway (an unsatisfiable ordering between the provider call and `SearchTools`'s registration, `SearchTools.__init__`'s `source_index or {}` falsy-bind trap, and a `synthetic_description` plumbing gap with nowhere to source a knowledge base's description from). Removing the entry removes all three by construction. A knowledge base is therefore discovered purely through its tool's own `name + description` — the same path any built-in tool matches through.

**confirm-lapse cue (Mission 24 Flight 7, `confirm-lapse` leg)**: `the_one/interfaces/web/static/js/ui/confirm.js`'s shared two-step `App.ui.confirm` helper gained a purely visual `.confirm-lapsed` class distinguishing "the operator armed this and let it time out" from "never armed." Mechanically feedback-only (the leg's own AC-3 escalation gate): the arm/commit decision is unchanged and still depends solely on `.confirm-delete`; `.confirm-lapsed` gates nothing.

- **Only the timer-expiry path sets it.** A new `lapse()` function (`itemEl.classList.add('confirm-lapsed')` then delegates to the pre-existing `revert()`) is what `setTimeout` now calls instead of `revert` directly. `revert()` itself is untouched and still has its other two callers — a transport-failure `.catch` and `system-page.js`'s credential-rotation SUCCESS path (which calls `revertFn()` after a successful rotation) — neither of which may be mislabeled "lapsed," which is why the class is set by a wrapper around `revert()` rather than inside it.
- **Cleared unconditionally at the top of every `App.ui.confirm` call** (arm or commit branch alike, before that decision is made), so a returning operator's next interaction always starts from a clean visual slate.
- **Uniform across every `App.ui.confirm` call site** — no per-call-site opt-in, and no site-specific styling exception.
- **No aria-live announcement** — deliberate, not an omission: `self-configuration-tab.js` already announces its own ARM transition via a `role=status` region (needed there because its persistent `aria-label` overrides the swapped title text as the accessible name); giving LAPSE an equivalent announcement would mean either a per-call-site opt-in (which the leg's AC-5 forbids) or the helper owning a generic live-region contract, a larger surface than this leg covers.
- **Closed squawk 0088's third invariant** as a side effect: `renderAllItems` (`knowledge-folders.js`) must never rebuild a folder's `<summary>` DOM node while it may be armed, because a rebuilt node loses both the armed class and the timer's closure over the original element. `tests/browser/test_flow_studio_knowledge_inventory.py::test_armed_folder_confirm_survives_items_signature_tick` pins it — phase-locked to the app's own 5-second status poll so the arm's 3-second `revertMs` window cannot race a naturally-timed tick — and was falsified-then-reverted against a deliberately reintroduced regression to prove it actually pins the invariant rather than today's call graph. See `squawks/0088-armed-confirm-summary-invariant-unpinned.md`.

**Windows vector-store bench (Mission 24 Flight 7, `measurement` leg)**: `ci/tasks/windows-vector-bench.yml` is a new, ad-hoc Concourse task (not part of the standard five-lane `ci` job — invoked by hand, one rung at a time) that runs `scripts/bench_vector_store.py` on the `platform: windows` worker against a single synthetic-corpus size (`-v bench_n=<N>`, deliberately no default — an unset rung size fails the task rather than silently benchmarking the wrong size). It follows `ci/tasks/windows-installer-build.yml`'s two hard-won lessons: a fresh per-run `venv` (never the persistent `C:\Python311` site-packages, which previously shipped a stale `hf_xet`) and manual `$LASTEXITCODE` checks after every native PowerShell command (`$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` does not make PowerShell react to a native executable's exit code).

- **Per-cell backend recording (DD-10)**: `scripts/bench_vector_store.py` gained a per-cell `usearch_acceleration` table column, alongside the pre-existing host-level fact, so a fresh per-run venv — the fix for lesson one above — is exactly where a silent SIMD/acceleration backend difference could otherwise enter undetected one layer below the host-level fact.
- **The ladder** (AC-7/AC-8/AC-9 of the `measurement` leg): two 200k calibration runs establish a variance band declared BEFORE the 500k rung runs; the 500k rung (build 76, 1h17m49s) is checked against that pre-declared band. **Operator decision 2026-09-20: stop at 500k** — the 1M rung was never launched; its numbers are a verified linear extrapolation from two independent anchors instead of a direct measurement (the flight log names the i8 quantization's +8.18% residual drift). Re-confirmed Flight 3's flush-cadence constants against the measured curve; no product constant changed.
- **Must never run concurrently** with `ci/tasks/windows-installer-build.yml` or a `make test-browser` full run on the same host — a multi-hour rung and another heavy job sharing one uncontainerised Windows worker is how Flight 2's build 59 died (client-death-took-the-build-with-it). Launch detached (`setsid nohup … & disown`) and poll with `fly watch`, never hold the local `fly` client in the foreground.
- **`scripts/sweep_fusion_constants.py`** (new, same leg) is the companion `RRF_K`/`candidate_k` sweep that closed the mission's fusion-balance open question as "fixed by evidence, not tuned" (DD-4) — no `RRF_K`/`candidate_k` constant changed as a result; the sweep is the evidence trail, not a live gate.
- See `missions/24-knowledge-bases/flights/07-mission-close/flight-log.md`'s "Leg measurement (3)" section for the full band declaration, per-rung raw figures, and verdicts, and the mission's Known Issues entry for the Windows-unaccelerated `usearch` note this leg's live per-row confirmation updated.

### Web-session idle reaper

The `_sessions: dict[str, Session]` in `the_one/interfaces/web/app.py` is the warm in-memory cache for active UI chat sessions. Each entry pins a live `Agent` (LLM HTTP client, full message history). A background idle reaper (F05 L01, Mission 12 Flight 05) bounds memory growth in long-lived processes.

- **Reaper**: `_web_session_reap_loop()` / `_reap_web_sessions_once()` in `app.py`. Wakes every `max(30, TTL // 10)` seconds. Evicts sessions where `not subscribers_for(sid)` AND `not is_session_processing(sid)` AND `(monotonic() - last_activity) > TTL`.
- **Safety invariant**: sessions with ≥1 live WS subscriber (open chat tab) are NEVER reaped, regardless of idle age. Sessions mid-processing (tool calls up to 120s) are protected by the `is_session_processing` guard.
- **Teardown**: eviction calls `await agent.aclose()` outside the `_sessions_lock` (two-phase pattern — snapshot+pop under lock, close after release). `Agent.aclose()` delegates to `LLMProvider.aclose()`; Anthropic/OpenAI providers release their persistent `httpx` client; Ollama (per-request client) is a no-op.
- **Rehydration**: evicted sessions rehydrate losslessly from `chat_sessions` DB on next join (the DB is the durable backing store; the in-memory dict is a warm cache).
- **TTL**: `THE_ONE_WEB_SESSION_IDLE_TTL_SECONDS` env var, read at call time via `os.environ.get`, int-parse with fallback to 1800 (30 min). See `.env.example`.
- **Lifespan**: reaper started just after `start_periodic_sweep()` on startup; `_web_session_reaper_shutdown_drain()` called on shutdown after in-flight task drains, before PTY registry drain.

### Chat WebSocket protocol (`/ws`)

`websocket_endpoint` in `the_one/interfaces/web/app.py` runs one strictly sequential loop per socket, with at most one bound session. Client frames are `{action, ...}`; a frame without `action` is a plain `message`. Session-scoped frames (`user`, `assistant_chunk`, `assistant`, `thinking`, `tool_*`, `system`, `usage`, `done`, `cancelled`, `auth_*`) carry no `session_id`: they go to every subscriber of the session, so a socket must be unsubscribed (`leave`, or a swap) to stop receiving a session's frames.

| Action | Reply | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `join` `{session_id}` | `session_joined` (ok or degraded), or `join_failed` `{session_id, error: "not_found"}` | A degraded join of the already-bound id unsubscribes the socket too (M25 F6 L2, squawk 0119). An unknown id unbinds whatever was bound and **creates nothing** (M25 F4 L2; it used to auto-create a default-agent session). Without a `session_id`: re-binds the bound session, or auto-creates when unbound (no client path uses it). |
| `leave` | none | Unsubscribes from the bound session, touches its `last_activity`, binds nothing. Never cancels a running turn (it completes and persists), writes nothing to the DB. A no-op when unbound. |
| `start_session` `{session_id, agent_name, message, images}` | `session_joined`, or `start_failed` `{session_id, error}` | Creates the session on the first send (M25 F4 DD1); contract below. |
| ~~`new_session`~~ | none | **Removed** in M25 F4 L3b (squawk 0111): no client sends it. The frame now falls into the message path, whose validator finds it empty: no reply, nothing created. |
| `message` `{message, images}` | the turn's frames, or a `system` error | Auto-creates a default-agent session when unbound. |
| `list_sessions` / `delete_session` `{session_id}` / `cancel` | `session_list` / `session_deleted` / `cancelled` | A `cancel` sends **exactly one** `cancelled` per cancel (M25 F6 L2, squawk 0120; a second `cancel` arriving while the first batch is still unwinding replaces the per-session token, so the contract is at most one `cancelled` frame per `cancel` message), including when it hits a task not yet started or waiting on the session lock: a per-cancel token (`_cancel_tokens`) plus `_started_tasks` / `_running_tasks` markers pick one sender, and a started, running turn is the sole sender (after saving partial text). Nothing in flight sends nothing. |
| (server push, no request) | `sessions_changed` `{sessions}` | Sortie 05 leg 2. The server sends the FULL list (the `session_list` per-row payload, newest first) to EVERY connected socket after a write that changes what the Chats list shows (a connector chat created or updated, a web chat created/saved/titled, a delete, a purge, an agent rename), coalesced to at most one push per ~250 ms. The client only refreshes its local `sessions` array and primes the store; it never runs the rebind logic, the idle rules or any render of its own, so a push cannot open, move or close a chat. See "Live Chats list push". |
| (server push, no request) | `session_updated` `{session_id}` | Sortie 05 leg 3. Sent to the subscribers of ONE session after a connector turn on it (`dispatch_inbound`'s `finally`, after the per-session lock is released, suppressed on error; sent on a successful turn, a failed step and a failed `factory.create`, not when nothing was admitted). The client re-joins that chat (`join` of the same id) to read the new history. See "Live open connector chat". |
| (turn frame) | `step_limit` `{steps}` | M31 F6 leg 06 (DD8 F2). Sent when the turn ended because the agent ran out of steps (`Agent.stopped_at_step_limit`, `steps` = the agent's `max_iterations`), **immediately before `done`** in each non-paused branch: on the step path (`_process_and_broadcast`) after `_save_session_to_db`; on the resume path (`_resume_paused_session`) inside `if not re_paused:`, before that path's `done` (its later save is not moved). **Broadcast-only** through `_send_to_session`, never `add_event`: not in `Session.history` (so no `session_joined` replay) and not in the DB. Not sent when the step raised. The REST `POST /api/chat` result gains `"step_limit": true` (key absent otherwise), through `_process_and_broadcast`'s `turn_info` out-parameter (the flag is captured once right after the agent loop; the handler holds no session lock). Session-scoped, so the gate accepts it only while `joined`. The client renders a muted `chat-step-limit` notice (`App.ui.notice`) with "max iterations" linking to `#agents/{name}` when the agent has a stored record (HAT, L10: with a live Continue button the sentence reads "Stopped: this agent used its {n} steps for this message. Click Continue or raise max iterations in its settings." and the button sits on its own line below, centred in `.chat-step-limit-actions`; with no button, a blocked composer or a held message, it reads "... Send another message to continue, or raise max iterations in its settings."; the CLI and connector lines are unchanged); `steps` below 1 leaves the count out. **Continue (M31 F6 L10d):** the notice carries a `chat-step-limit-continue` button that sends `continue` through `send.sendText` (held while a turn runs; never reads the composer or staged images); one live button at a time, held by `transcript.js` (`liveContinue`), dropped by any user message, `clearLog()` or a newer notice, and absent when the composer is blocked or the hold queue is non-empty at notice time. |

**Step-limit flag (M31 F6 leg 06, DD8 F2).** `Agent.stopped_at_step_limit` (public bool, default `False`) is cleared as the first statement of `step()`, of `resume_after_auth()` (before its no-op return) and in `reset()`, and set only when `_run_iterations`' `while` ends with no `return` (a last iteration with text and tool calls, or `resume_after_auth` with a zero remaining budget, or `max_iterations <= 0`); a text answer, the degraded backstops and an auth pause return and leave it unset. It is set before the generator returns (squawk 0219), so a consumer reads it right after its `async for`: the web paths (above), `dispatch_inbound` (captured into a local, then `_send_step_limit_line` AFTER the persist: a never-raising helper sending the neutral line "(Stopped: reached its step limit for this message.)" to the originating target through the same client, always, including after a `send_to_platform_channel` turn; no client or a failed send is logged and dropped; the line is not stored) and the CLI (`_print_step_limit_notice`, a dimmed line). The notice never reaches the model, so it is not platform prompt text and needs no eval run.

**Shared payload validation.** `message` and `start_session` both go through `_validate_message_payload(payload) -> ValidatedPayload(user_input, images, content, error, empty)`: `message: null` is `""`, any other non-`str` is `"Message must be text."`; then emptiness, image shape, count and per-image size, then the content build (`_build_user_content`). `message` keeps a silent `continue` on empty and sends `system` frames with the same texts. The content build runs **before** the auto-create, so a rejected payload never creates a session (and a `/reset` carrying a malformed image is an error, not a clear).

**`start_session` contract** (pinned by `tests/test_ws_start_session.py`):
- **Validation, then nothing created.** `session_id` must be a `str` matching `[0-9a-f]{32}` (the client generates it); `agent_name` a non-empty `str` other than `"default"`; the payload must be non-empty, not exactly `"/reset"`, and pass the shared validator. Each rejection is one `start_failed` and leaves the socket's binding untouched. `start_failed.session_id` echoes the id when it was a `str`, else `null`.
- **Idempotency rule.** An id that already exists is joined exactly like `join` (even another agent's session): ok → bind, `session_joined`, pause snapshot; degraded → unbind whatever was bound, degraded frame. The message is processed **only if it was never delivered** (empty history, nothing in `_session_inflight`, lock not held: a disconnect between the create and the spawn), so the first message is processed exactly once. Re-sending the same `start_session` (a reconnect while starting) is therefore always safe.
- **Reservation.** `_start_reservations: dict[str, asyncio.Future]` is taken by a synchronous check-and-set (`_acquire_start_reservation`) before the lookup and held through the spawn; the holder resolves and pops it in a `finally`. A second handler for the same id waits `asyncio.wait_for(asyncio.shield(fut), _START_RESERVATION_WAIT_S)` per wait (the shield keeps one waiter's timeout from cancelling the shared future), sends `start_failed` on timeout, and otherwise re-reserves and looks up again: it joins what the holder created, or becomes the creator if the holder failed.
- **Strict create.** `_create_session(agent_name, session_id=sid, strict=True)` pops `_sessions[sid]` and re-raises on a persist failure **or** a cancellation (`BaseException`); `strict=False` (every other caller) still logs and swallows. Only the create sits in the `try`, so a failure after it never reports `start_failed` for a created, bound session.
- **Order after a create.** Unsubscribe the old session; bind and subscribe the new one before the first send (so the outer disconnect handler cleans up); `session_joined` with `is_processing: true` (`_build_session_joined_ok(..., is_processing=True)`: the spawned turn does not hold the lock yet); pause snapshot; then `_spawn_web_message`, the same helper as `message`. `session_joined` therefore always precedes the turn's frames.
- **`_restore_session` skips the rebuild** while the session's lock is held **or** a turn is registered in `_session_inflight`, so a first turn spawned but not yet holding its lock is never rebuilt from the empty DB snapshot.
- **Clients re-send `start_session` while starting, never `join`.** The reservation does not gate `join`: a `join` landing between `_create_session`'s in-memory insert and its DB insert finds no row and answers `join_failed` (correct for a not-yet-created id). `_restore_session`'s not-found path no longer pops the cached session while the id is in `_start_reservations`, so the create still completes with the session cached (pinned by `TestRestoreNotFoundVsReservation`).

**Turn-start re-sync of the web session cache (sortie 05 leg 1, DD4).** The cached web `Session` can go stale when another writer (a connector turn through `dispatch_inbound`, a REST rename or purge) changes the stored row. `Session.db_fingerprint` is `_fingerprint(messages, title)` (sha256 over `title or "New chat"` and `messages or "[]"`, the defaults `_row_to_model` applies on read). It is set in `_restore_session` (both branches, from the row it already read), in `_create_session` (from the row it creates) and in `_save_session_to_db` (from the exact encoded values passed to `repo.update`, only after it returns; no read-back). `_locked_message_processing` calls `_resync_session_from_db` right after taking `session_locks[id]`, in its own `try/except Exception` (a failed read logs a warning and the turn proceeds; `CancelledError` propagates). `db_fingerprint is None` means no baseline and no read happens (bare test Sessions; a non-strict create whose row never landed); a missing row changes nothing; an equal fingerprint stops there; a different one replaces the agent's non-system messages (keeping its system message), `history` and `title` from the row and adopts the row's fingerprint. It runs even when the agent is paused for auth, because `Agent.step` clears the deferral anyway. Known edges: a write landing between `_restore_session`'s read and its cache update leaves an older fingerprint, so the next turn re-syncs (benign); resume-after-auth runs without the lock and without a re-sync; REST `/api/chat` has no re-sync; a second tab's re-join drops a pending deferral. Tests: `tests/test_web_session_resync.py`, and `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_live_updates.py` through the `app_with_stub_connector` fixture and `seeders.stub_inbound` (stub test seam).

**Live Chats list push (sortie 05 leg 2, DD1-DD3).** Three pieces.

- **The change signal** (`the_one/db/_session_changes.py`, stdlib-only leaf): `add_listener` / `remove_listener` / `notify_sessions_changed()` (every listener in a `try/except` with `logger.exception`; never raises; no-op with no listener) / `clear_for_test()` (called from `tests/conftest.py::reset_lifespan_singletons`). `ChatSessionRepository.create`, `update`, `delete`, `delete_all_for_agent` (only when it deleted rows), `delete_older_than` (only when it returned ids), `purge_messages` and `update_last_inbound`, and `SQLiteAgentStore.rename` (its `chat_sessions.agent_name` re-point) call it once, AFTER their `write_transaction` block exits, so a listener reading the row back sees committed data. `append_to_session_metadata` / `set_session_metadata_key` do not (metadata is not on the list). A call that raises (missing row) does not notify. `ChatSessionRepository.list_summaries()` is `list()` without reading `messages` or `session_metadata` (`NULL AS ...`; `_row_to_model` defaults `messages` to `"[]"`), the read behind the push.
- **The socket registry** (`the_one/sessions/_broadcast.py`, beside the per-session subscriber registry): `add_socket` / `remove_socket` / `all_sockets()` (snapshot copy) / `async broadcast_to_all(payload)` (a socket whose send raises is dropped), cleared by `clear_subscribers_for_test()`. `websocket_endpoint` registers every accepted socket and unregisters it in a `finally` around `_websocket_loop` (the receive loop, unchanged).
- **The coalescing listener** (`app.py::_SessionsPushListener`, registered in the lifespan just before `yield`): a signal (from any thread; an on-loop check decides between a direct schedule and `call_soon_threadsafe`, a closed loop is ignored) arms ONE trailing push `WINDOW` (0.25 s) later; later signals fold in. The push clears `pending` BEFORE it reads, so a write landing during the read arms another push and none is lost; no sockets means no DB read; a failed read logs a warning and sends nothing. Shutdown removes the listener FIRST (right after `yield`, before every drain) and `close()` cancels and awaits the push task before `close_connection()`. Out-of-order snapshots (a push crossing a `session_list` reply) are accepted: the next change corrects them. Pinned by `tests/test_session_changes.py` and `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_live_updates.py`.

Client (`chat.js` keeps `toSessionSummary(s)`; `chat/socket.js` holds the frames): `toSessionSummary(s)` is the one mapping from a server row to the REST summary shape; the `session_list` reply and the `sessions_changed` branch both prime `App.store` `sessions` through it. The push branch sets `state.sessions`, primes, and returns (`test_store_static.py::test_chat_session_list_primes_sessions_in_the_summary_shape`). `renderChatsList` omits a listed row whose id is the starting `clientId` (the pending row stands for it until `session_joined` swaps it). A chat deleted elsewhere while open loses its row but its pane stays (recorded edge).

**Live open connector chat (sortie 05 leg 3, DD5-DD6).** `dispatch_inbound` wraps its locked block in `try/finally`; the `finally` (after the lock is released, inside `contextlib.suppress(Exception)`; a `CancelledError` still propagates) sends `{"type": "session_updated", "session_id"}` through `broadcast_to_session`. Early returns before a session is resolved (not admitted, pairing, `/new`, no reply) send nothing. In `chat/socket.js`: `session_updated` is session-scoped (`session-state.js::accepts` leaves it to `joined`); the branch checks the id against `boundId()`, and a frame for the id being joined sets `refreshAfterJoin` (re-join once more after that reply, since the update can land between the server's join read and its reply). Flags keyed by session id: `ownTurn` (a turn THIS client sent: `performSend` in `chat/send.js`, `startSend` in `chat/session-flow.js`; distinct from `inFlightSessions`, which an `is_processing` join also sets), `refreshAfterTurn`, plus `updateRejoin` (`{id, pinned, scrollTop}`) and `refreshAfterJoin`; `clearLiveRefresh(id?)` (`chat/session-flow.js`) is their one clear (error path, `onclose` non-`starting` branch, `applyStartFailed`, `openConversation` to another id, `pickAgent`, `resetToIdle`, `clearConversation`). With an own turn outstanding the re-join waits for its `done`/`cancelled`; otherwise `rejoinForUpdate(id)` records the pin state and offset and calls `openConversation(id)`. A `session_joined` answering such a re-join: not processing with a held message sends it through `sendHeld` (shared with `done`); processing marks one more re-join for the next `done`; an unpinned reader's `scrollTop` is restored after the forced scroll to the bottom. A connector turn never emits `done`, so a join mid-turn used to keep the thinking indicator forever: the re-join's `is_processing: false` reply clears it. A paused agent: `_send_pause_snapshot_if_needed` runs after every `join` reply (same-id re-joins included), so the login group is replayed and the re-join needs no deferral. Edge: a web send made while the chat is `joining` is ignored (the text stays in the composer), so a re-join briefly blocks Send. Tests: `tests/test_inbound_session_updated.py`, `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_live_updates.py`.

### Chat start flow (client, M25 F4 L3b)

The chat area (`static/js/areas/chat.js` and the modules below it, see "Chat module map") runs on a **pure session state machine**, `App.chat.createSessionState()` (`static/js/areas/chat/session-state.js`, loaded before `chat.js`): no DOM, no WebSocket, no timers; every transition returns its effects as data, so the table below is unit-tested as `page.evaluate` input and output (`tests/browser/test_flow_chat_session_state.py`). States: `idle`, `joining(id)`, `joined(id)`, `pending(agent)`, `starting(clientId, agent, payload)`. **There is no "null means idle"**: every former `currentSessionId` null check is a state check or `activeId()` (the client id while starting, the session id while joined, else null; busy, the F09-04 hold slot and Stop key on it). `_currentSessionId` stays "the joined or joining id" (`boundId()`); `_sessionState()` is the test seam.

**Chat hold queue (M31 F6 L10a).** `state.pendingQueuedMessages[sessionId]` is an ARRAY of strings in send order (max 10, `holdQueue.MAX`); an empty queue has no key (an empty array is truthy), so readers test `holdQueue.count(sid)` and every discard goes through `holdQueue.discardAll(sid)`, which returns the count. `hold-queue.js` owns `list/count/push/shift/discardAll/refresh/showFull` plus the chips: one `App.ui.chip` per message ("Queued" lead, testids `pending-queued`/`pending-queued-cancel`, `data-index`), cancel named "Cancel queued message" for one and "Cancel queued message {i} of {n}" for several (all chips rebuilt on every change), and one visually hidden status line `pending-queued-status` ("{n} messages queued") inside the persistent polite `pending-queued-region`. Removing a chip moves focus to the next chip, else the previous one, else the message box. Each `done` sends only the first held message (`sendHeld`; the rest stay chipped); a closed or rebinding socket keeps the queue. A Stop, `/reset`, conversation switch, socket reset and an agent error drop the whole queue (Stop and the agent error say "Queued message discarded." or "{n} queued messages discarded."; nothing is auto-sent after a failed turn). At the cap a Send does nothing, keeps the text and shows the muted `pending-queued-full` note under the composer. A failed start restores the first held message to an empty box and reports the rest as discarded.

**Chat module map (M31 F6 L02, DD3).** `areas/chat.js` was split with no behaviour change into `chat.js` plus eight `App.chat.<name>.create(ctx)` modules under `areas/chat/` (the studio house pattern: one shared `ctx`, one `state` object, call-time access to later-created modules; ES5, `'use strict'` IIFEs). `chat.js` keeps the descriptor, `state`, `chatState`, construction (list, pane, composer, drawer), `renderPane`, `renderChatsList`, `toSessionSummary`, the subscriptions, the welcome, `applyComposerState`, `setSessionHealth`, `refreshAgentsAndSetup`, the composer/Stop wiring and the test seams, with `registerPickerHost` after every module's creation. The modules, created leaves first: `hold-queue.js` (the hold queue and its chips), `attachments.js` (staging, chips, caps, the attach listeners), `transcript.js` (`addMsg`, the scroll pin, `renderHistory`, `clearLog`, streaming, thinking and model loading, traces, tool bubbles, delivered results, the notices, the live `assistant_chunk` body; on `App.ui.message`/`thinking`/`trace`/`notice` since L05c, see "Chat transcript on the library"), `send.js` (`send`, `performSend`, `sendHeld`, `setInputValue`, `isCurrentBusy`, `updateStopControl`), `sign-in.js` (popups, login flows, the login group, `settleClose` above the gate and the `auth_*` handlers), `turn-frames.js` (the gated frame chain `handle(data)` and `handleSystem`, including the model-loading row), `session-flow.js` (pick, open, reset, clear, idle rules, hydrate, validate, heal, `startSend`, `applyStartFailed`, the live-refresh flags, `pendingJoinId`) and `socket.js` (`ws`, `boundOn`, `connect`, `onopen`, `onclose`, the single `sock.onmessage` and the session frames). Shared mutable variables are `state.x` (`inFlightSessions`, `ownTurn`, `refreshAfterTurn`, `updateRejoin`, `refreshAfterJoin`, `pendingQueuedMessages`, `sessions`, `listSeen`, `firstListPending`, `rebinding`, `validating`, `pickIntent`, `currentAgentName`, `currentSessionPlatform`, `joinedTitle`, `liveToolNames`, `startBubbleNodes`, `bannerActive`, `setupState`, `setupIncomplete`); the socket is `ctx.ws()`; `self._pendingJoinId` goes through `sessionFlow.pendingJoinId()`/`setPendingJoinId()`. **Frame order (socket.js, pinned by reading and `test_oauth_modal_flow.py`):** `boundOn = sock` on `session_joined`, the session lifecycle frames (each returns), the popup close for `auth_resolved`/`auth_failed` (`signIn.settleClose`), the gate `chatState.onFrame(data).accept`, then `turnFrames.handle(data)`. The `session_joined` handler is one block in `socket.js`. A static test that reads chat code reads the module that now holds it (`tests/test_store_static.py`, `tests/test_consent_link_static.py`).

**Routine messages (M33 F1 L7, DD7).** A user event with `routine_name` (live frame or stored) is built by `transcript.addUserEvent(ev, {ts, history})`, the one place a user event becomes a bubble: attribution is the routine's name (side `you`, right-aligned), `App.ui.message` gets `marker: {label: 'Routine'}` (an `App.ui.chip` size sm inside `span.ui-message-marker-chip`, any role, after the operator chip and before the peer label and body; null is a no-op, a missing or empty label throws), `addMsg` sets `data-routine-id` on the bubble root, and there is no `[routine] ` prefix (other non-web sources keep theirs). In `turn-frames.js` a live `user` frame with `source === 'routine'` whose `session_id` equals `chatState.boundId()` sets `state.inFlightSessions[sid]` (never `ownTurn`), then `updateStopControl()`, `applyComposerState()` and `showThinking()` (after the bubble); Stop sends `cancel`, sends are held, `done` clears it and drains the hold queue, and a mid-run re-join restores the state from `is_processing`.

**Chat transcript on the library (M31 F6 L05c, flight DD4, DD7, DD8, DD11).** The four area renderers (`message-bubble.js`, `thinking-indicator.js`, `trace-shell.js`, `telemetry-overlay.js`) and their `chat.css` rules are deleted; the log is built from `App.ui.message`, `App.ui.message.attribution`, `App.ui.thinking`, `App.ui.trace` (with `.find` and `.thinking`), `App.ui.telemetry` and `App.ui.notice`. The contract:

- **Bubbles.** `transcript.addMsg(cls, text, attribution, opts)` keeps its signature (`cls` is the role, plus `api` or `error`); it builds `App.ui.message({role, source, tone, body|text, visibility, visibilityStyle: App.ui.message.visibilityStyles.chip, peerName, testid: 'message'})` (an assistant body is `App.markdown.render(text)`) and an attribution (`you`/`agent`/`peer` side, `timeLabel` from `App.time.formatClock`, `ts` kept as `data-ts`). `visibilityStyles` exists so the area never spells the owned class token `chip` (the ownership ratchet counts string literals); the area's allowance holds no row.
- **Streaming.** `onAssistantChunk` builds `App.ui.message({streaming: true})` and its attribution and accumulates the **full received text** (`streamSource`, reset per bubble, i.e. per iteration); `pacingTick` / `commitSafeBoundary` / `drainPacing` drive the bubble through `appendText`, `tail()` and `commit(App.markdown.render(prefix), prefix.length)` (the safe-boundary logic is the area's, DD4); `finalizeStreamingBubble` calls `finalize(...)` (idempotent) and then `attribution.setCopy(source)`. **F1:** a finished agent reply has one "Copy reply" (testid `message-copy`) copying its stored content (replay and the live non-streaming `assistant` frame pass `copyText`), the received text for a Stopped reply, and none for an empty reply or a delivered tool result; confirmations announce in the one `chat-copy-status` node (`role=status`, in the area panel outside the `role=log` list).
- **`clearLog()`** is the ONE log wipe (DD11 4): it destroys the thinking and model-loading handles (and removes their nodes), resets the stream and the trace map and the anchor, and empties the log. The wipe sites (`session-flow.js` `renderPending` and `clearConversation`, and `renderHistory`) call it; no 120 ms dot interval survives one. `Loading model...` builds a system-tone `App.ui.thinking` ("Loading model", testid `model-loading`) kept as a handle and settled in place by `Model loaded.`; it is also dropped on an agent error, `done` and `cancelled`. The thinking label is "Thinking" and the control's dots are the ellipsis (a static ellipsis under reduced motion).
- **B2, the thinking disclosure's place.** The live `thinking` frame carries no message id and arrives after its reply, so the transcript keeps a per-iteration anchor: the streaming bubble, or the last assistant bubble added since the iteration began; it is cleared at `tool_call`, `tool_result`, `user`, `done` and `cancelled`. The disclosure (`App.ui.trace.thinking`, testid `thinking-disclosure`) is inserted **before** the anchor; with no anchor (a thinking-plus-tool-call iteration) it is appended in stream order. On replay the anchor is only the bubble built from the immediately preceding `assistant` event.
- **Traces.** `getOrCreateTrace(parentId)` builds `App.ui.trace({id, label: 'Tool activity', chevron: 'icon', countStyle: 'dot', testid: 'trace-shell'})` ("Tool activity · N") and re-wraps an existing one with `App.ui.trace.find` after a re-join; `traceShellFor` returns the node (the sign-in group places itself after it).
- **Notices.** A `system` frame whose content starts `Agent error: ` (kept by leg 06, which changes only the wording after the prefix) becomes a danger `App.ui.notice` (testid `chat-agent-error`): "The agent couldn't finish this reply." and the raw text after the prefix under a collapsed plain `App.ui.disclosure` "Technical details"; an open streaming bubble is finalized first. A `cancelled` frame appends the muted "Stopped." (`chat-stopped`) and, when held messages went with the Stop, "Queued message discarded." (one) or "{n} queued messages discarded." (`chat-discarded`): `stopTurn` records the count in `state.discardedOnStop[id]` before it clears the queue, and the `cancelled` handler also reports a held message still present (a Stop from another tab) and consumes the flag. Neither line is stored, so a `refreshAfterTurn` re-join right after wipes both. Other system messages ("Context cleared.") stay system bubbles.
- **Telemetry.** `chat.js` builds one `App.ui.telemetry({thresholds: fn reading App.config, testid: 'chat-telemetry'})` into the composer's telemetry slot (handle `ctx.telemetry`; `_telemetry()` is the test seam). `session_joined` calls `reset()` then, for a persisted `last_usage`, `update(... max_context_tokens: 0)`; the `usage` frame calls `update` (the perf marks stay in `turn-frames.js`); `setMax` touches only the maximum. **DD11 5 and 0.3:** the join's context-window fetch is skipped when the join `status` is not `ok` (a deleted agent: a request would only 404 in the console) or when the loaded, ok `agents` store holds no such agent (an unloaded store still fetches); its response is applied only while `chatState.boundId()` and `currentAgentName` are unchanged.
- **The zone relabel** is `App.ui.message.relabel(messagesEl, fn)` (`fn` uses `App.time.formatClock`, null for an invalid date).
