- **Looks decided here:** A4 (the agent error), A8 ("Thinking…", "Loading model…"), A15 (the timestamp at full strength: `--fg-faint` on `--bg`, pinned by `test_message_timestamp_passes_body_aa` and a usage pin), A18 (the telemetry's 2px focus offset), B2, B3 (an icon chevron and "· N"), B5 (the "Operator only" chip), B9 (the notices) and F1 (Copy reply).
- **Tests.** `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_transcript_library.py` (the `chats-transcript` lane) holds the new cases; every other chat test names a bubble through `_chats.message_sel(role)` / `_chats.messages(page, role)` and the readout through `_chats.telemetry(page)`. `tests/browser/_chats.py` also has `emit_frame` (a synthetic server frame through the real `ws.onmessage`).

| From | Event | To | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pending(A)` / `starting(_, A)` | `pick(A)` | unchanged | none (the header button, a re-click, a re-hydrate) |
| any other | `pick(agent)` | `pending(agent)` | `leave` if joined, joining or starting; discard the held message (`activeId()`); clear `pendingJoinId`, `startedFrom`, `suppressAutoJoin`; the pending pane (the new-chat state since leg 4b) |
| `pending(agent)` | `startSend(cid, payload)` (socket open, not rebinding) | `starting` | `start_session` (`cid` = 32 hex from `crypto.getRandomValues`); optimistic bubble; busy |
| `starting(cid)` | `session_joined(cid)` ok | `joined(cid)`, `startedFrom = {agent, cid}` | `sync('chat','sessions/'+cid)`; `renderHistory` only when the history is non-empty (else the bubble stays); every other block of the handler runs (busy from `is_processing`, the hold chip, chrome, list refresh) |
| `starting(cid)` | `session_joined(cid)` degraded with empty history, or `start_failed(cid)` | `pending(agent)` | remove the bubble; clear busy, thinking, Stop; restore the text if the composer is empty; re-stage the images; the held message becomes the text if empty, else is dropped with a notice; the error in the pending pane |
| `pending` | any `session_joined` | unchanged | dropped; refresh the list (an abandoned start may have created it) |
| any | `openConversation(id)` | `joining(id)` | `join(id)`; clear `startedFrom`, `suppressAutoJoin` |
| `joining(id)` | `session_joined(id)` | `joined(id)` | render the history |
| `joining(id)` | `session_joined` of another id | unchanged | dropped (a superseded join: `join` never auto-creates since L2) |
| `joining(id)` | `join_failed(id)` | `idle` | notice "That conversation no longer exists" (the welcome canvas); `suppressAutoJoin`; refresh the list |
| `joined(id)` | any `session_joined` | unchanged | dropped, no render (a starting re-send can produce two replies on one socket) |
| `joined(id)` | `session_deleted(id)` | `idle` | today's delete reset; the idle rules on the next list |
| any | `reset()` | `idle` | clear `suppressAutoJoin` |

- **The frame gate.** Session-scoped frames (everything but the six below, `session_updated` included) render only in `joined`. `session_list`, `session_deleted` and `sessions_changed` are always accepted (the list); `session_joined`, `start_failed`, `join_failed` follow the table. The OAuth popup close of `auth_resolved`/`auth_failed` runs before the gate (global); only their inline rendering is gated.
- **The idle rules** (`idleRules(list, pendingJoinId)`) run on every `session_list` REPLY (never on a `sessions_changed` push, which returns before them), only while `idle`: `pendingJoinId` if listed, else `sessions[0]` unless `suppressAutoJoin`, else the welcome. `pending` and `starting` never auto-join and are never covered by the welcome.
- **`serializeState`** is the machine's `serialize()`: `new/{encodeURIComponent(agent)}` while pending or starting, `sessions/{id}` while joining or joined, null while idle. `hydrateState(null)` (bare `#chats`) returns early, so the router re-syncs to whatever is open.
- **The route** `#chats/~new/{agent}` (DD2): every start intent (the agent picker since M25 F4 L4a: from the Chats list's New chat, the empty state's New chat or the new-chat state's "Pick a different agent" since leg 4b; the welcome item and the header New Chat until leg 4b) is `requestPick(agent, {opener})` = `App.router.go(App.router.href({destination:'chats', create:true, agent}), {replace, opener})`, with `{replace:true}` when already on a `~new` route (the picker's opener is where a guard Stay returns focus); the hydrate performs the pick. **Hydrate sets the state synchronously** (the router serialises right after): an agent listed in the peeked agents store picks at once; otherwise (a cold load, or a cache that lacks it) the chat goes pending at once with a neutral pane (the header's agent line blank and the new-chat state empty since leg 4b, composer disabled) and is validated when `App.store.get('agents')` resolves (a stale cache refetches). An empty, `default`, unknown or deleted agent, or a malformed `%` escape, **heals**: a pending chat it made resets to idle, `sync('chat', null)` (replace), and the idle rules run; a joined or starting conversation is left alone (the post-hydrate sync re-canonicalises to it).
- **The rail restore (AC9).** Leaving Chats while starting drops the first send's sync, so `lastRoute('chats')` stays `chats/~new/{agent}`. A hydrate of that route while `joined(id)` with `startedFrom = {agent, cid: id}` (no pick or open since) keeps the session and re-syncs to `sessions/{id}`. An entry point's pick sets a one-shot `pickIntent`, so a deliberate New Chat is always a pick.
- **Reconnect (AC8).** `boundOn` records the socket of the last binding (a `start_session`/`join`/`leave` sent, a `session_joined` received); reconnect actions run only for a binding made on an EARLIER socket. **Starting** re-sends the same `start_session` at `onopen` (never `join`). **Joined/joining** act at the fresh socket's first `session_list` (a per-socket flag): re-join when the id is listed (through `joining`), else idle with the notice (never a `join` of an unknown id). **`rebinding`** (set in `onopen` while joined or joining, cleared when that resolves) and a socket that is not OPEN make Send keep its text and Stop do nothing, so no message reaches an unbound socket. `onclose` while starting keeps the held slot, busy and thinking; otherwise it clears them as before. A stale socket's handlers are ignored; one reconnect timer.
- **Busy, hold, Stop (AC6).** `isCurrentBusy()` is true whenever starting. A second Send while starting is held in the queue keyed on the client id (the server uses it as the session id), never sent as a plain `message`; `done` sends it on the joined path. Stop while starting sends `cancel`, ordered after `start_session` on the socket (see squawk 0120: a `cancel` processed before the spawned turn's first step gets no `cancelled` reply). `/reset` while pending or starting is a client-side notice ("Send a message to start the chat first"). Send in idle or joining does nothing (keeps the text).
- **A lost reply on an OPEN socket is no longer recovered** (squawk 0111's 10s timer and its `App.chatStartJoinTimeoutMs` hook are gone): the server always replies, and only a socket close re-sends. Reconnect tests: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_start_reconnect.py` (renamed from `test_flow_chat_start_recovery.py`); the start flow itself: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_start_flow.py` (written on the old DOM in leg 3b, migrated onto the picker and the new pane in legs 4a and 4b).
- **The interim pending UI** (leg 3b, on the old DOM): the header badge, the chrome with an empty log, no welcome. The pending row moved to the Chats list in leg 4a, and leg 4b replaced the pane (the header, the new-chat state; "Chats pane" below).

### Chats row activity (M31 F6 L10b)

- **Server seam** (`the_one/sessions/_locks.py`): `track_turn(sid)` is an async context manager that IS the per-session lock acquisition (web `_locked_message_processing` and connector `dispatch_inbound`). It yields a `TurnHandle`; on acquire it calls `notify_sessions_changed()` (the start push); in a `finally` OUTSIDE the lock, it always notifies again (an early return or exception before `ran` still clears busy) and bumps `_turn_counts[sid]` only when the body set `turn.ran = True`, so the end push reads `is_session_processing` false. A cancel while waiting for the lock neither bumps nor pushes; a cancel or error during the turn bumps. The connector sets `ran` after the agent is built (early returns do not count). `is_session_busy(sid)` = lock held OR `_session_inflight[sid]` (the spawned-not-yet-locked registry, moved here; ONE dict object that `app.py` imports as `_session_inflight` and never rebinds; `clear_locks_for_test` leaves it). `turn_seq(sid)` = `"{boot_id}:{n}"` (random per-process `boot_id`; never pruned, like `session_locks`). Not tracked: the resume-after-auth path (a paused turn reads idle).
- **Projections:** `_session_to_list_payload` (WebSocket `session_list`, `sessions_changed`) and `_session_summary` (REST) both carry `busy` and `turn_seq` (pinned in `tests/test_db_api.py`, `tests/test_web_list_sessions_projection.py`).
- **Client** (`areas/chat/chat-activity.js`, `App.chat.activity.create()`): `observe(rows, openId)` then `stateFor(row, openId)` -> `'working'` (busy) | `'new'` (idle, not open, `n` above this window's `seen`) | null. First loaded observe baselines silently (an unloaded collection is never observed); a changed epoch re-baselines silently; `latest` keeps the larger `n`; a row first seen later counts from 0; the open (joined) chat's `seen` follows `latest` on every render. Per tab, not persisted. `renderChatsList` passes `view.activity` to `chatsList`, which sets `item.activity`.
- **Control:** `App.ui.listPane` `item.activity` (see `docs/ui-control-inventory.md`).

### Chats list and agent picker (client, M25 F4 L4a)

**The Chats list column** (`static/js/areas/chat/chats-list.js`, `App.chat.chatsList`; flight DD5, reference spec 5.1) is an `App.ui.listPane` (testid `chats-list`, title "Chats", `rowStyle: 'chats'`, no filter) mounted in the chat sidebar panel with DOM APIs (the old `sidebar.innerHTML` sink, the "Select Agent" `agent-select` dropdown, `syncDropdownToCurrent`, the dropdown `change` handler and `history-buckets.js` are gone). The aside is `--list-w` (300px) at every width, like Agents and Library (`base.css`; `--sidebar-w` is retired). Its header **block** is the full-width **New chat** (`App.ui.button` primary `lg`, the `square-pen` icon node, testid `chats-new-btn`), which calls `App.chat.openPicker({opener: btn})`; it is natively `disabled` while setup is incomplete (`refreshAgentsAndSetup` sets it from `setupIncomplete`; the pane's guidance says why).
- **Groups** (`bucketFor(row, now)`): `updated_at || created_at` in LOCAL time, whole calendar days ago (rounded, so DST days count once): day 0 (or a future date) Today, 1 Yesterday, 2-6 "Last 7 days", 7-29 "Last 30 days", 30+ and a missing or invalid date "Older" (sentence case, A28; day 7 is Last 30 days so a weekday label never repeats today's). Newest first in each group (a stable sort); empty groups are not passed to the pane.
- **Times** (`timeFor`, by hand, never `toLocaleString`): Today `h:mm AM/PM` (local 12-hour, `App.time.formatClock` in `static/js/time.js`, shared with the message attributions), Yesterday and Last 7 days the short weekday (`Mon`), Last 30 days and Older the short month and day (`Sep 12`), a missing or invalid date `''`.
- **Rows**: `label` the title (`Untitled chat` for an empty one), `meta` the agent (`Default agent` for a null `agent_name`), a connector row's `platform` as the neutral library badge pill (`{text, kind: 'unknown'}` -> `App.ui.badge`, `badge badge--unknown`) showing the display name (`App.chat.chatsList.platformName`: "Discord", unknown slugs title-cased; no class is ever built from the value), `href` `App.router.href({destination:'chats', id})`, `dataset {id}`, testid `chat-row`, and the delete **row action** (trash icon, `aria-label` "Delete chat: {title}", testid `chat-row-delete`): `App.ui.confirm(li, ..., {onArmChange})` arms then confirms (the action's name follows the armed state through `onArmChange`, derived from the row's title; no MutationObserver), then the WebSocket `delete_session` (with the socket closed at commit the arm reverts and a "Not connected, so the chat wasn't deleted. Try again in a moment." toast shows, DD11 6); the server's `session_deleted` and list refresh re-render, and the listPane's row-action contract moves focus to the neighbouring row (an armed `<li>` survives rebuilds).
- **The pending row** (state `pending` or `starting`): id `'__pending__'` (`chatsList.PENDING_ID`), "New chat", "now", the agent as meta, `href` its own `#chats/~new/{agent}` (a link, so `aria-current="page"`), `dataset {pending:'true'}` (no `data-id`), no action, first in Today, and the selected row.
- **One render** (AC6): `renderChatsList()` in `chat.js` (which keeps it) reads `App.store.peek('sessions')` (the rows) and the session state (the pending row; the selection = the pending row, else `boundId()`). It runs from `App.store.subscribe('sessions', ...)` -- the `session_list` handler only primes the store, it never renders the list itself -- and from every transition that moves the pending row or the selection: `renderPending` (pick), `openConversation`, the accepted `session_joined` (joined, and the starting-to-joined swap), `resetToIdle` and `clearConversation` (idle, a delete of the open chat, `join_failed`). **The subscription lives as long as the chat area**, which is never destroyed (`deactivate` keeps the WebSocket and the DOM), so it is never released. Off Chats a REST-primed list (another area's `get('sessions')`) renders into the hidden sidebar panel with the state idle; nothing throws. The local `sessions` array stays for `idleRules` and the reconnect list check; "one source" is about rendering.
- **Empty**: "No conversations yet." through the pane's `emptyText`.

**The agent picker** (`static/js/areas/chat/agent-picker.js`; DD2, DD4, A16; reference spec 5.6). `App.chat.openPicker({opener})` returns a Promise resolved once the picker is open: from another destination it first runs `App.router.go(App.router.href({destination:'chats'}))` and continues on a commit (it **rejects** on a guard Stay); a call before the chat area's init waits in a queue that `App.chat.registerPickerHost({setupIncomplete, onPick})` -- called LAST in the chat area's `init` -- flushes; with setup incomplete it resolves without opening (inert); a second call while open focuses the open picker's input. The picker is **built fresh on every open** (so the query clears on the next open, reference spec 10 item 11): an `App.ui.searchList` (label "Find an agent", placeholder "Find an agent by name, model or provider", `inputTestid` `agent-picker-search`, `rowTestid` `agent-picker-row`) in an `App.ui.modal` chooser (`layout:'chooser'`, `width:640`, `top:72`, `dismissible:true`, title "Start a chat with…", `closeButton {label:'Esc', ariaLabel:'Close (Esc)', testid:'agent-picker-close'}`, `initialFocus` the search input, testid `agent-picker`, a fragment footer "Picking an agent starts a new chat with it" / "Enter to start" in `.modal-dialog-footer-end`). Its body is `.agent-picker-body` (`chat.css`, not area-scoped because the `<dialog>` lives under `<body>`): the field in a `0 20px 12px` band at 40px/14px, the list under a hairline, padded `0 12px 12px`, capped so the card stays within the board's 720px.
- **Rows**: `lead` the provider dot (`badge badge--{type} badge-dot`, aria-hidden; the type is the `providers` store row of the agent's `provider_config_id`, as the Agents list resolves it: a type outside `App.ui.badge.KINDS` is `badge--unknown`, a missing provider `badge-dot--none`), `label` the name, `description`, `meta` the model (`metaMono`), `dataset {agent}`. Only live `agents` store rows appear, minus a `default` name, so the synthetic default and deleted agents never do.
- **Groups**: an empty query shows **"Recently used"** -- up to 5 (`RECENT_MAX`), the agents with the latest `updated_at || created_at` over `sessions` rows whose `platform` is null (a connector session is not the operator's use, A32) and whose `agent_name` is live -- then **"All agents"**, every agent A-Z (`localeCompare`, as the Agents list), the recent ones included. A query (trimmed, case-insensitive substring over name, model, provider type slug and description) shows one **"{n} matching"** group, **A-Z** (A16, a recorded deviation from the board's data order). No match: `No agents match "{q}".`; with no agents loaded yet "Loading agents…", with none at all "No agents yet." (defensive: New chat is disabled then). The picker waits on `agents` and `providers` (`App.store.get` for each not yet loaded) and `refresh()`es as each resolves while it is open; `sessions` is only peeked (the WebSocket primes it on Chats).
- **Picking** (Enter on the active row, or a click) closes the modal with the agent; **from the modal's `onClose`**, after focus is back on the opener, it calls the host's `onPick(agent, {opener})` = `requestPick` (the picker owns neither the route nor `pickIntent`), so a leave-guard dialog never stacks under the chooser and a guard Stay leaves focus on the opener. Escape, the close button and the backdrop close it with focus back on the opener and nothing picked.
- **Tests**: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_list.py` (groups and boundaries 0/1/2/6/7/29/30/400 days, times, cross-agent rows, the connector badge, the pending row, the selection, row delete with neighbour focus, empty, setup incomplete, 300px at 1024 and 1440) and `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_picker.py` (open and focus, every close path, Recently used with its exclusions and cap, All agents A-Z, matching, Enter picks, a re-pick replaces, a guard Stay, from another destination, provider dots); `tests/test_store_static.py::test_chats_list_renders_from_the_sessions_subscription_only` pins the one-render rule.

### Chats pane (client, M25 F4 L4b)

**The detail pane** (`static/js/areas/chat/chat-pane.js`, `App.chat.chatPane`; flight DD3, DD6; reference spec 5.2, 5.4) is built with DOM APIs: the chat area's content sink is gone, and `tests/test_ui_guards.py`'s `DYNAMIC_SINK_BASELINE` has no `areas/chat*` path (`test_chat_area_has_no_baselined_sink` also pins that the chat files' only static sites are `= ''` clears: the model-loading row and the thinking indicator were converted from static literals). `welcome.js` is folded in and deleted. `create({onNewChat(btn), onPickDifferent(btn)})` returns the two siblings of the content panel and patch-renderers over nodes built ONCE (a store notification never rebuilds a focused control):
- **The welcome** (`div.chat-welcome`, testid `chat-welcome`, kept): the mascot (prepended by `chat.js`, which keeps the welcome), the "Hello." **`h1.chat-welcome-greeting`**, the idle notice (`p.chat-welcome-notice`, the only `role=status` inside), the prompt and the actions row. `renderWelcome(setup)`: setup complete is the **empty state**, the prompt "Start a conversation with one of your agents." and **New chat** (`App.ui.button` primary with the square-pen icon, testid `chat-empty-new-btn`, `openPicker({opener})`); setup incomplete is the **guidance**, the prompt hidden, `guidance(setup)` (the former `welcome.js` copy and router hrefs, DOM-built: `.chat-welcome-guidance` with `.chat-welcome-link`s) and no New chat (the list's `chats-new-btn` is disabled too). The welcome is SHOWN only by the idle rules (`showWelcome` from `runIdleRules`: idle with no chat to auto-join); `refreshAgentsAndSetup` only re-renders a welcome already shown (it no longer shows it while idle, so the guidance never flashes before an auto-join), and `openConversation` shows the chrome at once, so the welcome never shows while joining. The retired agent list (`.chat-welcome-list` / `.chat-welcome-item`, `renderWelcomeList`'s ready mode, the welcome keydown handler, `availableAgents`) is gone.
- **The chrome** (`div.chat-chrome`, kept: `test_flow_context_overlay_thresholds.py` queries it): **the header** (`header.chat-header`, testid `chat-header`; 48px, `padding: 0 16px 0 24px`, a hairline; a `<header>` inside `<main>`, so no banner landmark; hidden while idle): an **`h1.chat-header-title`** (13px/600, ellipsis) and `span.chat-agent-line` (testid `chat-agent-line`, 12px `--fg-muted`: "with {agent}", "Default agent" for a null agent). Then **the new-chat state** (`div.chat-new-state`, testid `chat-new-state`, only while pending; centred, inner max 480px, 8px gaps): a `badge badge--{kind}` whose TEXT is the agent's model (A20; `agentInfo(name, agents, providers)` resolves the kind through the picker's `App.chat.agentPicker.providerType`, now exported; a type outside `App.ui.badge.KINDS`, no provider or an unknown agent is `unknown`; no model, no badge), the agent name (`h2`, 18px/600), the description (14px `--fg-secondary`, hidden when empty) and **Pick a different agent** (`btn-ghost btn-sm`, testid `chat-pick-different-btn`, `openPicker({opener})`; a pick on the `~new` route replaces it). Then the message log. While pending the chrome carries `.chat-chrome--new`: the log shrinks to its notices (`flex: 0 1 auto`, no padding when `:empty`), so a start failure's error or the `/reset` notice shows under the new-chat state. `chat.js` appends the composer's wrapper after the log (since leg 5 it holds the telemetry row, the staged-image row, the queued-message region and the box; "Chat composer" below).
- **`renderPane()`** (`chat.js`) maps the session state onto the pane: pending/starting is "New chat" with "with {agent}" (the agent line blank while a cold `~new` route validates; the new-chat state empty then), joining/joined is the session's title **read from the `sessions` store by id** (so it follows the auto-title and a rename), falling back to the `session_joined` payload's title (`joinedTitle`) and then "Untitled chat", with the agent likewise (a joining id with no store row shows a blank header until its reply); idle hides the header. It runs from every transition (`renderPending`, `openConversation`, `resetToIdle`, `clearConversation`, `startSend`, `applyStartFailed`, `finishValidation`, the accepted `session_joined`) and from the `sessions`, `agents` and `providers` subscriptions (never released, like the list's). `updateAgentBadge`, `h2.session-agent-badge` and the header's hover-expanding `new-chat-btn` are retired (`chat.css` lost `.header`, `.session-agent-badge`, `.new-chat-header*`, `.chat-welcome-list`, `.chat-welcome-item*`).
- **One `h1` per state**: the header's in pending, starting, joining and joined; "Hello." in the empty and guidance states (none during the brief idle before the first `session_list`).
- **The composer** (`areas/chat/composer.js`, since replaced by `App.ui.composer` in `static/js/ui/composer.js`) was extracted in leg 4b as a DOM builder carrying the old markup over unchanged; leg 5 restyled it ("Chat composer" below). `chat.js` wires every listener on the returned references (the attach listeners, `chat/attachments.js`) (no `content.querySelector` lookups).
- **Tests**: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_pane.py` (chats lane): the header in pending, starting, joining and joined, the title following the auto-title and a store prime, its geometry; no welcome while joining (a cold auto-join and an auto-join from the empty state, behind a held `session_joined`); the new-chat state (badge text = model, `badge--ollama`/`--mock`/`--unknown`, description, re-pick); a start failure's error under it; the empty and guidance states; one visible `h1`, the landmarks and the resolver in every state (nothing allow-listed since leg 5 named `chat-image-input`, squawk 0110). The composer's carried-over-markup test was replaced by `test_flow_chat_composer.py` in leg 5.

### Per-chat drafts (client, M31 F6 L10c)

`state.drafts` (null-prototype map) holds `{text, images}` per conversation, keyed `s:{id}` (a session) or `n:{agent}` (a pending new chat); `state.draftOwner` is the key the composer currently shows. `sessionFlow.switchDraft(newKey)` (called after the held-queue discard in `pickAgent`, `openConversation`, `resetToIdle` -> null, `clearConversation` -> null) is a no-op for the same key, otherwise saves the owner's draft (empty deletes), restores the new one through `send.setInputValue` and the silent `attachments.setStaged`, and sets the owner. The socket reconnect re-join does not call it. While `starting` the key stays `n:{agent}`; the start's `session_joined` (`fromStart`) calls `promoteDraft(id)`. `session_deleted`/`join_failed`/heal call `dropDraft`; `session_list` and `sessions_changed` call `pruneDrafts` (keeps the owner, `n:` keys and listed ids). `addFiles` captures the owner and files a late-read image under that draft. Tests: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_drafts.py`.

### Chat composer (client, M25 F4 L5)

**The composer** is `App.ui.composer` (`static/js/ui/composer.js`; flight DD6, A25; reference spec 5.3, 5.5; on the library since M31 F6 L05a, which deleted `areas/chat/composer.js` and the `.area-chat .ui-composer*` rules): `chat.js` builds it once with the legacy test ids and the callbacks `onSend` (`send.send(raw)`: the area's guards and clearing stay there; Enter while busy goes to the hold queue), `onStop` (`stopTurn`), `onAttach` (`attachments.attach()`: the busy/blocked guard, then `openFilePicker()`) and `onFiles` (`attachments.addFiles`). The modules write it only through the handle (`ctx.composer`): `setBusy` from `send.updateStopControl`, `setBlocked` from `applyComposerState` (one `computeBlocked()` in `chat.js`: `state.bannerActive` or a pending chat with setup incomplete or validating), `setValue`, `focus` (a completed picker pick, `requestPick`; `sign-in.js`'s focus hand-off), `fileInput` (`data-staged`), `slots.attachments` / `attachmentsStatus` / `queued` / `telemetry`. The bullets below were written against the legacy class names; the same classes now carry a `ui-` prefix (`.ui-composer`, `.ui-composer-box`, and so on).
- **Layout.** `el` is the wrapper `.ui-composer` (`padding: 12px 24px 20px`, last child of `.chat-chrome`), holding top to bottom: the **telemetry row** (`.ui-composer-telemetry`, `justify-content: flex-end`; `chat.js` fills it with the `App.ui.telemetry` readout), the staged-image row (`chat-attachments`, display still driven inline because of the `.chip` `[hidden]` footgun) and its status, the queued-message region (`pending-queued-region`, now built here), then the **box** (`.ui-composer-box`: fills the wrapper's content width (the 760px cap was dropped in M25 F6 DD16, so the telemetry readout's right edge meets the box's), a flex column with an 8px gap, `--surface`, a hairline, `--radius-md`, `10px 12px`). The chicklets and the queued chip fill the same width. The old `.input-area` row (and its `border-top`) is gone, and `input-area` is a `RETIRED_SELECTORS` entry.
- **The telemetry row shows only while joined** (`renderPane` sets `telemetryRow.hidden = name !== 'joined'`): never in pending (the new-chat state, 5.4), starting or joining. The readout (an `App.ui.telemetry`, testid `chat-telemetry`, since M31 F6 L05c; its classes are `.ui-telemetry*`) keeps its content, its 0.7 resting opacity (`_TELEMETRY_RESTING_OPACITY`) and its hover/focus expansion; only `position: absolute` (and the queued region's z-index workaround for it) are gone. The board draws the row as `padding: 0 24px` outside the wrapper; inside it the wrapper's own 24px side padding is that inset, so the readout's right edge is still 24px from the pane's.
- **The textarea** (`textarea.ui-composer-input`, testid `chat-input`, `rows=2`, `enterkeyhint=send`): `resize: none; border: 0; background: transparent`, 14px at line-height 1.5, and `outline: none` only because the box carries the ring. It is named by a real `<label for>` "Message" (`.visually-hidden`, inside the box, which is `position: relative`), with no `aria-label`. Its placeholder is "Message {agent}…", or "Message…" with no agent (also while a cold `~new` route validates, when the header's agent line is blank); `renderPane` sets it, so it follows a pick, a join and a store prime. **It grows with its content** through `fit()` (called on `input` and by `setInputValue`, the one path for every scripted write: the clears on send and on a hold, the restore on a start failure) to `max-height: calc(var(--line-height-normal) * 8em)` (168px, about eight lines), then scrolls; `fit` holds the box's height while it measures, so a log pinned to its end does not drift on each keystroke.
- **Keys.** Enter (no Shift) calls `preventDefault` and sends; Shift+Enter keeps the native newline; an Enter with `e.isComposing || e.keyCode === 229` does nothing, so an IME commits (WebKit's composition-commit Enter can arrive with `isComposing` false). Ctrl/Alt/Meta+Enter still send, as before. `aria-keyshortcuts="Enter"` stays on Send. A pasted multi-line draft stays multi-line and is sent as one message.
- **The focus ring (A25)** is `.ui-composer-box:focus-within { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 2px }`, the app's pinned form on the box: visible while the textarea or a toolbar button has focus. A disabled textarea (health banner, setup incomplete, a cold `~new` validating) cannot take focus, so it shows none. This is a deliberate exception to the "`:focus-within` container treatments degrade to a border-color change" variant below: the box is the control's visible boundary (the textarea has no border of its own), so it carries the full ring.
- **The toolbar** (`.ui-composer-toolbar`, flex, centred, 8px gap): **Attach images** (`App.ui.button` ghost `sm` `iconOnly`, so `btn-ghost btn-sm btn-icon`, 24x24 with the `paperclip` icon, `aria-label="Attach images"`; the attachment is image-only, so not the board's "Attach a file"), the hidden file input (`chat-image-input`, `aria-label="Attach images"`: squawk 0110, since the shared resolver selects every `input:not([type=hidden])` and does not skip `hidden` or `aria-hidden`), the **model id** (`span.ui-composer-model`, testid `chat-model`: the agent's `llm_model` through `agentInfo`, 11px `--font-family-mono` in `--fg-muted`, ellipsized; empty for an unknown agent or none), **Stop** (`App.ui.button` danger, hidden unless busy) and **Send** (`App.ui.button` primary, the send icon and "Send", `margin-left: auto`). While Stop shows it takes the auto margin and Send follows it 8px later, so the pair stays at the end. `--fg` and `--fg-muted` on `--surface` are gated by `test_composer_text_pairs_pass_body_aa`.
- **Tests**: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_composer.py` (chats lane): the wrapper, box, label, textarea and toolbar against 5.5 (computed styles and token probes), the placeholder and model id through a pick, a join and store primes; Shift+Enter, the IME guard (synthetic `isComposing` and `keyCode` 229 keydowns are not prevented and send nothing), Enter sending both lines as one `start_session`, a pasted multi-line plain send; the ring in both themes on the textarea, Attach and Send, none when blurred or disabled (falsified by removing the rule); Stop just before Send while a turn runs; the 42/84/168px heights; the telemetry row hidden in pending and starting, shown joined directly above the box (an 8px gap) with the readout 24px from the pane's edge at opacity 0.7; the chicklets above the box in its column; and the resolver over `body` on `#chats` with a chat open, both themes, with no failure.

### Agent drawer (client, M25 F4 L6)

**Routine fact and Routines group (M33 F1 L7, DD10).** `socket.js` calls `agentDrawer.setSessionMetadata(joinedId, data.session_metadata)`, `refreshNotes()` and `refreshRoutines()` on every `session_joined` (a `session_updated` re-join included). The drawer keeps the metadata keyed by session id, renders it only while the view's `sessionId` matches, and drops it when the view becomes null, pending or another chat. A `Routine` fact (`chat-drawer-routine` is set on the last `dd`: "{name} · last ran {day} {h:mm AM/PM}") appears when `session_metadata.routine` is set. A read-only `routines` group follows Notes (named agents only; the default and deleted agents omit it): `GET /api/agents/{name}/routines` per open (404 = none), A-Z, meta "Done" (`finished_at`) over "Off" (`enabled` false) over "Next {day} {time}"; `refreshRoutines()` re-fetches without flicker (the shown list stays until the response; token, gen and agent guards); a no-op while closed or without a named agent; `chat/turn-frames.js` also calls it (M33 F4 L5) on a live non-error `tool_result` of `schedule_routine`/`change_routine`/`cancel_routine` (a held-for-approval result refreshes harmlessly; an approval made in another tab is not followed). Days and times go through `App.time` (Today, a weekday within 6 days, else `Mon D`). Testids `chat-drawer-routines`, `chat-drawer-routine-item`.

**The agent drawer** (`static/js/areas/chat/agent-drawer.js`, `App.chat.agentDrawer`; flight DD7, DD15 A13, A20, A21; reference spec 5.7) is an `App.ui.drawer` that FILLS the static `#chat-drawer` shell slot ("App shell — invariants"). `chat.js` creates it once in `init` (`App.chat.agentDrawer.create({slot})` -> `{drawer, toggle, render(view), setActive(on), destroy()}`), appends its toggle at the END of the chat header, feeds it from `renderPane()` and shows or hides it from the area's `activate` / `deactivate` (`setActive`).
- **When it shows.** Only while the chat area is active AND there is a chat view AND the current mode's open state is true; one `apply()` decides it, opening with the toggle as the opener. The **view follows the header**: `{agent}` in pending, starting, joining (with a `sessions` row) and joined; `{loading: true}` while the header's agent line is blank (a cold `~new` validating its agent, a joining id with no row); `null` while idle (no header, no toggle, drawer `hidden`). So the first send (pending, starting, joined) and a switch between chats (joined, joining, joined) never hide and re-show it. Off Chats it is always `hidden` and `<main>` keeps its full width.
- **Modes and state.** One object `{mode, open: {push, overlay}}`. `mode` is `push` at `(min-width: 1280px)` and `overlay` below, from a `matchMedia` `change` listener (on the `MediaQueryList`, not the document or window; removed by `destroy`); a resize across 1280 switches the mode and that mode's state applies. Defaults: **push open, overlay closed**, so EVERY Chats browser test at Playwright's 1280x720 default has the drawer open and a 588px pane (1280 - 72 - 300 - 320; 748px at 1440), while the 652px pane at 1024 is not covered on arrival. The open state persists in `localStorage` `chat-drawer-open` as `{push, overlay}` (JSON; reads and writes in try/catch, blocked storage keeps the defaults); only the user's changes are written (the toggle, and Escape through `onOpenChange`; `apply()` opening or closing for visibility is not).
- **The toggle** (`chat-drawer-toggle`): `App.ui.button` ghost icon-only (`btn-ghost btn-icon`, 32x32, the 16px `panel-right` icon; the board renders it unsquared at 42x32), `aria-label="Show this chat's agent"`, `aria-pressed` = the open state, `aria-controls="chat-drawer"` (always resolvable: the drawer is hidden, never removed), `margin-left: auto`; `hidden` with no chat view. Below 1280 the open overlay covers the chat pane from the top, header included, and the reference draws no close control inside the drawer, so the toggle is `position: relative; z-index: 11`: it stays on top (it then sits in the drawer header's right corner, which the overlay's header keeps clear with `padding-right: 56px`). Escape inside the drawer closes it and focus returns to the toggle.
- **Header** (48px, A21: its hairline meets the chat header's): the agent name (`h2.chat-drawer-name`, 13px/600, ellipsis) and a `badge badge--{type}` whose TEXT is the **provider type** (A20; `App.chat.agentPicker.providerType`, `badge--unknown` outside `App.ui.badge.KINDS`, hidden with no provider).
- **Body** (`.chat-drawer-content`, a 16px-gap column, 12px): the description (`--fg-secondary`); the facts `<dl>` (`chat-drawer-facts`, a two-column grid, `dt` `--fg-muted`): **Model** (`llm_model`, mono), **Provider** (the provider's display NAME from the `providers` store, "—" with none), **Chats** (the `sessions` rows with that `agent_name`, connector sessions included, DD4); then **"What it can use"** (A13; `chat-drawer-uses`: an `h3`, then four `.chat-drawer-group[data-group]`, each an `h4` label over a list; a plain `<div>`, not a named `<section>`, so no region landmark): **Knowledge bases** (`kb_ids` against the `knowledge-bases` store, by name, A-Z), **MCP servers** (`mcp_source_ids` found in the PLAIN `mcp-sources` store, A-Z, with "{n} tools" where `App.usage.mcpToolCount(source)` knows it; unknown ids are skipped, which drops the self-configuration loopback source, attached but hidden from the plain list), **Built-in tools** (the categories with at least one effective tool, "{n} of {total}"; effective = globally enabled in `GET /api/tools/configs`, a missing row meaning enabled, AND `AgentConfig.is_tool_enabled`, mirrored by `App.chat.agentDrawer.isToolEnabled`: `enabled_tools` gates built-ins only (`None` = all), the `mcp__`/`kb__` prefixes exempt, `disabled_tools` universal; parity pinned case by case against the Python), **Self-configuration** ("On" / "Off" from `GET /api/agents/{name}/self-configuration`). An empty group says "None"; a failed load says "Couldn't load" for that group only ("Loading…" while pending).
- **Footer: Open agent settings** (`chat-drawer-settings-link`, `App.ui.button` secondary with the `settings` gear): `App.router.go(App.router.href({destination: 'agents', id: name}), {opener})`, then, on a commit, `App.studio.clearAgentFilter()` (reference spec 10, item 18). `studio.js` defines `App.studio.clearAgentFilter` at script load as a safe no-op and replaces it in `init` with the agents-list handle's `clearFilter` (in `studio/agents-list.js`), which does `agentsList.setQuery('')` plus a scroll of the selected row into view (the hydrate has already selected the agent; its row may have been filtered out). The Agents pane survives a visit to Chats (only `setActiveSection` destroys it, on Agents to Library). Leg 7's palette calls the same function.
- **Three non-agent states, none with the settings link** (the footer wrapper is `hidden`): **default** (a null agent, or `"default"` not in the `agents` store): "Default agent", the note "The default agent", Chats only; **deleted** (a name not in the LOADED `agents` store): the name, "This agent no longer exists", Chats only; **loading** (the store not loaded, or the header's agent line blank): "Loading…", never "deleted" (the chat area joins only after its agents read settles, so a FAILED read is how a joined chat meets an unloaded store) (`chat-drawer-note`).
- **Data.** The drawer subscribes to `agents`, `providers`, `knowledge-bases`, `mcp-sources` and `sessions` (re-rendering on each; `destroy` unsubscribes). Only while open with a known agent: one `GET /api/tools/configs` per open, one self-configuration `GET` per open and agent, and `App.store.get('knowledge-bases' | 'mcp-sources')` once per open (cached, so usually no request); a response from an earlier open is dropped. A rename reaches it through the next `sessions` notification. The body holds no focusable control (the footer's button is built once), so a re-render never drops focus.
- **One built-in category table** (AC9). `static/js/tool-categories.js` (`App.toolCategories`, frozen) replaced the three copies (`studio.js`'s per-agent Tools tab table and the `admin/tools-grid.js` / `admin/tools-editor.js` maps, squawks 0028 and 0032) with every derived shape: `order` and `labels` (the Settings tabs), `byCategory` (title-cased label -> tool names; studio's `toolCategories` callback into `tools-tab.js`, and the drawer), `categoryOf` (tool -> slug; the Settings tiles' badges and the tool editor's subtitle). Its script loads before `studio.js`, `admin/tools-*.js` and `agent-drawer.js`. It lists every tool in `_TOOL_CLASSES`, including M23 F4 L3's `find_platform_destination`, which no copy had (so Connectors is 3 tools: the per-agent Tools tab shows 22 tool tiles, the Settings Connectors tab 3 tiles); `tests/test_tool_categories.py` parses `_TOOL_CLASSES` and the table and asserts the same set, so a new built-in cannot be silently uncategorised.
- **The agent editor's Chat** (DD8; `agent-chat-btn`): an `App.ui.button` secondary with the `chat` icon, inserted with DOM APIs into the header's `.form-actions` before Cancel by `renderAgentEditor` on a SAVED agent only (absent in create mode), re-created on every header render, outside `checkDirty`'s Save/Cancel bookkeeping (which reads `.save-btn` and `.cancel-btn` only). It calls `App.chat.chatWith(name, {opener})` (`agent-picker.js`): once the chat area exists, its picker host's `chatWith` runs the pick's guarded `go(href({destination: 'chats', create: true, agent}))` WITH the one-shot `pickIntent` (so the AC9 rail restore cannot keep a joined session started from the same agent instead), and before its init a plain `go` (the state is idle). Unlike a pick it is not inert while setup is incomplete. From a dirty editor the router asks once: Stay keeps the edit with focus back on Chat; Continue lands on `#chats/~new/{name}`, pending. The studio `dirty` flag stays one closure flag (DD8): Chat, the drawer's link and the palette all leave through `go()`.
- **Tests**: `tests/browser/test_flow_chat_drawer.py` (chats lane: push at 1280 and 1440, overlay at 1024 with the toggle on top, the toggle and Escape, persistence across a reload and a resize, the facts and the Chats count, "What it can use" and a failed load, one fetch per open, the `is_tool_enabled` parity, Open agent settings with the filter cleared and the row in view, the default, deleted and loading states, hidden off Chats and while idle, landmarks and the resolver in both themes), `tests/browser/test_agents_chat_action.py` (agents lane), `tests/browser/test_ui_drawer_contract.py` (contracts lane), `tests/test_tool_categories.py` and `test_drawer_text_pairs_pass_body_aa` (unit).

### Jump-to palette (client, M25 F4 L7)

**The jump-to palette** (`static/js/jump-to.js`, `App.jumpTo`; flight DD9, DD10, DD14, DD15 A10, A11, A17; reference spec 9) is a shell composition, not a factory: a fresh `App.ui.searchList` inside an `App.ui.modal` chooser on every open.

- **Opening it.** The rail footer's **Jump to** control (`rail-jump-to`: `App.ui.button` ghost icon-only, the `search` icon, 44px, `aria-label="Jump to (Ctrl K)"`, `aria-keyshortcuts="Control+K"`; a click opens the palette with the control as the opener) and **Ctrl K** (Meta K on macOS). The shortcut is ONE `keydown` listener on `document` in `app.js` (`onJumpToKeydown`, installed once by `installJumpToKey()` after `mountRail()`); it looks `App.ui.modal` and `App.jumpTo` up at keydown, because `app.js` loads before `ui/*` and `jump-to.js`. On a match it calls `preventDefault()` (so the browser's own Ctrl K never runs over the app) and works from inside inputs and textareas. Then: an IME composition (`isComposing`, or `keyCode` 229) is ignored; the palette open -> focus its input (only while it is the topmost `dialog[open]`); `App.ui.modal.anyOpen()` (the leave-guard confirm, the agent picker) -> nothing, so the topmost-modal rule holds; else `App.jumpTo.open(document.activeElement)`.
- **Platform.** `App.shortcuts` (in `app.js`): `isMacPlatform(nav)` (pure; `navigator.userAgentData.platform`, else `navigator.platform`, against `/mac|iphone|ipad/i`), `isJumpToKey(e, mac)` (macOS: Meta and not Ctrl; elsewhere: Ctrl and not Meta; never Shift or Alt; `e.key.toLowerCase() === 'k'` or `e.code === 'KeyK'`, so Caps Lock and non-Latin layouts match) and `jumpToKeys(mac)` (`{hint: 'Ctrl K' | '⌘ K', aria: 'Control+K' | 'Meta+K'}`, the copy of the rail control's name and `aria-keyshortcuts` and the palette footer).
- **The dialog.** `ariaLabel` "Jump to" (no title, no header row), `layout: 'chooser'`, 600 wide at top 96, dismissible, `id` `jump-to-dialog`, testid `jump-to`, `initialFocus` the input. The body (`.jump-to-body`) is the search list, whose field wrapper is turned into the input bar (`.jump-to-bar`: the 18px search icon first, the visually hidden label "Jump to", the borderless 14px input `jump-to-input` with the placeholder "Jump to an agent, chat, library item or setting", then the palette's own **Esc** button `jump-to-close`, accessible name "Close (Esc)", which calls the modal's `close()`). The ring is the bar's (`:focus-within`, inset; A25). Results scroll inside 420px, padded 8px; rows 8px 12px. The footer: "{Ctrl K | ⌘ K} from anywhere" and "Enter to open", both on the left. CSS in `components.css` ("Jump-to palette").
- **Candidates** (DD10, A17), in board order: agents A-Z (every `agents` store row, by `name`); chats newest first (`sessions` by `updated_at`, else `created_at`; equal timestamps keep the store's order); the **New chat…** action; model providers (sortie 02: `data-kind="provider"`, `Library · Model provider`, opening `#library/providers/{id}`), knowledge bases, MCP servers and connectors (each A-Z); the Settings pages in nav order from `App.settingsPages`. Rows (`jump-to-result`, `role=option`, `data-kind` = `agent|chat|action|provider|knowledge-base|mcp-server|connector|settings`, `data-id`) show the label and the "where" text as the search list's meta (not mono): `Agent`, `Chat · {agent_name | Default agent}` (an empty title reads "Untitled chat"; the server stores an empty title as "New chat", so that fallback is defensive, as in the Chats list), `Action`, `Library · Model provider|Knowledge base|MCP server|Connector`, `Settings · {group}` (Settings has no Providers page since sortie 02 leg 2). One unlabelled group (no captions). **Matching:** a case-insensitive substring over `label + " " + where`, trimmed; the first **8** matches (`App.jumpTo.CAP`), except the EMPTY query, which shows `emptyMix(candidates())` (operator decision at the DD13 preview, option C, M25 F4 L9): up to `RECENT_CHATS` (3) newest chats, **New chat…**, up to `RECENT_AGENTS` (4) recently used agents (`App.chat.agentPicker.recentAgents` over the live, non-`default` agents; connector-only agents are excluded, A32; a missing or throwing picker leaves this part empty), then the remaining board-order candidates as fill, skipping any already shown, 8 in total (a chat row and an agent row for one agent can both show). `candidates()` stays pure board order. No match: `No matches for "{q}".` `App.jumpTo.candidates()` and `match(query)` are exported for tests.
- **Active row and keys** (A10, A11): the search list's (the first result active, ↑/↓ clamped, Enter opens, the pointer sets the active row, focus stays in the input). **A store refresh keeps the highlighted row by identity** (M31 F6 L03b, D4, squawk 0185, superseding M25 DD10's "the first row follows the data"): the row Enter would pick never changes when the chats, agents or other collections land, whether or not the user moved; `searchList` owns the rule (identity is `(group index, id)`), and `jump-to.js` no longer tracks a first id.
- **Cold data** (AC11). `open()` calls `App.store.get` for `agents`, `sessions` (**`{force: true}`**: the WebSocket primes it only while Chats is active), `knowledge-bases`, `mcp-sources` and `connectors`, and refreshes the list as each resolves, ONLY while this palette is still the open one (a closed palette's destroyed list is never touched). A failed collection is left out silently.
- **Opening a result** (AC10; the Flight 1 DD8 chaining rule). The opener is CAPTURED at open (`<body>`/`<html>` count as none). A pick closes the palette FIRST; then, from the modal's `onClose` (focus already back on the opener), it navigates with `App.router.go(href, {opener})` (the opener, else `#main-content`): an agent `href({destination: 'agents', id})` and, once that go commits, `App.studio.clearAgentFilter()`; a chat `href({destination: 'chats', id})` (which discards a pending chat); a knowledge base, MCP server or connector `href({destination: 'library', section: 'knowledge'|'mcp'|'connectors', id})`; a Settings page `href({destination: 'settings', section})` (the table's route segment; never a hand-built `#settings/...`). **New chat…** calls `App.chat.openPicker({opener})` alone (it goes to Chats itself and rejects on a guard Stay, swallowed). So a dirty editor's confirm is the ONLY modal (`anyOpen()` is false when `go()` runs): Stay keeps the edit, the palette stays closed and focus returns to the palette's opener (`#main-content` if it left the DOM).
- **Tests.** `tests/browser/test_flow_jump_to.py` (the `shared` lane, as the complement): the rail control (name, `aria-keyshortcuts`, 44px, pinned 12px above the bottom) and the dialog's shape; Escape, the Esc button and the backdrop returning focus; the resolver and the active row in both themes; Ctrl K from Agents, a field and Settings (`defaultPrevented`); Ctrl K with the palette open (focus to the input), with the picker open and with the confirm open (nothing); Shift/Alt/Meta variants and an IME composition (nothing), Caps Lock and a non-Latin layout (opens); the platform detector and key matcher; macOS through an init script (Meta K, "⌘ K"); the empty-query mix (and its 3/4 caps, a connector-only agent, no sessions, a deleted agent); matching each kind; an empty install; each kind opened; New chat… opening the picker; a pick from a dirty editor (one confirm opened with `anyOpen()` false, Stay keeps the edit and focus); the forced `sessions` read, a failed collection left out, and a closed palette never refreshed. The WebKitGTK premise is the probe's `--press-keys ctrl+k --assert-selector '[data-testid=jump-to]'` ("UI test apparatus").

### Ollama pull catalog (sortie 01)

The Ollama provider's Pull Model control lists every pullable `name:tag` with its exact download size and confirms every pull. `the_one/llm/ollama_library.py` owns the data; `api.py`'s `GET /api/ollama-library/search` and `GET /api/ollama-library/size` are thin wrappers.

- **Sources.** `https://ollama.com/library` (the entry-scoped parser `parse_library_page`: per `<li ... items-baseline>` block, the single-segment model name, the `text-blue-600` size-tag spans and the `text-cyan-500` cloud badge; one entry per size tag in page order, or the bare name when a model has none), `https://ollama.com/api/tags` (extra names, added after their model's page tags, and a cloud signal; bare and `:latest` dedupe through `_key`, which normalises to `name:latest`), and the registry manifest `https://registry.ollama.ai/v2/library/{name}/manifests/{tag}` (`Accept: application/vnd.docker.distribution.manifest.v2+json`, `follow_redirects=False`). The size is `sum(layers[].size) + config.size`. `/api/tags` sizes are NOT used (they are cloud-weight sizes).
- **Statuses.** `ok` (manifest 200), `cloud` (404 with the page's cyan badge or an `/api/tags` listing; a 200 stays `ok` whatever the badge), `missing` (404 without a signal), `unknown` (anything else: transport error, timeout, 3xx, 403, 429, 5xx, bad body). The panel greys out `cloud` and `missing`, and `unknown` stays selectable.
- **Cache.** One shared in-memory map (`_sizes`, per-key writes through `_record`, which never lets an `unknown` overwrite a known entry within its TTL), persisted atomically (tmp then `os.replace`, no await between) to `{data_dir}/ollama-sizes.json` together with the last library listing. TTLs: `ok`/`cloud` 7 days, `missing` 1 day; `unknown` is memory-only for 300 s; entries the 45 s budget left unsettled are not cached. Pruning (names no longer listed) happens only in a fill whose page parse succeeded. A corrupt file or a failing `os.replace` logs a warning and degrades to no disk cache. With the page unreachable the last persisted listing is served whatever its age.
- **Single-flight fill.** A request that finds the listing stale (300 s) or any entry missing/expired starts, or joins, ONE detached module task (`_fill`) and waits on `asyncio.shield(task)` for at most `_WAIT_S` (8 s); after that the route answers from the shared map with `loading: true` and the panel re-requests every 2 s. Per fill: one `httpx.AsyncClient(limits=Limits(max_connections=16))` and one semaphore (nothing module-level is loop-bound), a 10 s per-request timeout, a 45 s budget, and an early stop when all of the first 16 requests fail at transport level or with 403/429 (then a 300 s cooldown stops further fills). `shutdown_drain()` runs at the top of the lifespan shutdown block (own `try/except`): it cancels a fill on the current loop and persists what settled; it is a no-op with no fill, a finished fill or a fill on another loop. Tests call `_reset_for_tests(cache_path=...)` before and after each test.
- **Routes.** `search` returns `{"loading": bool, "models": [{"name", "size": int|null, "status"}]}` (annotated `dict[str, Any]`; no `TypedDict`, which breaks `api.py`'s import on Python 3.11), stable-sorted on the case-insensitive model-name part so `gemma3:1b` precedes `gemma3:12b` and `llama3.2:*` is not interleaved with `llama3.2-vision:*`; `q` is a case-insensitive substring filter. `size?model=` re-checks ONE entry (5 s timeout) on the Pull click: it never joins, starts or cancels a fill, never prunes, echoes the requested name (a bare name stays bare), and returns the shared map's entry after recording, so a failed re-check with a cached `ok` still returns that `ok`. An invalid model is a 422 with a string `detail`.
- **Validation.** Manifest URLs are built only from a name matching `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,127}` and a tag matching `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}` (both `re.fullmatch`); a failing entry is dropped and logged. See SECURITY-MODEL.md "Ollama library and registry fetches".
- **Seam.** `THE_ONE_OLLAMA_LIBRARY_TEST_JSON` (dev mode only; ignored with a warning otherwise) supplies `{"models": [{"name", "size", "status"}]}` for both routes with no network and no `ollama-sizes.json` read or write. It is stateless: the loading case and the size route's 4xx are driven per test with Playwright `page.route`. `tests/browser/conftest.py::_spawn_app` sets a default catalog (`OLLAMA_LIBRARY_TEST_CATALOG`) before `extra_env`, so no browser test reaches ollama.com.
- **Editor (`provider-editor.js`; built once per mount, M31 F1 L7, flight DD1).** `renderProviderEditor(container, provider, providerTypes, callbacks)` builds the whole editor from `App.ui` controls (`editorShell`, `section`, `field` with `textInput` / `select` / `toggle` / `secretInput`, `status`, `dangerZone`) and keeps every handle in one per-mount object; after the mount a type change (the `hidden` attribute on the rows, the key row's label, placeholder and hint through the field handle), Save (title, subtitle, expiry, snapshot), Cancel (values reverted, the key hidden again) and Test patch through those handles. Only the create-to-edit transition re-mounts, through `providers-section.js` and its `mountToken` / `editorGen` guards. Fields keep their ids (`#pf-*`), testids and `data-field` attributes (on the control, never a wrapper); the three info icons are the `info` option (the control's `aria-describedby` points at the info button's hidden description, an id generated by the library). The Test result is `App.ui.status` (`provider-test-result`, `data-state` success / warning / danger / pending, hidden until a test runs); on `reauth_required` its text span also carries `plan-test-reauth`. Delete is `App.ui.dangerZone` with `beforeArm`: the first click asks `/api/providers/{id}/agents` (a provider in use shows `delete-blocked-message` for 3 s and does not arm; a failed check fails open), the second click on the armed button commits. **Secrets:** `App.ui.secretInput` writes the key only through `.value` (never an attribute, title or aria-label), reveal flips only the input type, and nothing logs a payload. **Key re-mask fix:** after a successful edit Save the key field is set to the server's masked value (`setValue(updated.api_key)`, which also hides it) and `originalMaskedApiKey` follows, so the typed plaintext no longer lingers and is no longer re-sent by the next Save (an untouched key is always sent as `__MASKED__`); Cancel restores the masked value. **Plan sign-in (M31 F1 L8).** `wirePlanSignIn(h, container, provider, requestCreateSave)` is built from library controls through a per-state builder that replaces one node (`h.planState.replaceChildren`): signed out is `App.ui.button` (`plan-signin-btn`), connected is `App.ui.status` (`plan-connected-indicator`, success) plus Re-authenticate and a Reset `App.ui.button` armed through `App.ui.confirm` ("Confirm reset?", the library armed style; a state re-render replaces an armed button, which returns to "Reset connection"), and awaiting code is `App.ui.consentLink(url, {surface: 'chat', help: false, urlTestid, openTestid, copyTestid})` with the provider's `plan-signin-*` testids (Open is a real `target=_blank` link, Copy shows "Copied" and announces "Link copied"), then an `App.ui.textInput` (`plan-code-input`, never written to an attribute) and a primary Confirm code button. `plan-signin-error` is built once per mount, outside the replaced node (`h.planErr`), so an error after a re-render is kept. `pendingAutoSignInId`, `requestCreateSave` and `clearProviderSignInHandoff` are unchanged (pinned by `test_auto_start_is_one_shot_and_only_after_a_sign_in_initiated_create`). The create-to-edit focus target is the latest mount's `shell.titleEl` (module `latestMount`); the dirty snapshot and Cancel iterate `h.fieldEls` (the watched controls), not a DOM query. The token expiry line is `App.ui.status` (`provider-token-expiry`, ok / soon / past mapped to the success / warning / danger LED, `data-state` kept as ok | soon | past) rebuilt on `App.time.onChange` with the Connection section as the owner. The editor and `providers-section.js` have zero HTML-string sinks and zero ownership-allowance rows. Tests: `tests/browser/test_flow_provider_editor.py` (including `test_masked_key_round_trip_and_remask_after_save`), `test_flow_provider_focus_after.py`, `tests/browser/test_ui_secret_input_contract.py`, `tests/test_admin_reasoning_effort_field.py`.
- **Panel (`provider-models.js`; mounted once, M31 F1 L6, flight DD1).** `renderProviderModels(container, provider)` builds the panel's shell ONCE with DOM APIs and `App.ui` controls and returns a controller `{refresh(), destroy()}`; nothing after mount rebuilds the shell from markup. The editor keeps the controller and calls `refresh()` on Test success; a module-level `activeController` is destroyed before any new mount, so an editor re-render never orphans a poll or a pull. The Ollama-or-basic dispatch is decided at mount from the saved `provider.type` (a type change in the form does not re-dispatch). `refresh()` re-fetches `/models` and `/models/status` and RECONCILES: the rows belong to `App.ui.modelList` (M31 F6 L08, flight DD10: `js/ui/model-list.js`), which the panel feeds two sections (Running, Available; a section label shows only with rows) and the basic list one unlabelled `density: 'compact'` section. The control keys rows by model name (a surviving row node is updated in place; a model that changes section moves its node; a gone model's row is removed) and owns the row state machine: its buttons' clicks set the row busy and call `onAction(name, action)`, the panel runs the fetch and then `setRowBusy(name, 'done')` (the next reconcile ends the busy state) or `setRowBusy(name, null)` (failure: label restored, an armed Delete reverted, held focus returned) plus `setRowError`. Focus in a surviving row survives; when its button hides or the row goes it moves to the row's first action (Unload, Load, Delete), then the next row's, then the previous row's, then `onFocusLost` (the panel focuses Refresh). The panel keeps `div.provider-models-region[aria-live=polite]` around the loading, error and empty lines and the control's list. Overlapping `refresh()` calls take a generation number and a stale response is dropped (also the basic list and the library fetch). A pull in progress (progress handle, AbortController) survives any refresh; the pull select's chosen value survives while the model is still offered (reset after a pull finishes). The library fetch runs through one poll timer, and shows cached `models` while `loading` is true (squawk 0139). The row Delete is `App.ui.confirm` armed with "Confirm delete?". Options are built with DOM APIs (`name (size)`, `name (cloud only, not downloadable)`, `name (not downloadable)`, `name (size unknown)`); sizes use the local decimal `formatSize` across the panel (not `App.formatBytes`). Pull re-checks the size, then opens an `App.ui.modal` (never `App.confirmDialog`) naming the size and the provider's host; the pull starts only from the modal's `onClose` with the confirm value and only if the panel is not destroyed, then focus moves to the progress Cancel. `App.ui.modelList.capabilityChips(caps)` returns a `span.cap-chip-row` node of `App.ui.chip` (size `sm`; the "no tools" chip is tone `danger` with a `title`) or `null`, and the agent editor appends it (`App.studio.renderCapabilityChips` is gone). Pull progress reads "Pulling {model}… {pct}%" (floor of completed over total, 0% before any total, never Ollama's raw status). The VRAM value is plain text in `admin-pf-vram-summary`; the Ollama empty state reads "Use Pull to download one."; the editor's help line is "Each of your Agents chooses one of these in its Model section." with "Agents" the link. `provider-models.js` is 719 lines (no split; its line-guard entry is removed). The size line is `div.admin-pf-pull-size` with `admin-pf-pull-size--warn` and `data-state="warn"`. Testids are `admin-pf-pull-{select,btn,size,progress,cancel,error}` and `admin-pf-pull-confirm{,-cancel,-go}`. Tests: `tests/test_ollama_library.py`, `tests/test_provider_api.py::TestOllamaLibrarySearch`, `tests/browser/test_flow_provider_ollama_pull.py`, `tests/browser/test_flow_provider_models_patch.py`.

### macOS runtime seam (M32 F3 L5, DD6/DD7)

One implementation in `platform/env.py` + `platform/interpreter.py`, behind `is_frozen()` and `sys.platform == "darwin"`.

- **Login-shell `PATH` (DD7).** `env.prime_login_shell_path(timeout=5.0)` runs `$SHELL` (else the passwd shell, else `/bin/zsh`) as `-l -i -c` printing `/usr/bin/printenv PATH` between two markers (works under fish). stdin and stderr are `/dev/null`, stdout a tempfile (not a pipe), `start_new_session=True`; on timeout the process group is `killpg`ed. It runs once per process, under a lock, synchronously: from `main.cli_entry` for `serve` and `doctor`, and from `DesktopSession.run` (after the `is_ours` return) for `app`. The result is cached **including a fallback** ("static fallback", one log line, never re-run). An unprimed process never primes lazily. Returns `"login shell"`, `"static fallback"` or `"not applicable"` (off frozen darwin; no state change). `path_source() -> (source, reason)` feeds `doctor`'s `path:` line (frozen darwin only).
- **Order** (`clean_subprocess_env`, primed): login entries, inherited `PATH`, static list, `THE_ONE_EXTRA_PATH`, de-duplicated; the bundle strip runs over the combined `PATH`. A Terminal-launched frozen `serve` gets the same order. The static POSIX list gained `~/.volta/bin` and `~/.local/share/fnm/aliases/default/bin`.
- **Interpreter (DD6).** `walk_python_candidates` walks every non-empty `PATH` entry (`shutil.which(cand, path=entry)`, candidate-outer), skipping Windows Store aliases and, on darwin, the Command Line Tools stub: `/usr/bin/python3|python` (by `normpath`, not symlink resolution) unless `xcode-select -p` exits 0 and prints an existing directory (5 s bound, cached). `THE_ONE_PYTHON` is never filtered. `doctor`'s runtime list uses the same walk for `python`/`python3`.
- **`doctor` on darwin.** `tray: dock — macOS keeps the app in the Dock`; the `desktop shell` gate uses `bundle_has_modules(("webview.platforms.cocoa", "objc", "AppKit", "WebKit"))` and never imports them or pystray (frozen OK detail `Cocoa backend present`, which must never say "skipped"). Tests: `tests/test_macos_runtime_seam.py`, `TestMacosDoctor` in `tests/test_doctor.py`.

### Windows PTY backend (M15 F3)

`WindowsPtySession` (`the_one/platform/pty_windows.py`) is the Windows concrete
implementation of `PtySession`. It spawns `cmd.exe` via `pywinpty`'s `PtyProcess`
(ConPTY-backed, Windows 10 1809+ / Server 2019+). The platform dependency is
`pywinpty>=2.0; sys_platform == "win32"` — installed only on Windows via the env
marker in `pyproject.toml`.

**Split-module structure.** The PTY subsystem is split across three files:
- `platform/pty.py` — platform-neutral ABC (`PtySession`) + `create_pty_session()`
  factory dispatcher. No POSIX-only stdlib at module scope; imports on every OS.
- `platform/pty_unix.py` — `UnixPtySession` (POSIX concrete backend). Never imported
  on Windows.
- `platform/pty_windows.py` — `WindowsPtySession` (Windows/ConPTY concrete backend).
  Never imported on Linux or macOS (the factory's `os.name == "nt"` guard keeps it out
  of the Linux import cascade). The top-level `import winpty` is therefore safe.

**Socket-timeout idle-read mechanism (ConPTY-specific).** The obvious approach
(`proc.read()` or setting `read_blocking=False`) does NOT work for idle detection.
`PtyProcess.read()` performs a blocking `fileobj.recv()` fed by an internal daemon
thread; the `read_blocking`/`PYWINPTY_BLOCK` flag is captured by that thread's closure
at construction time — changing it after spawn is a **no-op**.

**WARNING:** do NOT call `proc.read_blocking = False` (or `proc.read_blocking = True`)
after `PtyProcess.spawn` — the thread has already captured the flag and will not observe
the change. A future maintainer who sees `read_blocking` in the pywinpty API and tries
to use it for idle detection will introduce a silent regression.

The correct approach (used by `_read_until_idle_blocking`): call
`proc.fileobj.settimeout(idle_window)` then `proc.fileobj.recv(_READ_CHUNK)` directly
(raw bytes, bypassing `proc.read()`). Catch `socket.timeout` as the "no data this
window" signal; catch empty `recv` or `EOFError` as EOF.

**`_WINDOWS_ENV_ALLOWLIST` in `platform/env.py`.** In addition to the POSIX 8-key
`_ENV_ALLOWLIST` (`PATH`, `HOME`, `USER`, etc.), `_build_subprocess_env` unions in
`_WINDOWS_ENV_ALLOWLIST` on `os.name == "nt"`:
`SystemRoot`, `PATHEXT`, `COMSPEC`, `TEMP`, `TMP`, `USERPROFILE`, `USERNAME`.
These are non-secret OS essentials that `cmd.exe` requires to function correctly.
`_ENV_ALLOWLIST` is NOT modified — `test_execution_helpers.py:273` pins it to the exact
8-key POSIX tuple and that pin must stay green.

**Curated Windows CI lane (DD7 revised — 2026-06-13).** The `windows-tests` lane
(`ci/tasks/windows-tests.yml`) runs a curated file list (~427 tests) on the Concourse
`platform: windows` worker rather than the full suite. The full suite remains the
source of truth on Linux; the Windows lane covers only the platform-divergent OS surface
(`test_platform_*`, `test_pty*`, `test_filesystem*`, `test_config*`,
`test_execution*`, `test_encryption`, `test_sandbox_factory/contract`,
`test_logging_config`). (Formerly the `windows` job in `.github/workflows/test.yml`,
now `workflow_dispatch`-only break-glass — see `ci/README.md`.)

**THE RULE:** if you add a new test file that exercises platform-divergent behavior
(PTY/shell, env allowlist, filesystem paths, encryption, execution, sandbox factory),
you MUST add it to the curated file list in `ci/tasks/windows-tests.yml` — or it will
NOT run on Windows. The maintenance comment in that task file states this rule inline.
Platform-agnostic test files (DB layer, provider/LLM/MCP-protocol) are
legitimately absent from the curated list.

**`_wrapped` is always `False` on Windows.** `UnixPtySession._wrapped` is set at
`start()` time from `spec.command == "bwrap"` and controls SIGKILL-fast vs
SIGTERM-grace teardown. On Windows, `PassthroughSandbox` never produces a `bwrap`
command, so `self._wrapped` is structurally `False` and the bwrap teardown branch is
never taken.

**Windows is unsandboxed (DD9).** The sandbox factory returns `PassthroughSandbox`
on non-Linux; `warn_if_isolation_unenforced` still fires for boxed profiles (the seam
contract is preserved). See `SECURITY-MODEL.md` "Windows: interactive tools run
unsandboxed" for the full model, including the Job Object lifecycle semantics.

### Origin allowlist

`OriginAllowlistMiddleware` gates state-mutating HTTP (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE) and WS upgrades by `Origin`/`Referer` against `THE_ONE_ORIGIN_ALLOWLIST`. Default: `http://127.0.0.1:*` and `http://localhost:*` — port-wildcard with strict-digits enforcement to reject `http://127.0.0.1.evil.com`-class confusion attacks. Missing-Origin requests pass through (server-to-server clients are NOT browser-originating). WS upgrades close at the ASGI layer (`{"type": "websocket.close", "code": 1008}`) BEFORE the route handler can `accept()` — `Depends()` runs post-`accept()` and cannot do this. Policy: `SECURITY-MODEL.md`.

### Continue Signal

A virtual tool (`CONTINUE_SCHEMA`, defined inline in `core.py`) that the LLM calls to indicate it needs another iteration. Not a real tool — the agent loop intercepts it and loops.

**Capability gate**: `Agent.step()` consults `LLMProvider.supports_tools()` once per turn. When tools are unsupported (e.g. Ollama Gemma — `/api/show` capabilities lack `"tools"`), the wire `tools=` array stays empty (Ollama 400s if a tools array is offered to a non-tool model). M16 F03 L08C (#105): the degraded path is **chat-only** — the system prompt is replaced with the tool-free platform prompt (`Agent.degraded_system_prompt`, built at agent-build time; no catalog, no `tool_code` format spec, no continue-tool semantics) on an ephemeral request copy (self.messages is NOT mutated). Recovery is also gated OFF at the provider level: `OllamaProvider.complete()` and `.stream()` gate `_recover_into_tool_calls` on `supports_tools()`, so a spontaneously-emitted `tool_code` fence from a non-tool model passes through verbatim as assistant text instead of being parsed/stripped/dispatched. The model converses naturally; it never runs a tool. Studio surfaces the degraded model via the red `no tools` chip on the model selector. (Prior approach — direct tool protocol injection via `degraded_tools.build_degraded_tool_prompt` — was trialled as 08A and found unreliable for 4B models; retired with `the_one/agent/degraded_tools.py` in 08C.)

**Degraded redundant-call backstop**: M16 F03 L09 (#105): a degraded model (e.g. gemma3:4b) can get *stuck* — re-emitting a tool call whose result it already has, then terminating with an empty message (no closing answer). `_run_iterations` keeps a turn-local executed-map keyed on `(name, canonical-json(raw args)) → (result content, is_error)`, populated only after a successful, non-paused dispatch. On the degraded path each response's `tool_calls` are partitioned into new vs already-executed; **only new calls dispatch** (the redundant re-emission is dropped). When *all* calls are redundant: if the response has no text, the empty terminal assistant message is **replaced in-place** (not appended) with a synthesized closing surfacing the prior result(s) — so persisted history carries no empty message with orphaned `tool_calls`; if the response *does* have text, that text is the answer and its leftover undispatched `tool_calls` are cleared. The backstop is **gated on `not supports_tools`** — the capable path is byte-identical to before (a capable model may legitimately re-poll changing state, e.g. `shell("date")`). The auth-pause/`_deferred_tool_calls` flow is untouched (the map records only after a non-paused dispatch, so a paused call is never treated as redundant on resume).

### Model capability surface

`LLMProvider.supports_tools()` is async, default `True` for cloud providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, mock). `OllamaProvider` overrides via `ollama_api.get_model_capabilities(base_url, model)`:

- Fetches `/api/show`, reads the `capabilities` array.
- Caches per `(base_url, model)` with 5-minute TTL.
- Returns `frozenset[str] | None`. `None` means "couldn't determine" — the agent treats that as `True` (trust the provider; surface errors via the 400-body diagnostic).

`GET /api/providers/{id}/models` async-gathers per-model capabilities for Ollama providers. Failures are non-fatal: a single capability fetch failing degrades to `null`, the rest of the list still renders.

The Pull Model dropdown is **deliberately NOT enriched** — `/api/tags` doesn't carry capabilities, so per-option chips would mean N×`/api/show` round-trips per dropdown open.

### Dev iteration

The app runs host-native at `localhost:8484` via `the-one serve` (started by `make up`). `serve` has no `--reload`, so source edits under `./the_one/` take effect on `make restart`. Tests run host-native against the repo `.venv` — `.venv/bin/python -m pytest ...` (or the `make test-*` chains) always see the latest test code.

### Diagnostics (`the-one doctor` / `the_one/diagnostics.py`)

`run_doctor(out=None) -> int` (`the_one/diagnostics.py`) is the frozen-runtime spawn diagnostic (M15 F5, extended by M24 F01 with vector-store/embedding probes, and by Flight 2 Leg 2 with `fts5`; the vector-store side narrowed from two probes to one in Leg 6, DD-9). It builds the real child-spawn env via `_build_subprocess_env()`, runs a fixed list of probes, and prints one `  {name}: {status} — {detail}` line per probe (`status` is `OK`/`FAIL`), followed by an advisory `runtimes:` block from `detect_runtimes()`, then a `RESULT: PASS`/`RESULT: FAIL` line. Returns `0` only when every gate's `ok` is `True`; `1` otherwise. `out` defaults to `sys.stdout`; tests pass an `io.StringIO()`.

**Consumers of the exit code + stdout** — the report format is a contract, not incidental logging:
- `the_one/main.py`'s `cli_entry()` `doctor` branch: `sys.exit(run_doctor())`.
- `tests/test_doctor.py`: asserts `code == 0`/`!= 0` and greps specific `"{name}: OK"`/`"{name}: FAIL"` substrings out of the captured stream.
- `packaging/windows/build.ps1` (called by `windows-packages.yml` and `ci/tasks/windows-installer-build.yml`): runs the frozen `the-one.exe doctor`, captures stdout to a file, and PowerShell-matches the exact same per-gate `"{name}: OK"` strings (`child spawn`, `desktop shell`, `usearch`, `fts5`, `embedding model`, `file watcher`, `window chrome`, `time zones`, `mcp sdk`) plus `RESULT: PASS`. Renaming a probe's `name` breaks these greps silently — they are not exercised by `make test-unit`.

**The eleven gates**, in the order `run_doctor` runs and prints them:

| Probe | Function | Frozen-only? |
|---|---|---|
| `child spawn` | `_probe_child_spawn` | No — unconditional |
| `python interpreter` | `_probe_interpreter` | No — unconditional |
| `desktop shell` | `_probe_desktop_shell` | Yes |
| `llm providers` | `_probe_llm_providers` | Yes |
| `model catalog` | `_probe_model_catalog` | Yes |
| `usearch` | `_probe_usearch` | Yes |
| `fts5` | `_probe_fts5` | No — unconditional |
| `embedding model` | `_probe_embedding_model` (via `LocalStaticBackend`) | No — unconditional |
| `file watcher` | `_probe_file_watcher` | import no — round-trip yes |
| `window chrome` | `_probe_window_chrome` | No — unconditional (skips off Windows) |
| `time zones` | `_probe_time_zones` | No — unconditional |
| `mcp sdk` | `_probe_mcp_sdk` (import seam `_import_mcp_sdk`) | No — unconditional |

Frozen-only gates downgrade a failure to an informational `ok=True` line when `is_frozen()` is False (a dev host may legitimately lack an optional extra, or native extension import/load behaviour may vary by build); they still fail the build when frozen. The skip detail's shape varies by probe: `_vector_probe_outcome` (used by `_probe_usearch` and the round-trip half of `_probe_file_watcher`) uses an em-dash form, `f"skipped — {reason}"`; `_probe_desktop_shell`, `_probe_llm_providers`, and `_probe_model_catalog` instead use a parenthesized form — `"skipped (gui extra not installed)"`, `f"skipped (extras not installed in dev): {detail}"`, and `f"skipped (seed did not resolve {model} on this host)"` respectively. `child spawn`, `python interpreter`, `fts5`, `embedding model`, `file watcher`'s import half, `time zones` (M28 F1 L1, DD9: proves `canonical_zones()` has over 300 names, `localtime` is refused, a `ZoneInfo` loaded straight from the tzdata package converts a winter and a summer Los Angeles instant correctly, and `tool_lines`/`date_line` give the exact strings; detail `<N> zones (tzdata <version>); system zone: <name or none detected>`; detection never affects ok; ASCII-only; the frozen build must bundle tzdata's data files and submodules) and `mcp sdk` (M29 F1 L2, DD11: imports `mcp`, `mcp.types.version` and `the_one.self_configuration.surface`, builds a `truststore` SSL context; detail `mcp <version>, handshake <revision>`; ASCII-only; the frozen build must carry `mcp_types`, `httpx2`, `httpcore2` (plus their dist metadata), `truststore` and `opentelemetry`) and `window chrome` (M25 F5 L3, DD8: loads `dwmapi` and calls `DwmSetWindowAttribute(NULL, 35, &v, 4)`, expecting an error HRESULT; reports `caption colours supported (build N, probe 0x…)` from build 22000, else `dark mode only (…)`; off Windows `skipped (not Windows)`; ASCII-only detail; the GitHub `windows-2022` runner, build 20348, takes the dark-mode-only branch) gate unconditionally — a failure fails `run_doctor` on every host, dev included (`fts5` per DD-11: FTS5 is a mission constraint in every build, not an optional extra like the vector-store gate). `file watcher` is the one **hybrid** gate (Flight 4 M24 F04 L01, DD-0/DD-9): `import watchfiles` gates unconditionally, but the native change-notification round-trip gates only when frozen, via `_vector_probe_outcome`. Runtime-detection lines (`detect_runtimes`) are separate from the twelve gates and never fail the run — missing `node`/`uv`/etc. is advisory, not a gate.

**Probe contract**: every `_probe_*` function returns a `ProbeResult(name, ok, detail)` dataclass. Eight of the ten wrap their body in a broad `except Exception` and report `ok=False` with the exception folded into `detail`, so they never raise (an escaping exception would take down `doctor` and both CI gates for reasons unrelated to the thing under test): `_probe_desktop_shell`, `_probe_llm_providers`, `_probe_model_catalog`, `_probe_usearch`, `_probe_fts5`, `_probe_embedding_model`, `_probe_file_watcher`, `_probe_window_chrome`. The other two catch narrowly and CAN propagate an unexpected exception out of `run_doctor`: `_probe_child_spawn` catches only `(OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError)`, and `_probe_interpreter` catches only `FrozenInterpreterError`. Per the `report-what-you-got` convention (see "Project Conventions" above), every probe must still assert a CONCRETE resolved value — a returned rowid, a returned index key, a vector's dimension and norm — never merely "no exception was raised"; a capability that silently degrades (an extension that loads but answers nothing) is exactly the failure class a bare try/except would miss.

**Frozen-only gating idiom — `_vector_probe_outcome(name, reason)`**: the shared helper for frozen-only gating, routed through by the vector-store probe (`_probe_usearch`) and, since Flight 4, the native round-trip half of `_probe_file_watcher` (a second backend's probe, removed in Leg 6, used to share it too — the helper's name-agnostic `(name, reason)` signature is what let it widen to a second caller with no shape change).
