Frozen → `ProbeResult(name, False, reason)` (hard `FAIL`); not frozen → `ProbeResult(name, True, f"skipped — {reason}")`. Both routed probes still always ATTEMPT the real work regardless of frozen state — only the *failure* is downgraded, never the attempt — so a passing dev host is real signal, not a rubber stamp. It exists because native extension import/load behaviour genuinely varies across builds (e.g. numkong linkage — see `_numkong_extension_state()` below — and, for `_probe_file_watcher`, whether this host/filesystem delivers native change events at all), so a dev-host failure there is not evidence of a packaging defect. **`_probe_embedding_model` deliberately does NOT use this helper** — `model2vec` is a base dependency and the bundled weights are package data present in every checkout, so an unfrozen failure IS a real defect (most usefully a missing package-data glob in a non-editable wheel install, which a frozen-only gate would never catch). Likewise, `_probe_file_watcher`'s **import** half never routes through this helper (only its round-trip half does) — a missing or unloadable `watchfiles` extension is a defect on every host, from the flight the app imports it directly and unconditionally. `tests/test_doctor.py::TestEmbeddingProbeGating::test_does_not_use_the_frozen_only_helper` enforces the embedding-probe rule by `inspect.getsource`-scanning the probe function body for the string `_vector_probe_outcome` — a source-level regression guard, not a behavioral one; `TestProbeFileWatcher::test_source_names_every_dd0_knob` is the file-watcher probe's analogous source-level guard, though it checks for DD-0's pinned-knob names rather than this helper's absence.

**Offline invariant (embedding probe)**: as of Flight 2 Leg 3, the two DD-5 guards live in `the_one.embeddings.local.resolve_model_dir()` (called by `LocalStaticBackend.load()`), not in `diagnostics.py` — `_probe_embedding_model` constructs a FRESH `LocalStaticBackend` (never the process singleton `get_embedding_backend()`, so the offline test that monkeypatches `the_one.embeddings.local.EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIRNAME` can't race the singleton) and calls through the seam rather than around it. `resolve_model_dir()` resolves `importlib.resources.files("the_one.embeddings").joinpath("data", "potion-retrieval-32M")` and asserts `.is_dir()` (and that `model.safetensors` exists inside it) BEFORE ever importing `model2vec` — a missing packaged directory raises `EmbeddingModelMissingError` instead of falling through to `model2vec`'s `_resolve_folder` → `snapshot_download()` network fetch. `force_hf_offline()` then sets BOTH `os.environ["HF_HUB_OFFLINE"] = "1"` AND, if `huggingface_hub.constants` is already present in `sys.modules`, its bound `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` attribute directly — the constant is read once at `huggingface_hub` import time, so setting only the env var is too late for a process (like the pytest suite) where the package may already be imported. See the `probe-must-not-pass-by-download` convention. The probe's detail line now reads `f"{dim}-dim {native_dtype} model -> float32 seam, norm {norm:.4f}, truncated={n} (...)"` (ASCII `->`, not U+2192 — AC-15/build 606620 found the frozen Windows CI lane's piped stdout is cp1252, which can't encode the arrow character) — the model's native dtype AND the float32 seam it's upcast through, plus the DD-1 truncation count for the probe text; the norm check is `abs(norm - 1.0) > NORM_TOLERANCE` (1e-3) rather than a bare nonzero check, which also subsumes the old all-zeros failure mode.

**`numkong=` / `_numkong_extension_state()`**: `_usearch_acceleration()` reports `numkong=yes|no, simd=<kernel>` by reading `usearch.compiled.USES_NUMKONG` and calling `hardware_acceleration()`. When `numkong=no`, it appends `_numkong_extension_state()`'s own diagnosis, because `numkong=no` alone cannot distinguish two very different causes: (a) the `numkong._numkong` extension is present but fails to import HERE (e.g. a Windows `.pyd` whose dependent DLL can't resolve — `usearch/__init__.py` swallows exactly that with a bare `except ImportError: pass`, a real packaging defect), vs (b) the extension loads fine and this `usearch.compiled` binary simply was never linked against it (an upstream wheel property, unfixable by packaging). `numkong=no, simd=serial` on every published Windows `usearch` wheel (verified 2.16.0 through 2.26.2 — zero `nk_*` symbol references, no `_numkong*.pyd` import table entry, empty delay-import directory) is case (b): an upstream wheel property, not a packaging defect. `packaging/windows/the-one.spec`'s long "numkong / SIMD on Windows" comment block records the verification in full so a future maintainer doesn't re-run the search.

**`_peak_rss_bytes() -> tuple[int | None, str | None]`**: best-effort peak (not current) resident-set-size of this process, threaded into the embedding probe's detail line (DD-4 read path — no CI task measures memory, so the probe self-reporting is the only observability). POSIX: `resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss` — **KiB on Linux, bytes on macOS/BSD** (`sys.platform == "darwin"` branches the multiply-by-1024). Windows: `ctypes`-drives `K32GetProcessMemoryInfo` (falling back to `psapi.GetProcessMemoryInfo` if the kernel32 export is absent) into a hand-declared `_ProcessMemoryCounters` structure. **The HANDLE lesson**: `kernel32.GetCurrentProcess.restype` and `.argtypes` MUST be declared explicitly before the call — without an explicit `restype`, `GetCurrentProcess()`'s `-1` pseudo-handle is truncated by ctypes' default `c_int` return type and re-widened incorrectly on a 64-bit process, so the subsequent memory call receives an invalid handle and fails with `ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE` (verified on windows-worker). Returns `(None, reason)` on any failure — the reason is threaded into the probe's detail line so a Windows API failure is diagnosable from `doctor` stdout, never a bare "unavailable". Never raises.

**Packaging notes for a maintainer** (`packaging/windows/the-one.spec`):
- A Flight 1 native-loadable-extension collection lesson, from a vector-store candidate removed from the closure in Leg 6: `collect_data_files` unconditionally excludes every `importlib.machinery.all_suffixes()` pattern (`.so`/`.abi3.so`/`.cpython-311-*.so`, never `.dll`/`.dylib`), so it silently drops a native library that is data to the module graph (nothing imports it; it's opened by path at runtime) — an entry would appear to work on Windows/macOS while shipping nothing on Linux. Such a library needs `collect_dynamic_libs` with explicit `search_patterns` instead, routed into `binaries=` (not `datas=`) so both binary-dependency scanning and `upx_exclude` (consulted only for `EXTENSION`/`BINARY` typecodes, a dead no-op against `datas`) see it. No current spec entry needs this mechanism — `usearch.compiled`/`numkong._numkong` are ordinary importable extension modules PyInstaller's module graph collects unaided.
- The `potion-retrieval-32M` model directory IS correctly collected via `collect_data_files("the_one", includes=["embeddings/data/**/*"])` — the native-loadable-extension lesson above does NOT apply here, because every file in that directory is `.json`/`.safetensors`/`.md`, none of which are in `all_suffixes()`.
- `the_one.embeddings` needs an explicit `hiddenimports` entry even though `embedding_datas` ships its data correctly: nothing `import`s the package — it is reached only by NAME, as the string argument to `importlib.resources.files("the_one.embeddings")` in `_probe_embedding_model`, which PyInstaller's static analysis cannot see. Without the entry the package is tree-shaken out of the PYZ (`files()` raises `ModuleNotFoundError`) while its 62 MiB of weights sit in the bundle unreachable.
- `upx_exclude` carries usearch's/numkong's native artifacts plus the embedding closure's native payloads (four entries): `"compiled*"` (usearch), `"_numkong*"`, `"tokenizers*"`, `"_safetensors_rust*"` — UPX-packing a native/Rust extension is a known load-failure source; these entries are insurance against a future UPX install (no current CI toolchain provides UPX, so `upx=True` is a no-op today). `"vec0*"` (a removed vector-store candidate) and `"_hf_xet*"` (excluded from the closure entirely, squawk 0049) were dropped in Leg 6.
- `excludes = ["hf_xet"]` (added in the AC-15 build-606657 fix, M24 F02 L06): excluding `hf-xet` from the *dependency closure* (`[tool.uv] exclude-dependencies`) is not sufficient on its own — `huggingface_hub` still carries a guarded `import hf_xet`, so PyInstaller bundles the module whenever it happens to be importable in the BUILD environment, closure notwithstanding. The persistent-worker lesson: Concourse's Windows worker runs tasks uncontainerised, so `C:\Python311`'s site-packages survive across builds, and build 606657 shipped a stale `hf_xet` left over from before the closure change — the doctor gate (which never imports `hf_xet`) still passed, masking it. `ci/tasks/windows-installer-build.yml` now installs into a fresh per-build `venv` for the same reason: a persistent build env can equally hide a MISSING dependency, not just a stray extra one — proven out by AC-15 build 606698, where the now-fresh venv exposed a real `pip install -r <uv export>` failure (huggingface-hub's own wheel metadata still declares `hf-xet`, so pip's hash-checking mode refuses the whole install rather than skipping just that package) that the persistent env at build 606657 had been silently absorbing by leaving its own stale `hf_xet` copy in place; the fix there is `--no-deps` on that install step (`uv export` is already a complete closure — pip has nothing left to resolve) plus a `$LASTEXITCODE` check after it, since neither the venv nor `excludes` alone would have caught a wholesale install failure. `PyInstaller.excludes` is the belt to the closure's suspenders — it makes the bundle correct regardless of what else is sitting in the interpreter that runs the freeze.

**Test conventions** (see also the `autouse-fixture + dedicated-real-load-file` convention above): `tests/test_doctor.py` has a function-scoped `_stub_embedding_probe` fixture (`@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)`) that patches `diagnostics._probe_embedding_model` for every one of that file's 19 `run_doctor(` call sites, so `make test-unit` never pays for the 62 MiB load. The dedicated real load lives in `tests/test_doctor_embedding_model.py::TestRealLoad` and must never be skipped or mocked — it is the only host-side proof the bundled asset actually loads. Tests throughout both files assert on the emitted `detail` string (dimension, dtype, norm, byte size, `numkong=`), not bare `ok`, per `report-what-you-got` — a monkeypatch a probe's broad `except` would otherwise swallow silently would leave a test asserting nothing but `ok is True`. Because `sqlite3.Connection` is an immutable C type (its methods can't be monkeypatched away), `test_frozen_fail_when_enable_load_extension_is_absent` instead swaps the whole `diagnostics.sqlite3` MODULE reference for a `types.SimpleNamespace(Connection=type("Connection", (), {}))` stand-in. The Windows-branch pattern (`tests/test_doctor_embedding_model.py::TestPeakRssWindowsBranch`): monkeypatch `os.name = "nt"`, stub `ctypes.WinDLL` with `raising=False` (it doesn't exist on non-Windows `ctypes`) to return a fake `kernel32` object whose `GetCurrentProcess`/`K32GetProcessMemoryInfo` are recording callables, then assert `restype`/`argtypes` were declared on those callables BEFORE they're invoked — proving the production code fixes the HANDLE-truncation bug rather than merely returning a plausible number.

## Naming rule (sortie 06)

One rule names agents, model providers, knowledge bases and MCP servers (connectors stay out: display-only names). `the_one/naming.py` is a stdlib-only leaf: `check_name(value)` trims, then refuses (with a `NameRuleError` carrying one plain sentence) an empty or non-string name, a `/` or `\`, exactly `.` or `..`, a `Cc` control or bidi override (U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069), and more than 64 code points. Spaces, dots, accents and emoji stay legal. `name_rule_detail(msg)` builds the 422 body: `[{"type": "name_rule", "loc": ["name"], "msg": ...}]`, the shape `App.api.fieldErrors` maps (the rename route uses `loc ["name"]` too, not `new_name`).

- **Checking routes (seven):** `POST /agents`, `POST /agents/{name}/rename`, create and update for `/providers`, `/knowledge-bases` and `/mcp-sources`. Creates write the trimmed name back before `model_validate` and any guard (so `" self-configuration "` meets the reserved-name check trimmed). The three PUTs check **only when the body carries `name`**, on the merged dict, so a legacy row with a bad name stays editable until it is repaired. `PUT /agents/{name}` forces the path name and needs no check. `POST /agents` with an existing name is a 409 (`name_conflict`, `loc ["name"]`) and never overwrites.
- **Exempt routes:** every other POST/PUT under the four prefixes never sets a name (rules, notes, self-configuration and its revisions, KB items and reindex, provider oauth/test/model actions, MCP test/connect). `tests/test_api_name_rule.py` lists each in `CHECKED` or `EXEMPT` with a reason and fails on an unclassified route.
- **Legacy JSON import:** `migrate_json_to_sqlite` skips (one WARNING) a file whose body name fails the rule, and also skips when the body name already exists, so a stem that differs from the body name is not re-saved on every start. A valid file is stored unchanged.
- **Not on the models:** the rule is applied at write boundaries only. Putting it on `AgentConfig` and the other models would make a stored legacy name fail to load.
- **`default`** stays a legal name; a stored `default` configures every no-agent chat.
- **Renames carry their references (leg 2, DD8):** an agent rename (`SQLiteAgentStore.rename`) also rewrites, in the same transaction, every `connectors.config.default_agent` and `channel_map` value equal to the old name (a rewritten connector gets a fresh `updated_at`; a malformed config is skipped with a WARNING; the parse is shared with `connector_bindings`). An MCP-server rename (`update_mcp_source`, after the row commits) calls `the_one/mcp/rename_references.py::carry_mcp_rename`: for each agent in `SQLiteAgentStore.list_by_mcp_source(source_id)` it moves `mcp_overrides[old]` to `[new]` and rewrites `mcp__{normalized old}__` `disabled_tools` entries, through `load`/`save` (the column is encrypted), never raising (a failing agent is a WARNING and is returned in the failed list). It **copies/adds instead of moving** when another source attached to the same agent still has the old raw name (overrides) or normalised prefix (disabled tools), never clobbers an existing `[new]`, and saves only an agent that changed. Known limit: `mcp__foo__` also prefixes a `foo__bar` source's tools. A knowledge-base rename is unchanged in behaviour; its tool-name computation, slug-unchanged early return and guarded store acquisition live in `knowledge/kb_rename.py::carry_kb_rename(existing, updated, get_agent_store=...)`, so the leg-4 repair can call it outside a request.
- **Boxed scratch directory:** `tools/sessions.py::_scratch_slug` turns the agent name into `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, at most 40 characters (`agent` when empty), so the directory under `data_dir/boxes/` is always one path level.
- **The startup repair (leg 4, DD11-DD12):** `the_one/db/name_repair.py::repair_unsafe_names(conn, *, agent_store, mcp_repo, kb_repo)` runs in the lifespan right after `migrate_json_to_sqlite` and before `reconcile_on_startup` (after the key reconcile, so the encryptor is ready; it is Python, not a `schema.py` migration, because an MCP rename must rewrite agents' encrypted `mcp_overrides`). It scans each of the four tables with a raw `SELECT` (never `list`/`load`, which decrypt), and for each name `check_name` refuses computes `naming.repair_name(value, *, kind, taken)`: `/` and `\` and whitespace controls become spaces, other `Cc` and bidi characters are deleted, whitespace collapses, trim, cut to 64, trim; an empty/`.`/`..` result becomes `Renamed agent` / `Renamed provider` / `Renamed knowledge base` / `Renamed MCP server`; a `casefold` collision with `taken` (every stored name of the kind plus names assigned earlier in the pass, surface rows included) appends ` 2`, ` 3`, ... within 64. Renames go through the carry paths: `SQLiteAgentStore.rename` then `cascade_rename` (its own `try`: a failure leaves the rename standing with a WARNING that the surface marker may be stale); `mcp_repo.update` then `carry_mcp_rename`; `kb_repo.update` then `carry_kb_rename`; providers a raw `UPDATE llm_providers SET name, updated_at` in `write_transaction` (agents reference them by id; no decrypt/re-encrypt). Self-configuration surface rows are skipped (`reconcile_on_startup` owns them); an MCP source whose secrets fail to load is skipped with a WARNING. Each rename logs one WARNING (`renamed <kind> 'old' to 'new': its name broke the naming rule`); each row and the whole pass are try/except, so the app always starts; a clean database or a second pass does nothing and logs nothing. `default` and a name that is only padded are not repaired (they pass `check_name`). Release notes for the next release should tell operators that names with `/`, `\`, control characters or over 64 characters are renamed once at startup (see the log).

## Provider type extension checklist

Seven layers must coordinate. Missing any one causes either silent rejection (validation) or runtime errors. (Brought current in sortie 03: the type enum has one source of truth, and the frontend lives in Library, not Administration.)

### Provider URL validation

`_is_safe_provider_url` (`interfaces/web/api.py`) enforces `https://` for non-localhost hosts on LLM provider `base_url`. Called at endpoint time from `create_provider`, `update_provider`, and `test_provider` — NOT a Pydantic validator (must run on the resolved `base_url` post-`model_copy`, after `__MASKED__` substitution). Two skip layers: (a) `_URL_VALIDATION_SKIPPED_TYPES = frozenset({"mock", "ollama"})` bypass entirely; (b) for the rest, `http://` is allowed only when host is `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, or `*.localdomain`. RFC1918 / link-local / `0.0.0.0` require `https://`.

### Checklist

1. **`PROVIDER_TYPES`** — `db/_provider_types.py`, the single source of truth; `tests/test_provider_types_consistency.py` pins the next three (and the live CHECK) against it.
2. **Pydantic `Literal`** — `db/models.py` `LLMProviderConfig.type`. Add the new value to the `Literal[...]` tuple (spelled out; an import-time assert pins it to `PROVIDER_TYPES`), or `model_validate` rejects.
3. **`PROVIDER_TYPE_MAP`** — `llm/registry.py` (consumed by the factory and the provider API branches). Add `"<type>": "<registry-name>"`, or the module-load assert (keys must equal `PROVIDER_TYPES`) fails at import.
4. **Registry `loaders`** — `llm/registry.py:_try_load_builtin`, plus the `extra_aliases` entry when the type rides another package's extra. Add the lazy-loader lambda, or `get_provider("<type>")` raises a misleading `ImportError("requires extra dependencies")`.
5. **SQLite `CHECK` constraint** — `db/schema.py` (table definition AND a migration). The `llm_providers.CHECK (type IN (...))` clause is rendered from `PROVIDER_TYPES` (`_PROVIDER_TYPES_SQL`), so a fresh database admits the type, but an existing one needs a rebuild: `CURRENT_VERSION` + 1 with the v33/v44 ceremony (`executescript` with `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF`, `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS llm_providers_new` prelude, copy EVERY column including `secrets`, `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO schema_version` last). Bump the `CURRENT_VERSION == N` pins in `tests/test_db_migration.py`.
6. **API gating / branches** — `interfaces/web/api.py`: type-specific gating in `create_provider`/`update_provider` (e.g. dev-mode gate for `mock`; the plan-token checks); branch in `test_provider`; branch in `list_provider_models`; entry (value and label) in `GET /api/providers/types` (and the count tests in `tests/test_provider_api.py`).
7. **Frontend** — Library › Model providers, `interfaces/web/static/js/areas/studio/providers-section.js` `TYPE_DEFAULTS["<type>"]` (unconditional — needed for editing existing providers even if hidden from creation; an `apiKeyLabel`/`apiKeyPlaceholder`/`apiKeyHelp` relabels the key row, and `apiKeyHelp` must contain no closing brace: `tests/test_admin_reasoning_effort_field.py` reads an entry up to the next one); `KNOWN_BADGE_TYPES` in `studio.js`; `BADGE_KINDS` in `ui/feedback.js`; `--badge-<type>` and `--badge-ink-<type>` tokens in BOTH theme blocks of `tokens.css`, the `.badge--<type>` rule in `components.css`; the contrast-audit rows (`tests/test_design_tokens_a11y.py`, `tests/browser/design_tokens.py`), the kind-count pins (`tests/test_ui_badge_kinds.py`, `tests/browser/test_ui_choice_contract.py`) and `REASONING_CAPABLE_TYPES` when relevant. The admin-scoped `.admin-provider-badge--*` rules are not extended (Administration no longer hosts providers).
8. **`_init_provider_state(self, kwargs)`** (optional hook on `LLMProvider`) — when the new provider needs to capture configuration outside the base field set (e.g. OpenRouter's `app_url`/`app_name` analytics headers; Ollama's lazy `max_context_tokens` state).

## Claude Plan providers: OAuth and Token (sortie 03)

Two provider types authenticate a Claude subscription with the same bearer request path (`AnthropicPlanProvider._build_client`: `auth_token` bearer, `api_key` force-nulled, `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20`). `anthropic-plan` (labelled "Claude Plan (OAuth)") signs in through the PKCE flow and refreshes through `llm/plan_credentials.py`. `anthropic-plan-token` ("Claude Plan (Token)") takes a token pasted from `claude setup-token` (about a year) and has no sign-in, refresh or credential source.

- **Token type.** `AnthropicPlanTokenProvider(AnthropicPlanProvider)` (`llm/anthropic_plan_provider.py`, `name = "anthropic-plan-token"`), built by `AgentFactory` with `access_token=provider_config.api_key` and no `provider_config_id`. The token lives in the Fernet-encrypted `llm_providers.api_key` column and uses the existing mask and preserve contract. `_before_request` raises `PlanTokenMissingError` before any HTTP call when no token is stored (an SDK client with `auth_token=None` would read `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` from the environment, so `/test` and `/models` also check first and build no client).
- **Server-stamped `token_set_at`.** `secrets["token_set_at"]` (`now_iso()`) is written only by the server (`_PLAN_TOKEN_MANAGED_KEYS` in `api.py`, deliberately NOT in `anthropic_oauth.OAUTH_MANAGED_SECRET_KEYS`): on create, on a changed token, on a type change into the token type, or when absent. A re-paste of the identical token (whitespace-stripped) keeps the old stamp; a client-sent value never seeds or overwrites it. `_provider_response` returns `token_set_at` and `token_expires_around` (+`PLAN_TOKEN_LIFETIME_DAYS` = 365, defined in `anthropic_oauth.py` so the API layer can read it without the SDK; that module is the one import site), non-null only for the token type; `oauth_active` is true only for `anthropic-plan`.
- **`update_provider` order.** `fresh_key_supplied` is captured from the RAW body before masked-key resolution; `data["secrets"] = {}`; `model_validate` the merge; then, on the RESOLVED `updated`, pin the OAuth keys only for `anthropic-plan` (and force `api_key=None` there), or check the token (required, `sk-ant-api…` refused with a sentence that never echoes the value, and a type change INTO the token type must carry a freshly pasted token) and stamp for the token type; otherwise both key sets are dropped. Applied with `model_copy(update=...)`.
- **Plan error translation (both types).** `AnthropicPlanProvider` overrides `complete()` and `stream()` (the parent's signature; `stream` iterates the parent through `contextlib.aclosing` and translates only before its first chunk) and routes `anthropic.AnthropicError` through the overridable `_translate_plan_error`. The base maps a `RateLimitError` whose `exc.body["error"]["message"] == "Error"` (the bare 429 a plan answers for models it will not run for third-party apps; never matched on `str(exc)`) to `PlanRequestRefusedError`; the token subclass also maps `AuthenticationError` to `PlanTokenRejectedError`. The three errors are plain `Exception` subclasses raised `from` the SDK error.
- **Editor.** `TYPE_DEFAULTS['anthropic-plan-token']` relabels the API-key row "Token" with a placeholder and a help line (`provider-api-key-help`, tied by `aria-describedby`); the type-change handler patches them through the field handle (`setLabel`, `setHint`, the placeholder). Edit mode shows `provider-token-expiry` (an `App.ui.status` built by `renderTokenExpiry` from the last SAVED row, refreshed from the PUT response because an edit-mode Save does not re-mount); `App.studio.planTokenExpiry(setAtIso, expiresIso, nowMs)` is the pure wording/state function (`past` when `now >= expires`, `soon` when `expires - now <= 30 days`). Dates come from `App.time.formatDate`.
- **Schema v44** rebuilds `llm_providers` so the CHECK admits the type, copying every column including `secrets`.
- Tests: `tests/test_anthropic_provider.py` (token provider, translation), `tests/test_provider_api.py` (`TestPlanToken*`), `tests/test_db_migration.py::TestSchemaV44`, `tests/browser/test_flow_provider_plan_token.py` (the `providers` lane).

## Streaming protocol

Provider-agnostic at the agent layer; per-provider native streaming sits behind `LLMProvider.stream(...)`. The base class supplies a default implementation that calls `complete()` and yields exactly one `StreamChunk(is_final=True, ...)`. Subclasses MAY override.

### `StreamChunk` contract

```python
@dataclass
class StreamChunk:
    content: str = ""               # NEW tokens since last chunk; delta semantics
    is_final: bool = False
    tool_calls: list[ToolCall] = []  # populated on final chunk
    usage: TokenUsage | None = None  # populated on final chunk where exposed
    message: Message | None = None   # synthesised assistant Message; final chunk
```

**Delta semantics**: `content` is NEW tokens, NOT cumulative. The agent accumulates by string concatenation. The final chunk MAY carry a delta (some providers emit stop-event trailing text); the accumulator checks `chunk.content` regardless of `is_final`.

**Final-chunk contract**: providers populate `message` so `Agent.step` can prefer it. When `message` is None, the agent rebuilds from accumulated `content` + `tool_calls` + `usage`.

### Per-provider notes

- **Anthropic**: native via `client.messages.stream(...)`. Catches `BadRequestError` mentioning "temperature", strips the kwarg, retries once. Gated by `yielded_any` so the retry is only safe BEFORE any chunk has emitted (Anthropic raises pre-stream in practice).
- **OpenAI**: native via `chat.completions.create(stream=True, stream_options={"include_usage": True}, ...)`. The `include_usage` flag is **required** — without it the trailing metadata chunk has no `usage` and end-of-stream telemetry breaks. Tool-call deltas accumulate by `index`.
- **Ollama**: native via `ollama_api.chat_stream`. True SSE (`data: {...}` + `data: [DONE]` terminator), distinct from `pull_model`'s JSONL. Reuses the OpenAI-shape tool-call accumulation.
- **Mock**: honours `config["stream_chunks"]: list[str]`. When unset, falls back to default-impl **inline** (NOT `super().stream(...)` delegation) for clarity.

### `_StreamAccumulator` — shared streaming helper

`the_one/llm/_streaming.py` — M12 F6 L1 consolidation of the near-identical per-provider streaming internals.

**Responsibility**: encapsulates the two cross-provider concerns that previously lived as duplicated blocks in `openai_provider.py` and `ollama_provider.py` (tool-call delta accumulation) and `anthropic_provider.py` (byte→interim-usage emitter):

1. **Tool-call delta accumulation** (`feed_tool_call_delta`, `finalize_tool_calls`) — keyed by `index` (OpenAI / Ollama streaming convention). Providers normalise SDK objects to `str` at the edge before calling `feed_tool_call_delta`; the accumulator is str-append-only. Ollama must `json.dumps(args)` when `arguments` arrives as a dict before handing the increment here.
2. **Byte→interim-usage emission** (`maybe_emit_interim`) — returns a `StreamChunk(usage=…)` when the cumulative byte count since last emission crosses `threshold` (shared 200-byte default). Providers call this AFTER yielding the content chunk, then yield the returned interim chunk (if not None). Preserves the exact per-provider emit ordering.

**Per-provider bytes-per-token** (M16 F08 L01): a SINGLE public `bytes_per_token` accessor lives on the `LLMProvider` base (default 4.0); OpenAI/OpenRouter 4.0, Ollama 3.5, Anthropic 3.7 override the class attribute. Both consumers — the `_StreamAccumulator` (passed `self.bytes_per_token` at stream-open) and the context-budget governor (`_execute_tool` → `govern_tool_content`) — read this one value, so they cannot drift. (Replaced the prior subclass-only `_bytes_per_token_for_interim()`.)

**Anthropic adopts only the byte-emitter half** — its tool calls arrive whole via `get_final_message`, not as streaming deltas.

**Import discipline**: `_streaming.py` imports ONLY from `the_one.llm.base` (acyclic: `base` is the leaf; `_streaming` depends only on `base`; providers depend on both).

### WS event vocabulary

| Event | When | Notes |
|------|-----|-------|
| `assistant_chunk` | Per-content-delta during streaming | |
| `assistant` | Direct-action shortcut path only | Suppressed when chunks fire |
| `tool_call` | Tool-call emitted by the model | Carries `parent_message_id` + `tool_call_id` |
| `tool_result` | Tool execution result | Carries the same pair |
| `usage` | Per-iteration final-chunk usage | |
| `done` | End of agent step | |

The streaming WS contract is **chunks-only-no-final-assistant**: when the chunk path fires, the legacy `assistant` event is suppressed server-side. The client has the accumulated text via chunks. The legacy event survives for the direct-action shortcut path (early-return in `Agent.step` before any LLM call).

**`parent_message_id` namespace convention**: live sessions use `{session.id}-msg-N`; replayed sessions use `replay-msg-N`. The frontend treats these as opaque grouping keys — different namespace prefixes between live and replay are not collisions.

### `chunks_seen` invariant

The server distinguishes streaming from direct-action via a per-iteration `chunks_seen` flag captured in the agent-step closure. The flag is set inside `_broadcast_chunk` and reset **unconditionally at the end of each Role.ASSISTANT block** in the `async for msg in agent.step(...)` loop. Unconditional matters for multi-iteration loops: a tool-call-only iteration (empty content, non-empty `tool_calls`) must still reset the flag for iteration N+1.

### Persistence contract

The synthesised final assistant `Message` is **NOT broadcast** as `{"type": "assistant"}` over the WS during streaming (the client already has the text). It IS:

- Appended to `session.history` via `session.add_event` so rejoin/refresh re-renders from server-side history.
- Appended to `result_messages` so the HTTP API caller receives the full message list.

This split — stream-deltas-over-WS, persist-full-message-server-side — is the F4 leg-04 contract. Enforced end-to-end by `test_streaming_message_persists_to_history`.

---

## Markdown rendering

Hand-rolled CommonMark-subset library at `static/js/markdown.js`. Public API: `App.markdown.parse(text) → AST`, `App.markdown.render(text) → DocumentFragment`, `App.markdown.NodeType`.

### Supported subset — contract for callers

paragraphs, ATX headers (`#`..`######`), fenced code blocks (the info string's first whitespace token is the language, kept only if it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_+#.-]{1,32}$`, else no class: exactly one `language-x` class token), blockquotes (single level), unordered + ordered lists (one nesting level), **task items** (`- [ ] x` / `- [x] x` / `- [X] x`, ordered too: `LIST_ITEM` `meta.task = {checked}`, rendered as `li.markdown-task` with a leading disabled `<input type=checkbox>` named from the item's FIRST paragraph text, "Task" when empty; a marker needs whitespace or the line end after `]`, so `- [x](https://a)` stays a link), horizontal rules (`---`/`***`), emphasis (`*` or `_`), strong (`**` or `__`), **strikethrough** (`~~x~~` only, node `STRIKE` -> `<del>`; a single `~` and an unmatched `~~` stay literal; the closer search skips code spans), inline code, links (including balanced-paren URLs), **bare-URL autolinks** (`http://` / `https://` at a word boundary, ended by whitespace, `<`, `>`, `\` or a control character; a trailing `.,;:!?*_~` and an unbalanced `)` are trimmed; an unsafe candidate stays text, so an autolink never emits `href="#"`), GFM pipe tables (rendered inside `div.markdown-table-wrap`, `tabindex=0 role=region aria-label=Table`, `overflow-x: auto`, so a wide table scrolls in its own region), soft breaks (single `\n`), hard breaks (trailing TWO spaces + `\n`).

**Out of subset**: footnotes, definition lists, column alignment, `www.` / `mailto:` / `<https://…>` autolinks, reference-style links, HTML passthrough, backslash escapes, recursive nested emphasis. The test corpus locks the subset in; expanding requires test-case expansion.

### Inline context

`scanInline(text, ctx)` takes `{noEmphasis, noLinks}`. Emphasis/strong bodies are scanned with `noEmphasis` (strike and autolinks still apply inside them); link labels are scanned with `noLinks`, so a label never holds a nested link or an autolink (`[a [b](https://x)](https://y)` is one anchor). The autolink and `~~` branches sit before the `*`/`_` branch so delimiters inside a URL are not eaten. `parseLink` skips code spans when counting label brackets (`` [`]`](u) ``).

### Nested-emphasis v1 contract

`***word***` renders as `<em><strong>word</strong></em>` (outer-em-first). Recursive nested emphasis is v2 scope. `**bold *with em***` renders as `<strong>bold *with em*</strong>` — the inner `*with em*` is literal. Mechanism: the `noEmphasis` context flag once an emphasis run opens.

### XSS guarantee and link policy (D3 (a))

Renderer constructs DOM via `document.createElement` + `textContent` exclusively. The library NEVER uses `innerHTML` — `grep -n innerHTML markdown.js` returns zero hits (including comments). The code-block copy button is `App.ui.copyButton` (the old `injectIcon` DOMParser seam, `App.icon` and `App.showFlash` calls are gone and those aliases deleted in M31 F6 L09a, and the file holds no ownership-allowance row). `safeHref(href)` returns the ORIGINAL string or `#`: any backslash or control character (C0, tab, newline, DEL) anywhere rejects (a browser parser treats `\` as `/` and drops tab/newline, so `/\evil` resolved off-site), `//host` is rejected, `http`/`https`/`mailto`/`tel` pass, and a `/` or `#` link passes only when `new URL(href, location.href).origin === location.origin` (an opaque `"null"` origin or a missing `location` is never same-origin). Same-origin links keep navigating the app window. "No innerHTML anywhere in the markdown pipeline" is a structural property — future contributors cannot accidentally introduce XSS via crafted markdown. Sibling `App.safeUrl` (`app.js`) has the same blind spot and is logged as squawk 0275.

### Reply presentation (A1, B1)

Under `.ui-message--assistant` in `components.css`: h1-h3 use `--font-size-h1/h2/h3` (18/16/15px; the largest reply heading is the page-heading size), h4 is body-lg and h5/h6 body, all semibold with `0.5em` margins; `.markdown-table-wrap` scrolls sideways; `li.markdown-task` hides the bullet. `.markdown-copy-btn` keeps only placement and the quiet-at-rest reveal opacity (a visibility affordance, not a disabled state).

### Robustness for streaming

`parse(null)`, `parse(undefined)`, `parse('')` all return `{type: 'document', children: []}` — no exception. Streaming callers can pass partial buffers without try/catch ceremony.

### Mid-stream rendering NOT supported

Partial markdown syntax has no canonical semantics (`**bold` mid-token has no end). The streaming path uses `textContent` accumulation followed by a single swap on `done`. Chat integration: `transcript.addMsg(cls, text, ...)` markdown-renders assistant messages (`App.markdown.render(text)` is the `App.ui.message` body); user/tool/system stay text. Detection uses a first-token whitespace split (`cls.split(' ')[0] === 'assistant'`) so `'assistant-error'` would NOT trigger markdown.

### Extension contract

Adding a new construct: (1) `NodeType` constant, (2) tokeniser/parser case, (3) renderer branch, (4) ≥3 parametrised test cases. All four in the same leg. PRs missing test cases fail review.

---

## Design system

**Design Language v3 — "quiet instrument panel"** (Mission 17 Flight 2). Neutral surface ladders, hairline borders, one restrained violet accent, exactly two shadows, no decorative effects. The v2 alien-console vocabulary (glows, glass/backdrop-filter, bezels, chamfer clip-paths, shell chrome, btn-v3 gradients, `//` section markers) is retired and ENFORCED-gone: `tests/test_ui_guards.py::test_no_retired_design_vocabulary_in_css` fails on any reintroduction in `static/css` (comments included). Flight 3 closed the last deferred remnant: the selected-tab L-bracket `clip-path: polygon` in `components.css` is gone (rebuilt as a 2px accent bottom-border), and its count-1 `CLIP_PATH_POLYGON_ALLOWANCE` entry was deleted with it — the guard now expects zero retired-vocabulary matches.

### Token roles and ladder semantics

Defined in `static/css/tokens.css` (single source; hex values live there, not here):

- **Surfaces** — a three-step ladder `--bg` → `--surface` → `--surface-elevated` (dark: lifted charcoal; light: white/near-white). Elevation reads as the next ladder step, NOT as a bigger shadow — especially in dark. `--surface-tool` and `--code-block-bg` are role variants on the same ladder (the code well sits one step *below* bg in dark).
- **Borders** — hairlines one step above their surface: `--border` → `--border-hover` → `--border-strong`. Separation between panels is carried by 1px hairlines, never by fills, shadows, or chrome.
- **Text ladder** — `--fg` → `--fg-secondary` → `--fg-tertiary` → `--fg-muted` → `--fg-faint`, plus on-color inks (`--fg-on-accent`, `--fg-on-danger`). Every tier, `--fg-faint` included, clears body AA (4.5:1) on all four ladder surfaces in both themes and is strictly ordered by contrast on each (pinned by `test_fg_ladder_is_strictly_ordered`); `--status-danger-muted` (delete-affordance text) is body-AA gated too (squawk 0206).
- **Accent** — violet (`--accent`), with `--accent-translucent(-hover)` washes. `--accent-api` is badge-tier (chat user-vs-API differentiation), NOT an interactive accent.
- **Deep violet (M25 F1 L3, DD6/DD7)** — solid control fills use the **fill tokens**, not `--accent`: `--accent-fill` (dark `#7f3eef` / light `#6d28d9`), `--accent-fill-hover` (`#6d28d9` / `#5b21b6`), and the ink on them `--fg-on-accent-fill` (`#ffffff`). Rule at every migrated site: the fill, any paired border and the text/tick/thumb on it move together (a `test_migrated_site_consumes_the_gated_tokens` usage pin per site). `--accent` stays for focus rings, links, selection borders, translucent washes, meters (`.context-bar`, `.admin-pf-pull-fill`) and the indeterminate `.tool-switch` thumb; `--fg-on-accent` stays for anything still on `--accent` / `--accent-api`. Dark `accent_fill` on `surface_elevated` is 3.07:1 (squawk 0206 retired the former 2.95:1 band exception); white ink on it is 5.47:1. `accent_fill_hover` vs surfaces is not gated (below 3.0 in dark); no state-bearing control uses it.
- **Control boundaries** — `--control-border` (dark `#6e717a` / light `#868690`, ≥3.0 on bg/surface/elevated) is the unchecked `.checkbox-primary` border and the off track of `.ui-toggle-track` (the library toggle; the legacy `.tool-enable-switch-track` was deleted in M31 F5 L07) and `.tool-switch`; `--control-thumb` (`#ffffff`) is every toggle thumb in both states. The indeterminate `.tool-switch` track stays an accent wash with no boundary (accepted; the gated thumb carries the state). `--fg-faint` is never reused for interactive edges.
- **Status** — `--status-success/warning/danger` + soft `-bg-soft`/`-border` washes. Status text gates at body AA on bg/surface in BOTH themes. LEDs/dots are flat; labels carry the meaning.
- **Badges** — provider and MCP-transport brand colors, theme-stable (the seven tool-category and approval kinds had no product producer and were deleted in M31 F6 L09a, D6). Per-badge ink tokens (`--badge-ink-*`, Flight 3) give each fill a luminance-matched text color; every badge×ink pair clears **body AA in both themes** and is GATED in `tests/test_design_tokens_a11y.py` (`test_badge_ink_passes_body_aa`).
- **Geometry/motion** — spacing/radius/shadow scales (below), the `--list-w`/`--settings-nav-w` column widths (`--sidebar-w`, the chat aside's 240px, was retired in M25 F4 L4a), `--motion-*` durations (collapse to 0 under `prefers-reduced-motion`). The `--panel-radius*` compat aliases were retired in Flight 3: all 35 consumer refs were migrated onto the `--radius-*`/`--space-*` scales and the 4 alias definitions deleted from `tokens.css`.

### Accent restraint rule

Accent appears on **interactive and selected states only** — active nav item, selected tab, focus rings, primary buttons, links. Never as ambient decoration, never on static chrome, never as a glow. The palette carries two accents' worth of identity (violet + badge colors); restraint is what keeps the panel quiet.

### Both-themes-designed contract

Dark and light are each designed on their own merits — neither is a value-flip of the other (dark: charcoal ladder + 400-family statuses; light: zinc ladder + 600/700-family statuses). Any change to one theme block must be designed (and AA-verified) in the other in the same change; a leg never lands with one theme unfinished. The nav rail is ladder-native in both themes (the light theme's dark-navy palette island was retired in v3).

### Color contrast (WCAG AA regression contract)

Token contrast pairs are audited by `tests/test_design_tokens_a11y.py` against:
- **4.5:1** for body text (WCAG 1.4.3)
- **3.0:1** for large text, UI components, non-text contrast (WCAG 1.4.11)

The test file hardcodes token values to mirror `tokens.css`; canonical mirror lives in `tests/browser/design_tokens.py` (`DARK` / `LIGHT` dicts). **Before changing a color token, re-run `pytest tests/test_design_tokens_a11y.py`.** If CSS values change, update `design_tokens.py` in lockstep — the lockstep is ENFORCED by `tests/test_ui_guards.py::test_design_token_mirror_in_sync` (mirror-driven value-sync against the top-level theme blocks of `tokens.css`, hex-normalized). Coverage (v3, gates accreted M17 F2–F6): the full fg ladder × bg/surface/surface-elevated/surface-tool at body AA; accent/accent-api/status-danger-muted at UI AA on the ladder surfaces; on-color pairs (accent/danger fills, code well); status colors at body AA on bg/surface; **per-badge ink × fill** (F3); and the **composited-a11y** family — text/marks on rgba washes reconstructed with `_alpha_composite` over a known base surface (danger/warning soft-fills, the tinted user bubble + translucent assistant/table-zebra surfaces, the indeterminate tool-switch thumb, the resting telemetry ghost) — all in both themes.

**SC2 completeness net** (F7): `test_design_tokens_a11y.py::test_sc2_every_fg_role_token_is_gated_or_exempt` enumerates the fg-role families (`--fg*/--accent*/--status*/--code-block*/--control*` (`--control` added M25 F1 L3)) **from `tokens.css` itself** (via `parse_tokens_css_theme_blocks`, NOT from the mirror) and asserts every token is either GATED (literal-hex, mirrored, and referenced by a contrast assertion) or EXEMPT (rgba wash / structural, documented in `_CONTRAST_EXEMPT` with rationale). This closes the one hole the mirror-sync guard cannot: that guard is strictly mirror→CSS, so a literal-hex fg token added to `tokens.css` but NOT the mirror would slip both nets — the completeness net drives off the CSS source of truth and fails loudly naming any such token.

`tests/test_token_definitions.py` parses every `var(--x)` reference across `static/css/*.css` and asserts each token has a definition. Catches typos like `var(--accnet)` that CSS silently treats as `unset`.

### Deliberate AA exceptions

There are no deliberate sub-AA exceptions (squawk 0206 retired the `--fg-faint` 3.0-4.5 band and the dark `--accent-fill` on `surface-elevated` band). Hairline separators (`--border`, `--border-hover`) are decorative and outside the gate.

**Exception policy**: a sub-AA pair may exist only as a *band-pinned, consumer-scoped, test-annotated* exception (floor AND ceiling asserted, consumer roles named, a `# DELIBERATE EXCEPTION` comment). An exception the suite doesn't pin is a bug, not an exception.

### Focus visibility convention

Pinned form (v3): `outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 2px;` — **no halo, no box-shadow**. Three sanctioned variants (M17 F2 L2a):
- **Inset rings** keep negative outline-offsets where an outset ring would clip against a scroll container (`.list-item`, `.entity-tile`, `.ui-nav-groups-item`, `.tab`; the retired section nav's items and the retired welcome agent options, `.chat-welcome-item`, M25 F4 L4b, had one too).
- **`.btn-stop-turn`** keeps a danger-colored outline (its accent IS danger).
- **`:focus-within` container treatments** degrade to a border-color change (e.g. `.chip-list-editor`), not a ring. The one exception is the chat composer's `.ui-composer-box` (M25 F4 L5, A25), which carries the full pinned ring on `:focus-within` because its textarea has `outline: none` and no border ("Chat composer" above).

Both themes meet the 3:1 non-text contrast threshold against adjacent surfaces.

### Two-shadow elevation system

Exactly two shadows exist: `--shadow-popover` (dropdowns, toasts, transient floats) and `--shadow-modal` (modal dialogs). Everything else expresses elevation via the surface ladder + hairlines. Do not introduce a third shadow; if a component seems to need one, it is either a popover, a modal, or not elevated.

### Spacing and radius scales

`--space-1..8` (4/8/12/16/24/32/48/64 px) and `--radius-sm/md/lg` (6/8/12 px), theme-agnostic in `:root`. Declarations being rewritten adopt the scales; wholesale migration of untouched declarations is deferred work (Flight 3+), not license for ad-hoc pixel values in new CSS.

### Type discipline

Geist, sizes per `fonts.css` (13px UI chrome / 14–15px content). Weights: **400 body, 500–600 emphasis — no 700 in chrome**. Tabular numerals for tables/telemetry. No uppercase-shouting in chrome: labels and section titles are sentence-case.

### Component vocabulary (M17 F3 — one vocabulary, used identically everywhere)

The shared component set lives in `static/css/components.css`; `docs/ui-control-inventory.md` is its catalogue (the dev-mode Design language page was retired in M31 F1 L1) records which controls are library-defined (see "Control library (`App.ui`)" below). All components consume `--space-*`/`--radius-*`/`--motion-*` — no ad-hoc pixels.

- **Buttons** — four variants, class names retained from v2, restyled deep violet (the approved Buttons board's `.deep` rules): PRIMARY `.btn-filled-primary` (`--accent-fill`, white label, hover `--accent-fill-hover`, no opacity hover), SECONDARY `.btn-outlined-primary` ("raised": `--surface-elevated` fill, `--border` hairline, `--fg` ink, hover strengthens only the border to `--border-strong`), GHOST `.btn-ghost` (transparent, `--fg-secondary`, hover `--surface-elevated` + `--fg`), DANGER `.btn-muted-destructive` (transparent, `--status-danger-muted` hairline, `--status-danger` label, hover `--status-danger-bg-soft` fill only). All **32px** tall / `padding: 0 12px` / `--radius-sm` / 13px (`--font-size-body`) medium-weight label / flat fills (no gradients) / pinned focus (2px accent, +2px offset) / disabled `.4`; every variant transitions `background-color`, `border-color` and `opacity`. Size modifiers keep their heights, padding and label sizes and set no font-weight: `.btn-sm` 24 (10px label) / `.btn-lg` 36 / `.btn-xl` 40. **`.btn-icon`** makes a 32×32 square (zero padding, 16px svg), or 24×24 with a **14px** svg when combined with `.btn-sm`; an icon-only button must carry an `aria-label`. Icon svgs inside standard buttons render at 16px. Contextual overrides (`.save-btn`, `.delete-btn`, `.mcp-refresh-tools-btn`) no longer pin padding or label size; the armed `.delete-btn.confirm-delete` / `.admin-pf-model-delete-confirm` solid-danger semibold state is deliberate and stays. Two compact rows are CSS-only documented exceptions (`.tool-actions .btn-outlined-primary`, `.mcp-source-tile-row1 .mcp-refresh-tools-btn`). **One primary per region** — at most one `.btn-filled-primary` in any visual region; every other affordance is outlined/ghost (e.g. the unified header create CTA is `.btn-outlined-primary`, so an empty state showing header + canvas CTA never has two primaries). Computed-style contract: `tests/browser/test_deep_violet_computed_styles.py`.
- **Fields** — label-above stacked `.field-group` is the vocabulary primitive (the legacy label-left `.field-row` 100px gutter was deleted in M31 F6 L09a). The group's label row is the shared `.field-group-label` (Flight 6) — a flex header carrying the `<label>` plus any trailing affordance (info icon, inline action) on one baseline above the control; `renderField` and the studio/admin editors all mount their labels through it, so the label/control gap and alignment are one recipe, not per-editor. **Inline validation** = `.is-invalid` on the group + a `.field-error` message (color **and** text, never color alone); `.field-hint` for help. Pinned focus form. `.field-select` is self-sufficient (full quiet-surface + hairline + pinned-focus treatment on its own class) — it IS the vocabulary dropdown for static single-select.
- **Tiles / cards** — surface + hairline + `--radius-md`; elevation reads as the surface-elevated ladder step, **no shadows at rest**; hover = `--border-hover` + surface-elevated; accent reserved for the selected state.
- **Tables / lists** — 40–44px rows (`.list-item` min-height 40px), hairline separators only, hover = surface shift.
- **Tabs** — selected = 2px accent **bottom-border** keyed off `aria-selected` (no chamfer/clip-path); unselected reserves the 2px with a transparent border so selection never jumps layout. Arrow keys move focus (inset ring), Enter commits selection + panel swap.
- **Modals (DD8, M25 F1; implemented M25 F2 L2)** — every modal is a native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()` through `App.ui.modal` (`static/js/ui/modal.js`); `App.confirmDialog` (`dialog.js`) is a thin adapter over it. The top layer stacks dialogs in open order and makes the rest of the page inert. The wrapper owns: an **explicit opener** (`open(opener)`, never `document.activeElement`: a mouse click does not focus a button in WebKit; the confirm adapter takes an optional `opener`, else `activeElement` when that is not `body`, else `#main-content`), explicit focus on open (the `autofocus` action, else the first action, else the first focusable, else the card) and explicit focus restore to the opener (else `#main-content`); **Escape** on `keydown` on the dialog calling both `preventDefault()` and `stopPropagation()` and closing the topmost layer only (without `preventDefault` a real Escape can still raise a second browser close request, because Chromium's CloseWatcher groups dialogs opened without intervening user activation); `cancel` as a prevented fallback routed through the same path; a `close` listener that **ignores a stale `close` event while `dialog.open` is true** (`close()` queues its event as a task) and, for a genuine close that bypassed the router, runs the full cancel path exactly once; a backdrop click that closes only when `dismissible` and both `mousedown` and `click` targeted the `<dialog>` itself (a drag out of the card never closes); a manual **Tab/Shift+Tab wrap** (native modals let Tab escape to browser chrome; the `<dialog>` and the card are excluded from the focusable list, so focus on either enters at the first/last deterministically in both engines); **Enter is native** (it activates the focused button; the old document-level Enter-to-confirm handler is gone, because a visibly focused Stay must never discard work); the `<dialog>` is created on open and **removed from the DOM** on close; ordering is close, remove, restore focus, then `onClose`, which fires exactly once per open. `::backdrop` uses the `--scrim` token (rgba wash, both themes) with no blur; popups inside a dialog handle Escape on their own root and are never portalled to `body`. The shell does not scroll `body`, so no scroll lock. **No singleton in the factory** (modals stack); `App.confirmDialog` stacks too (M25 F2 L5 removed its single-open policy; it takes an `opener` and keeps an open count; `App.nav` owns de-duplication). The rule is verified by `tests/browser/_dialog_rule_checks.py` against the test-only `_dialog_rule.js` harness (`test_ui_dialog_rule.py`) AND against the real wrapper (`test_ui_modal_dialog_rule.py`, a `DialogAdapter` with `backdrop_token='--scrim'`), in Chromium and WebKit; factory contract tests are `test_ui_modal_contract.py`.
- **Toasts** — `.app-flash`: `--surface` + `1px --border` hairline + `--fg` ink + `--shadow-popover`; the `.error` variant swaps to `--status-danger` ink + danger hairline on the same surface (both inks gated body-AA on `--surface`).
- **Empty states** — one-line statement + exactly one primary action (`.empty-canvas-cta`, an outlined-primary instance sharing the grid's create handler); the inviting canvas copy carries the invitation.
- **Badges** — per-badge ink, body-AA gated both themes (see Token roles → Badges).

### Section titles — eyebrow with a rule

`.section-title` is an **eyebrow with a rule** (M25 F6 L4, flight DD13, operator option D, 2026-10-06): a flex row of sans 10px semibold text, `letter-spacing: 0.08em`, uppercase, `--fg-tertiary`, then a 1px `--border` hairline (`::after`, `flex: 1`) to the container's edge; `gap: 10px; margin: 22px 0 10px`, `:first-child` `margin-top: 0`. **The retired eyebrow is back, with a rule, by operator decision** (an accepted SC8 deviation from the reference spec section 3 group label, which was 11px medium `--fg-muted`). The `//` prefix markers and the mono face stay retired (`test_no_retired_design_vocabulary_in_css`). The same recipe (10px, gap 8px, padding `10px 10px 6px`, the first visible group at 10px and every later one at 16px through a sibling rule, `white-space: nowrap`, a `min-width: 0` rule that shrinks first) styles the `App.ui.listPane` and `navGroups` captions.

**One treatment, any element.** The retired `h2.section-title` size bump is deleted (D6): every `.section-title` wears the one recipe whatever its element, and `h2` keeps only its heading semantics. **Variants:** `.danger-zone-title` keeps `--status-danger` for text and rule (D3); `.section-title-row` (a title plus an info icon) draws the rule on the ROW's `::after`, after the icon, and its `.section-title` suppresses its own (D1); the provider models sub-labels (`.admin-pf-section-label`, `.admin-pf-models-title`) stay quiet (D4). **Opt-out (D5), retired (M31 F3 L09):** `.section-title--plain` (the old quiet label) lost its last user when the Knowledge inspect panels got their own stable `h3` (`kb-panel-title`, L06b), and the rule was deleted as dead CSS. **One rule per boundary (D2):** an `.editor-section + .editor-section` that opens with a `.section-title` or `.section-title-row` loses its `border-top` (a `:has()` rule), because the title's rule marks the boundary; a section with no leading title keeps its divider. Pinned by `tests/browser/test_flow_settings_section_labels.py` (settings lane), `test_list_pane_caption_is_an_eyebrow_with_a_rule` and `test_nav_groups_geometry_and_colour` (contracts lane), and `test_section_label_*` (contrast: `--fg-tertiary` clears 4.5:1 on `--bg`, `--surface` and `--surface-elevated` in both themes).

**Bare links (M25 F6 L6).** `base.css` carries `a:any-link { color: var(--accent); }`, so a link with no class rule (the provider page's "Available models" help-line link, the agent Knowledge tab's empty-state link, chat markdown links) no longer renders in the browser-default blue. The layer order `tokens < base < components < areas < utilities` means any class-level link rule still wins. `--accent` as link text is AA on every surface (lowest measured 5.62:1). A link that needs another colour gets its own class rule; do not remove the UA underline from links in running text (WCAG 1.4.1, links must not rely on colour alone).

### Label-association guard pattern — static + runtime (Flight 5/6 methodology asset)

`<label for="X">` associations are enforced by a TWO-LAYER guard because neither layer alone is sound:

- **Static** (`tests/test_ui_guards.py::test_field_group_label_for_resolves_to_control`, F5) scans the JS sources and asserts every literal `<label for="X">` has a matching control id somewhere in source (WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2). Cheap, no browser — but it reads SOURCE, so it is blind to associations that break only at mount time.
- **Runtime** (`tests/browser/test_flow_chiplist_label_resolver.py`, F6) mounts the chip-list-bearing editors in a real browser and resolves each `label[for]` through the DOM's own `HTMLLabelElement.control` (which returns `null` for a missing OR non-labelable target), asserting it lands on the real `.chip-list-input`.

The runtime layer exists because the string-built `renderChipListEditor` (deleted in M31 F5 L06; the MCP editor's chip lists are now `App.ui.chipList` with `inputId`) mounted via `outerHTML=` (`mountChipListEditors`), which STRIPS the placeholder `<div id="X">`'s id — so a label that looks correctly wired in source dangles at runtime, and the static scan provably cannot catch it. The general lesson: **any label whose control is (re)mounted by an `innerHTML`/`outerHTML` write needs the runtime resolver guard, not just the source scan.** Both guards are load-bearing mission-introduced invariants — keep both green.

### UI test apparatus (M25 F1 L1)

Test infrastructure for the `App.ui` control library (`static/js/ui/`). Nothing here depends on library code existing; every guard passes vacuously until it does.

- **Library `testid` rule (DD2).** Factories take a caller-supplied property named exactly `testid` (own options, a nested option object, or a per-item object). The library never derives an id. `tests/_ui_guard_helpers.py` extracts `testid: 'x'` as a Literal and `testid: 'prefix-' + …` as a Parametric (both feed the registry diff in `docs/ui-testid-registry.md`; `\b` excludes `rowtestid:`). `find_testid_value_violations` (guarded by `test_testid_values_are_registry_visible`) fails any `testid` key whose value the extractor cannot see: outside `ui/` only a single-quoted literal or `'prefix' +` is allowed; inside `ui/` only forwarding (`<expr>.testid`); quoted keys and double-quoted values are rejected everywhere. Since M25 F4 L1 an option named `<lower>Testid` (`App.ui.searchList`'s `inputTestid` and `rowTestid`) is a test-id key too: it is extracted and value-checked exactly like `testid` (forwarding inside `ui/` is `<expr>.<lower>Testid`), so its ids stay registry-visible. Comments are scanned, so write JSDoc examples as `testid: <id>` without quotes. `extract_testids` / `classify_sinks` / `relpath` take the directory scanned (probe tests use `tmp_path`).
- **No HTML-string sinks in `ui/` (DD4).** `find_ui_dir_sink_tokens` scans every `static/js/ui/*.js` (comments included) for the three sink tokens; once `ui/` exists it must hold at least one file. Write "inner-HTML" in prose.
- **Shared resolver (DD3).** `tests/browser/_a11y_resolve.js` is the only copy of the label/description rules (name sources, `aria-describedby`/`aria-labelledby` resolution, `aria-controls` exempt when `aria-expanded="false"`); a root selector matching nothing FAILS. Apply it with `browser._a11y_resolvers.assert_controls_labelled_and_described(page, root_selector)` (or `collect_label_failures`, which never raises). The WebKitGTK probe evaluates the same file.
- **Engine fixtures.** `tests/browser/_ui_engines.py` provides `engine_browser` / `engine_page` (params `chromium`, `webkit`; registered via `conftest.py`; every test using them carries `@pytest.mark.browser`). Never reuse pytest-playwright's fixture names. `load_harness(page, theme)` loads tokens, CSS, `icons.js` and `static/js/ui/*.js` from `index.html` order into a blank page (no app spawn) under a themed `#harness-root`. Each webkit run prints and records the launched engine version.
- **Skip/require policy.** `webkit_policy(env, launch_error)` (pure; unit-tested in `tests/test_ui_engines_policy.py`): a WebKit launch failure skips the webkit param, or fails when `THE_ONE_REQUIRE_WEBKIT=1` (set in `ci/tasks/browser.yml` and the break-glass `test.yml`). `THE_ONE_WEBKIT_FORCE_UNAVAILABLE=1` is a test-only seam to exercise the skip path.
- **Userland WebKit (no sudo).** `.venv/bin/python -m playwright install webkit` downloads the browser; if host libraries are missing, `bash scripts/setup-webkit-userland.sh` unpacks them into `${THE_ONE_WEBKIT_DEPS:-~/.cache/the-one-webkit-deps}` and writes `webkit-wrapper.sh`. The fixture then launches WebKit through that wrapper (`executable_path`) with `GST_PLUGIN_PATH` in the launch env; the wrapper is needed because Playwright's MiniBrowser script overwrites `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. Chromium is never affected.
- **WebKitGTK probe (real desktop engine).** `/usr/bin/python3 scripts/webkitgtk_probe.py --url 'http://127.0.0.1:8486/#route' --data-dir <scratch> --root-selector <css> --theme dark|light` runs the resolver in system WebKitGTK (system python only: it has `gi`; the venv does not) and prints JSON (`engine_version`, pinned `env`, `theme`, `results`, `ok`). **Production safety:** it refuses port 8484 (production), port 8485 (the Flight Director's preview instance, since M25 F4 L7) and the platformdirs default data dir; always target a dev instance on 8486 or higher (`THE_ONE_PORT=8486 THE_ONE_DATA_DIR=<scratch> THE_ONE_DEV_MODE=true make up`, then `make down` with the same env). **Real keys (M25 F4 L7, DD9):** `--press-keys COMBO` (repeatable, e.g. `ctrl+k`; modifiers `ctrl`, `shift`, `alt`, `super`, `meta`; a bad combo exits 2 before any GTK) sends a GTK key event to the focused WebView after the setup scripts, `--focus-selector CSS` focuses a page element first, and `--assert-selector CSS` is polled for `--assert-timeout` seconds (default 5); the resolver runs over `--root-selector` only when it matched, and a miss fails the run. `--key-method event` (default) is a `Gdk.EventKey` press/release pair (the keymap's hardware keycode, the seat's keyboard) through `WebView.event`; `gtk-test` (`Gtk.test_widget_send_key`, an X `XSendEvent`) never reached the page under WSLg's XWayland. The JSON's `keys` block reports the method, each combo's `sent`, `webview_has_focus`, `assert_found` and `key_verdict`: `"partially verified (GTK-level key)"` (a GTK-level synthetic key proves less than an OS-level one) or `"unverified"`.

- **WSL2 mirrored networking (`THE_ONE_TEST_NETNS=1`).** On WSL2 hosts with mirrored networking, Windows' rotating temporary IPv6 addresses make Chromium's network-change notifier abort in-flight requests, even to 127.0.0.1, so browser lanes fail 1-4 random tests with `net::ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED`. Set `THE_ONE_TEST_NETNS=1` and `scripts/run-tests.sh` runs pytest inside an isolated loopback-only network namespace (`unshare -rn`, `lo` brought up; process-group kill and env passthrough unchanged). It fails loudly (exit 2) if `unshare -rn` or `ip` is unavailable rather than falling back. CI (containers) is unaffected.

#### Windows test copy (M25 F5 L1a)

`scripts/windows_test_copy.py` builds, freezes, launches, observes and stops a **test copy** of the working tree on the operator's real Windows desktop (their installed `the-one-app.exe` runs on 8484 in the same session; DD10 of Flight 5). Run from WSL: `.venv/bin/python scripts/windows_test_copy.py <command>`. This half (1a) sends no keyboard or mouse input and paints nothing; Flight 5 leg 1b adds input and captures. The file is importable on Linux (every `ctypes.windll`/`winreg` use is lazy); its pure core is covered by `tests/test_windows_test_copy.py` in `make test-unit`.

- **Two halves, one file.** The WSL CLI validates and copies through `/mnt/c`; the Windows half is this file's own source piped on stdin to `C:\Python311\python.exe -`, with a one-line `_WIN_REQUEST = ...` prefix (JSON in, a `@@RESULT@@` JSON line out). Standard library only, so it works before the venv exists and nothing is ever installed into `C:\Python311`.
- **Commands.** `doctor-host` (session record: build, WebView2 version, DPI, themes, `WEBVIEW2_*`/`THE_ONE_*` at user/machine level, networking mode, locked-session test, excluded port ranges, `LongPathsEnabled`), `snapshot --out F` / `--compare F`, `setup`, `freeze`, `doctor [--frozen] --data-dir D`, `launch --port P --data-dir D [--cdp-port C] [--frozen | --script S] [--max-minutes M] [--tree-id T]`, `tree`, `windows`, `rect`, `stop [--tree-id T]`. Exit codes: 2 refusal (message names the rule), 3 hands-off timeout, 4 failed snapshot compare.
- **Scratch root** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\the-one-f5\` (sentinel `.the-one-f5-root`; every write checks the sentinel and that the RESOLVED target is inside the RESOLVED root): `src\` (copy of `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard --deduplicate`, manifest-based sync that never deletes `dist\`, `build\`, `*.egg-info`, `__pycache__`), `venv\` (the CI closure verbatim: `uv export --no-dev --no-emit-project --extra gui --extra all-llms`, `pip install -r --no-deps`, `pip install -e . --no-deps`; skipped while the `uv.lock`+`pyproject.toml` stamp matches), `src\dist\the-one\` (the frozen build, `pyinstaller` into the venv, never Inno), `pids\`, `audit.log`, `logs\`, `spike\`, `data-*\`, `cache\{pip,uv,pyinstaller}`. Run `setup` and `freeze` in the background (`run_in_background`); each logs to `logs\`.
- **Refusals.** Ports: 8484, 8485, below 8490 (app) / 9390 (CDP), a Hyper-V excluded range, anything listening (Windows `Get-NetTCPConnection` is authoritative, WSL `ss` a supplement). Paths: normalised lexically (`/mnt/c`, `\\?\`, UNC, `..`, trailing dots/spaces, relative, mixed case) AND resolved (junctions, 8.3 names); a data dir equal to, inside or containing the operator's data dir, or outside the root, is refused; the root must be under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp` with a leaf `the-one-f5*`. `.env`: a frozen `launch`/`doctor` is refused while the operator's data dir holds one (a frozen build reads it whatever `THE_ONE_DATA_DIR` says). Hands-off: `launch` and a frozen `doctor` wait up to 60s while `GetLastInputInfo` is within 5s and newer than `R\last-injection` (1b writes it; missing = no injection; tick wraparound handled), then exit 3. The child environment drops every inherited `THE_ONE_*`/`WEBVIEW2_*`; the CDP variable is set only with `--cdp-port`, only in the child's environment. A unit test greps the helper source for environment/registry writes and by-name kills.
- **Process identity.** Records `(pid, creation time, path)` in `R\pids\<tree-id>.json`, from ONE source (ctypes: Toolhelp parents, `OpenProcess` identity, `NtQueryInformationProcess` command lines). `tree` adds live descendants (a child counts only if created at or after its parent) and processes whose command line has `--user-data-dir=` inside the tree's data dir (WebView2 after its parent died). `stop` opens each process ONCE (`PROCESS_TERMINATE|QUERY_LIMITED`), re-reads creation time and path on that handle, compares with the record, requires the path under the root / `C:\Python311` / the WebView2 runtime, then `TerminateProcess` on the same handle; never `taskkill`, never by name; interpreter first, watchdog last; waits until every member exited and both ports are free; each kill is appended to `audit.log`. The watchdog (`pids\watchdog-<id>.py`, venv `pythonw.exe`, recorded in the tree) runs the same stop after `--max-minutes`.
- **Snapshot.** The installed app (owner of the 8484 listener whose path is outside the root and any recorded tree), the operator data dir's `api-credential`/`.env`/`window-chrome.json`/`window-state.json` (existence, mtime, sha256), its top-level entry names, and the user/machine `WEBVIEW2_*`/`THE_ONE_*` variables. Compare allows (reports) a `window-state.json` change and a new installed-app PID; anything else, a new top-level entry or an environment change exits 4. Known limit: registry writes by WebView2 or the app (e.g. `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Edge\BLBeacon`) are not covered. Bracket EVERY Windows session with `snapshot --out` / `--compare`.
- **Spike scripts** go through `launch --script S` (file under `R\spike\`, run by the venv `pythonw.exe`, same refusals/records/watchdog); `--port` is then the spike's own HTTP port and its pywebview `storage_path` must sit under its data dir.
- **Findings (leg 1a, Windows 11 26200, WebView2 154, 96 DPI).** (5) `window.chrome.webview` exists at document start in both windows of a two-window pywebview app; `window.pywebview` does NOT exist at `DOMContentLoaded`/`load` and `pywebviewready` fires afterwards (main window about 20ms after load), so a page must wait for the event or poll. (6) At 100% scaling the outer rect of an 800x600 window is 800x600, the client rect 784x561 and `innerWidth`x`innerHeight` equal the client rect exactly (caption 31px, 8px side and bottom frame; `DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS` is 786x593). (8) `BrowserForm.Handle` reads fine off the UI thread (`InvokeRequired` is True, no exception), the HWND exists while `hidden=True` and does NOT change across `show()`, `hide()`, `show()`. (10) `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=C` set in the child environment works on the real copy (source and frozen) even though pywebview 5.4 with `storage_path` ignores its own setting; Playwright `connect_over_cdp("http://127.0.0.1:C")` from WSL reaches it over mirrored networking. (12) The local freeze takes about 1.5 minutes (PyInstaller 91s, `pip install pyinstaller` 22s); a from-scratch `setup` is about 5.5 minutes (copy 130s over `/mnt/c`, closure 147s), a re-run about 8s. A hard kill leaves a ghost tray icon until hovered.

#### Windows test copy, input and capture (M25 F5 L1b)

Leg 1b adds the input and capture half of `scripts/windows_test_copy.py` (flight DD10, "Input safety on a live desktop"). The operator's installed `the-one-app.exe` shares the test copy's window title, so every command below carries a gate.

- **Commands.** `own-window show|close|do`, `front`, `front-trial`, `capture`, `pixel`, `keys`, `release-all`, `click`, `drag`, `show-window`, `focus-save`, `focus-restore`, `resize` (M25 F5 L4), plus the read-only `foreground`, `screen`, `hands-off`, `input-log`. Every command takes the global `--run-id` (a tag for that session's rows in `R\input.log`). Refusals exit 2 and send nothing; the hands-off wait exits 3; a mid-gesture abort exits 5; a locked session exits 6.
- **The gate tree.** One tree, chosen by the pure `select_gate_pids(trees, target_id)` (`--tree-id`, else the single recorded tree; several without a name are refused). The own windows are never a tree: `own` and `own-b` are reserved ids that `launch` refuses and the gate skips. `click` alone runs with no tree (only an own window can then pass its gate).
- **The own windows (`own-window`).** A and B are small stdlib windows (200x100, class `TheOneF5Own{a,b}`, title `the-one-f5 helper`, A light blue, B light orange) run by `C:\Python311\pythonw.exe` (no venv launcher, so the window's owner PID is the recorded PID) and recorded in `R\own.json` / `R\own-b.json` (pid, creation time, path, HWND). They are shown `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` with `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`, so showing one never takes the foreground (verified), though a new one lands above the foreground window in z-order. A gate accepts an own window only after an identity check (the process identity AND `GetWindowThreadProcessId(hwnd)` match the record), only as a click target, never a key target and never a drag end. Default place: the bottom-left of the work area (`place_own_window`); lifetime `--seconds` (60 default, 600 max, `clamp_own_seconds`); `--topmost` at most 60s. A click on an own window runs `SetForegroundWindow` on itself in its own `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` (the process just received input), which is how it becomes the "other app in front". `own-window do` writes a command file (`R\own\cmd.json`, `cmd-b.json` for B) with an action from the allowlist (`front-tree`, `restore-focus`), a nonce and a 5s expiry; the own process validates it (`validate_command`: allowlist, nonce never seen, not expired), re-checks hands-off, the gate identity and (for `restore-focus`) the focus decision table, then writes `R\own\result-<nonce>.json`. `own-window close` verifies identity, posts `WM_CLOSE` (hands-off first when the window is in front) and falls back to the identity-checked kill (`audit.log`). `stop` with no tree id also closes both.
- **Hands-off.** `op_hands_off` runs before every gesture and every focus change (`keys`, `click`, `drag`, `front`, `own-window show`/`do`, `show-window`, `focus-restore`, `launch`, `stop` and `own-window close` when the window they close is in front). Order immediately before each `SendInput`: hands-off, then the foreground or pointer gate, then the send. `record_injection(send, read_tick, write)` sends one batch, THEN reads `GetTickCount`, THEN writes `R\last-injection`, so the helper's own input never reads as the operator's; a multi-batch gesture checks `injection_interference` (operator input newer than the helper's own last tick by more than 16ms, wrap-safe) before every later batch and aborts with everything released. `SetCursorPos` alone does not register as input on this host (`restore_moved_last_input` is `false`); the helper still records a tick after a cursor restore.
- **Keys.** `parse_keys` grammar: `^` Ctrl, `+` Shift, `%` Alt (each applies to the next key), `{NAME}` named keys (`NAMED_KEYS`: Enter, Tab, Esc, Backspace, Delete, Insert, Home, End, PgUp, PgDn, arrows, Space, F1-F12), plain text, and `{^}` `{+}` `{%}` `{{}` `{}}` for those characters typed literally. It REFUSES (exit 2, logged `refused`, nothing sent): any Win-key token, Alt+Tab, Alt+Esc, Ctrl+Esc, Ctrl+Shift+Esc, Alt+F4 and every Alt+F-key, Ctrl+Alt+anything (and any character that needs AltGr on the layout), PrintScreen, media/volume/browser/launch keys, and malformed or unknown specs. Keys go out as virtual keys from `VkKeyScanW` on the current layout (with scan codes). Before EACH key-down `GetForegroundWindow()` (resolved with `GetAncestor(GA_ROOT)`) must belong to the gate tree: an own window or anything else in front is a refusal (nothing sent) or, mid-gesture, an abort. Every pressed key is released in a `finally`; `release-all` releases every modifier and mouse button that reads as held (`GetAsyncKeyState`), and a helper call that finds `input.log` ending in a `begin` row whose helper process is gone runs it first (a `begin` row records the helper's identity, so a gesture that is still running in another process is never released from under it).
- **Pointer.** `click X Y [--double]`: hands-off, then `WindowFromPoint` plus `GA_ROOT` must be the gate tree or an identity-checked own window BEFORE any cursor motion (a refused click never moves the cursor), then `SetCursorPos`, then `GetCursorPos` must equal the target and the same check passes again, then a coordinate-less `SendInput` button event (no `MOUSEEVENTF_ABSOLUTE`); the button is released in a `finally`. `cursor_restore_decision(saved, left_at, now_at)` puts the cursor back to its saved position only when it is still where the helper left it (output carries exact `before`/`target`/`after` and the decision). `drag X1 Y1 X2 Y2 [--caption] [--steps N]`: `--caption` plans with `plan_caption_drag` (the start inside the main window's `caption_zone`: DWM frame bounds, top 31px, left of the 138px of caption buttons, scaled by DPI; the post-move frame bounds inside the monitor's work area; the end no nearer than 12px to its edge, so no snap fires), moves with `SetCursorPos` steps while the button is held (this moves a real window on this host), then re-checks the frame against the plan (within 4px, else `moved-unexpectedly`); the default (content or resize) mode plans with `plan_content_drag` (start and end inside the window's CURRENT frame bounds) and the end point's `WindowFromPoint` must be the gate tree itself (an own window does not count, so an end covered by own B is refused). Both modes re-check `GetCursorPos` against the planned point at every step (and, in default mode, `WindowFromPoint`) and abort with the button released if either fails. Snap-to-edge is not exercised: it stays with the operator at Flight 6.
- **Captures.** stdlib GDI only: `BitBlt` (with `CAPTUREBLT`) of the screen into a DIB, a stdlib PNG writer, patch statistics in pure Python; files go under `R\evidence\`. Containment: `--rect` must lie inside the DWM EXTENDED FRAME BOUNDS (attribute 9, never `GetWindowRect`, whose invisible 8px margins show the operator's content) of a visible window of the gate tree (the main window, the splash, a dialog) or of an own window. Occlusion: an exact z-order test (`z_above`, `occluders`: earlier in `EnumWindows` order than the target; skipped when invisible, cloaked, zero-size, `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` or layered at zero alpha) before AND after each grab; a covering window refuses a single capture (`covered_by_own`, `covered_by_tree`, `covered_by_other`) and taints a series frame (its pixels are discarded). A target that is itself layered below full alpha (the hidden-creation flash at opacity 0) is refused too, since it shows what is behind it. `--allow-own-cover` (a diagnostic) takes the capture when every covering window is an own window and says the pixels are the helper's. `--print-window` (`PrintWindow` with `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT`) is exempt from occlusion and says so. `--series N --interval-ms M`: at most 200 frames, interval at least 16ms, at most 256 MB of raw frames (`check_series`), timestamps from `time.perf_counter` (QueryPerformanceCounter, system-wide), frames held raw and encoded after; each frame re-resolves the window (`no-window`, `outside`, `tainted:<reason>`, or `ok` with the rect's mean RGB). `pixel --x --y [--size 9]` reports the patch mean and its darkest and lightest pixel by relative luminance. A locked session (`OpenInputDesktop` null) refuses captures (exit 6).
- **Focus.** `focus-save` writes `R\focus.json` (foreground hwnd, owner pid, process creation time, path, class; no title): the first save of a session wins, a save is refused while a fresh record exists or while the foreground is the gate tree or an own window, a stale record (identity gone or older than 2h) is replaced, `--discard` deletes it (`focus_save_decision`). `focus-restore` acts only when the foreground is the gate tree, an own window or nothing (`focus_restore_decision`: `restore`, `already-foreground`, `operator-moved-on`, `gone`), applies the decided technique, reads `GetForegroundWindow()` about 300ms later and reports `restored` or `failed`, then deletes the record.
