path only, before the area's callback; `tests/test_store_static.py::INVALIDATION_ROWS` pins one
window per row):

| Mutation | Site | Collections |
|---|---|---|
| agent create / rename / save (PUT) / delete | `studio/agent-editor-controller.js` | agents |
| self-configuration enable/disable, approval-mode, revision approve/dismiss/restore | `agent-data.js::_refreshAgentAfterSelfConfigurationChange` (single funnel) | agents |
| knowledge base create | `knowledge-create.js` | knowledge-bases |
| knowledge base rename / delete | `knowledge-detail.js` | knowledge-bases, agents |
| MCP source create / save (two `App.api.fetchJson` sites, M31 F5 L10) | `mcp-server-editor.js` (each before its `onSaveSuccess`, whose `sync` names the new item) | mcp-sources |
| MCP source delete | `mcp-server-editor.js` | mcp-sources, agents |
| MCP source connection delete (Reset connection and Forget tools list; both patch in place, M31 F5 L08b) | `mcp-connection-status.js::runDelete` | mcp-sources, agents |
| MCP Refresh tools / test-connect success (writes `config._cache`; also the auto-run after create and Phase 2 after sign-in) | `mcp-connection-status.js` (the Test flow's `.then`, every outcome) | mcp-sources |
| the agent editor's MCP tools load (`POST …/test/connect`, also writes `config._cache`) | `agent-data.js::fetchMcpSourceTools` | mcp-sources |
| connector create / save / delete | `connector-editor.js` | connectors |
| provider create / save / delete | `provider-editor.js` | providers |
| provider OAuth sign-in (code exchange) / sign-out | `provider-editor.js` | providers |
| agent rename (re-points `chat_sessions.agent_name`) | `studio/agent-editor-controller.js` (the rename `.then`, beside `invalidate('agents')`) | sessions (M25 F4 L1) |
| agent Purge now (`DELETE /api/agents/{name}/chat/sessions`) | `studio/agent-editor-controller.js::handlePurgeHistory` (success `.then`, after the `deleted` count) | sessions (M25 F4 L1) |
| a `start_session` join (the first send creates a session) | `chat/socket.js` `session_joined` handler: its unconditional `refreshSessionList()`, whose `session_list` reply PRIMES the collection (no `invalidate`) | sessions (M25 F4 L3b; `test_store_static.py::test_start_session_join_refreshes_the_list_that_primes_sessions`) |

**MCP editor controller and teardown (M31 F5 L03).** `App.studio.renderMcpSourceEditor` returns `{destroy, setBusy, setError, runTest}` and reports it through `callbacks.onMounted` on every mount (Reset and Forget no longer re-mount, M31 F5 L08b). `mcp-editor-lifecycle.js::createMount()` is the per-mount context: `alive()`, `ifAlive(fn)`, an idempotent `destroy()` (never `App.focusAfter.cancel()`, aborts no request), one consent session slot (`setSession` cancels the previous and notifies the `onSessionChange` listeners: Reset and Forget, and the controller's one `syncTestHold`; since M31 F5 L10 the lifecycle never writes a button) and the per-mount `handles` the controller keeps its module handles on. A destroyed mount skips its DOM writes and mount-bound callbacks but still runs `App.store.invalidate(...)` and `onListStale()` for a server change that already happened (a late Delete calls `onListStale`, not `onDeleteSuccess`). `mcp-glue.js` holds the live controller (`App.areas.find(a => a.id === 'studio')._currentMcpEditor()`) and destroys it before every remount, in `_closeMcpForm`, `reset()` and `_resetMcp` (called by `studio.js` `deactivate`). A cancelled consent session stops its poll but does not close the popup.

**MCP editor controller on the library (M31 F5 L10, DD4/DD7/DD9).** `mcp-server-editor.js` builds the editor from `App.ui.editorShell` (breadcrumb "Library / MCP servers", title "New MCP server" or the name, falling back to "MCP server"; subtitle "{type label} · {command or URL}" from `mcpTypes`, testid `mcp-editor-subtitle`, the raw type for a legacy row; `errorRole: 'alert'`; Test only when the MOUNT-TIME source is stdio or streamable-HTTP), `App.ui.section` and `App.ui.dangerZone`, with no HTML string. Sections in B1's order: Connection (fields, then the status line), Environment variables or Headers, Sandbox, Server, Tools, Danger zone. **Save model.** Save and Create use `App.api.fetchJson` and are busy (`shell.setBusy`) until the response (a create stays busy until the glue's remount; the glue's `setBusy(false)` un-busies a failed remount). `current` is the last saved row: an edit Save replaces it from the response BEFORE `envHeaders.remaskAfterSave`, so `getSource()` readers, Cancel, the server info and the status line see the saved row. The fields' dirty baseline is `JSON.stringify({connection: baselineFrom(current), sandbox: baselineFrom(current)})` where `connectionFields.baselineFrom` and `sandboxFields.baselineFrom` are pure and go through the controls' own normalisation (chip de-duplication, `deriveStdioRuntimeSelection` with its `uvx` default, `null` = create defaults, one shared key-order `shape()`); a stored type must be among the mount-time Type options. The fields stay editable during a Save: if `values()` still equals what the click sent, the saved row is written back with `setValues` (which normalises), otherwise the DOM is left alone and the edited field stays dirty. `collectFormData` validates on the handles and writes nothing: it returns `{payload}` or `{error, fields}` (A2 wording: "Enter a name.", "Choose how it connects.", "Choose or enter a command." (on `runtime`, or on `command` for Custom), "Enter the server's URL."); Save puts each on its field (`connectionFields.setError`, cleared when that field changes or on the next Save) and focuses the first, the silent Test path shows `error` in its result area. A failed request goes through `App.api.fieldErrors` (a `loc` to its field, the rest to the editor error). **One Test-hold writer.** `syncTestHold()` is the only code that sets Test's state: `shell.setTestState(hold ? {disabled: true, label: session ? 'Waiting for sign-in…' : null} : null)` with `hold = connStatus.isTesting() || mount.sessionActive()`, called at Test start and `settle` (through `connectionStatus`'s `onTestHoldChange`) and from the one `mount.onSessionChange` registration; the shell ORs busy into it, so a keystroke cannot re-enable a running Test. A plain Test keeps the label "Test". Delete is a `dangerZone` action ("Delete MCP server", "Confirm delete?"); a failed Delete shows in the editor error, reverts the button and returns focus to it (row 29); a Delete that lands after `destroy` invalidates and calls `onListStale` only. Copy is sentence case ("MCP server saved", "MCP server created", "MCP server deleted").

**MCP editor, final contract (M31 F5 L12).** *Modules* (all under `js/areas/studio/`, each `App.studio.mcpEditor.<name>` unless noted; none has an HTML-string sink or an ownership row; every file is at most 510 lines): `mcp-server-editor.js` (`App.studio.renderMcpSourceEditor(container, source, mcpTypes, callbacks)` -> `{destroy, setBusy, setError, runTest}`, the controller); `mcp-editor-lifecycle.js` (`createMount()`: `alive`, `ifAlive`, `destroy`, the consent session slot, `onSessionChange`, `handles`); `mcp-editor-shared.js` (a stateless namespace: `MASKED_SENTINEL`, `hasOAuthState`, the stdio runtime table and its helpers, `uniqueStrings`); `mcp-connection-fields.js` (`create({source, mcpTypes, onChange, onTypeChange})` -> `{el, values, baselineFrom, setValues, setError, clearErrors, focus, setType, destroy}`); `mcp-sandbox-fields.js` (`create({source, onChange})` -> `{el, values, baselineFrom, setValues, setType, destroy}`); `mcp-env-headers.js` (`create({source, type, onChange})`, above); `mcp-connection-status.js` (`create({container, mount, callbacks, getSource, testBtn, serverInfo, setError, titleEl, onTestHoldChange})` -> `{el, render, runTest, showInvalid, isTesting}`); `mcp-oauth-consent.js` (`launchConsentPopup({consentUrl, statusEl, sourceId, settle, rerun, mount})` -> `{cancel, isDone}`); `mcp-server-info.js` (`create({cache})` -> `{serverEl, toolsEl, update, setTools, destroy}`); `mcp-glue.js` (`App.studio.mcpGlue.create(ctx)`: the section dispatch, the mount with its callbacks, the create-to-edit remount, the pending-id rule). *Save model.* The controller's dirty state is `JSON.stringify` of the two `baselineFrom(current)` shapes compared with the live `values()` plus `envHeaders.isDirty()`; `current` is replaced from an edit Save's response before the re-mask; `syncTestHold()` is the only writer of Test's disabled state and label; **`setSaving(b)` is the one writer of the Save-in-flight state** (the `saving` flag, `shell.setBusy` for Save, Cancel and Test, and the danger-zone Delete's `disabled`), so Delete cannot be armed and confirmed while a Save is in flight and `performDelete` returns while `saving`; the glue's `setBusy(false)` goes through it. A create path that throws in `callbacks.onSaveSuccess` is logged, not shown as a failed save. *Side-effect inventory, final state.* Create then Save: the glue remounts (create -> edit), then auto-runs Test for streamable-HTTP only (no popup). Edit then Save: invalidate `mcp-sources`, flash "MCP server saved", the baseline re-taken from `current`, the title and subtitle updated, focus returned by `App.focusAfter`. Cancel (edit): `setValues`/`revert` to `current`, no remount; (create): discard and a bypass go to the Library with focus on the group. Delete: armed `dangerZone` action, DELETE, invalidate `mcp-sources` + `agents`, flash, `onDeleteSuccess`; an error goes to the editor error and the button reverts. Test: the header button, the in-flight form values sent, then a clean record on `_cache.last_check` (DD6), `onListStale`, an identity-guarded refetch. Sign-in: consent block (`App.ui.consentLink`), a poll up to 2 minutes, a re-run on success. Reset and Forget patch in place (no remount). Transport switch: `setType` on the field modules, rows kept (env becomes headers). *Stable DOM contract:* every `mf-*` id and `data-field`; the testids `save-mcp-source-btn`, `cancel-mcp-source-btn`, `delete-mcp-source-btn`, `editor-unsaved`, `mcp-editor-subtitle`, `mcp-test-connection`, `mcp-test-status`, `mcp-connection-status`, `reset-mcp-connection-btn`, `mcp-command-runtime`, `mcp-command-custom`, `mcp-command-hint`, `mcp-sandbox-section`, `mcp-env-headers-section`, `mcp-kv-table`, `kv-add-row-btn`, `mcp-server-info-section`, `mcp-tools-section`, `mcp-tools-list`, `mcp-server-instructions`, `mcp-server-meta` (the Registry holds the full rows; `mcp-connection-led` is decorative, and `mcp-tools-last-updated` and `mcp-kv-tbody` are retired). *Invalidation:* see the store table (create/save and delete rows). *The masked column:* the Value column is a `secret` column of `keyValueTable` in stored mode (no eye, hint "Stored. Type a new value to replace it."); a stored value is never in the DOM. Squawk candidates carried to the debrief: `setSaving` writes `danger.button.disabled` directly where `dangerZone.setDisabled(value, index)` is the idiomatic route; a Delete armed before a Save starts comes back still armed after the Save.

**MCP editor connection status (M31 F5 L08b).** `mcp-connection-status.js::create` builds the tail of the Connection section once per mount: an `App.ui.status` line (`mcp-connection-status`) that reads `App.usage.mcpState(getSource())` (the Library list's own derivation; the words come only from `App.usage.mcpStateLabel`, the clock from `App.time.formatClock`), Reset connection (only when `oauth_active`) and Forget tools list (a cached streamable-HTTP server with no sign-in), and the Test result area (`mcp-test-status`, hidden at rest and after a success). Test is an `App.ui.button` in the header (edit mode only; leg 10 moves it to `editorShell` `actions.test`). After EVERY Test outcome, the sign-in poll, Reset and Forget, an identity-guarded refetch copies `_cache`, `oauth_active` and `has_access_token` into the controller's current source (via `getSource()`) and re-renders; a clean Test sets the line optimistically first, a Test of unsaved values records nothing and labels its result. A dirty form that cannot be sent (no URL, say) shows its message in the result area and sends no request (`collectFormData(..., {silent: true})`). Reset and Forget buttons are inserted once and only toggle `hidden` (squawk 0088), and are disabled while a consent session runs. `App.mcpErrorMessage(raw, {transport})` picks the stdio or HTTP wording of the generic connection failure. `GET /api/oauth/callback`'s page is self-contained (squawk 0214): inline styles, a pinned token subset, no `/static/` reference.

**MCP environment variables and headers (M31 F5 L07, DD4/DD5).** `mcp-env-headers.js` is `App.studio.mcpEditor.envHeaders.create({source, type, onChange})` returning `{el, values, isDirty, revert, setType, partition, remaskAfterSave, destroy}`. `el` is an `App.ui.section` (testid `mcp-env-headers-section`, "Environment variables" or "Headers") holding one `App.ui.keyValueTable` (`mcp-kv-table`, `kv-add-row-btn`) with a Key column, a secret Value column (`maskedBy: 'sensitive'`, `storedKey: '_originalMasked'`, `clearOnUnmask`) and a Sensitive switch column whose header is exactly "Sensitive" (the info button is a toggletip beside it). Every row carries a module-assigned `_rid`; the module keeps its own baseline rows, so `isDirty()` (by `_rid`, on key, value and sensitive) and `revert()` (Cancel) do not read the controller's stale `source`. The controller's dirty state is its fields snapshot OR `envHeaders.isDirty()` in `checkDirty`, `recaptureSnapshot` and the Save success path. **`partition(plain, secrets)` is the payload contract** (the server contract is unchanged): a blank key is skipped; a sensitive row whose value still equals its stored mask sends `__MASKED__`, never the mask shape; any other sensitive row sends its value (`''` when empty); a plain row goes to `plain`; the last row wins for a duplicate key. It returns the per-row record the controller stashes at the Save click (`handles.lastPartition`). A renamed stored key clears its value (the table's rename rule), so the old key is omitted (the server deletes it) and the new key is stored with whatever the operator typed (defect 15). `remaskAfterSave(submitted, response)` (defect 4) puts an unchanged, sent, sensitive row into stored mode with the server's mask, or out of it when the server dropped the key, then takes the post-Save baseline from the live rows except rows edited, removed or added during the request, so an edit made while the PUT was in flight stays dirty. Known limitation (the server's contract, a squawk candidate): a secret typed as `***` or `***...abcd` matches the server's `_looks_masked`, so a PUT keeps or drops it, and the Test override path only recognises the literal `__MASKED__`. No attribute holds a secret (a test scans the editor subtree). The `.mcp-kv-*` rules, the `.kv-remove-row-btn` rule and the legacy `.tool-enable-switch*` rules are deleted; the contrast pins moved to the `App.ui.toggle` rules.

**`sessions` and the WebSocket (M25 F4 L1, DD4).** The chat area's `session_list` handler calls
`App.store.prime('sessions', rows)` on every reply (`test_store_static.py::test_chat_session_list_primes_sessions_in_the_summary_shape`),
so no other client site needs an invalidation row: the client's only per-session delete is
the WebSocket `delete_session`, which comes back as a `session_list` and so a prime; agent
delete leaves sessions in place (`DELETE /api/agents/{name}` does not touch them). A
`start_session` join refreshes the list too (the row above, M25 F4 L3b). `LITERAL_LIST` covers `/api/chat/sessions`,
so no client file fetches the list literally.

**Deliberately not invalidating** (the Library list shows a name, a count of agents and an MCP
tool count, none of which these change): knowledge-base folder add, item delete and retry;
connector pairing-code POST and connector test. Self-configuration surface MCP rows never appear
in the plain `mcp-sources` list, so the self-configuration funnel invalidates `agents` only. The
knowledge-base rename path still writes Studio's section-local array in place
(`onSaveSuccess`); with store copies that is harmless, and the invalidation is what refreshes
the store.

**`App.usage` ("used by" counts, M25 F3 L2, DD9).** A section of `store.js` (no new file), pure
functions with no fetching and no DOM. `agents` is the list-shaped agents array
(`App.store.peek('agents').value` or a `get` result; a missing or non-array value counts as no
agents); counts come from data, never from the rendered page (A23):
- `providerAgents(agents, providerId)`: agents with `provider_config_id === providerId`;
- `kbAgents(agents, kbId)`: agents whose `kb_ids` contain it;
- `mcpAgents(agents, sourceId)`: agents whose `mcp_source_ids` contain it;
- `connectorAgents(connector)`: the DISTINCT agent names in `config.default_agent` plus every
  `config.channel_map` value (inbound routing reads `channel_map` first, then the default),
  first-seen order, skipping empty and non-string values; its `length` is the count, and `[]`
  when neither is present;
- `mcpToolCount(source)`: `config._cache.tools.length`, or `null` when the tools were never
  loaded (not zero). The Library MCP rows read `mcpState` instead (M31 F5 L11).

The agents list shape carries `provider_config_id`, `kb_ids` and `mcp_source_ids`
(`list_agents` -> `_agent_response` -> `AgentConfig.model_dump`), verified against a live
`GET /api/agents` and pinned by `test_usage_reads_the_live_list_shape`. A Library-only session
may not have loaded `agents` yet, so a consumer `get`s it and re-renders on the notification.

**The Library subscriptions (M25 F3 L4a).** Studio registers four more subscriptions in `init`, for the page's lifetime: `knowledge-bases`, `mcp-sources` and `connectors` always feed the section caches (`knowledgeBases`, `mcpSources`, `connectors`, which back `find*`, the pending consumes and the knowledge detail's cache hit) and re-attempt a pending MCP or connector consume; they and `agents` render the Library list only while its pane exists (never scrolling it).

**Studio's second cache.** Studio assigns `self._agents` / `self._allProviders` in `loadAll`, and
Agents and Library share the studio module (no re-activation between them), so Studio
**subscribes** to both collections: a load Studio did not itself start (`loadAllDepth === 0`) replaces
its copy and re-renders the Agents list (every notification since M25 F3 L3: the list sits
beside an open agent or create form, so it is never skipped). Rule for new consumers: read the five lists through
`App.store.get`, never a literal `fetch('/api/agents')`, `('/api/providers')`,
`('/api/knowledge-bases')`, `('/api/mcp-sources')` or `('/api/connectors')`
(`tests/test_store_static.py` greps for it; by-id, mutation and `?include_agent_surfaces=1`
URLs are exempt); call `invalidate` after any new mutation of those resources. Tests:
`tests/browser/test_store.py` (agents, providers), `tests/browser/test_store_library.py`
(the Library collections, the request counts, the invalidation map through the UI, `App.usage`).

### Hash routing — the router (`static/js/router.js`)

`App.router` (M25 F2 L3; DD1-DD4) owns the URL. Everything below is contract.

**Scheme** (destination-first): `#chats[/{sessionId}]`, `#chats/~new/{agent}` (the pending new chat, M25 F4 L3b: `href({destination:'chats', create:true, agent})` builds it with the agent through `enc()`, so a leading `~` is `%7E`; `parse` returns `{destination:'chats', create:true, agent}` and a bare `~new` parses as plain Chats; `resolve` maps it to the chat sub `new/{seg}` with the segment still encoded, folding bare `#chats/~new` and extra segments to a redirect; `routeFor('chat', 'new/x')` is `chats/~new/x`; `describeRoute` titles and announces it "New chat with {agent}" under the key `chats//~new/{enc(agent)}`, so a re-pick of another agent announces, and the first send's sync to `chats/{id}` is silent by construction because that key does not end in `/~new`; the legacy `#chat/new/...` maps to `#chats`, as the route never had a legacy form); `#agents[/{name}[/{tab}[/tools/{sourceId}]]]` (tabs: `general`, `rules-and-notes`, `knowledge`, `tools`, `self-configuration`; the old `…/mcps[/{id}]` redirects to `…/tools[/{id}]` in `resolve()`'s `agents` branch, M33 F1 L06); `#library` (Library's own route, the start page; a bare `#library/providers|knowledge|mcp|connectors` is an alias for it, `redirected: true`), `#library/providers[/~new|/{providerId}]` (sortie 02), `#library/knowledge[/~new|/{kbId}]`, `#library/mcp[/~new|/{sourceId}]`, `#library/connectors[/~new|/{connectorId}]` (knowledge bases and connectors became creatable, and connectors routed, in M25 F3 L4a: their `SECTIONS` rows are `create: true`, so the area subs `knowledge/new` and `connectors/new` map to `~new` both ways; **accepted risk:** a knowledge base or connector whose id is exactly `new` -- knowledge-base ids allow `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$` through the API -- is unreachable at its item route, the same risk MCP sources already carry; `~new` itself cannot collide, because `enc()` writes a leading `~` as `%7E`); `#settings` -> `#settings/appearance`, `#settings/prompts`, `#settings/encryption` (the former single General page, split in M25 F3 L5a), `#settings/tools[/{name}]`, `#settings/knowledge-indexing`. `#settings/general` is no longer a route: it is an **alias** in `resolve()` for `#settings/appearance`, any trailing segments dropped (`#settings/general/x` -> `#settings/appearance`), so a go(), a typed hash and a cold load all land on Appearance with the alias never written (`redirected: true`; the boot write and the traversal rewrite are a single `replaceState` of the target). `lastRoutes` is in memory only (nothing persists it) and is written from the resolved route, so `lastRoute('settings')` can never return `settings/general`. `~new` is the reserved create segment (an agent named "new" is legal). `#agents/~new` is the agent create route (M25 F3 L3, DD8; Flight 2 redirected it to `#agents`): `href({destination: 'agents', create: true})` builds it, `parse` returns `{destination: 'agents', create: true}`, `resolve` maps it to the studio sub `agents/~new` (extra segments are dropped by a redirect), and `routeFor('studio', 'agents/~new')` emits it UNESCAPED (exactly two segments; a longer raw-`~` sub is an agent path and keeps its `%7E`). The sentinel round-trips because studio's `serializeState` writes an agent name's leading `~` as `%7E` (`agentSeg`), so a raw `~new` in an agents sub can only mean create: `#agents/~new` is create, `#agents/%7Enew/general` the agent named `~new`, `#agents/new/general` the agent named `new` (pinned in `test_router_core.py` and end to end in `test_flow_agents_list.py`). `App.router.href({destination, section, id, tab, sub, create})` / `parse(hash)` are the route builder and parser (one owner of `encodeURIComponent`; on OUTPUT a segment starting with `~` is written `%7E...`, while `serializeState`'s already-encoded segments pass through unchanged, so an agent named `~new` never serialises to the reserved segment).

**Alias layer.** The areas still speak their own subs (`hydrateState(sub)` / `serializeState()`): chat `sessions/{id}`; studio `agents[/...]`, `knowledge[/...]`, `mcp-sources[/new|/{id}]`, `connectors`; admin `appearance`, `prompts`, `encryption` (M25 F3 L5a; the legacy subs `general` and `system` map to `appearance` in `hydrateState`), `providers[/new|/{id}]`, `tools[/{name}]`, `knowledge`. The router maps both ways through one alias table (`routeFor(area, sub)` and `resolve(hash)`); `tests/browser/test_router_core.py` checks the JS mapping against `_nav.py`'s mapping functions over a fixed corpus so the two cannot drift. Splitting studio later is a table edit.

**Two URL primitives.**
- `go(route, {replace, bypassGuard}) -> Promise<boolean>` is for user INTENTS: it runs the leave guard, writes the URL (push, or replace for `{replace}` / a route equal to the current one), commits and hydrates; `true` = navigated, `false` = the user stayed. A `go()` to the current route is NOT skipped (it replaces and hydrates). Unknown routes resolve `false`.
- `sync(areaId, sub, {name})` is area-internal canonicalisation: always `replaceState`, never guards, never hydrates; `name` is an optional display-name hint for the route's item (below). It is DROPPED when the mapped destination is not the current one, while a decision is pending (`isDeciding()`), or when `location.hash` is not the router's current URL (element-id fragments excepted). A background write (chat's reconnect auto-join while the user is in Settings) can therefore never move the user. Every former `replaceSub` call site is `sync('<area>', ...)` with its own area; `replaceSub` is removed.

**Commit sequence** for `go()` and traversal (pinned by `test_go_commit_sequence`): 1 resolve the target (redirect/alias); 2 guard unless bypassed (concurrent requests join the pending decision; the latest target wins); 3 write the URL (a traversal is already there; a null state is stamped); 4 commit `current` and `lastRoute(destination)` BEFORE hydrate (so syncs during hydrate are kept); 5 shell bits (aside `hidden`/label, the `onCommit` subscribers, i.e. the rail's `aria-current` and hrefs); 6 `mountArea`; 7 `hydrateState(sub)`; 8 `sync(area, serializeState())`, the post-hydrate canonicalisation (skipped when the area serialises nothing, e.g. chat before a join).

**History.** Every write stamps `history.state = {seq}`; a replace keeps the seq; boot adopts the surviving seq or stamps one. `popstate` is the single authoritative traversal event (proved in Chromium and WebKit by `test_router_popstate_engines.py`); a `hashchange` whose URL equals the one `popstate` just handled is dropped. **Stay** after a traversal reverts with `history.go(-delta)` from the LIVE seq delta; the revert's own `popstate` (URL equals the current route) is a no-op. A null-state arrival (a typed URL; in-app `#` links are intercepted by `App.nav`) has no delta, so Stay RE-PUSHES the stayed route: one duplicate history entry, accepted and tested; the screen and URL agree. **Continue** never pushes; it hydrates the latest location. A traversal that arrives while a non-router `App.confirmDialog` is open (`App.confirmDialog.isOpen()`) is reverted as Stay, and the open dialog stays in charge; a `go()` in that state resolves `false` the same way.

**Redirects (DD2)** are derived from the alias table plus explicit exceptions, applied with `replaceState` on boot and on every traversal, BEFORE the unknown-route check: `#chat` -> `#chats`, `#chat/sessions/{id}` -> `#chats/{id}`, `#studio` / `#studio/agents` / `#studio/tools` -> `#agents`, `#studio/agents/...` -> `#agents/...` (`#studio/agents/new` is an agent NAMED "new"), `#studio/knowledge|mcp-sources|connectors` -> `#library/...` (`mcp-sources/new` -> `mcp/~new`), `#admin` / `#admin/general[/..]` / `#admin/system[/..]` -> `#settings/appearance` directly (M25 F3 L5a: an explicit exception, never through the `settings/general` alias, so no chained redirect), `#admin/tools` -> `#settings/tools`, `#admin/knowledge` -> `#settings/knowledge-indexing`; bare `#library` / `#settings` -> their root sections.

**Boot and unknown hashes.** At boot an unknown or empty hash (or an element-id fragment) goes to `#chats`. After boot it restores the CURRENT route (replace), so a sanitised `href="#"` or an agent-authored `#installation` anchor does nothing. No destination id equals an element id (asserted).

**Route announcer and document title (flight 02 review; WCAG 4.1.3 / 2.4.2).** `router.js` creates one empty `#route-announcer` (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`, global `.visually-hidden`, test id `route-announcer`) at `start()`. A `commit()` that changes destination, section or item (the `destination/section/(~new|id)` key, the id `enc()`-encoded so no id reads as `~new`; a tab change does not) writes `"{Destination}[ — {section}][ — {item}]"` into it; boot's first commit never announces. `document.title` is `"{same parts joined by ' — '} · GleepGlorp AI"`, set on every commit (boot included) and on every `sync` (canonicalisation may name the item); title and announcement come from the same `describeRoute`, so they always match. Section labels: `SECTION_LABELS` holds the Library ones; the **Settings** ones are merged in LAZILY from `App.settingsPages` (`settings-pages.js`, loaded before `router.js`) by `sectionLabel()` on the first `describeRoute` (M25 F4 L7, AC9), so the page names live in one table (`tests/test_settings_pages_static.py` pins that the router's Settings `SECTIONS` routes and dev row equal the table's). Focus is never moved by navigation (DD5 alone moves it to `#main-content` after a guard Continue). Pinned by `tests/browser/test_router_announce.py`.
- **Item names (`describeRoute(route, nameHint)`, M25 F3 L2, DD8).** Spoken items: an agent name and a tool name (the id is the name), `New` on a `~new` route, and the display name of a provider, knowledge base, MCP server or connector. Those four come from `App.store` through an explicit map, `ITEM_COLLECTIONS` (`library/providers` -> `providers` (noun "Model provider"; sortie 02), `library/knowledge` -> `knowledge-bases`, `library/mcp` -> `mcp-sources`, `library/connectors` -> `connectors`), read synchronously with `peek` (never a fetch) and guarded (`router.js` loads before `store.js`, and `describeRoute` first runs from `start()`; only those four known names reach `peek`, which throws for an unknown one). Order: the `nameHint`, then the store's `name` for the id, then a name an earlier `sync` hint gave that same key (a just-created item stays named until its collection reloads), then the **type noun** (`Provider`, `Knowledge base`, `MCP server`, `Connector`): a cold deep link (the commit names the item BEFORE the area mounts and fetches) or an unknown id never speaks the raw id. A cold item is announced with the noun; the editor's later same-key `sync` may retitle with the name but never re-announces. Opaque chat session ids are still not spoken (they change the key only). Items longer than 30 characters are truncated with `…`.
- **`sync` announces exactly one transition** (DD8, cycle-2 issue 2): from a `~new` key to an item in the SAME destination and section, i.e. a save-create (provider, MCP; and the destination legs' create flows). It reads `prevKey = lastAnnounceKey` BEFORE recomputing, announces when `isCreateToItem(prevKey, key)`, then records the new key in every case. Everything else a `sync` does is silent: canonicalisation (same key), Chats session syncs (`chat.js` on `session_joined`), Agents and studio section syncs, a `~new` -> section-root or other-section sync, and the follow-up syncs after a create (the previous key is the item by then). A save-create `sync` that is DROPPED (`isDeciding()` or the hash moved) leaves the `~new` key in place, so the next accepted `sync` to an item in that section announces once: the same transition, late. Save sites pass `{name: updated.name}` (`admin.js` provider `onSaveSuccess`, `studio.js` MCP `onSaveSuccess`) and invalidate their collection first, so the announcement names an item the store does not hold yet. `tests/test_store_static.py::test_sync_reads_the_previous_key_before_overwriting_it` pins the ordering.

**Leave guard (`App.nav`, M25 F2 L5).** `static/js/nav.js` (loaded right after `router.js`; it looks `App.confirmDialog`/`App.router` up lazily and is callable before `router.start`) is the ONE leave guard; the interim per-area `confirmLeave` descriptors, both `_confirmLeaveImpl`s and both per-area `beforeunload` listeners are gone.
- **Sources.** `App.nav.registerSource(areaId, {isDirty, discard})`; studio and admin each register one for the page's lifetime (re-registering replaces; no per-editor entries). `isDirty = dirty && editorOpen()`: studio's `editorOpen` is agents with a selection or `_createMode`, `mcp-sources` with `mcpSourceFormMode`, `connectors` with `connectorFormMode`, `knowledge` with `_selectedKbId`; admin's is `providers` with `providerFormMode`, `tools` with `toolFormMode`, or `knowledge` (the whole page is a form). A stale `dirty` with no editor open therefore never prompts (`_dirty` has a setter on both areas as a TEST SEAM for that proof). Chat registers no source (the composer draft is not "unsaved work").
- **Discard is teardown only, never a URL write.** Studio's `discardOpen_` = `closeOpenItem()` plus emptying `sectionContentEl` for non-agent editors (MCP, connector, KB); admin's `_discardEditor` = `_closeOpenItem()`, or just `dirty = false` on the knowledge-settings page (re-entry re-renders from the saved settings; the hydrate branch checks `!dirty`). Both reset `dirty` in a `finally`, so a throwing discard still navigates and leaves no phantom flag. Each area keeps an `editorGen` that every teardown bumps (`closeOpenItem`, `_closeOpenItem`, `_setCategory`'s reset); async editor resolvers (provider/MCP/connector/tool/KB `onSaveSuccess`, the provider/MCP/connector type-list waits) capture it at mount and bail when it moved, so a save that resolves after a discard cannot resurrect the editor.
- **`confirmLeave(ctx) -> Promise<boolean>`**, `ctx = {opener, router, caller, target}`: nothing dirty resolves `true` with no dialog; otherwise ONE `App.confirmDialog({opener, ...})` ("Unsaved changes", Stay focused). Continue calls every dirty source's `discard()` (each in try/catch) and resolves `true`; Stay resolves `false`. Join rules: repeated calls from the SAME non-router `caller` while pending return the same promise; any other call (a different caller, or a router call) while a decision is pending resolves Stay, because joining would run both actions on Continue.
- **Router adapter and the synchronous fast path.** `router.js` `runGuard()` settles in the same tick when nothing is dirty (the `test_router_core` commit-sequence tests pin this); it is `if dialogOpen() || App.nav.isPending() -> Stay; if !App.nav.isDirty() -> settle(true); else App.nav.confirmLeave({router:true, opener, target}).then(settle)`. **Do not promisify the clean case** (an extra microtask breaks the commit sequence). `isPending()` is true only for NON-router decisions; router-to-router joins stay in `deciding` (latest target wins, traversals retarget it).
- **Paths.** (1) the rail (the top bar until M25 F2 L6) and (2) section nav (since M25 F3 the Library list rows and the Settings nav's links, all plain `<a href>` through the interceptor) and (4) item selection and (5) the item header's breadcrumb link (which replaced every editor's Back control in M25 F3) are `go()`; (3) in-app links via the interceptor; (6) browser Back/Forward and typed URLs via `popstate`; (7) `beforeunload`; (8) no inline gate remains: agent `enterCreateMode` became the `#agents/~new` route (M25 F3 L3) and the connector editor's inline gate gave way to router `go()`s (L4a; its Back was retired in L4b), so the router adapter is the only app caller of `App.nav.confirmLeave`. Its non-router path (`{caller}`, the same-caller join, and a router `go()` resolving Stay while it is pending) is now **test-only**, pinned by `test_nav_guard.py::test_go_while_inline_nav_dialog_pending_resolves_stay_and_does_not_join`, which calls `App.nav.confirmLeave({caller: 'test'})` itself over a dirty connector create form. Create-mode Cancel stays unguarded. Background navigations (`bypassGuard`) that leave a DEAD item (the KB poll 404 / KB delete, post-delete of an agent/MCP source/provider, the agent save-to-delete race) call the area's `discard()` first; post-SAVE bypasses (e.g. `replaceWithAgent_` after create) do not (same item, `dirty` already cleared, and a discard would lose the "Saved" flash). Editor tab switches inside one agent are unguarded (the form state persists across tabs).
- **Link interceptor.** One bubble-phase `click` listener on `document` handles `e.target.closest('a[href^="#"]')` (so an anchor with an icon child works) and reads `getAttribute('href')`. It skips `defaultPrevented`, a non-primary button, ctrl/meta/shift/alt, `target` and `download`. `App.router.resolve` decides: a **route** (new or legacy hash, e.g. an agent-authored `#settings/tools` or `#studio/mcp-sources`) -> `preventDefault()` + `go(href, {opener: anchor})` (a same-route click is the router's replace); an **element id** (`#main-content`, the skip link) -> native; **unknown/empty/bare `#`** (`[x](#)`, `#installation`) -> `preventDefault()` with no history entry. Before `router.start`, routes are still prevented and `go()` returns false.
- **Focus.** The dialog's opener is the explicit `opener` (the anchor for links, the rail link for a rail click), else `document.activeElement` when it is not `body`, else `#main-content`. **Stay** returns focus to the opener (the modal restores it). **Continue** runs `#main-content.focus()` from a `setTimeout(0)` after resolution, i.e. after the modal restored focus and called `onClose` and after the router's microtask commit; a clean pass never moves focus.
- **`beforeunload`** is a single `nav.js` listener that calls `isDirty()` inside try/catch. A native prompt wins over an open leave dialog (accepted). Verified with a real reload in Chromium only (`page.on('dialog')`); WebKit gives no equivalent guarantee.
- **Desktop shell.** The desktop launcher's embedded webview loads the same page, so the same listeners apply; a native webview may ignore `beforeunload`, in which case only the in-app guard protects (close-to-tray hides rather than unloads the page).
- **`App.confirmDialog`** accepts `opener` and has no singleton: a second call stacks, `isOpen()` is backed by an open COUNT. Production never stacks two (`App.nav` owns de-duplication); `App.confirmDialog(` is called only through `nav.js` and the Notes section's Clear all (`tests/test_nav_guard_static.py`). Tests: `tests/browser/test_nav_guard.py`, `test_router_guard.py`, the dirty-confirm flow files.

**Section-root routes render exactly the root (all areas).** `hydrateState(sub)` for a section root (`agents`, `library`; admin's category roots) closes any open item or create form, URL-less ones included: studio's `closeOpenItem()` (called from hydrate's root branches, NOT from `setActiveSection`, where it would close an item being opened) and admin's `_setCategory` on an unchanged category with an item open. A go() to the current section root (since M25 F3 L4b the item header breadcrumb's root link, which replaced the Library editors' interim Back) therefore closes the open item and shows the section root's empty state.

**Studio alias-layer rules (the Agents/Library leak fix).** (a) The outgoing-section cleanup lives in `setActiveSection`; `hydrateState` captures `prev` and passes it in and no longer assigns `_activeSection` itself (it used to, so `prevSection === section` on the hydrate path and the cleanup never ran). (b) `_refresh` re-selects an agent only on the Agents section. `doSelect`'s entry refuses to force-switch sections for async callers (self-configuration refresh, save-create, the delete fallback) unless the user is still in Agents, and its completion is discarded after the user left Agents or opened something else (`selectSeq` token). `tryConsumePendingSelect` and `_refresh` call `doSelect`, never `selectAgent`, so a hydrate never re-prompts and re-opening an agent renders it fresh from the server. (c) The studio section nav served Library only and was rendered once (retired in M25 F3 L4a by the Library list; the shared `areas/layout/section-nav.js` module was deleted in L5a with its last caller, the Settings nav).

**Item history, hydrate origin, dead ids (M25 F2 L4).**
- **Item opens push.** An item open (an agents or Library list row, provider/tool rows, the create actions for agents, providers and -- through the Library list's Add menu -- knowledge bases, MCP sources and connectors) is an intent `go()`: pushed, guarded by the router only. Completions never push: `doSelect`, `session_joined`, tab switches and MCP-source focus (`sync`, replace). Save-create (provider/MCP/connector via `sync`, agent and knowledge base via a bypass `go()`), post-delete and the KB poll bounce replace the current entry. The item header's breadcrumb link (M25 F3; it replaced the in-app Back buttons) is an intent `go()` to the section root; because it PUSHES, browser Back after it re-opens the item (the DD4 push-on-Back behaviour, kept from Back). Create-mode Cancel is a bypass `go()` that replaces (intentional discard, no gate). Connector open/edit was URL-less until M25 F3 L4a, which routed it and deleted its inline `App.confirmDialog` gate (agent create had one too, until M25 F3 L3 made it the `#agents/~new` route); every converted path has its inline gate deleted (one dialog per intent). No editor has a Back control since M25 F3 (Agents L3, Library L4b, Settings L5b): the list stays beside or above the open item. The per-call-path table is in the flight log.
- **Hydrate origin.** `hydrateState(sub, {fresh})`. The router passes `fresh: true` for traversal, boot and typed hashes (the named item may be gone; resolve it against a FRESH fetch) and `fresh: false` for `go()` (cached list, no refetch, no root re-render: studio skips `_setActiveSection` for an agent/MCP/connector item inside the section already showing, and closes the open form when the intent's item is not cached yet, so the old item never re-renders; admin skips `_setCategory` for an item inside the showing category; the consume paths render the item).
- **Dead ids.** A pending item absent from the fresh list heals to the root (Library items through `setActiveSection('library')`, others with `sync`; never kept pending): providers (`fetchProviders`, now single-flight), MCP sources and connectors (the pending-item rule's forced fetch, M25 F3 L4a), KBs (the detail GET 404, the status 404, and `_kbFresh` bypasses the cached list), agents (`resolvePendingAgent`, also covers an empty inventory), tools (`selectTool` 404; the pending name stays set until the detail loads so the URL does not flicker). `serializeState` for studio prefers a pending agent over the open one so an in-flight hydrate never overwrites its own URL.
- **Tools tab, MCP section.** studio owns the focused source: `hydrateState` writes the URL's value to `_selectedMcpAgentSourceId`/`_pendingMcpAgentSourceId` (so `#agents/x/tools/s2` while on s1 focuses s2 for the same agent); `mcps-tab.js` (the Tools tab's MCP section) reads it from the callback and has no `hashchange` listener or URL parsing. In-app links are built with `App.router.href`.
- **Chats.** The session row is `go('#chats/{id}')`; hydrate joins exactly once (the row no longer calls `joinSession`). A start is `go('#chats/~new/{agent}')` (pushed; replaced from another `~new` route); the first send's `session_joined` canonicalises to `chats/{id}` with a silent `sync` (M25 F4 L3b, "Chat start flow").

**Rail restore.** Each destination keeps its selection: the rail link of a non-current destination points at `lastRoute(dest)` (in memory for the session) and the current destination's link points at its root. This reverses the M10 F6 L7 "return to the listing" contract for other-destination clicks (reference board B3 section 10 item 2).

**Test seams.** `App.router.resolve(hash)`, `routeFor(area, sub)`, `current()`, `lastRoute(dest)`, `isDeciding()`, `root(dest)`; studio/admin expose a read-only `_dirty`. The router contract tests (`tests/browser/test_router_*.py`) use literal hashes and are exempt from the `_nav` migration guard at file level.

**Lifecycle**: cold entry runs `init() -> activate() -> hydrateState(sub)`; a same-module destination or section change runs `hydrateState(sub)` only. `hydrateState` runs synchronously after `activate` returns, but `activate` may have kicked off async work — `hydrateState` MUST stash a pending target and resolve it when async work completes; it MUST NOT assume data is ready synchronously. It must not write the URL (the router's step 8 does).

### Keyboard operability patterns

**Listbox/option** — used where a parent row is clickable AND contains a child action affordance (the nested-button rule prohibits `<button>` inside `<button>`). Parent stays `<div role="listbox">`; rows are `<div role="option" tabindex="0" aria-selected="...">`. Keydown fires the activation handler on Enter or Space (preventDefault on Space). All options use `tabindex="0"` (no roving) — WCAG-compliant for 5-20 items.

**Deliberate accepted deviation**: `role="option"` technically should not contain interactive children. In practice NVDA and VoiceOver handle this correctly (announce the option, then the inner button separately).

**Button reset for `<span>` → `<button>` conversions**: add `background: transparent; border: none; cursor: pointer; padding: 0;`.

**SVG-resilient click dispatch**: use `e.target.closest('.chip-remove')` (not `e.target.classList.contains`). Clicking on a child SVG yields `e.target = <svg>` and the `classList` check fails silently.

**`:focus-within` reveal**: hover-only delete affordances (hidden by `display: none`) reveal via the parent's `:focus-within` selector when the user Tabs into the child button.

---

## Testing patterns

### Pytest markers

| Marker | Tests | Default `pytest` | CI |
|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | unit | runs | runs |
| `live` | real LLM APIs (paid) | skipped | skipped |
| `browser` | Playwright functional | skipped (`-m browser`) | runs `-m browser` |
| `pixel` | Playwright visual snapshot | skipped (`-m pixel`) | **never runs** |
| `requires_bwrap` | bwrap integration | skipped | skipped |

Default `addopts` deselects `live`, `browser`, `pixel`, and `requires_bwrap`.

### Test fixtures and conventions

Tests monkeypatch built-in tool registration via `the_one.tools.builtins._TOOL_CLASSES` (module-level dict) — the supported seam; do NOT reach into `BuiltinToolProvider` internals.

The four established test patterns (asyncio_mode auto; `monkeypatch.setenv` HOME for tilde-expansion tests; httpx `MockTransport` `_install_mock` recursion avoidance; kill+reap timeout-budget assertions) live in `tests/CONVENTIONS.md`.

Tests inject a fake `SearchBackend` (and other Protocol-keyed services) by overriding `cross_configs[SearchBackend]` on the per-call view — no monkeypatching of module internals. This IS the supported test seam.

**`_TOOL_CLASSES` ordering is policy — cross-Config source note (M14 F4 L7)**: the
`get_tools` cross-config resolution loop walks `_TOOL_CLASSES` in declared order and maps
each distinct `cls.Config` to the **first** tool name that carries it. `FilesystemConfig`
is shared by all 9 filesystem tools (`read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, …), but the
first one in declaration order is `read_file` (not `shell`, whose Config is
`ExecutionConfig`). This means tool-config tests that need to set filesystem-policy fields
(e.g. `path_allowlist`) must key them under `"read_file"`, not `"shell"` or any other
filesystem tool. Reordering `_TOOL_CLASSES` silently changes which tool name resolves
`FilesystemConfig` — a policy consequence, not cosmetic. A module-level `assert` in
`tools/builtins.py` guards this invariant and fires immediately on any reorder.

### Sandbox testing footguns

Three patterns that cause confusing false-passes or false-failures in `@requires_bwrap` and sandbox-adjacent tests:

1. **Spec-level teardown dispatch** — `self._wrapped = spec.command == "bwrap"` is the single ground truth used by `UnixPtySession.close()` to pick SIGKILL-fast vs SIGTERM→grace teardown. Tests that want to assert teardown behavior must not mock the backend after `start()` returns — the dispatch bit is set during `start()`. Check `_wrapped` directly or rely on the test's observable (e.g. timing) rather than inspecting backend state.

2. **PTY-echo sentinel** — the PTY echoes the command line back on the master fd before the shell runs the command. If a test asserts `"connected"` is in the send output, it will **always pass** (the echo contains "connected" before the shell runs). Use the `"con"+"nected"` string-concat trick to keep the target literal out of the source text sent to the shell. Assert on the OUTPUT portion (after the echo) when the shell output and echo are interleaved. See `test_pty_sandbox.py:test_pty_network_denied_by_default_allowed_when_enabled` for the canonical example.

3. **`@requires_bwrap` workspace must be under `$HOME`, not `/tmp`** — bwrap always mounts a fresh `tmpfs` at `/tmp` (via `--tmpfs /tmp`). Any workspace or file under `/tmp` is invisible inside the sandbox. Always use `home_workspace()` from `tests/_sandbox_fixtures.py` (creates `~/.the-one-test-{suffix}`) for bwrap test workspaces; never use `tmp_path` or `/tmp`-rooted paths. The same applies to denylist secrets: create them under `$HOME` via `make_denylist_secret(workspace)` before bwrap starts, because `--ro-bind-try` silently skips absent paths (see `--ro-bind-try` silent-skip caveat in `SECURITY-MODEL.md`).

### Browser test isolation

Each browser test gets its own app subprocess on a random port (`socket.bind(('', 0))`) with isolated `tmp_path` data_dir. Teardown sends SIGTERM, escalates to SIGKILL after 10s. Browser navigates to `http://127.0.0.1:{port}` (not `localhost`) to avoid IPv6 first-attempt delay on WSL2.

Playwright fixtures (`playwright_instance`, `browser`) are **module-scoped**, NOT session-scoped — session scope kept the asyncio loop alive across modules and broke standalone smoke tests. Module scope still amortises Chromium within a module.

Tests rely on `data-testid` attributes on load-bearing UI elements rather than CSS classes, so they survive UI redesigns.

### Test databases come from the session template

`tests/_schema_template.py` builds ONE migrated SQLite file per pytest process (real `ensure_schema`, `journal_mode=DELETE` so it is a single file) and hands out copies. Replaying the migration chain costs about 0.4 s per database; a copy costs about 1 ms, and the suite builds thousands.

- **API**: `template_path()` (build once, thread-safe, removed at exit), `copy_template(dest)` (refuses an existing `dest`, mode 0o600), `connect_template_db(dest)` (copy + the usual `aiosqlite` connection, `Row` factory, `foreign_keys=ON`), `connect_template_memory()` (a fresh `:memory:` copy via the backup API).
- **Rule**: a test that only needs a current-schema database uses one of these. `ensure_schema(` / `init_db(` are called directly only from the files in `tests/test_schema_template.py::ALLOWED_DIRECT_CALLERS` (real-migration, schema and connection tests, each with its reason). `test_no_direct_schema_builds_outside_allow_list` is an `ast` guard over `tests/` that fails on any other caller; `test_allow_list_entries_exist_and_are_used` fails on a stale entry. A new migration/partial-schema test adds itself to the dict with a reason.
- **Equivalence**: `test_template_matches_fresh_replay` compares the template with a real replay (`sqlite_master`, `schema_version`, every table's rows, pragmas), so the template cannot drift from `ensure_schema`.
- **Never open the template itself through production code** (`get_connection`/`init_db` would convert it to WAL and mutate the shared file). Copy only.
- **Browser servers** are seeded the same way: `tests/browser/conftest.py::_spawn_app(seed_db=True)` copies the template to `<data_dir>/the-one.db` before `Popen`, so boot replays no migrations. Pass `seed_db=False` only for a test that asserts first-boot or migration behaviour.

### Parallel test runner contract (`scripts/run-tests.sh --parallel N`)

CLAUDE.md carries the commands; this is the lifecycle contract. `scripts/run-tests.sh --help` prints the lanes, budgets and host policy.

- **Shape**: `--parallel N` (no `--lane`; combining them is exit 2) runs every canonical lane (`CANON_LANES`) as a child `run-tests.sh --lane <lane>` under its own `setsid`, at most N at once, longest `BUDGET` first (ties keep canonical order). Each lane writes `<dir>/<lane>.log`, `.xml` (junit) and `.pgids`, where `<dir>` is `$TEST_LANE_DIR` or a fresh `mktemp -d`. `--plan` prints the schedule and exits. Host policy: at most 3 browser lanes at a time locally; never a local gate and a CI build together.
- **`TEST_TIMEOUT`** default: 1200 s for a single run, 2400 s with `--parallel` (it bounds the WHOLE run; children inherit it). An explicit caller value always wins.
- **Cleanup (TERM first)**: on EXIT/INT/TERM the parent TERMs every live child (each child's own EXIT trap group-kills its pytest and watchdog), waits up to 5 s, then KILLs the child groups and every pgid recorded in a `<lane>.pgids` file (each child appends `pytest <pgid>` and `watchdog <pgid>`; a cleanly finished child removes its file).
- **Timeout**: the parent's watchdog (own session) sends USR1 at `TEST_TIMEOUT`; the parent traps it as exit 137, and the watchdog SIGKILLs it only if it is still alive 15 s later. A single run's watchdog group-kills pytest at the cap (exit 137, "TIMEOUT" message).
- **Parent-death guard**: children get `RUN_TESTS_PARENT_PID`; their watchdog polls it every second and, if the parent is gone (SIGKILLed), writes `<lane>.pgids.parent-died` and group-kills pytest. Residual window: a parent killed before its first child's watchdog starts (well under a second) can leak that one pytest group.
- **Exit code**: any lane rc of 137 or 143 means timeout or killed, and the run exits 137. Otherwise a lane with rc 0 but a missing or empty junit xml counts as failed (rc 1), and the exit code is the FIRST failing lane's rc in schedule order (deterministic). The last line is `PARALLEL_RESULT rc=<n> total=<s>s lanes=<k> failed=<comma-list or ->`; an aborted run prints it too, listing the unfinished lanes as `failed`. Over-budget lanes are flagged `OVER BUDGET` in the summary, a warning only.
- **Re-measuring the lane budgets**: lane membership changes (a new file glob, a lane split) or a large runtime shift means re-measuring. On a quiet host (no CI build, nothing else heavy) run `TEST_TIMEOUT=3600 PYTHON=.venv/bin/python bash scripts/run-tests.sh --parallel 3`, read each lane's `wall=` from the summary, set `BUDGET[lane]` in the script to measured wall + about 25%, rounded up to the next 30 s, and update the comment above the table. `tests/test_run_tests_lanes.py` pins the plan's shape (every canonical lane once, numeric budget, longest first, the timeout defaults, the exit-2 argument errors), not the values, so the numbers are a reviewed edit.

### Browser apparatus doctrine (goldfinch) — live visual verification

goldfinch (MCP-driven browser) is the **canonical apparatus** for live visual verification against a running dev instance (`make up`); Playwright covers scripted regression, goldfinch covers exploratory/evidence work. **Behavior-test runs do not use it (M25 F2–F4):** goldfinch stopped delivering input mid-run in M25 Flight 2 and its input delivery is still unverified, so witnessed `/behavior-test` runs drive a CDP-connected headless Chromium with real input events (Flight 3 DD12), after an input-delivery probe; the rules live in `.flightops/agent-crews/behavior-tests-execution.md` ("Run rules"). Hard-won rules (sources: M17 F1 run log; M17 F2 L1/L2a/L2b flight-log anomalies):

- **printToPDF + computed-style probes are the default evidence path, not a fallback.** `captureScreenshot` wedged (timeouts) in three consecutive F2 legs; `captureWindow` is admin-only. printToPDF is provenance-certain pixels, BUT print emulation mutes background fills — computed-style probes are the primary color evidence, and active/selected states are judged via a11y checked-state (`readAxTree`), not print pixels.
- **The `captureScreenshot` wedge is progressive, and reload WORSENS it — escalate to UnknownVizError, don't retry.** The timeout is not transient: once `captureScreenshot` starts wedging on a tab it degrades further on `reload`/`navigate` (the doomed capture pipeline compounds), so the reflex "reload and retry" makes it worse, not better. Treat a wedged `captureScreenshot` as an **UnknownVizError** — an apparatus fault, NOT a product signal — and escalate to a different evidence path (printToPDF for pixels, computed-style probes for color, `readAxTree` for state, or a CDP page — below) rather than burning retries on the same broken primitive. Never let an apparatus wedge masquerade as "the UI didn't render."
- **PDF fill-palette cross-check — recover theme evidence under print-CSS muting.** Because print emulation flattens background fills, a printToPDF of a dark-theme view can look nearly identical to the light one, which defeats "prove the theme applied." Cross-check technique: render the SAME view to PDF in both themes and compare the surviving, non-muted signals — text ink color, hairline border colors, and the accent on selected/interactive elements (accent strokes survive muting better than large fills). If the fill-palette reads identical across the two PDFs on the signals that SHOULD differ, the theme did not actually switch (or you captured the wrong tab); matching computed-style probes are the tiebreaker. printToPDF proves structure/geometry; the fill-palette cross-check + computed styles prove theme.
- **goldfinch cannot observe `:focus` / `:focus-visible` — `document.hasFocus()` is always false there; use a CDP-driven `chrome-devtools` page for focus evidence.** A goldfinch-driven tab never holds real window focus, so `document.hasFocus()` returns false and `:focus-visible` never matches — focus-ring and keyboard-operability evidence gathered through goldfinch is a **false negative**, not a product bug. For any focus/keyboard acceptance (focus rings, Tab traversal, focus-trap, `:focus-visible`), drive a **CDP-connected `chrome-devtools` page** instead: it holds real focus (`document.hasFocus() === true`), `:focus-visible` matches, and its `take_screenshot` writes full-color pixels (sidestepping print-emulation muting). This is the sanctioned apparatus for the "Focus visibility convention" acceptance surface.
- **Multi-tab provenance discipline: unattributed frames are non-evidence.** Every capture/probe must be attributed to a specific tab (wcId + URL) you opened or verified. Close your own tabs when done; never assume a foreground tab is the one you drove.
- **a11y rendered/notRendered discrimination**: use the accessibility tree (`readAxTree`) to distinguish "element exists but hidden" from "rendered and exposed" — DOM presence alone over-claims.
- **Foreground the tab before trusting computed styles after a theme flip.** A backgrounded tab's rendering is throttled: rules with `transition: background-color` freeze at their FROM state, so probes read the OLD theme's colors while `var()` on the same element already resolves NEW values. Foregrounding (or a printToPDF, which foregrounds first) unfreezes it instantly.
- **Never-painted tab freezes transitions at FROM — `getAnimations().finish()` before probing (when you can't foreground).** A stronger form of the throttle above (F2 L2 anomaly, seen again L3): a tab that has NEVER been foregrounded produces **zero rendering frames**, so a transition on a freshly-set attribute/class (e.g. `aria-current` toggling a `transition: background-color` wash) sits at `currentTime 0` / `startTime null` indefinitely — `getComputedStyle` reports the FROM value forever even though the rule verifiably matches. This is an apparatus artifact, NOT a product bug: a synthetic element inserted with the attribute pre-set (no transition to play) styles correctly, and the transition lands in any painting tab. When you can't foreground the probe tab, call `el.getAnimations().forEach(a => a.finish())` to jump every running transition to its end state **before** reading computed styles.
- **Hard-reload after `make restart`.** The SPA links assets with versioned URLs (`?v=X.Y.Z`); the browser serves cached versioned responses even after the server restarts with new CSS/JS. Recovery: `fetch(href, {cache:'reload'})` over `document.styleSheets`/`document.scripts`, then `location.reload()`.
- **Port-ownership pitfall (the squatter class).** A foreign process squatting the app port serves ITS build while `make up`'s fresh server dies with "address already in use" — every 200 and every rendered frame then comes from the WRONG build. `make verify-serving` asserts the pid listening on `$THE_ONE_PORT` matches the port-suffixed pidfile (`.the-one.<port>.pid`) and fails loudly on mismatch or unresolvable pid. Before trusting visual evidence, confirm the served instance is yours (verify-serving, or served-source grep + pid-owns-port).
- **Record and restore operator state.** Theme preference (`theme-preference` localStorage key, per visited origin) is recorded before toggling and restored after; the dev instance is left as found.

### Visual snapshots — local-only

Baselines under `tests/browser/__snapshots__/` are **gitignored** — local regression artifacts, not shared source. First run: `pytest -m pixel --snapshot-update`. Subsequent runs compare; >2% diff fails and writes `<name>-actual.png` + `<name>-diff.png`. CI never runs `-m pixel`. Pixel diffs are a developer's local feedback loop, not a regression gate.

### UI schema metadata


**Operator-facing tool text (M31 F4 L05a).** Every field of every built-in tool Config carries `Field(title=..., description=...)` in plain sentence case, written for the operator (squawk 0231, A7, B7); the tools-config routes serve them in the schema. `ui_choices` labels are plain too ("1MB (default)", never model jargon); the shared timeout list is built per Config by `execution._timeout_default_ui_choices(default)`, so "(default)" marks shell's and web's 30s and `python_exec`'s 90s on their own dropdown. `tests/test_tool_config_text.py` fails on a missing description or an auto-generated title. **None of this is model-facing**: a tool's `to_schema()` is exactly `{name, description, parameters}`, and a test asserts no summary, title, description or preset label appears in it (structural, no committed golden).

Two class attributes on `Tool` are served by `GET /api/tools/configs` (the list) and the detail route and read by nothing else: `summary` (one short plain sentence, at most about 80 characters, B1) and `config_fields` (a tuple of Config field names the tool shows, or `None` for all, B6). The detail route filters `schema.properties` and `required` to `config_fields`; the agent Tools tab reads the same route, so it hides the same fields. **`config_fields` is display-only.** The Config still accepts every field; a PUT never resets a hidden field (`_merge_hidden_fields` copies each hidden field's stored value, from the saved config plus decrypted secrets, over whatever the body carries, so the stored value always wins and a `{"config": {}}` reset leaves hidden fields as stored); if a stored hidden value no longer validates the PUT retries without it (Config default, logged), and a failing retry is the operator's 422. Falling back to the Config default is acceptable because a field is hidden only where that tool never reads it. The resolver applies a stored hidden value at agent build time like any other. An agent override on a field that later becomes hidden stays stored and still applies; the Tools tab just no longer shows it (leg 05c carries it over on a Save). The audit: the six Filesystem tools that never read file contents (`write_file`, `list_dir`, `glob`, `delete_file`, `move_file`, `mkdir`) hide `read_size_cap_bytes` and `allow_binary_files`; `shell` and `python_exec` hide the pty fields; `shell_session_start` shows only the pty and network fields; `shell_session_send` shows the timeouts, output cap and denylist; `shell_session_close` and `shell_session_list` read no setting and show none. Web tools hide nothing (`download_file` reads no `timeout_seconds_max`, but the Config's `default <= max` check would then reject a visible edit on a field the operator cannot see).
Pydantic Config schemas may declare UI-presentation hints via `Field(json_schema_extra={"ui_hint": "...", "ui_choices": [...]})`. `ui_hint` is the rendering strategy (`"dropdown"`, future: `"slider"`, `"toggle"`); `ui_choices` is a list of `{"value": ..., "label": ...}` dicts. The admin form generator (`App.toolConfigFields`, `areas/shared/tool-config-fields.js`; it was `App.adminFormHelpers.renderField` until M31 F4 L07a) renders finite-set fields as dropdowns rather than free-form inputs. Server-side `_enrich_schema_with_markers` injects `x_marker` per property so the renderer can dispatch on marker class — admin and Studio share the same renderer with mode-specific widget shapes. It also materialises `default` for every `default_factory` field (squawk 0248): Pydantic omits `default` from `model_json_schema()` for those, and both editors fall back to `prop['default']` for a never-saved tool, so without it the Built-in tools editor showed `path_denylist`/`command_denylist` empty and a Save stored `[]` over the built-in denylist. The factory is called directly (never `Config()`, whose `model_post_init` appends the runtime secrets directory) and the value is made JSON-safe.

---

## Performance instrumentation

`App.perf` (`static/js/perf.js`) is the canonical surface for ad-hoc UI performance measurement. Gated per `gated-debug-instrumentation` convention (URL `?perf=1` OR `localStorage.getItem('the-one-perf') === '1'`). The `enabled` flag is captured at module-load time — toggling localStorage mid-session requires a reload.

**Browser support**: `performance.memory` is Chromium-only and only exposed in same-origin-isolated contexts; values are throttled to 100KB precision. `App.perf.captureHeap()` returns `null` when unavailable; callers must handle null.

Public surface: `mark`, `measure`, `captureHeap`, `dump`, `summary`, `reset`. Every method short-circuits when disabled. Measurement procedures and budgets for specific scenarios (area-switch cycles, long-chat rendering, large session-history list, many-agents-in-studio) live in flight design documents, not this file.

### Missing Playwright Chromium breaks raw `-m browser`

If `~/.cache/ms-playwright/` is missing — raw `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -m browser` errors with hundreds of "Executable doesn't exist" failures. Recovery: `make _ensure-playwright` (idempotent re-install, ~30-60s). The `make test-browser` chain already chains this; only the raw invocation hits the foot-gun. See `host-test-entrypoints-reach-via-make` convention.

> **Two connector-specific patterns removed here (Mission 23 connector reset)**: `connector-live-status-via-poll` (a DOM-connected-guard polling loop for reflecting live gateway connection state) and `save-reconnects-the-gateway` (evict-and-rewarm-in-background on connection-relevant config saves). Both lived entirely in the deleted connector editor and pool; the general "poll anchored to `element.isConnected`, stop on unmount" and "evict/re-acquire warm pool entries only on connection-relevant field changes" shapes are still sound and worth re-citing if a future UI surface or pooled resource needs them.

## Linux packaging (M32 F2)

Deep reference for `packaging/linux/` and the Linux half of the desktop shell. Operator-facing text is `docs/linux-install.md`; the build/pin detail is `packaging/linux/README.md`, `packaging/linux/checks/README.md` and `packaging/linux/vm/README.md`. The flight log (`missions/32-installers-everywhere/flights/02-linux-packages/flight-log.md`) holds the evidence.

**Build flow.** `packaging/linux/build.sh [--version X] [--no-excludes]` does the two steps that need the git checkout on the host (`packaging/stage.py`, `uv export`), renders `packaging/product.toml` through `packaging/render.py` into `$STAGE/rendered/` (`@UPPERCASE@` placeholders; a key with no value fails; the version comes from `the_one/__init__.py`, and PEP 440 `0.11.9.dev1` is Debian `0.11.9~dev1`), then runs `build-inner.sh` in the pinned container (`build.Dockerfile`, image `m32/linux-build:f2`) as the invoking uid, no network, no `--privileged`. Inside: PyInstaller freeze (`the-one.spec`, excludes from `excludes.txt`), `audit.py`, then the AppImage (`appimagetool` 1.9.1 with the pinned type2 runtime) and the `.deb`. `desktop-file-validate` and `appstreamcli validate --no-net` run on what ships. Output is under `$M32_BUILD`, outside the repo. The metainfo ships as `<metainfo_id>.metainfo.xml` (appimagetool's own metadata step is a harmless warning).

**Runtime hook and fallback.** The bundle does not carry the GTK/WebKit stack; it uses the host's through PyGObject. `rthook_host_gtk.py` runs first: it points gi at the host typelibs, keeps the bundle off `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` and the other loader variables (WebKit's helper processes inherit nothing under the AppImage mount), and, in the AppImage only, loads six fallback libraries (`libgirepository-2.0`, the Ayatana AppIndicator chain) host first by bare soname and from `usr/lib/gleepglorp-ai-fallback/` by absolute path (`RTLD_GLOBAL`) only when the host lacks them. The fallback typelib directory is appended to `GI_TYPELIB_PATH` after the host ones, only when no host directory has an AppIndicator typelib, and the variable is dropped once gi has read it. PyInstaller's stock gi/gio/glib/gtk hooks run after this hook and re-point four variables into the bundle, so `the-one.spec` drops them. Never use `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` for the fallback. The `.deb` has no fallback directory (its Depends pull the real packages). Debug knobs: `THE_ONE_PKG_DEBUG`, `THE_ONE_PKG_FORCE_FALLBACK`, `THE_ONE_PKG_ABI_PROBE`.

**Tray decision and the three close states.** See "Linux tray decision and host facts" above for the detection. `_on_closing` is registered in every tray mode, before the tray decision. With a tray icon, close hides to the tray. With `_tray_mode == "none"`, `_no_tray_hide` hides the window, tracks visibility, sends a one-time notification asynchronously (marker `.no_tray_notice_shown`, separate from the tray's) and never blocks. With `failed` or no mode, the close is allowed and the app exits. A second launch brings the hidden window back.

**GTK traps (DD12).** (1) A locked `closing` event plus pywebview's blocking geometry getters deadlocked every Linux close; geometry is read from `Gtk.Window` directly on the GTK main thread, and on Wayland only the size is saved (position is X11 only, because the resize/move events include client-side decorations). (2) On GTK, pywebview's `show()` re-hides a window created hidden; only `restore()` maps it, so `_show_window` uses it. (3) Window routes that call into pywebview are plain `def`, so they run in the threadpool.

**Window routes and `the-one quit`.** `GET /api/window/state` returns `{desktop, mode, visible, tray, version, pid}` (`mode`: `window`, `booting`, `browser`, `serve`, `quitting`), with `visible` read from the real GTK state; `POST /api/window/quit` runs the tray Quit's sequence and returns `{quit, pid, state}` (`quit` false in `serve` mode; a quit that lands during boot is a pending flag checked before the show and at the publish step, under `_window_lock`); `POST /api/window/focus` takes an optional `activation_token`. `the-one quit [--wait|--no-wait]` (`main._quit_command`) reads the credential file without creating one, calls the configured port only, and prints distinct lines for not running, too old (404/405) and rejected credential. `--wait` shares `shell._wait_for_exit` (75 s bound).

**Takeover (DD13).** A second launch of a newer version asks the running older one to quit through the quit route, waits for its pid to exit (`_pid_alive`: `os.kill(pid, 0)` on POSIX, `OpenProcess(SYNCHRONIZE)` plus `WaitForSingleObject` on Windows; never `os.kill` on Windows), then starts. `_is_older` compares `X.Y.Z[.devN]` (unparseable means not older). An instance with no quit route (older than this release) is focused instead and the log says to restart.

**AppRun self-integration (DD5).** `AppRun` with no argument or `app` writes, best effort and only when content changed, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/applications/gleepglorp-ai-appimage.desktop` and the 512 px icon (an absolute `XDG_DATA_HOME` only, else `~/.local/share`), escaping the AppImage path per the Desktop Entry spec; other arguments pass through. The entry has a `quit` action running `AppRun quit`.

**Checks, audit, VMs.** `audit.py` (in `build-inner.sh`) fails the build on an `excludes.txt` match, a `*.typelib`, or an ELF needing glibc above 2.39. `packaging/linux/checks/gates.sh` runs the container gate suite (doctor pinned/unpinned, library provenance, window, upgrade, single instance) scored by `score.py`; `gtk-smoke.sh` runs the real-GTK smoke on a private Xvfb. `packaging/linux/vm/` runs stock Ubuntu 26.04 and Debian 13 GNOME guests (QEMU/KVM in `m32-vm-*` containers, fresh qcow2 overlays over read-only bases, QMP for keys/screens). Safety rules: invoking uid, never `--privileged` or host networking, ports on `127.0.0.1` 2290-2299, never 8484/8485 or the operator data dir, nothing installed on the host. The behavior test is `tests/behavior/linux-package-install.md`.

**Hosted workflow.** `.github/workflows/linux-packages.yml` is `workflow_dispatch` plus `workflow_call`, `contents: read`, no secrets, publishes nothing. A `build` job builds both packages in the same container; a `checks` matrix (two distros, two formats, bare images, private Xvfb) runs `gates.sh` single-version (the upgrade check is N/A there); `summary` fails unless four score lines pass. On the `.deb` cells G3 and single-instance must also pass.
