## macOS packaging (M32 F3)

Deep reference for `packaging/macos/`. Operator text is `docs/macos-install.md`; the contributor's checklist is `docs/macos-contributor-check.md` and its behavior test `tests/behavior/macos-app-install.md`; the build/pin detail is `packaging/macos/README.md`. Runtime behaviour of the app on macOS is in "macOS dock mode (M32 F3 L04, DD2-DD5)" and "macOS runtime seam (M32 F3 L5, DD6/DD7)" above. The flight log (`missions/32-installers-everywhere/flights/03-macos-app/flight-log.md`) holds the evidence.

**Build flow.** `packaging/macos/build.sh [--version X] [--from-head]` renders `packaging/product.toml` through `packaging/render.py --json`, stages the tree (`packaging/stage.py`), creates a venv and installs the `uv export` closure with `--require-hashes --no-deps`, freezes with pinned PyInstaller 6.22.3 (`the-one.spec`; `BUNDLE` takes name, id and versions from `$M32_PRODUCT_JSON`), runs `codesign --force --deep -s -` (ad-hoc), then builds the `.dmg` (`hdiutil create -fs HFS+ -format UDZO`, the `.app` beside an `Applications` symlink, one retry). It writes `macos-<version>/build.env` and does not run the checks; the workflow does, as its own step. `M32_SKIP_DMG=1` / `M32_REUSE_VENV=1` serve the N+1 build. The bundle is `GleepGlorp AI.app` with the executable `Contents/MacOS/the-one`; `CFBundleVersion` is `major.minor.(patch*10000+n)` (n the dev number, 9999 final), `LSMinimumSystemVersion` 14.0 because the bundled numpy's 13 arm64 extensions carry `minos` 14.0 (everything else 11.0); `.dmg` name `GleepGlorp_AI-<version>-arm64.dmg`.

**Audit rules.** `audit.py APP --product-json product.json [--report-only]` walks every Mach-O: it must have an arm64 slice, link only `/System/`, `/usr/lib/`, `@rpath/`, `@loader_path/` or `@executable_path/`, and have `minos` no higher than `min_os`. It reports the counts (the hosted run: 152 files, 0 fat, max minos 14.0, 0 problems). `checks.sh` passes `${M32_AUDIT_ARGS---report-only}`, so an empty-but-set value enforces; the workflow flips it from report-only to enforcing once the report is clean.

**Log file.** `frozen_entry_mac.py` redirects stdout and stderr to `~/Library/Logs/GleepGlorp AI/app.log` (append, line-buffered, truncated at start over 10 MB) only when `app` starts without a terminal. The lifecycle driver and the guide read it: `macos dock: delegate installed (route=subclass, ...)`, `dock: window hidden on close`, `reopen: decision=show`, and per quit `terminate: applicationShouldTerminate received (decision=drain-then-reply)`, `drain started`, `drain done in 0.Ns`, `reply sending`, `applicationWillTerminate (AppKit exiting)`.

**Lifecycle driver.** `lifecycle.sh APP_N APP_N1 APP_ID VERSION_N VERSION_N1 OUT_DIR` runs L0-L10 and LEND, each step a child under its own cap, one `PASS`/`FAIL` row with evidence, driving the runner's GUI session through System Events (close button `AXPress`, window frame read/set), `open -a` / `open -n`, `lsappinfo front`, the quit Apple event by bundle id, `the-one quit --wait` and a Cmd-Q keystroke; `M32_PRODUCT_PORT` and `M32_DATA_DIR` keep the Linux rehearsal off 8484 and the real data. The credential must exist before L1 (created by `checks.sh` gate 4). First hosted green: run `40214604211` (13 PASS in `checks.sh`'s summary and 13 PASS in the lifecycle's, with the crash audit enforcing). Known fidelity gaps: L0 installs N from the mounted `.dmg` (falling back to the build dir only if the `.dmg` is absent), `open -a` stands in for a Dock click, and Dock-menu Quit and logout are not driven; Gatekeeper's approval flow cannot be observed on the runner. The crash check reads `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports` and distinguishes "none" from "unreadable" (the LCRASH row and the per-quit checks); the workflow runs the audit enforcing.

**Probe.** `packaging/macos/probe/` (`delegate_probe.py`, `probe.spec`, `probe.sh`) is a throwaway app plus premise script that answered the flight's design questions on the hosted runner before the product was changed: pywebview's Python-defined `AppDelegate` can be subclassed (route `subclass`; the inherited `applicationShouldTerminate:` prototype is `I@:@`, not `NSUInteger`), `NSTerminateLater` plus an `AppHelper.callAfter` reply is delivered, `open -a` fires `applicationShouldHandleReopen:`, and a quarantined, ad-hoc signed app is refused by Gatekeeper ("Not Opened", Done / Move to Trash, no Open button on macOS 15.7; `spctl` rejected). Deleting a running app bundle crashed the old copy in one run (`FileNotFoundError` reading pywebview's `js` directory) and not in the other, hence "quit first". `probe` mode of the workflow keeps it runnable.

**Desktop test-patch rule and the pywebview/PyObjC upgrade checklist (squawk 0262).** (1) `tests/test_desktop_split_guard.py` parses every `tests/**/*.py` and fails, naming file and line, when a test patches or assigns through `the_one.interfaces.desktop.shell` a name `shell.py` does not itself define at its top level (an imported name such as `__version__` or `httpx` does not count). The rule for test authors: **patch a name in the module that defines it** (`takeover.py`, `window_control.py`, `session_state.py`, `boot_policy.py`, `macos_dock.py`), never through `shell` (a `raising=False` patch of a moved name silently patches nothing); **lifecycle state lives on `session_state.state`** (reset by an autouse fixture around each test); and since Mission 32 Flight 4 **platform decisions live on `boot_policy`**: the frozen `BootPolicy` (`WINDOWS`, `LINUX`, `MACOS`, `OTHER`; `current()` is the only platform read, `state.policy` the lazy per-run binding), so `shell.py` and `window_control.py` hold no `sys.platform == "darwin"` branches (the split guard fails on one in `window_control.py`); a new per-platform behaviour is a new policy flag plus a row in `tests/test_boot_policy.py`'s decision table, not an `if sys.platform`. (2) The macOS Dock mode subclasses pywebview 5.4's Cocoa app delegate (`macos_dock.py`: `_install_delegate`, `_begin_activity`), and its PyObjC bodies carry `# pragma: no cover` because they only run on a Mac. **Any pywebview or PyObjC version bump needs both** `tests/test_pywebview_cocoa_canary.py` green (it reads `webview/platforms/cocoa.py` with `ast`, never importing it) **and** a hosted lifecycle run on the branch: `gh workflow run macos-packages.yml --ref <branch> -f mode=lifecycle`, read as 13 PASS in its summary. A green canary alone does not prove the delegate still works. (3) macOS CI is **hosted-only and dispatch-limited**: there is no per-push macOS build (no local runner, and hosted minutes are rationed), `macos-packages.yml` and the macOS half of `release.yml` run only when dispatched or on a release tag, and what a runner cannot show (Gatekeeper's approval screens, a physical Dock click, logout, a real display) is the contributor check (`docs/macos-contributor-check.md`).

## Release workflow (M32 F4)

`.github/workflows/release.yml` is the one release path; it replaced the Windows-only `windows-installer.yml` (deleted) and Flight 1's `packaging-probe-windows.yml`. Operator-facing format of the notes is `docs/releases/README.md`.

- **Triggers.** `push` of a `v*` tag, or `workflow_dispatch` (a dry run). Dispatch inputs: `version` (empty = `__version__`; else `X.Y.Z` or `X.Y.Z.devN`), `platforms` (comma-separated, no spaces, from `windows,linux,macos`), `macos_mode` and `windows_mode` (`full|lifecycle`). The dispatch block is **frozen** once `packaging/release/release.stub.yml` is on `main` (GitHub dispatches only a workflow present on the default branch and validates inputs against it); `tests/test_release_workflow.py` pins the real block to the stub's, byte for byte. Concurrency is `release-<ref>` on a tag push and `release-dispatch` otherwise, `cancel-in-progress: false`, so a dry run can never take a pending tag run's slot. Top-level `permissions: contents: read`; only `publish` has `contents: write` and only `cleanup` has `actions: write`. No secret beyond `github.token`.
- **Jobs.** `audit` (the release-closure `pip-audit`, `uv==0.10.0`, `uv export --frozen`) and `version` (checkout `fetch-depth: 0`, then `scripts/release_check.py`) run first; `windows`, `linux` and `macos` each `needs: [audit, version]`, call their reusable `*-packages.yml` with `version: needs.version.outputs.version` (and the mode), and run on a tag push or when `platforms` names them; `publish` (`github.event_name == 'push' && startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')`, `needs` all three plus `version`) never runs on a dispatch; `cleanup` (after `publish`) deletes this run's `windows-installer`, `linux-packages` and `macos-dmg` artifacts: on a tag push only after `publish` succeeded, on a dispatch dry run whatever the platform results.
- **The version gate** (`scripts/release_check.py`, stdlib, loads `packaging/render.py` by path because `import packaging.render` would hit the PyPI `packaging` distribution). The tag path is `--event push` **and** a `refs/tags/v` ref; a `workflow_dispatch --ref v0.12.0` is a dry run. On a tag it requires `vX.Y.Z` equal to `__version__`, a final release (no `.devN`), and the tagged commit to be an ancestor of `origin/main`; on a dispatch it takes the strict grammar or `__version__`. Every version goes through `render.load`, so each platform's derived field (`deb_version`, `bundle_version`, `bundle_short_version`) is valid. **The version lives only in `the_one/__init__.py`**: `pyproject.toml` has `dynamic = ["version"]` with `[tool.setuptools.dynamic] version = {attr = "the_one.__version__"}` and `uv.lock` carries no `the-one` version line (lock lanes: uv 0.9.30 in the Concourse tasks and `windows-tests`, 0.10.0 in the Linux/macOS workflows, `release.yml`'s `audit` and the lock).
- **Publish.** `scripts/release_assets.py --version V` selects exactly four files by exact name (`the-one-setup-V.exe`, `GleepGlorp_AI-V-x86_64.AppImage`, `gleepglorp-ai_<render.deb_version>_amd64.deb`, `GleepGlorp_AI-V-arm64.dmg`; missing, duplicate or extra package files fail) and writes `SHA256SUMS`. The step then looks the tag up with `gh release list --limit 200 --json tagName,isDraft,name` (`gh release view` does not find a draft) and logs one branch: **new** (create a draft with the title and body from `docs/releases/$TAG.md` through `scripts/release_notes.py`, upload with `--clobber`, publish), **existing draft** (retitle from the notes file, upload, publish: the re-run path after a partial publish) or **published** (uploads only; a hand-written title is never replaced). The package artifacts (`windows-installer`, `linux-packages`, `macos-dmg`) are kept 1 day from a release (the `retention-days` `workflow_call` input, default 3 for a standalone dispatch; logs and evidence stay 7), and a `cleanup` job (the only one with `actions: write`) deletes them once `publish` succeeded (on a dry run, once the platform jobs finish): Actions storage accrues hourly (squawk 0302), so a failed publish re-runs the whole release rather than `publish` alone later.
- **Dry run.** `gh workflow run release.yml --ref <branch> -f version=0.12.0.dev1 -f macos_mode=lifecycle -f windows_mode=lifecycle` builds the chosen platforms and publishes nothing. **Never dispatch a standalone platform workflow (`windows-packages.yml`, `linux-packages.yml`, `macos-packages.yml`) while a release is running**: their fixed concurrency groups hold one pending slot, and a standalone dispatch could displace the release's pending job (DD7).

## Windows packaging (M32 F4)

`packaging/windows/build.ps1` is the one recipe, called by `windows-packages.yml` and the Concourse task `ci/tasks/windows-installer-build.yml` (fresh venv, locked closure installed with `--require-hashes --no-deps`, `pyinstaller==6.22.3`, `packaging/stage.py --target windows`, the strict `doctor` needles, ISCC). The spec freezes the **staged** tree (`M32_STAGE` is required; the project is never installed). Names and the app GUID come from the `[windows]` section of `packaging/product.toml`, rendered into the gitignored `packaging/windows/product.iss` by `packaging/render.py --out`. **The installer identity is frozen:** `the-one.iss` keeps `AppId={{CAEEFB46-2698-4048-AD9F-0120D0B7174A}}` literally (a test pins it to `app_guid`); Inno escapes only the opening brace, so every shipped install has the uninstall key `...\Uninstall\{CAEEFB46-2698-4048-AD9F-0120D0B7174A}}_is1` with two closing braces. "Fixing" that line would orphan every existing install, and the lifecycle reads the `}}_is1` key. **Quit sequence** (`[Code]`): ask the app to quit (`the-one.exe quit --wait`, `ExecAsOriginalUser` in setup, `Exec` in the uninstaller, where the setup-only function is unavailable), poll up to 90 s until neither exe is running, then `KillRunning` (`taskkill /F`) always last; `CloseApplications=force` stays as the backstop, and an app older than v0.12.0 (v0.11.8 and earlier have no `quit`) is closed by force once. `lifecycle.ps1` (with `lib.ps1`; `windows-packages.yml` `mode=lifecycle`) proves rows L1-L9 on a hosted runner (install over a running old app, close to tray, second launch, geometry, graceful upgrade, quit, uninstall while running); the runner is an elevated `runneradmin` with silent UAC, so the graceful path under a real UAC prompt and a non-admin upgrade are field checks. See `packaging/windows/README.md`.

---

## Routines (M33)

Operator-defined, per-agent scheduled prompts. Flight 1 leg 1 (`routines-store-and-schedule`) is the store, the pure schedule calculator and the REST surface; the turn path (leg 3) and the runner with Run now (leg 4) follow below; the scheduler is leg 5 (below).

- **Write service (M33 F2 L1, DD3).** `routines/service.py` is the single home of the routine write rules: `validate_prompt` / `validate_schedule` / `validate_enabled`, `create_routine`, `update_routine` (the whole update state machine), `delete_routine`, `routine_view` (no `target`), `limits_view` and `poke_scheduler` (called by each successful write). The REST routes in `api.py` are thin adapters; the agent's routine tools (later legs) go through the same functions. The service never calls `get_connection()` / `get_encryptor()` and never raises `HTTPException`: repositories and an async zone loader (awaited only when a schedule change or re-enable needs it) come from the caller, and failures are typed (`RoutineFieldError(field, message)` in `limits.py`, `RoutineScheduleError`, `RoutineLimitError`, `RoutineNameTaken`, `NameRuleError`) for the caller to map.
- **Start immediately (M33 F4 L4, L4-DD1/DD2).** The operator routes accept the wire start `{"start": {"now": true}, "repeat": ..., "ends": ...}` (exact dict equality). `service.resolve_now_start(raw, now, zone) -> (raw', is_now)` rewrites it to the canonical `{date, time}` of the FLOOR minute of `now` in the operator zone (`recurrence.render_start`), then `normalize` runs unchanged (no `recurrence.py` or `scheduler.py` change; a normalizer cannot express "due now", its occurrences are strictly after now). `create_routine` / `update_routine` call it only when `not agent_caps and schedule_pair is None`; for a now-start `_require_now_start` replaces `_require_future` (refuses "This schedule has no runs left." when no occurrence exists at or after the floor minute, e.g. a weekly rule that excludes the save day and ends that day) and `next_run_at` is the EXACT save instant (an every-minute routine has 30 s of grace; a floor-minute value could be recorded Missed). In `update_routine` the now-start's `next_run_at` is set in the re-anchor branch and is persisted only when the routine ends up enabled and not Done (a disabled routine is re-anchored and does not run; a Done routine is revived like any schedule change, so Save + Immediately re-runs it). The scheduler records the run as an ordinary "Scheduled" run (lateness of a few seconds is under `ON_TIME_SECONDS`), claims the row through `advance_if_due` (no double run), and a one-off becomes Done. The agent path never calls the helper, so `agent_change` / the tools refuse the shape with "The schedule needs a start with a date and a time." (tool schemas and text unchanged). `POST .../routines/preview` returns `immediate: true`, `next_runs[0]` = now then two grid occurrences; the form composes "sentence · First run: when you save". A now-start on a routine whose run is still going gets the existing "still running" skip. Form: the Starts group is `App.ui.radioGroup` (`routine-form-start-on/-in/-now`, `routine-form-in-result`); form state `startMode` ('on'|'in'|'now'), the unselected options' inputs are disabled, the On date/time is remembered while In or Immediately is chosen so flipping back is clean, and `scheduleBody` sends the now-start only for 'now'.
- **Agent-written routines (M33 F2 L2, DD4-DD8, DD14).** Schema v46 (every `ALTER` guarded by `PRAGMA table_info`): `agents.routines_self_config_enabled` (default 0) and `agents.routines_require_approval` (DDL default 1) are operator-owned: `AgentConfig` carries them (approval defaults off since M33 F4 L5), `_row_to_config` reads them, `SQLiteAgentStore.save` does not write `routines_self_config_enabled`, and writes `routines_require_approval` in the INSERT only (a new agent gets the model default, off; it is out of `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, so a save never changes an existing row; existing rows keep their stored value, no migration), `create_agent` pops them from the body, `update_agent` overwrites them with the stored values before validation (so a PUT can never change them and the response echoes the stored truth), and only `set_routines_self_config(name, *, enabled=None, requires_approval=None)` (targeted UPDATE, `KeyError` for an unknown agent) or the item route changes them. `agent_routines` gains `untrusted` (0) and `untrusted_sources` (JSON list or NULL; `Routine.untrusted`/`untrusted_sources`, in `routine_view`). New table `agent_routine_proposals` (`id`, `agent_name` FK cascade/cascade, `routine_id` FK `ON DELETE SET NULL`, `routine_name`, `action` create|update|delete, `payload` JSON (the validated request: a once's `in` already an absolute `at`), `base` JSON (raw stored `name, prompt, schedule, anchor_at, enabled, finished_at` for update/delete), `status` applied|pending|dismissed|superseded, `untrusted`, `untrusted_sources`, `session_id`, `created_at`, `resolved_at`; index `(agent_name, status)`). Retention (M33 F3 L2, DD4): the purge sweep deletes an agent's resolved rows (`status != 'pending'`) whose `resolved_at` is older than the agent's cutoff, and Purge now deletes all of its resolved rows; pending rows are never deleted and there is no count-based pruning (`_prune`/`AGENT_PROPOSALS_KEPT` are gone).
  - **Repository composition.** `AgentRoutineRepository.create/update/delete` take keyword-only `precheck`, `agent_caps`, `flag` and `proposal` and run them inside the mutator's ONE `write_transaction` in a fixed order: re-read the row (`KeyError` if gone), `precheck(row)` (for create it receives `None`; approval's stale check lives here so the compare is atomic with the write), the DD8 caps over the agent's rows read in the transaction, the write with `flag` applied (`("merge", sources)` ORs `untrusted` in and merges sources de-duplicated in order, capped at `SOURCES_CAP`; `"clear"` zeroes; `None` leaves it), then `proposal.run(conn, routine_id)` (a `ProposalOp`: `insert_applied(row)` or `resolve(id)`; the id is the new id for a create, the routine for an update, `None` for a delete). A failing proposal op rolls the routine write back. `delete` also supersedes the routine's other pending proposals in the same transaction. Never `await` the zone or the chat repository inside the transaction; the service resolves both first.
  - **`RoutineProposalRepository`** (`routines/proposals.py`): `list`, `pending_count`, `get`, `insert_pending(row, caps_check=)` (one transaction: refuses a same-named pending create ("A routine with that name is already waiting for approval.") or an existing routine of that name (`RoutineNameTaken`), supersedes an older pending row for the same routine, refuses past `AGENT_PENDING_PROPOSALS_MAX` = 20 ("You have 20 changes waiting for approval."), runs the caps check, inserts; returns `(proposal, replaced)`), `mark_dismissed` (idempotent; `ProposalStateError` on applied/superseded), `mark_superseded`, `supersede_pending(agent)`, `delete_resolved_older_than(agent, cutoff)` and `delete_resolved_for_agent(agent)` (retention and Purge now). Errors: `ProposalStateError` (409), `ProposalStaleError(current, proposed, base)` (409 `stale`), `ProposalGoneError` (409 `gone`).
  - **Caps (`limits.py`, M33 F3 L2, DD3).** `ROUTINES_ACTIVE_MAX` 50 (enabled and not Done) and `ROUTINES_OPEN_MAX` 200 (not Done, on or off), both RAISE-ONLY for every write (operator REST and agent tools alike): `check_routine_caps(before, after)` refuses only when a count ROSE and is now over its cap, so an agent already over a cap can still rename, edit, turn off or delete. `AgentRoutineRepository.create`/`update` run it unconditionally inside their transaction (precheck, caps, write, proposal; a delete never raises a count); `service.agent_change` runs it at proposal time inside `insert_pending` (after the supersede UPDATE) and approval re-runs it through `create_routine`/`update_routine` (422, proposal stays `pending`). Refusals: `MSG_ACTIVE_CAP` / `MSG_OPEN_CAP`. The `agent_caps` argument of the service functions now means only the agent-only minimum lead time (`AGENT_ONCE_MIN_AHEAD_MINUTES` 1, `service._min_ahead`); the repository no longer takes it. `AGENT_PENDING_PROPOSALS_MAX` 20 and `AGENT_ROUTINE_WRITES_PER_TURN` 5 are unchanged; `ROUTINES_PER_AGENT_MAX`, `AGENT_ACTIVE_ROUTINES_MAX`, `AGENT_RUNS_PER_DAY_MAX`, `runs_per_day` and `AGENT_PROPOSALS_KEPT` are removed. `limits_view()` returns `active_max` and `open_max` (no `routines_per_agent`).
  - **Retention and skips (M33 F3 L2, DD4/DD11).** `retention.run_purge_sweep` also deletes, per agent and with the same cutoff, Done routines with `finished_at` older than it (`AgentRoutineRepository.delete_done_older_than`: pending proposals for them are superseded first, runs cascade, and a routine with a `running` run is left for a later sweep) and resolved proposals; its result and the `POST /api/_test/purge-sweep` seam gain `routines_deleted` and `proposals_deleted`, and Purge now (`DELETE /api/agents/{name}/chat/sessions`) gains `proposals_deleted` (resolved proposals only; routines and pending proposals stay). Schema v47 adds `agent_routine_runs.repeats` (default 1) and `reason_code`. Every skipped run row carries a code: `still_running` (scheduler), `chat_busy`, `waiting_sign_in` (runner, through `RoutineRunRepository.finish(..., reason_code=)`); `classify` stays a 2-tuple. Consecutive `still_running` skips of one routine collapse (`record_still_running_skip`, read-then-update in one transaction): the newest row (by `started_at`) being a `still_running` skip increments its `repeats` and moves `finished_at`, `due_at` and the reason ("Skipped {n} times while the previous run was still going."); `started_at` and `trigger_kind` never move, and pre-v47 rows (null code) are never merged into.
  - **Service.** `agent_change(conn, agent, action, data, *, now, zone, taint, session_id)` re-reads the two switches at call time (off, or the `default` agent: `RoutineFieldError("routines", ...)` "Routine tools are turned off for this agent." / "Routines need a named agent."), resolves the routine (id, else case-insensitive name; a name matching a pending create gets the "still waiting for your operator's approval" sentence; the refusal echoes the name as the agent passed it), validates through the same validators as the operator path, snapshots `base`, then either holds a pending proposal (approval on; the caps check runs at proposal time over a hypothetical "after") or applies through `create_routine`/`update_routine`/`delete_routine` with `created_by="agent"`, `agent_caps=True`, the merge flag when tainted and an `insert_applied` op (approval off). `approve_proposal(conn, agent, id, *, override, now, zone)` returns an applied proposal as is, raises `KeyError` for a cross-agent id, `ProposalStateError` for dismissed/superseded, re-validates the schedule and target before the lock (a past `at` is a 422 and the proposal stays pending), re-reads the routine at approval and writes with `resolve(id)`; the stale compare (`precheck`, over the base keys the payload touches; a delete compares all but `finished_at`) raises `ProposalStaleError` unless `override`; a gone routine supersedes the proposal (update: `ProposalGoneError`; delete: returned, 200). Name clash and caps are re-checked in the transaction. `dismiss_proposal` wraps `mark_dismissed`.
  - **The flag.** Only the operator REST `PUT .../routines/{id}` with `prompt` in the body passes `flag="clear"`; toggles, schedule edits, Run now, approve and any agent-applied update merge or leave it.
  - **REST.** `GET`/`PUT /api/agents/{name}/self-configuration/routines` (`{enabled?, requires_approval?}` strict booleans, at least one key; both return `{enabled, requires_approval, pending_count}`; `default` is a 422 "Routines need a named agent.", unknown agent 404; turning `enabled` off supersedes the pending proposals), `GET .../routines/proposals?status=&limit=&before=` (`{"proposals": [...]}`, newest first), `GET .../proposals/pending-count`, `POST .../proposals/{id}/approve?override=` and `POST .../proposals/{id}/dismiss` (404 cross-agent/unknown, 409 stale `{"error":"stale","current_value","proposed_value","base_value"}`, 409 `{"error":"gone"}`, 409 state, 422 field/caps, 409 name taken). The existing `/self-configuration` GET/POST are unchanged.
  - **Squawk 0277.** `create_agent`/`update_agent` run `_check_agent_provider` after validation: `provider_config_id == ""` or an id with no provider row is a 422 `[{"type":"value_error","loc":["provider_config_id"],"msg":"That model provider doesn't exist."}]`; `None` stays legal; `update_agent` checks only a CHANGED id (an agent whose provider was deleted stays editable); `kb_ids` are not checked (M24 DD-14).
- **Agent routine tools (M33 F2 L3, DD1/DD2/DD7/DD8/DD9).** Four native tools (`routines/tools.py`: `list_my_routines`, `schedule_routine`, `change_routine`, `cancel_routine`) built by `RoutineToolProvider.get_tools_for_agent(config, *, agent_name)` (`routines/tool_provider.py`, a deliberate deviation from DD1's file name, mirroring `notes/tool_provider.py`). It returns `[]` for the `default` agent and unless `config.routines_self_config_enabled`; otherwise four fresh instances. `AgentFactory.__init__` takes `routines_provider` (default `RoutineToolProvider()`); `create` registers the tools beside the notes tools outside the `disabled_names` filter, seeds them into `loaded_schemas`/`loaded_tool_names`, and binds them with `bind_agent(agent)` after `Agent(...)`. `capabilities_from_registry` and the prompt layers are unchanged. The tools are not on the self-configuration loopback surface (its four-tool pin stands).
  - **Writes.** Every write checks `TurnContext.routine_writes` against `AGENT_ROUTINE_WRITES_PER_TURN` (5; with no `TurnContext` it refuses "Routine changes need a chat turn.", no fallback counter), computes `compute_taint`, resolves `session_id = turn.session_id or current_chat_session_id` (the chat a proposal came from, not a run target), accepts a JSON string of an object up to 2000 characters for `schedule` only, builds a `ZoneLoader` over `time_zones.load_effective_zone`, calls `service.agent_change`, and maps `RoutineFieldError`/`NameRuleError` (`.message`) and `RoutineScheduleError`/`RoutineLimitError`/`RoutineNameTaken` (`.detail`) to `ValueError(sentence) from None`. A success, applied or pending, counts one write; a refusal counts none. The switch is re-read inside `agent_change` at call time, so a cached `Agent` refuses after the item is turned off.
  - **Results echo only the id and the text the agent passed (DD7).** Applied: "Scheduled '{name}' (id {id}) for {next}." (plus " It was flagged for your operator to check."), "Changed routine {id}.", "Cancelled routine {id}."; pending: "Asked your operator to approve '{name}'. It won't run until they do." and the change/cancel equivalents ("This replaces your earlier pending change to that routine." when `replaced`). Every tool, `list_my_routines` included, re-checks the default-agent and Routines-switch gate at call time through `service.require_routine_tools_on` (a cached agent whose switch was turned off refuses with: Routine tools are turned off for this agent.). `list_my_routines` has `truncation_direction = "head"`, counts no write, and lists routines (name, id, who made it, on/off/done, schedule in words, next run, flag and sources, prompt; nothing about where it runs) then pending proposals; its first line is `FLAGGED_CONTENT_MARK` when anything listed is flagged, and "You have no routines." when empty.
  - **`routines/describe.py`.** `schedule_sentence(...)` (a safe wrapper over `recurrence_text.describe`; "an unreadable schedule" on failure), `describe_next(next_run_at, zone, *, now=None)` (`today 3:40 PM` / `tomorrow ...` / dated, 12-hour, through the `time_zones` formatters); tool results and the listing never say where a routine runs (every run is a new chat) and never carry a chat title or channel name.
  - **Taint module (`the_one/agent/taint.py`, `the_one/agent/tool_names.py`).** `compute_taint`, `_add_source`, `UNKNOWN_*` and `TRUSTED_TOOL_NAMES` moved here from `notes/tools.py` (which re-exports every name); the tool-name constants (`NOTES_TOOL_NAMES`, `ROUTINE_TOOL_NAMES`, `CURRENT_TIME_TOOL_NAME` re-exported from `time_zones`) are in the leaf `tool_names.py`, which imports neither `notes` nor `routines`. `TRUSTED_TOOL_NAMES` = notes tools, the four routine tools, `current_time`, `search_tools`, `continue` (a recorded behaviour change: `current_time` no longer flags notes). A `list_my_routines` result containing `FLAGGED_CONTENT_MARK` is untrusted with source `routine:listed`. A USER message whose `routine_untrusted_sources` is a non-empty list adds `routine:{routine_name or "unnamed"}` plus those sources; `[]` and `None` (a Flight 1 marker) add nothing. The current run adds the same from `turn.routine_sources`. The scan stays pure.
  - **Fields.** `TurnContext` gained `routine_id`, `routine_name`, `routine_sources`, `routine_writes`. `Message.routine_untrusted_sources: list[str] | None` is USER-only, written by the codec whenever not `None` (empty list included), read back, not spread into UI events and sent to no provider (a cross-provider test pins it). `runner.run_routine` puts `untrusted_sources` (the routine's sources when `untrusted`, else `[]`) into `turn_info["routine"]`; `_process_and_broadcast` builds its `TurnContext` from it; `_bind_turn_user_marks` binds the snapshot (a list on routine turns only) at every save site, including the pause break, so a later resume (whose `TurnContext` deliberately carries no routine fields and a fresh `routine_writes`) is covered by the saved message. Clearing the flag or deleting the routine later does not change a chat's history.
  - **Races (DD9).** A cancel of the running routine ends the run quietly: `_post_steps` and `finish` re-read no routine row (audited; `finish` of a cascaded run is a no-op), and a test pins no error-level log.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_routine_tools.py`, `tests/test_routine_tools_taint.py` (the DD7 deterministic proof), `tests/_routine_tools_env.py`, plus additions to `test_routine_turn_path.py`, `test_routines_runner.py` and `test_llm_provider_cross_provider_history.py`. `tests/test_notes_tools.py`'s trusted-set pin gained the five names. `evals/checks.py::_taint_expected` imports `TRUSTED_TOOL_NAMES` from `the_one.agent.taint` (its digest in `tests/test_evals_freeze.py` was updated for that one import line); leg 4 re-measures the evals.
- **Tables (schema v45).** `agent_routines` (`id`, `agent_name` FK `ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE`, `name`, `prompt`, `schedule` JSON, `enabled`, `finished_at`, `next_run_at`, `anchor_at`, `created_by` `operator|agent`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; `UNIQUE (agent_name, name COLLATE NOCASE)`; index `(enabled, next_run_at)`) and `agent_routine_runs` (`id`, `routine_id` FK `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `trigger_kind` `schedule|catch_up|manual`, `outcome` `running|ok|failed|skipped|missed|interrupted|stopped`, `reason`, `due_at`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `session_id`, `repeats`, `reason_code`; index `(routine_id, started_at DESC)`). **Schema v49 (M33 F4 L02)** dropped the seven `target_*` columns (`target_kind` and its CHECK, `target_session_id`, `target_platform`, `target_connector_id`, `target_channel_id`, `target_thread_id`, `target_peer_id`) and `agent_routine_runs.new_chat` by guarded `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN`s (the v38 idiom; a no-op where the tables are absent), and stripped the target from every proposal with frozen `_v49_*` helpers (`payload["target"]`, `base["target_kind"]`/`["target_session_id"]` removed; a pending `update` left with an empty payload is set `superseded` with `resolved_at`; an unreadable JSON row is left as it is and logged). The listed columns are the shape after v49. A rename follows through `ON UPDATE CASCADE`; the agent upsert (`ON CONFLICT(name) DO UPDATE`) never touches them. Any future agent-keyed table must declare `ON UPDATE CASCADE` too.
- **Package `the_one/routines/`.** `limits.py` (stdlib-only constants and `RoutineScheduleError` / `RoutineLimitError` / `RoutineNameTaken`, each with `.detail`), `recurrence.py` and `recurrence_text.py` (pure; never read the clock) and `schedule.py` (three wall-time helpers), `models.py`, `repository.py` (`AgentRoutineRepository`, `RoutineRunRepository`). Every repository timestamp goes through `_now_iso()` = `time_zones.utcnow().isoformat()` (called through the module so `frozen_clock` bites); all are `+00:00` ISO strings, so string comparison orders them, including a zero-microsecond value against a fractional one.
- **Calendar recurrence (M33 F3 L3-L4, DD1-DD7, DD12).** One stored shape: `{start: {date: "YYYY-MM-DD", time: "HH:MM"}, repeat: null | {every, unit, days?, month_by?}, ends: {kind: "never"} | {kind: "on", date} | {kind: "after", count}}`. `unit` is `minute|hour|day|week|month|year` (`every` caps in `recurrence.EVERY_MAX`); `days` (week only) are Monday = 0 numbers; `month_by` is `{by: "day"}` or `{by: "weekday", nth: 1|2|3|4|-1}`. `repeat: null` is a one-off. **Two kinds of start.** An instant-anchored schedule (a one-off, and the `minute` and `hour` units) stores the exact UTC instant in `agent_routines.anchor_at` and every calculation is elapsed time from it; `start` is only its wall-clock rendering in the zone. A wall-clock schedule (`day|week|month|year`) has a null `anchor_at` and uses `start` directly, resolved through `schedule.resolve_wall_time` (a spring-forward gap runs once at the end of the gap, a repeated time on its first occurrence). **Occurrence rules.** The start is inclusive when it matches the rule and otherwise only begins the series; "after N" counts from the start deterministically; an ended series is Done after its last occurrence; a month day that does not exist in a month is skipped (31st, Feb 29); `month_by` weekday rules pick the nth or last weekday. **Input.** `recurrence.normalize(raw, *, zone, now, min_ahead=None) -> (schedule, anchor)` accepts only the canonical shape (`start` as `{date, time}` or the relative `{in: {amount, unit}}`, then `repeat` and `ends`); the stored and returned schedule is always canonical. A body with a top-level `kind` (the older `once`/`daily`/`weekdays`/`weekly`/`interval` shapes, removed in M33 F4 L7) is refused with "A schedule is {start, repeat, ends}; the older kind-based shape is no longer accepted." (`MSG_KIND_SHAPE`, a 422 on `loc ["schedule"]` or the tool's error); a stray top-level `at`, `in` or `on` gets the generic "A schedule does not take 'at'." `check(schedule, anchor)` validates a stored pair verbatim (no `min_ahead`, no one-year start limit); proposals and approvals use it. `next_occurrence`, `occurrences_between`, `last_occurrence`, `grace` and `rezone` take the routine's anchor. Sentences come from `recurrence_text.describe(schedule, anchor, zone)` and `warnings(schedule)`; the browser words no schedule. **Refusals.** "This schedule has no runs left." (create, schedule edit, enable and approval; a 422, and a proposal stays pending), "A one-time routine must be at least a minute away.", "That time is more than a year away."; each defined once in `recurrence.py`. **Preview route.** `POST /api/agents/{name}/routines/preview` with `{schedule}` returns `{schedule, anchor_at, text, next_runs (up to three ISO instants), last_occurrence, warnings}`; it refuses like create (same 422 shape), writes nothing and is in `EXEMPT` in `tests/test_api_name_rule.py`. `routine_view` carries `schedule_text`; the proposals list carries `payload_/base_/current_schedule_text`; the approve route's stale 409 carries `current_/base_/proposed_schedule_text` (null where the schedule is not among the touched keys); an unreadable schedule gives null, never a 500. **Proposals** fix their schedule and `anchor_at` when made (`agent_change` normalizes once); approval applies that pair verbatim with `start` re-rendered by `recurrence.rezone` for the current zone, the has-future check runs before the repository transaction (so it wins over a stale 409), and `_BASE_KEYS` / `_PAYLOAD_TO_BASE["schedule"]` include `anchor_at`. **Schema v48** rewrites every stored schedule and proposal with a frozen converter (its own legacy parsing): daily, weekdays and weekly take `start.date` from `next_run_at`'s local date (from `created_at` when off or Done) and `start.time` from the legacy `time`; an interval's `anchor_at` is `next_run_at` exactly; a one-off's is `at`; `next_run_at` is untouched. Proposal bases take the routine's migrated pair verbatim when they matched. An unreadable schedule is left, the routine turned off and one guarded `upgrade_failed` run row written. The legacy calculator (`KINDS`, `validate`, `next_run`, `describe_schedule`) is gone; `schedule.py` keeps `parse_utc`, `_parse_hhmm` and `resolve_wall_time`. The evals harness classifies a stored schedule into the output `kind` its checks read (`_schedule_class`, ignoring `ends`; an output name, not input support).
- **The form on the calendar recurrence (M33 F3 L7, DD7).** The Routines tab shows `schedule_text`; the approval queue and its stale view show the server's `*_schedule_text`; `routines-describe.js` words no schedule (it formats next runs, and the preview line: `describeRunAt`, `describePreview`). Every stored recurrence opens in the form (leg 4b's read-only `scheduleLocked` path and `routine-form-schedule-readonly` are gone, and the form always sends the canonical shape). See **Fields**, **Custom recurrence** and **Preview** below.
- **DST (DD3).** A wall time that does not exist (spring-forward gap) runs once at the END of the gap: LA 2026-03-08 02:30 runs at 03:00 PDT. **The zoneinfo trap:** `zoneinfo` alone resolves that wall time to 03:30 PDT by applying the pre-gap offset; `resolve_wall_time` detects the gap by a round trip through UTC and walks whole minutes to the first instant with the post-gap offset (never bisecting on timedelta halves: that gave 02:30:37.5 for Lord Howe's 30-minute gap). A repeated wall time (fall-back) runs once, on its first occurrence (`fold=0`), and the next day's run is the next occurrence.
- **Grace (DD4).** `grace()` is 24 h for a one-off, else min(half the gap from `due` to the next occurrence, 2 h) (daily 2 h, every 2 h 1 h, hourly 30 min). `occurrences_between(start, end, ...)` counts occurrences in `(start, end]` and stops at a cap (1001); legs 3-4 use both for the catch-up and missed-run reasons. This leg never sets `finished_at`; it only reads and clears it.
- **REST (DD9),** under `/api/agents/{name}/routines` (`routines_api.py` since M33 F3 L1; its router is attached to `api.router` at the bottom of `api.py`, and its handlers reach `api.py` helpers such as `get_connection` through the `api` module at call time so tests' patches of `the_one.interfaces.web.api.*` still apply; `_poke_scheduler` stays in `api.py`): `GET` (`{routines, limits}`, name order), `POST` (201), `GET/PUT/DELETE .../{id}`, `GET .../{id}/runs?limit=&before=` (newest first by `started_at`, `limit` 1-200, default 50, `before` a `started_at` cursor). Row: `{id, agent_name, name, prompt, schedule, enabled, finished_at, next_run_at, created_by, created_at, updated_at, last_run, running}` (`running` is `registry.is_running(id)`; the list `limits` also carries `run_timeout_seconds`). Bodies are parsed by hand (no 422 echoes input); field errors are list-shaped on `loc [field]`, names go through `check_name`, a duplicate name (case-insensitive, per agent) is a 409 list on `loc ["name"]`. Limits: prompt 1-4000, 50 active and 200 not-Done routines per agent (raise-only, checked inside the write transaction; see Caps), a `once` at most 365 days ahead and not in the past (a one-off may be given as the relative start `{"start":{"in":{"amount":N,"unit":"minutes|hours|days"}}}`, converted to a `{date, time}` start and an anchor on save). `next_run_at` uses `load_effective_zone(conn).tzinfo` and `time_zones.utcnow()`.
- **The `default` agent** cannot have routines: the name is compared literally (as `factory.py` does) BEFORE the 404 load, so POST is a 422 "Routines need a named agent." even when a `default` row is stored, and every other route is a 404 `Agent not found: default`.
- **PUT precedence.** Any `schedule` key is a schedule change (normalized through `recurrence.normalize`, stored with its `anchor_at`, clears `finished_at`); a body without `schedule` never re-validates the stored one (an overdue unfinished one-off awaiting catch-up stays). A create or schedule change whose schedule has no future occurrence is refused with "This schedule has no runs left." (422 on `loc ["schedule"]`), whether the routine is on or off. `enabled: false` nulls `next_run_at`; `enabled: true` from false continues the grid (no re-anchor: a minute or hour routine keeps its `anchor_at`) and is refused with the same sentence on `loc ["enabled"]` when nothing is left (for example a one-off whose time passed while it was off). A target left out is not re-validated, so a `missing` chat target may stay until re-pointed.
- **No target (M33 F4 L02).** A routine has no target and a request's `target` key is ignored like any unknown key (a PUT whose only key is `target` is an empty update: 200, only `updated_at` moves). Every run starts a new web chat owned by the routine's agent and titled with the routine's name. `GET .../routines/{id}/runs` rows carry `chat_exists` (bool, from one `ChatSessionRepository.existing_ids` query for the page) beside `session_id`; there is no `new_chat`. `schedule_routine` and `change_routine` take no `target`; their `prompt` parameter descriptions tell the agent that each run starts a new chat that sees only the prompt, so the prompt must stand alone (what to check, what to do with the result, including any message to a platform).
- **Retention.** `run_purge_sweep` also deletes, per agent, finished runs (`outcome != 'running'`) whose `finished_at` is older than that agent's `chat_history_retention_days` (same cutoff as its chats, joined through `agent_routines`), and returns `runs_deleted`; `POST /api/_test/purge-sweep` surfaces it. "Purge now" (`DELETE /api/agents/{name}/chat/sessions`) deletes the agent's finished runs too and returns `runs_deleted`; the routines stay. `RoutineRunRepository.finish` on a missing row is a no-op (a routine deleted while its run goes).
- **The routine turn path (leg 3, `routine-turn-path`; no runner yet, nothing sets `turn_info["routine"]`).** `_spawn_routine_turn(session, prompt, turn_info)` spawns `_spawn_message_processing(session, prompt, "routine", None, turn_info)` and registers it through `_register_turn_task` (the registration `_spawn_web_message` also uses), so the chat shows busy, Stop cancels it and shutdown drains it. `turn_info` threads through `_spawn_message_processing` -> `_locked_message_processing` -> `_process_and_broadcast` and is passed on only when given (a stand-in `_process_and_broadcast` with the old signature keeps working). **Keys.** In: `routine` = `{id, name, untrusted_sources}`. Out: `started` (set right after `turn.ran = True`, i.e. the lock was held), `error` (`str(exc)[:200]` from `_process_and_broadcast`; the generic text from `_locked_message_processing`), `paused` (the MCP server's `source_name`, else `source_id`, else "an MCP server"), `cancelled` (set before the cancel re-raise), `step_limit`.
- **Silent cancel.** `_silent_cancel` (a set of tasks) and `_mark_silent_and_cancel(task)`: the `CancelledError` branch of `_spawn_message_processing` skips `_notify_cancelled_once` for a member and discards it in its `finally`; the caller also discards in its own `finally` (a task cancelled before its coroutine starts never runs the wrapper's). For a routine still waiting on the session lock, so the turn ahead of it sees no false `cancelled`.
- **The marker.** `Message.routine_id`/`routine_name` (USER-only; codec writes them only when set, `messages_to_ui_events` spreads them on USER events, no provider converter reads them: a test compares payloads). `_bind_turn_user_marks(session, pre_step_len, routine)` runs immediately before both save sites; bounds- and role-checked (never asserts); it sets the marker on a routine turn and the operator-only mark on a connector chat (it replaced the inline USER mark). The live `user` event of a routine turn adds `routine_id`, `routine_name` and `session_id`. `_create_session(..., title=None)` sets a given title before the row is written.
- **No delivery.** A routine turn posts nothing to a platform: `_process_and_broadcast` neither delivers the reply nor sends a step-limit line for a routine (the agent's `send_to_platform_channel` tool, when the prompt asks for it, is the only path; it is authorized at call time by the connector's binding and allowlist). The shared `_save_and_notify_cancel()` still saves with the marks and sends exactly one `cancelled`. `_process_and_broadcast` sends no `session_updated`.
- **Behaviour change outside routines.** The cancel path now runs `tag_post_step_visibility` and the user mark for a connector chat before saving: a cancelled web turn in a connector chat used to store untagged (peer-visible after reload) messages.
- **The runner (leg 4, `routine-runner`; Run now only, nothing schedules yet).** `the_one/routines/runner.py` imports nothing from `interfaces/`: the web app registers a `_RoutineTurnHost` (`set_turn_host`, in the lifespan after the connector manager; methods `get_or_restore`, `create_web_session`, `spawn_turn`, `mark_silent_and_cancel`, `discard_silent`, `mirror_metadata`, `broadcast`). `POST /api/agents/{name}/routines/{id}/run` (202 `{"status":"started"}`, 404 agent/routine, 409 "This routine is already running.") calls `registry.try_spawn(id, lambda: run_routine(id, "manual"))`; it never touches `next_run_at`/`finished_at`. `RunRegistry` (module `registry`): `try_spawn` checks and adds synchronously (no coroutine is created when running), `is_running`, `drain(5.0)` (leg 5: `scheduler.stop()` calls it; it also makes `try_spawn` refuse until `reopen()`, which the lifespan calls at startup, so a Run now after stop is a 409). Leg 5's scheduler spawns into the same registry with `schedule`/`catch_up`.
- **Run order.** Load the routine fresh by id (gone: return quietly); `host.create_web_session(agent_name, routine.name)` (a failure inserts one finished `failed` row, "Couldn't find or create the chat."); insert the `running` row with that `session_id`; restore (`provider_missing` / `agent_deleted` are `failed`); `turn_info["routine"]`; spawn; `asyncio.wait({task}, timeout)` (never `wait_for`); classify; post-steps; finish the row. **Every run is a new chat (M33 F4 L02).** There is no target resolution, no connector pre-check, no chat reuse and no pause memory: a fresh session has no lock contention (so the old "chat stayed busy" wait and its `chat_busy` code are gone), and a run that needs a sign-in simply fails with "Needs a sign-in to {server}...", the next run starting a new chat.
- **The continuation rule.** `inbound.is_continuable(row, seconds, now)` is the one continuation rule (no `ended_at`, parseable `last_inbound_at` with naive read as UTC, younger than the window); only `resolve_session` uses it (the routine runner no longer does, and a routine run never touches `last_inbound_at`).
- **Time limit.** `routine_run_timeout_seconds()` reads `THE_ONE_ROUTINE_RUN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` at call time (default 900), clamped to at least `_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_SECONDS` (60; tests patch it). Timeout before `started`: `mark_silent_and_cancel` (the turn ahead gets no `cancelled`); after `started`: `task.cancel()` (the partial reply is saved); either way the runner re-awaits the task for up to 5 s before the post-steps. A stalled platform send counts against the limit. A cancelled runner (drain) cancels its turn the same way, waits up to 3 s, records `interrupted` and re-raises; its `finally` discards the task from `_silent_cancel`.
- **Outcomes (`classify(state, turn_info, *, timed_out, timeout_seconds)`, pure).** done and clean: `ok`; step limit: `ok` "Stopped at the step limit."; `paused`: `failed` "Needs a sign-in to {server}. Sign in on its page in Library, then run it again."; `error`: `failed` with the line; any timeout (started or not): `failed` "The run took longer than N minutes."; cancelled without a timeout: `stopped` "Stopped from the chat."; runner cancelled: `interrupted` "The app stopped during this run." Minutes are `max(1, round(s / 60))`.
- **Post-steps** (only when the turn `started`, after the lock is released, each tolerating a deleted chat): `session_metadata["routine"] = {id, name, last_run_at}` (mirrored into the cached session); `session_updated` to the chat's subscribers; then the row is finished. On `interrupted` they are best effort inside the bounded window; the leg 5 startup sweep is the backstop.

- **The scheduler (leg 5, `the_one/routines/scheduler.py`).** `RoutineScheduler(clock, spawn, conn_factory, sleep)`; the lifespan builds it with `lambda: time_zones.utcnow()` (so a patch applied later bites) and the default spawn `runner.registry.try_spawn`, registers it with `set_scheduler`, and starts it last (after `_sessions_push`); `start()` creates one loop task whose first step is `startup(now)`, so boot is not delayed. Each step is in its own `try/except`. **`startup(now)`**: (1) `mark_running_interrupted("The app stopped during this run.")`; (2) one `run_pass(now)` on the stored `next_run_at` values (an outage-due routine is caught up or recorded `missed` before anything is recomputed); (3) the zone check (M33 F3 L4, DD12): the effective zone key vs `app_metadata` `routines_zone`: absent stores the key only, different runs `_check_zone` over `repository.list_not_done()` (every routine that is not Done, on or off) and stores the key, equal does nothing. An instant-anchored routine keeps its `anchor_at` and `next_run_at` and has `start` re-rendered in the new zone (`recurrence.rezone`); a wall-clock routine keeps its local time and has `next_run_at` recomputed when it is on (Done when the rule yields nothing); a wall-clock routine already due is left for the pass. Each rewrite is `repository.follow_zone_change()`, a compare-and-set on the raw stored `schedule`, `anchor_at` and `next_run_at` that also rebases pending proposals whose `base.schedule` equals the pre-rewrite schedule, in one `write_transaction` (an operator edit made between the read and the write wins). The loop repeats the zone check before each pass.
- **`run_pass(now)`.** Per `list_due(now)` row, each in its own `try/except`: `late = now - next_run_at`, `g = grace(...)`; `late <= g` runs (trigger `schedule` under 60 s late, else `catch_up`), otherwise it is a miss. **Advance first, compare-and-set:** `advance_if_due(id, due_iso, next_iso, finished_iso)` applies only `WHERE id=? AND next_run_at=? AND enabled=1 AND finished_at IS NULL`; a one-off gets `finished_at = now` and `next_run_at = NULL` (finished before it runs, even for a miss), a repeating routine `next_run(after=now)`. No row changed means the routine was edited, disabled or deleted after `list_due`: the edit wins, nothing is spawned or recorded. Then, with no `await` in between, `try_spawn(id, lambda: run_routine(id, trigger, due))`; a refusal (already running) inserts a `skipped` row "The previous run was still going." A miss inserts one `missed` row (`trigger_kind` `schedule`): "The scheduled time passed while the app was closed." for a one-off, else "Missed {n} runs while the app was closed." / "Missed 1 run …" / "Missed more than 1000 runs …" with `n = max(1, occurrences_between(due - 1 microsecond, now, cap=1001))`. Run now is independent: it never touches `next_run_at`/`finished_at`, and a due pass during a manual run records `skipped`.
- **Sleep floor.** `next_sleep(now, earliest) = clamp((earliest - now).seconds, 1, 60)`, 60 with no routine. The 1-second floor means a due row that cannot be processed (an unparsable schedule, a failing write) is retried at most once a second, logged each time, instead of spinning. `poke()` sets the loop's event (routine create/update/delete and `PUT /time-settings` call `api._poke_scheduler()`, which tolerates no scheduler), so a new near-term once routine does not wait for the 60-s tick.
- **Stop order.** `stop()`: set `_stopped` (a later `poke`/`run_pass` is a no-op), cancel and await the loop, `registry.drain(5.0)`. The lifespan calls it FIRST in shutdown, above the push-listener teardown, so the `interrupted` writes it causes are still pushed and settled; then `set_scheduler(None)`.
- **Accepted loss (DD5).** A crash, or a `stop()` cancel, between a routine's advance write and its spawn loses that one occurrence with no history row (a one-off then shows Done, a repeating routine skips one run). Routines not yet advanced in that pass are recovered at the next boot.
- **Timestamps.** Every schedule instant (`next_run_at`, `finished_at`, run times) is written through `_now_iso()`/`datetime.isoformat()` in the `+00:00` form, so the text comparison in `list_due`, `earliest_next_run`, `list_active_wall_clock` and the compare-and-sets is correct.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_routines_scheduler.py` (injected clock, fake spawn, recording sleep seam; plus real-lifespan cases over `routine_app`, which lives in `tests/_routine_app.py` since leg 5).

---

### Routines tab (M33 F1 L08)

The agent editor's **Routines** tab (`data-tab-id="routines"`, testid `tab-routines`, panel `agent-tab-panel-routines`, route `#agents/{name}/routines`, whitelisted in `studio-route.js`) sits between Tools and Self-configuration (six tabs; the tablist's last right edge at 1024px is 979px Chromium / 967.5px WebKit, no overflow, pinned by `test_flow_agents_scale.py`). Leg 8 is the list half; leg 9 is the form (below).

- **Modules.** `studio/routines-tab.js` (`App.studio.renderRoutinesTab(panel, agent, callbacks)` returns `{setAgent, activate, deactivate, destroy}`; the early-return create-mode and `default`-agent notices return an inert handle) `studio/routines-row-details.js` (`App.studio.routinesRowDetails.create({fetchRuns})`: one row's expandable details, M33 F4 L03) and the pure `studio/routines-describe.js` (`App.studio.routinesDescribe`: `describeNext(row)` -> the next-run cell ("Done", "Off", "Not scheduled", "Today / Tomorrow / {weekday} {h:mm AM/PM}", "Mon D, h:mm AM/PM" beyond a week), `describeWhen`, `describeTrigger`, `outcomeStatus(run, running)` -> `{state, verb, when, reason, label, text}` (`label` = verb and time, the last-run cell; `text` = verb and the stored reason once, a reason that opens with the verb stands alone; "Running…" for a live row); all times through `App.time`, so the form reuses them). There is no target: every run starts a new chat (M33 F4). Callbacks (`agent-data.js` -> controller bag): `fetchRoutines`, `updateRoutine`, `deleteRoutine`, `runRoutine`, `fetchRoutineRuns(name, id, {limit, before})`, `openRoutineForm`.
- **Lazy, idempotent lifecycle.** The tab makes NO request until `activate()`. `App.ui.tabs` fires `onChange(initial)` while it is being built, before any panel exists, so a mount that opens on Routines (direct route, agent switch, rename remount) is activated at the END of `renderAgentEditor` (`h.tabs.selected() === 'routines'`), while a later switch goes through the controller's `setActiveTab` (`activateRoutines` / `deactivateRoutines`, null-safe). Both `activate` and `deactivate` are idempotent (a re-select of the same tab re-fires `onChange` and must not double-fetch). `studio.js` `deactivate()` calls `deactivateRoutines` (leaving the area), `activate()` resumes it when the shown tab is still Routines, and `destroy()` also drops the `App.time.onChange` (owner: the panel) and `visibilitychange` listeners. A same-identity `h.patch` calls `routines.setAgent(a)`, which swaps the reference only (no refetch, no reset of an open details region, focus or the `gen` guard).
- **The list (M33 F4 L03; rebuilt as cards in HAT fix pass 1, after the operator's "should look more like the tools").** A plain `div[role=list]` (`.routines-list`, no `overflow` rule, so an open actions menu on the last card is not clipped) of one `App.ui.groupTile` per routine (the Tools tab's card): `routine-row` is the tile root (`role=listitem`). Title row: the enable switch (`master`, `tristate: false`, "Enable {name}", `routine-toggle`; hidden on a Done routine, which also loses the subtitle indent via `.routine-row--done`), the name (`routine-name`, the tile's `.ui-group-tile-name`), the "Made by the agent" `App.ui.chip` (inserted after the name; a Done routine shows "Done" in the next-run text, not a chip), then the flag and the "⋯" `App.ui.menu` as the tile's `actions`. Subtitle node: the server's `schedule_text` (`routine-schedule`), then "Next run: {…} · Last run: {…}" (`routine-next`; Done carries the tooltip "Edit the schedule to run it again."; `routine-last` is an `App.ui.status`; the stored reason is NOT a `title`, it is in the details' run list). Bottom: the "Details" expander (`routine-details-toggle`, named "Details for {name}", `aria-controls` the body) whose body (`routine-details`) is the prompt and the recent runs. The menu is the "⋯" `App.ui.menu` (`routine-actions`, icon only, "Actions for {name}", `align: 'end'`) with Run now, Edit and Delete (danger, last). `paint` drives the master through the tile's exposed `toggle` controller (`setDisabled`, `el.hidden`); `nameEl` and `expandBtn` are exposed too. Run now is `aria-disabled` while the routine runs and when it is Done, set after construction through `menu.setItemDisabled(index, bool)` so the 3 s refetch never rebuilds an open menu. Delete asks through `App.confirmDialog({danger: true, ...})` ("Delete {name}?", Delete / Cancel; Cancel and Escape return focus to the "⋯" button); after the delete reload focus moves to the next card's "Details" button, else the previous, else New routine. The header line reads "A routine sends a prompt to this agent on a schedule. Each run starts a new chat." with New routine right-aligned.
- **Rows are patched in place.** `apply()` reconciles `views[id]` (built once per routine, painted by `paint(v)`), so a 3 s poll never rebuilds a row: an open details region, an open menu and focus survive. Rows sort by name. The details (`routines-row-details.js`, one per card, inside the tile body `routine-details`) show the prompt (`white-space: pre-wrap`, textContent) under an h4 "Prompt" and an h4 "Recent runs" (the section title is the h3): the first page is `limit=5` on the first open, "Show more runs" appends the next 20 with `before` = the last line's `started_at`; each line is when, how (Scheduled, Run now, Caught up) and the outcome as `outcomeStatus(...).text`, with a visually hidden ", " after the when and how so a screen reader does not run them together, then **Open chat** (`#chats/{session_id}` through `App.router.href`, only when the run's `chat_exists`; named "Open chat, {routine name}, run at {when}" and renamed on a routine rename), "Chat deleted" when the run had a chat that is gone, nothing for a run with no chat. While open and loaded it reloads (keeping the count shown) when the row's last-run signature changes, and on `App.time.onChange`.
- **Polling.** Every 3 s while active, the document is visible, and a row is `running` or a Run now is pending (a pending Run now clears once the row shows a finished run that differs from the one seen at click, or after `limits.run_timeout_seconds` + 30 s); it also refetches on `visibilitychange` to visible. A `gen` guard drops stale responses; a 404 (agent going away) is ignored.
- **Form hooks.** New routine (`routines-new-btn`, the one New routine control, also with no routines; the empty state is text only) and Edit (`routine-edit`, a menu item) always show for a named agent. They call `callbacks.openRoutineForm({mode, routine, opener, agent, panelHost, limits, callbacks: {createRoutine, updateRoutine}})`; the controller forwards it to `App.studio.routinesForm.open(req)`, whose promise resolves with the saved routine (null on Cancel) and carries `close()`/`focus()`. The tab refetches and focuses the saved row's "⋯" button. The form sits in `.routines-form-host` above the list, one at a time (a second New routine focuses the open form), and the tab discards it on `deactivate()` (leaving the tab) and `destroy()`; unsaved text is never kept.
- **Save model.** As the Rules tab: each action saves at once and refetches; nothing joins General's dirty state or `App.nav`. Delete confirms in a dialog (see The list); a 409 from Run now is the row's "This routine is already running." line and starts polling.
- **Tests.** `tests/browser/test_flow_agent_routines.py` (`agent-routines` lane via `agent_routines_pat`; it was `agent-tabs` before M33 F2 L5). The six-tab pins live in `test_flow_agent_tabs.py`, `test_flow_agent_rules.py`, `test_flow_agent_editor_frame.py`, `test_flow_agents_scale.py` and `test_studio_editor_contracts_static.py`.

#### Routine form (M33 F1 L9)

- **Modules.** `studio/routines-form.js` (`App.studio.routinesForm.open`, the DOM), `studio/routines-custom-recurrence.js` (`App.studio.routinesCustomRecurrence.open`, the Custom dialog) and the pure `studio/routines-form-model.js` (`App.studio.routinesFormModel`: calendar arithmetic on ISO dates with UTC math and no local `Date` getter (`weekdayMon0`, `nthOf`, `isLastWeek`, `addDays`), `presets(startIso)`, `presetRepeat`, `matchPreset`, `availableMode`, `monthByOptions`, `defaultStart`, `startFromIn`, `defaultState(nowMs, zoneParts)`, `scheduleBody`, `formToBody(state, {mode, initial, flagged})`, `bodyToFormState(row)`, `validateCustom`, `customToValue`, `precheck`). The model and the dialog load before `routines-form.js` and `routines-tab.js` (`index.html`).
- **Fields.** Name (limit 64), Prompt (limit from `limits.prompt_chars`), a Starts group, and a Repeat `App.ui.select` (there is no target: every run starts a new chat). **Save** (`routine-form-save`) is disabled in edit mode until `formToBody`'s diff is non-empty (the same diff Save sends), so reverting a change disables it again (focus moves to Cancel when Save had it); a flagged routine always differs (its Save sends `prompt`, the review that clears the flag) and create mode is not gated. **Starts:** `App.ui.datePicker` (`routine-form-start-date`, `min` today in the operator zone, or the earlier of today and the stored start when editing, `today` built on `App.time.parts`) and `App.ui.timeField` (`routine-form-start-time`), defaulting to the next whole hour in the operator zone; "Or start in N minutes/hours/days" (`routine-form-in-amount`, `-in-unit`) fills the two fields from now plus the amount, rounded up to the minute, as you type (what is saved is what is shown). **Repeat** (`routine-form-repeat`): presets computed from the start date, in Google's order (Does not repeat; Every day; Every weekday (Monday to Friday); Weekly on {weekday}; Monthly on day {d}; Monthly on the {first..fourth} {weekday} when the date's nth is 1-4; Monthly on the last {weekday} when the date is in the month's last 7 days; Annually on {Month} {d}), then the extra `current` option (a Custom result, or an existing recurrence that matches no preset, labelled with the server sentence), then Custom…. A preset is held as a KEY in the form state (`mode`), so it keeps its meaning when the start date moves ("Weekly on Thursday" follows the new weekday); one that stops applying falls back to Monthly on day {d}. Matching an existing routine to a preset is exact (`matchPreset`: the recurrence for that date with `ends` never).
- **Body rules.** Create sends `{name, prompt, schedule}` with the canonical schedule `{start: {date, time}, repeat, ends}` built from the state (`repeat` null for Does not repeat; presets mean fixed repeat objects, Custom holds its own `repeat`/`ends`). Edit compares the serialized schedule with the opening state's and sends only what changed (a changed-then-reverted field is untouched), so a stored schedule is never re-sent unchanged; an instant-anchored (minute/hour) schedule that is re-sent resolves its `start` once at save into a new `anchor_at`. The client checks only the name, the prompt and a start date and time; every range and refusal ("This schedule has no runs left.", a start over a year ahead) is the server's.
- **Custom recurrence.** Repeat > Custom… opens an `App.ui.modal` "Custom recurrence" (`routine-custom`): "Repeat every" (`routine-custom-every`, a number, and `routine-custom-unit`: minutes ... years, labels follow the number), "Repeat on" (`routine-custom-days`, the `days` variant, weeks only, Sunday first, values 0 = Monday ... 6 = Sunday, the start's weekday preselected, `routine-custom-day-{0..6}`), a month radio group (`routine-custom-month-by`: On day {d} / On the {nth} {weekday} / On the last {weekday}, months only) and "Ends" (`routine-custom-ends`: Never / On {`routine-custom-ends-date`} / After {`routine-custom-ends-count`} occurrences; the non-chosen controls are disabled). Done validates against the DD1 ranges (`validateCustom`: field errors in the dialog, `routine-custom-*-error`), applies and closes; Cancel and Escape discard; Escape with the ends calendar open closes only the calendar (the date picker stops propagation). The dialog reopens with the recurrence the form holds now (a preset's too). Focus returns to the Repeat select.
- **Preview and warnings.** Any schedule change (debounced 300 ms, one `App.ui.util.latest()` so only the latest response applies) posts the schedule to `POST /api/agents/{name}/routines/preview`; `routine-form-preview` (a polite `App.ui.status` with no LED, never taking focus) shows the server's sentence, "Next: {run}" and, when the series ends, "Last: {date}". A 422 shows its sentence as the schedule error (`routine-form-schedule-error`), the preview's `warnings` are the Repeat field's hint (`routine-form-schedule-hint`, linked by `aria-describedby`).
- **Server schedule input (M33 F3 L4; the aliases removed in M33 F4 L7).** `recurrence.normalize` takes the canonical `{start, repeat, ends}` only: a `{date, time}` start is resolved in the effective zone (a gap gives the gap's end, a repeated time its first occurrence), then the past and one-year checks. Both POST and PUT load the zone before calling it.
- **Errors.** Client pre-checks (name, prompt, a missing start date or time) show on the field or the schedule status line (`routine-form-schedule-error`); a 422 list maps through `App.api.fieldErrors` (a 409 name conflict arrives as a `name` item); anything else lands in `routine-form-error`.
- **`App.ui.checkboxGroup`** (new library control, `ui/choice.js`, inventory row): a fieldset with a legend and one `App.ui.checkbox` per option; `value()` in option order. The nested-radio CSS defect is fixed: the checked-card rule is `.ui-radio-option:has(> .ui-radio-label > .ui-radio-input:checked)`, so an outer card is never styled checked by a nested group.
- **Tests.** `tests/browser/test_flow_agent_routines_form.py` (`agent-routines` lane via the `test_flow_agent_routines*` glob), `tests/browser/test_ui_checkbox_group_contract.py` (`contracts-controls`), a nested case in `test_ui_radio_group_contract.py`, and `on` cases in `tests/test_routines_api.py`. Capture surfaces `agent-routines` and `agent-routines-form`.

#### Self-configuration tab: two item cards (M33 F4 L06, mockup SC2)

The tab is a page eyebrow ("What this agent can change", an `h2` `App.ui.section`, `.self-config-items-section`), the **Agent prompt** and **Routines** cards, then **External access** (an `h2` section, last). `areas/studio/self-config-card.js` (`App.studio.selfConfigCard.create(opts)`, loaded in `index.html` before the other self-config modules) builds a card: an `App.ui.groupTile` with `expand: false` (the new opt-in that omits the expand button and body; catalogue row in `docs/ui-control-inventory.md`, contract test `test_group_tile_expand_false_omits_the_button_and_body` in `test_ui_tile_contract.py`) whose `master` is the enable switch (`tristate: false`, testids `self-configuration-toggle-btn` / `self-config-routines-toggle`), `actions` the "Require approval" switch (`App.ui.toggle`, visible label "Require approval", `aria-label` "Require approval for prompt edits" / "... for routine changes", testids `approval-mode-toggle-btn` / `self-config-routines-approval-toggle`) and `subtitle` the one-line description; the title (`nameEl`) carries `role=heading` `aria-level=3`. Under the tile row: a compact status area (`statusHost`: the existing status, error and live lines keep their testids), then two bordered `App.ui.disclosure` expanders, **Waiting for approval** (the count chip is the existing `pending-count` / `self-config-routine-pending-count` testid, read as part of the summary's name) and **History**. Both are collapsed on arrival; the count is the cue. At zero waiting the expander is hidden and replaced by plain muted text "Waiting for approval (0)" (`pending-queue-empty` / `self-config-routine-proposals-empty`, the focus target when a queue empties; `setUnavailable(text)` swaps in "Waiting for approval: unable to load ...", never an actionable item). Section modules fill the bodies (`ctx.cards.prompt|routines`: `.queue.body`, `.history.body`) and report counts through `ctx.setWaitingCount(item, n)` (set from each queue render; also straight to 0 when the item is turned off, so a withdrawal shows at once); an error or stale answer inside a queue calls `queue.open()`, which reopens an expander the operator collapsed while the request was in flight. Counts fetch on mount (the existing refetches), never lazily. The Agent prompt History body holds the restore list and the chronological log directly (no nested disclosure; `revision-history-details` is now the card's expander and `revision-history-details-summary` its summary, text "History"). The `default` agent keeps the "Routines need a named agent." note, as a plain section, with no Routines card. The approval switches stay enabled while the item is off (the flag is stored either way, as before). Testids: kept every pre-existing switch, status, queue, history and external id; new `self-config-card-{prompt,routines}`, `self-config-prompt-waiting`, `self-config-routines-waiting`, `self-config-routines-history-details` (each with a `-summary`); retired `revision-history-summary`. Tests: `tests/browser/_self_config.py` (`open_expander`, `expander`) opens an expander before a test touches its body.

#### Self-configuration tab: Agent prompt and Routines items (M33 F2 L5)

(Superseded in layout by the cards above; the behaviour below is unchanged.)

- **Two items.** The tab (`areas/studio/self-configuration-tab.js`, the assembler) builds, in order: Agent prompt (today's sections, relabelled in `self-config-status.js`: "Let this agent edit its description and system prompt", "Require approval for prompt edits", sections "Agent prompt" and "Prompt approval"; testids and the status words `enabled`/`disabled`/`Approval mode is on|off` are unchanged, so the existing self-configuration tests moved only by the two label constants in `tests/browser/_self_config.py`), its queue, history and External access modules, then Routines. The tab handle is now `{setAgent, destroy, deactivate, activate}`: `activate` (called from `setActiveTab` in `agent-editor-controller.js` when the tab becomes the shown one) refetches both queues.
- **Routines item.** `areas/studio/self-config-routines.js` (`App.studio.selfConfigRoutines.build(ctx)`): the "Let this agent manage its routines" and "Require approval for routine changes" switches (`self-config-routines-toggle`, `self-config-routines-approval-toggle`; each posts at once through `PUT /api/agents/{name}/self-configuration/routines`, disables while in flight, restores and shows `self-config-routines-error` on failure; turning Routines off with pending changes says "Pending changes were withdrawn."), one polite live line (`self-config-routines-live`), and the read-only "Changes by the agent" history (resolved proposals, 20 a page, `Show older` with a `before` cursor; one request per resolved status merged client-side, because the route filters one status at a time; `superseded` is worded **Withdrawn** everywhere). `areas/studio/self-config-routines-queue.js` holds the "Waiting for approval" list: keyed in-place reconcile (a refetch never rebuilds an item, so an open stale panel survives), Approve and Dismiss per item (both disabled while a request is in flight), the stale 409 (`detail.error === 'stale'`: a warning banner, the touched fields now vs proposed, "Approve anyway" posting `?override=true`; cancel items too), `gone` (item removed, "The routine is gone; the change was withdrawn."), and a 422 sentence in the item's own error line. After Approve or Dismiss focus moves to the next item's Approve (else the previous one's), or to the "Nothing waiting." empty state when the queue empties. The `default` agent shows "Routines need a named agent." and no switches.
- **Proposal display.** `base` is raw stored columns read straight into the display view; `payload` is the validated REST request. A create shows schedule and prompt (and the switch when it is off); a change shows only the touched fields as "before → after" (the prompt as two code blocks); a cancel shows the routine as it stands. The stale 409's `current_value`/`base_value` are stored-column maps over the touched keys and `proposed_value` is the payload.
- **Network callbacks** (`agent-data.js`, passed through `agent-editor.js` and the controller): `fetchRoutinesItem`, `putRoutinesItem`, `fetchRoutineProposals`, `approveRoutineProposal`, `dismissRoutineProposal`; the approve 409s keep their `{error, ...}` object on the thrown error's `detail`.
- **Origin and flag elsewhere.** The Routines tab shows "Made by the agent" (an `App.ui.chip`, `routine-origin`, `created_by === 'agent'`) and `App.ui.noteFlag({subject: 'routine'})` (`routine-flag`) on a flagged row, patched in place on every poll; the drawer's Routines group shows the flag (`chat-drawer-routine-flag`); `routinesFormModel.formToBody` always sends `prompt` when the routine being edited is flagged (the form's `ctx.flagged`), so saving the form (the operator's review, flight DD7) clears the flag server-side, and the form shows "Saving marks this routine as checked." under the prompt. `App.ui.noteFlag`'s new `subject` option is `'note'` (default) or `'routine'`; anything else throws.
- **Tests.** `tests/browser/test_flow_agent_routines_self_config.py` in the new `agent-routines` lane (`agent_routines_pat` = `test_flow_agent_routines*`, which moved out of `agent-tabs`; provisional 300 s budget until the Batch B gate); proposals and agent-made routines are seeded by `seeders.seed_routine_proposal` / `seed_agent_routine` (direct sqlite, since no REST route creates them) and one test runs the real chain (a mock `schedule_routine` tool call). The `noteFlag` contract is in `test_ui_feedback_contract.py`; the drawer flag is in `test_flow_agent_routines_self_config.py`.

## Time zones (M28)

`the_one/time_zones.py` (M28 F1 L1) is a leaf module: stdlib, pydantic, `tzlocal` and `tzdata` at module level, the DB repository imported lazily inside the async functions, and no `the_one.agent`/`tools`/`interfaces` import (pinned by an `ast` test).

- **Names.** `zone_names()` (every `Z` name plus every `L` link name) and `canonical_zones()` (sorted `Z` names) parse `tzdata/zoneinfo/tzdata.zi` once via `importlib.resources`, cached. `is_known_zone(key)` is `isinstance(key, str) and key in zone_names()`: `localtime`, `posixrules`, `../x` and non-strings are refused, and a zone string is never used as a path.
- **Detection.** `detect_system_zone()` returns `tzlocal.get_localzone_name()` when known, else `None`; it calls `tzlocal.reload_localzone()` when the last reload is over 60 s old (module timestamp), with warnings suppressed.
- **Setting.** `TimeZoneSetting` (`extra="forbid"`, `mode`, `zone`; the validators raise the stable `zone_unknown`/`zone_required` messages the PUT route classifies). Stored as `model_dump_json()` under `SETTING_KEY = "time_zone"` in `app_metadata`; an absent, unparsable or invalid stored value loads as the default with one warning. `resolve()` returns an `EffectiveZone(key, tzinfo)`; with no detected name the key is `None` and the tzinfo a fixed `timezone(offset, name)` from the host. `load_effective_zone(conn=None)` never raises.
- **Formatters** (`tool_lines`, `date_line`, `render_date_line`, `clock_text`, `offset_label`, `offset_minutes` (the int form for the client, squawk 0183), `zone_label`, `zone_rows`) are pure and locale-neutral: no `strftime`, fixed English weekday/month tables, the offset hand-built as `UTC+05:30` (ASCII hyphen-minus), the abbreviation omitted when `tzname()` is empty, starts `UTC+`/`UTC-`, or is purely numeric (`^[+-]\d{2,4}$`, e.g. `+14`, `-03`, `+0545`, which would only repeat the offset).
- **Seams.** `utcnow()` and `detect_system_zone()` are called through the module namespace; tests patch them (the `frozen_clock` fixture in `tests/conftest.py`).
- **Routes** (`interfaces/web/api.py`, behind the credential gate): `GET`/`PUT /api/time-settings` returns `mode, zone, system{zone,offset}, effective{zone,canonical,label,offset,abbreviation}, tool_enabled, preview{tool,prompt,prompt_without_tool,clock}` (`tool_enabled` is false only for a `tool_configs` row `current_time` with `enabled` false); `GET /api/time-zones` returns `{"zones": zone_rows(utcnow()), "links": zone_links()}`. A PUT 422 `detail` is always one sentence, never a list. `/api/info` gained `date_prompt_example` and `date_prompt_example_without_tool`, rendered from placeholders with no DB or `tzlocal` read.
